# Validation — 16 October 2026

This evidence covers the native Rust/Iced prototype's imports, bounded CAM previews, cumulative stock, adaptive stock surfaces and application workflows. In the Sign in, LinuxCNC post, Planner analysis, AI planner, Materials and speed/feed checks, Planner progress, Planner pictures, Workholding, Leftover stock and planner tools, UC100 machine control and UC100 machine setup sections, each statement is backed by a unit test in the source unless it is marked as measured, inspected, a live check or found in review; those are not repeated by a test. It does not establish production machining, collision verification (beyond the vise jaws and clamps a setup models, as boxes: see Workholding), production G-code beyond the checked LinuxCNC 3-axis and indexed A subset or machine kinematics. Windows is covered only by the runs recorded below (the test suite and the Control page), and Linux by the test-suite run recorded below (see the platform table in the README); neither has had a full verification.

## Sign in with ChatGPT

- Verified against a loopback stand-in for OpenAI's servers; no test that runs contacts OpenAI or the Keychain (the one Keychain test is ignored).
- The stand-in signs its ID tokens with an RSA key that each test run generates in memory (aws-lc-rs, a dev-dependency) and publishes as its signing keys; no private key is committed (issue #57).
- First sign-in registers the app, exchanges the code with PKCE and validates the ID token (RS256 signature, issuer, audience, expiry, nonce). Wrong state, denial, timeout and each invalid token are refused.
- With no home directory the workspace falls back to the shared temp directory, but the sign-in is never saved there: saving it fails with "Signing in is unavailable: this account has no home directory…", nothing is written, and nothing is loaded or cleared (`a_sign_in_is_refused_under_the_shared_temp_directory`; the same directory is used normally when it is the user's own). The fallback itself is `no_local_data_directory_falls_back_to_the_temp_directory`; the status at start names it (not a test).
- Tokens refresh before expiry and once after a 401, one refresh at a time, saved before use; fatal refresh errors and sign-out clear the saved sign-in. Before refreshing, the client reads the store again: a sign-in for the same account that another copy has already refreshed is adopted without a refresh request (two clients on one store make one token request), and it is refreshed with its own token only when it is itself near expiry. After a fatal refresh, a newer saved sign-in for the same account is used instead of being deleted, and another user's is not adopted. A saved set is adopted only when it expires later than the client's own and holds no refresh token the client has spent: when both saves of a refresh fail, the next expiry-driven refresh sends the rotated refresh-2, not the spent refresh-1 left in the store; a stored refresh-2 set that outlives the client's refresh-3 set is not adopted once refresh-2 is spent; and after a fatal refresh an older saved set is neither adopted nor deleted. A refresh request that may have reached OpenAI without an answer is not repeated with the same refresh token (`a_refresh_that_may_have_reached_openai_is_not_resent`: a token endpoint that reads the request and hangs up is sent refresh-1 once, a connect failure is still retried; `a_stalled_token_endpoint_fails_unanswered_in_bounded_time`: a response timeout is `Unanswered`, not `Retry`), and the store is read again before a retry (`the_store_is_read_again_before_a_refresh_is_retried`: after a 503 during which another copy saved refresh-2's set, that set is adopted and refresh-1 was presented once; checked in review: without the fix both openai tests fail). The client reports a refresh in flight from before its request until after its save, also while OpenAI has not answered yet (the close decision reads this). It also reports a refresh in its save, from OpenAI's answer until after the save, and not while the request is still out. The delete a close starts when it stops waiting for a sign-out marks that client's sign-in abandoned under the lock a refresh's save takes: a refresh whose request was still out when the delete ran finishes and uses its tokens, but its save writes nothing and the store stays empty (checked in review: with the flag check removed, the test fails with the refreshed sign-in saved).
- A plain rate limit (HTTP 429 without one of the `subscription_sharing_*` codes) is retried like a server error, three tries in all, after the pause `Retry-After` asks for in whole seconds (at most 30 s) or the usual one, and is reported as unavailable when the third also fails; the usage limit is not retried (`openai::tests::a_plain_rate_limit_is_retried_but_a_usage_limit_is_not` against the fake server, whose `Resp` can send `retry-after`; `openai::tests::a_retry_after_is_whole_seconds_capped_at_thirty`; issue #179).
- Cancel or Sign out after the token endpoint has answered, while the signing keys are still being fetched, loses no tokens: only the wait for the browser is aborted, the exchange runs to its end as a step of its own, and its result, arriving with nobody waiting, is revoked (the loopback server's JWKS answer is held 700 ms; the test runs both Cancel and Sign out through the app's real tasks and checks that the refresh token is saved or revoked, and that the account matches; before the fix the abort dropped the tokens and the test failed with "tokens were lost") (`smoke::tests::a_cancel_or_sign_out_after_the_token_endpoint_answered_loses_no_tokens`).
- An exchange that fails after the token endpoint answered (the signing keys answer 500, the ID token is expired because of a wrong clock, or the response has no ID token) revokes the refresh token it was issued, best effort and before the error is returned; the error shown stays the validation error, also when the revoke itself fails, and a code the endpoint rejected revokes nothing because no grant exists (the loopback server records each `/revoke`; before the fix none was sent and the test failed on the count) (`oauth::tests::an_exchange_that_fails_after_the_token_endpoint_answered_revokes_the_refresh_token`).
- Every sign-in carries an attempt number in its browser-step and exchange results. Cancelling during the exchange and signing in again adopts the second attempt's account and revokes the first attempt's refresh token: the first exchange ends while the second browser step still waits, and its result is revoked, not adopted; the second attempt stays running and is not dropped (before the fix the first attempt's account was adopted and the test failed) (`smoke::tests::cancelling_during_the_exchange_and_signing_in_again_adopts_the_second_account`). Late results of an earlier attempt (success, failure, browser step) change nothing, and starting a sign-in aborts a handle left behind (`smoke::tests::a_result_of_a_superseded_sign_in_changes_nothing_and_is_revoked`).
- Streamed replies require completion; failures, refusals, early ends and each plan-usage error map to a clear state. Unsupported structured output falls back to plain JSON once. A reply larger than 4 MB is refused, and a refusal is shown as one line of at most 300 characters.
- A `settings.json` that serde rejects (a wrong-typed field) is renamed aside, not overwritten; the account note names the kept file; its host ID and client ID are carried over when they can be read, so the saved sign-in is still restored (`settings::tests::an_unreadable_file_is_renamed_aside_and_its_ids_are_kept`, `smoke::tests::an_unreadable_settings_file_is_kept_aside_and_the_saved_sign_in_still_restores`; before the fix the file was replaced and the second test failed with no file kept aside). A saved sign-in with no client ID (the ID was unreadable too) cannot be refreshed or revoked: it is reported and deleted rather than ignored, and a read with a client ID only reads (`smoke::tests::a_saved_sign_in_with_no_client_id_is_reported_and_deleted_not_ignored`).
- Tokens never appear in settings or Debug output, and the account state the UI shows holds only the name, the email and whether plan usage is granted.
- Live check with the user's account (3 October 2026; not a test): the first attempt was rejected because the installation ID was not in `urn:uuid:` form. After matching OpenAI's reference kit (ID format, `/auth/callback`, client ID and `azp` checks), registration and browser approval succeeded.

## LinuxCNC post

- Golden program for a small Facing job; post settings (tool table/touch-off, retract, G61/G64, dwell) change the output as specified, and each program verifies in every mode.
- Two operations of one tool with opposite spindle rotation: the coolant and the spindle stop (M9, M5) before the second's S and M4, the coolant and the start dwell follow it, and the program verifies; turning the same way, the spindle keeps running from one to the next with no M5 between them, so programs without a reversal and the golden program are unchanged (issue #62; `post::tests::a_spindle_reversal_between_operations_of_one_tool_stops_the_spindle_first`).
- A reversal waits for the spindle to stop: after the M5 of a reversal between two operations of one tool the program dwells the profile's start dwell (`G4 P2.000`) with the spindle off, then the new S word with M4, the coolant and the start dwell follow, and the header says so (`Spindle reversal before operation 2: M5, then G4 P2.000 for the spindle to stop, then S M4 and the 2.000 s start dwell.`); with a start dwell of 0 nothing is written after M5 and the header says that nothing waits for the spindle to stop. The replay insists on the dwell after M5 with the spindle off: a program without the dwells fails with "expected a 2.000 s standstill dwell", and one with the dwell moved after the restart with "the standstill dwell runs with the spindle on". Turning the same way, one G4 and one M5 are written and the header has no reversal line, so the golden program and programs without a reversal are unchanged (issue #160; `post::tests::a_spindle_reversal_between_operations_of_one_tool_stops_the_spindle_first`, `post::tests::a_spindle_reversal_waits_for_the_spindle_to_stop_before_restarting_it`; the note's fixed start is among the comment starts `post::tests::no_comment_begins_with_text_from_the_user_or_the_model` checks). A UC100 run lowers the same reversal to the coolant off, the spindle off, a 2 s dwell tagged with its own block, the reversed start with the coolant and the start dwell (`run::tests::a_spindle_reversal_lowers_to_off_a_dwell_and_the_reversed_start`). The spindle start dwell help says that a reversal waits it after M5, and that nothing waits with 0 (`help::tests::workholding_commands_and_fields_have_specific_help`).
- A Model-origin job whose CAD origin is 3000 mm from the part exports and verifies: the emitter takes the datum off in f64, as the replay does, since in f32 a work coordinate beyond about 2048 mm is off by up to 0.00012 mm, which with the rounding to 0.001 mm can exceed the replay's 0.0006 mm tolerance. The fixture job is turned 30 degrees so that its coordinates lie off a grid that rounds alike either way; it failed the replay before the fix (issue #64; `post::tests::a_model_origin_3000_mm_from_the_part_exports_and_verifies`).
- Coolant: Flood writes M8, Mist M7, Air the machine's Air output (M7 or M8), Off nothing, and M9 ends what was switched on; Air on a machine without an Air output is refused. The coolant help says the same, and that the preview does not simulate coolant.
- A fractional spindle speed (8000.5 rpm) posts as S8001, rounded as the replay rounds it, and verifies.
- All six implemented strategies post as one program and replay exactly: every position within 0.0006 mm, feeds within 0.05 mm/min, and tool, H offset, spindle, coolant and work offset active on every move.
- Corrupted programs are rejected: a moved coordinate, a missing F, the wrong tool, an arc and a dropped line.
- Flipped setups produce separate G54/G55 programs.
- Refusals: other controllers, 5-axis and a fourth axis other than A around X, travel, duplicate tool numbers, selected-point origin, non-LinuxCNC offsets, Air without an output, upstream failures, a setup without enabled operations and the example project.
- Rapid-safety rule tested for accepted retracts and peck re-entry, and for refused unproven descents, sideways rapids, holder depth, feed, rpm and travel (including inch profiles).
- A rapid that descends wholly beside the stock box is accepted (a region shares no area with the box only when it lies beside it on X or Y), while a descent over the stock is still proven only as deep as earlier cuts removed it. Face, then an outside Contour on stock with no front margin, posts.
- The profile's path blending tolerance (G64 P, up to 1 mm) is added to every clearance proof, since the controller may leave the programmed path by that much at a corner (issue #63). A Ø10 mm tool passing 0.5 mm from a clamp with no clearance gap is clear with no blend and with the default 0.01 mm, and refused with 1 mm, the line naming the setup's 0 mm gap; a clearance plane half a millimetre over a clamp's top passes with no gap and no blend, is refused with 1 mm, the limit in the message counting the blend (Z26.0 for a top at Z25.0), and passes 1.5 mm over (`post_check::tests::the_profiles_blend_is_added_to_the_gap`). A rapid descent 6 mm deep, after a 4 mm facing with the same tool, whose Ø10 mm column touches the stock box at exactly the cutter radius is accepted with no blend and refused with 1 mm, the wider column overlapping stock that the facing did not remove (`post_check::tests::the_profiles_blend_widens_the_rapid_column`). Through the whole export and a UC100 run's check, which keeps the profile's blend although it writes no G64, a Pocket whose tool passes 0.5 mm from a toe clamp with no gap exports with the default blend and is refused with G64 P1, with the same line at export, in the run's check and in the browser's marks, and with no blend the run's check passes again; the golden programs do not change (`post::tests::the_profiles_blend_is_added_to_the_gap_at_export_and_on_a_uc100_run`). The Clearance gap help and the blending tolerance help say that the blend is added (`help::tests::workholding_commands_and_fields_have_specific_help`).
- The blend's dip at a corner is kept out of the stock: the clearance plane and every sideways rapid keep 2.5 times the path blending tolerance over the stock top, and the link between two operations of one tool is raised to that height, since at the corner where a retract turns into a traverse the tangent blend leaves the vertical up to P/(√2 − 1) ≈ 2.41 P below the traverse height (issue #161; `post_check::BLEND_HEIGHT`). A Facing whose rapids cross 0.5 mm over the top is clear with no blend and with the default 0.01 mm (a 0.025 mm margin), refused with G64 P1 both at the clearance plane ("The setup clearance plane is 0.500 mm over the stock top: less than 2.5 times the path blending tolerance (G64 P1.000, 2.500 mm), so the blend of a rapid there could dip into material. …") and as a sideways rapid ("Face top: a rapid moves sideways at X15.000 Y15.000 Z0.500, 0.500 mm over the stock top: less than 2.5 times …, so its blend could dip into material."), clear again 2.5 mm over the top, and with retracts to machine home only the rapid is named; a crossing below the top keeps its "below the stock top" line (`post_check::tests::a_sideways_rapid_within_the_blend_margin_over_the_top_is_refused`). Two Facing passes of one tool with clearance points 0.5 mm over the top link at that height with no blend and with the default, and climb to 2.5 mm over the top with G64 P1, while the golden pair 15 mm over the top links as before (`post::tests::a_link_between_operations_of_one_tool_keeps_the_blend_margin_over_the_stock_top`). The fixture job's Facing, retracting between its rows, with its retract and clearance 0.5 mm over the stock top posts with the default blend and is refused with G64 P1 by the one line naming its first crossing and the margin; it passed with G64 P1 before this change, and so does, rightly, the same Facing linking its rows with keep-down feeds, which never crosses at the clearance height (seen in the new test's first run) (`post::tests::a_job_half_a_millimetre_over_the_stock_top_is_refused_with_g64_p1`). The blending tolerance help says so (`help::tests::workholding_commands_and_fields_have_specific_help`).
- A Stock top center or Stock top front-left work zero is refused when the stock was defined in an orientation that is not square to the setup (30° about Z), and accepted again with Model origin. Frames at 0°, 180°, -90° and 90°/90° are square; 30° and 45° are not. Flipped (180°) setups still post.
- Stock faces cut by earlier setups: a Face that removes the whole stock top refuses both stock-based zeros in the turned setup. When an earlier outside Contour removes exactly one stock side (stock with a margin on that side only), Stock top center is refused naming that side (+X, +Y, -X or -Y); Stock top front-left is accepted when only the +X or +Y side is gone and refused when the -X or -Y side is gone, naming that side and the top face near the front-left corner, whose margin the Contour also took; Model origin is accepted in each case.
- Touch-off spots cut by earlier setups: a stock-based zero is refused only when a spot the operator touches off on lies where an earlier cut can have removed material, tested point by point with the stock simulation's own swept-cutter test (11 × 11 points per spot, edges included). The sides are tested in a band from 1 mm to N mm below the current setup's stock top, where an edge finder touches them above the vise, N being 10 mm or a quarter of the stock's height when that is less (rounded down to 0.1 mm): for Stock top center over the middle 10% of each side's length, for Stock top front-left over 5% to 15% of the -X and -Y sides' length from the corner. The top: for Stock top center a square 10% of its shorter side at its centre; for Stock top front-left a square 10 mm on a side (or a quarter of the shorter top side when that is less), 1 mm in from both edges at the corner. The reviewer's two-sided job (the fixture block with 2 mm side margins; setup 1 Face, Pocket, a Contour with bottom 10 or 18 mm and Drill; setup 2 "Underside" flipped about X with a Face) exports with Stock top center and with Stock top front-left, because setup 1's Contour cut only the lower band of setup 2's sides; before this change Stock top center was refused there naming all four sides near their centres (seen in the new test's first run). Stock with a 2 mm margin on +X only, whose Contour (bottom 30) cuts the top 14 mm, refuses Stock top center in a same-orientation setup 2 with "Later: earlier setups have cut the stock's +X side near its centre, so the Stock top center work zero is no longer on the stock. Choose Model origin for this setup.", although the whole-face test finds no face cut, and accepts Stock top front-left. The reviewer's inside-Face job (setup 1 faces only inside the part's top face, "Selected geometry", "Tool inside boundary", leaving a rim of stock at full height about 2.6 mm wide) refuses Stock top center with "Later: earlier setups have cut the stock's top face at its centre, so the Stock top center work zero is no longer on the stock. Choose Model origin for this setup." and now also Stock top front-left, with "Later: earlier setups have cut the stock's top face near the front-left corner, so the Stock top front-left work zero is no longer on the stock. Choose Model origin for this setup."; with 12 mm of stock beyond the part on -X and -Y the corner area is intact and front-left exports, while Stock top center is still refused. On a 100 × 60 × 30 mm stock (band 7.5 mm, corner area 10 mm), 0.5 mm deep cuts at the corner of the top and 5 to 11 mm in from both edges count for front-left only; a cut into the +X side near its centre from Z24 up counts for Stock top center only, and the same cut from Z17 to Z22, below the band, for neither; a cut into the -Y side in the band near the corner counts for front-left only; a short cut in the middle of the top counts for Stock top center only; a whole top faced 0.5 mm deep is named "top face" alone; and an arc around the corner just outside the stock counts for neither, although its cut boxes reach into the corner area. The whole-face test on the earlier cuts' boxes, which reach a tool radius below each cut, now only chooses the words (the face alone when it is wholly cut, the face and spot otherwise) and refuses nothing by itself. Accepted since: the reviewer's two-sided job with setup 1's Contour on a flat 25 mm tool (stickout 65) at bottom 8, whose first side cut is 11 mm below setup 2's top (the reviewer's probe), and the small cube job (examples/preview-cube.obj, 2 mm side margins, 1 mm on top and 3 mm below, 34 × 24 × 14 mm stock; setup 1 Face and a 10 mm Contour at bottom 2; setup 2 flipped about X with a Face), whose Contour stops 5 mm below setup 2's top and whose band is 3.5 mm, both with Stock top center and Stock top front-left. Before this change both were refused naming whole sides ("-X side, +X side, -Y side and +Y side" for Stock top center, "-X side and -Y side" for front-left; seen in the new test's first run for the 25 mm tool and in a probe for the cube). Every other datum test keeps its outcome and message, including the good plan's flipped setup with Model origin and the flipped fixture job with Stock top center; the turned job whose Face removes the whole top still names "top face" alone with the sides near their centres or near the front-left corner, as before. Two inputs of the 100 × 60 × 30 mm test changed, not their outcomes: the corner cut and the whole-top facing are 0.5 mm deep instead of 1 mm, so that they stay above the side band (a cut exactly 1 mm deep reaches the band's upper edge and would also name the sides there).
- Where to touch off: a stock-based zero's work-zero header line names the spots and where along each side they are. On the golden program's 20 × 20 × 10 mm stock it reads "Work zero: Stock top center in G54. Touch off Z0 on the stock top at its centre and X0 Y0 from the four sides near their middles, between 1 mm and 2.5 mm below the top.", and for Stock top front-left "Touch off Z0 on the stock top within 5 mm of the front-left corner, at least 1 mm in from both edges, and X0 Y0 from the -X and -Y sides between 1 mm and 3 mm from the front-left corner, between 1 mm and 2.5 mm below the top." (5% and 15% of each 20 mm side). The front-left line is longer than one comment and goes on in a "Work zero, continued:" line; it is read back across its lines, and no line is longer than 200 characters inside its parentheses. Touch-off program 1's "Before starting" instruction ends "to touch off" and the same words, read back across its continuation lines, and later programs keep "X0 Y0 are unchanged from program 1." without them. The two-sided job's 124 × 84 × 47 mm stock gives 10 mm for the band and the corner area and names each side apart: "from the -X side between 4.2 mm and 12.6 mm and the -Y side between 6.2 mm and 18.6 mm from the front-left corner, between 1 mm and 10 mm below the top"; a 30 × 14.2 × 13 mm stock gives 3.2 mm and 3.5 mm (rounded down) and, rounded inwards to 0.1 mm, 0.8 to 2.1 mm on its -X side (0.71 to 2.13 mm checked) and 1.5 to 4.5 mm on its -Y side; Model origin gives no such words. The comment test also writes a Stock top front-left header (program 1 of 2) at each user-text offset around the line breaks, in both tool modes and with the clearance-plane retract, so the "Work zero, continued:" start is checked. The Stock top center and front-left help cards and the planner's work-zero rule use the same sentences, with "between 5% and 15% of each side's length from the front-left corner" for the millimetre figures (inspected, not a test).
- Model origin: the header gives the part's box in work coordinates and the stock top, and says to touch off on a part wall or the stock top; for the good plan's flipped setup it reads X-60.000 to 60.000, Y-40.000 to 40.000, Z-40.000 to 0.000 mm with the stock top at Z3.000, and with the mesh's file origin 300 mm from the part the box is X-360.000 to -240.000. In touch-off mode, program 1's touch-off note says to touch off on a part wall or the stock top and enter its coordinate from those lines. The plan check passes the same values to the preview, which shows X0 Y0 from the part's −X −Y corner and Z0 from the stock top; the preview's line for the good plan's setup 2 is asserted as rendered.
- A stock top an earlier setup has cut is not offered. The top counts as cut when the stock-based zeros' test finds it cut (every sample point inside an earlier cut's box) or when any sample point lies where an earlier cut can have removed material (from the tool tip up, as the stock simulation carves). With the good plan's setup 2 turned −Y up after setup 1's Contour removed the side margins, the header has no "Stock top", reads Y0.000 to 40.000 Z-40.000 to 40.000 mm, and says "The stock top was cut by an earlier setup; touch off on a part face."; program 1 in touch-off mode says to touch off on a part face; the plan check passes and the preview line gives Z0 40 mm below the part's top with the same sentence. A user's job whose setup 1 faces the whole top, followed by a Model-origin setup in the same orientation, gets the same sentence. So does one whose setup 1 faces only inside the part's top face ("Selected geometry", "Tool inside boundary"), where the whole-face test alone does not find the top cut (checked in review: with the partial-cut test removed, this case fails). The good plan's flipped setup keeps Stock top Z3.000.
- Setups the planner adds keep setup 1's fixture, and a setup's notes are written to its header as a Setup notes comment (after the Fixture and any Workholding comment), cleaned like other comments and split between words into lines of at most 200 characters; each later line begins "Setup notes, continued:", and a word longer than a line is split over lines, not cut off. Every other comment that carries user or model text is split the same way, never cut off, each later line beginning with its lead word and ", continued:" (Job, Machine, Stock, Fixture, Workholding, Setup notes, T#, Operation #, Operation #/#, Suppressed, Before starting). With a 200-character tool name, the touch-off instruction of program 1 and of a later program, and the tool line, each run on over more lines and read back whole.
- No comment begins with text from the user or the model, since LinuxCNC runs a comment that begins with MSG, LOGOPEN, PROBEOPEN and the like as a command. Every comment kind the post writes was checked: the program banner, Job (job title, setup name, offset), Program i of k, Machine (machine name), Work zero, Part in work coordinates, the two Model-origin touch-off sentences, Stock (rotations), Fixture, Workholding, Setup notes, each tool (T#: tool name), each operation in the list and at its start (Operation #: and Operation #/#: with its name), Suppressed (operation name), the continuation lines of each of these, of Job, Machine, Stock and Before starting, Extents, Not checked (in both wordings: see Workholding) and the soft-limits sentence that follows it, the clearance-mode start note, the touch-off notes that name a tool (now "Before starting: load T# …"), the reference-surface note, the note not to touch off on the stock in later programs and the G49 note. A test puts "LOGOPEN,", "MSG," and "PROBEOPEN," at every offset from 0 to 400 characters in a note, a setup name, an operation name, a tool name, the job title, the fixture, the machine name and a rotation field, in every header the post writes (tool table, touch-off program 1 and a later one, clearance retracts, with and without Model origin and its stock top): every comment line, after leading spaces, begins with one of the post's fixed starts, each start occurs, and each program verifies. The good plan posted with those words in its title, setup 2's name, notes and fixture, an operation's name, a tool's name and the machine's name is ready (verified) in both tool modes, with the same result.
- Tools without numbers are numbered T1–Tn when a project is loaded, and that is announced: in the status after Open, Recover previous project and the workspace at start-up, and after `--export-gcode`'s final message (on success and on refusal). A project whose tools are already numbered gets no note.

## UC100 machine control (part 1)

- Profiles: a UC100 machine needs a profile file; the driver loads and checks it, and its messages (missing file, invalid TOML) are shown (`control::tests`, `a_uc100_machine_needs_its_profile_file`, `the_wizard_shows_the_uc100_fields_and_takes_a_picked_profile`).
- Simulated connection: Connect → E-stop → Reset → Idle → E-stop → Disconnect; the identity is SIMULATED; the simulator's state file is written and the real machine's is not (`connects_to_the_simulated_uc100_resets_and_e_stops`).
- Refusals: no UC100 machine, a missing profile; E-stop without a connection does nothing (`connect_reports_a_missing_profile_and_needs_a_uc100_machine`).
- Closing the app while connected disconnects (`closing_the_app_disconnects_the_machine`).
- The Control page renders disconnected, connecting, in E-stop and Idle, and the wizard locks the connected machine's UC100 fields (`the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`).
- Inspected on Windows (11 October 2026, debug build with `AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`, driver commit 7ffe369; not a test): a simulated machine with the driver's `jy5800-investigation.toml` showed Idle after Reset on the Control page, with the SIMULATED badge, the identity line (firmware 1041, hardware and bootloader SIMULATED), X, Y and Z at 0.000 and not homed, the driver's log and the top-bar E-STOP; Esc pressed (the key itself is tested: `smoke::tests::escape_sends_the_estop_from_anywhere_even_while_typing` feeds Escape with a text field capturing it and without, and gets `MachineEStop` both times; Space and Cmd/Ctrl+N/S/O are in `space_holds_the_job_only_when_no_text_field_takes_it` and `command_n_s_and_o_start_save_and_open_a_project`) in the window gave E-STOP ("E-stop pressed") with Reset offered; closing the window ended the app, and only the simulator's state file was written. On that PC `cargo test` gave 769 passed and 2 failed: the two tests that import `examples/four-setup-block.step` need the STEP helper, which that build leaves out.
- Not yet validated: connecting to the real UC100 over USB from AXIS CAM (macOS, attended, no motion).

## UC100 machine control (part 2)

- Status line: after Connect it shows the driver's state instead of "Connecting to …" (`the_status_line_shows_the_machine_state_once_connected`).
- Machine zero and soft limits: with soft limits on X, Y and Z and no switches, the soft limits are off until all three have a machine position; Set machine zero here declares X, Y and Z at 0, one per tick (the driver takes one at a time), and a move below X 0 is then refused; one axis alone; nothing is declared in E-stop (`machine_zero_turns_the_soft_limits_on`). Keep the last position after a reconnect keeps the position saved at the disconnect and sets it (`keep_the_last_position_declares_where_the_machine_was_left`).
- Homing: offered only when every axis has a home switch; with switches, Home homes X, Y and Z and sets them (`home_needs_home_switches_on_every_axis`).
- Soft limits off: a profile with no soft limits gets the same amber "Soft limits are OFF" box as an unreferenced machine, with its own sentence (add `soft_min` and `soft_max`), the unreferenced one saying to Home or set machine zero, and none once the limits are on; the machine drawing shows the assumed travel dashed and captioned "travel not configured", with the position set or not, never as "where the tool tip can travel", while a profile's real soft-limit box keeps that caption (`a_profile_with_no_soft_limits_gets_the_same_amber_warning_and_an_assumed_box`, issue #73). The dashing itself is not checked by a test (the Linux renderer's screenshots are unreliable).
- Jogging: a held jog keeps going past the driver's 400 ms timeout and stops on release; nothing jogs in E-stop and an axis the profile lacks is never sent (`a_held_jog_keeps_moving_and_stops_on_release`); a step moves exactly one step (`a_jog_step_moves_one_step`); a jog into a soft limit ends at the limit and needs a new press, and a step past it is refused (`a_jog_into_a_soft_limit_ends_at_the_limit`); a tick that read the Jog snapshot the driver published while ending a jog itself sends it again only while it is the same jog, so a jog the driver ended cannot be restarted by the keepalive, and a new press jogs again (`control::tests::a_jog_the_driver_ended_is_not_restarted_by_the_keepalive`); jog keys work only on the Control page, not while a text field has the focus, and not when the key is up before the focus answer arrives (`jog_keys_only_on_the_control_page_and_not_while_typing`, `control::tests::typing_finds_a_focused_text_field`). With the Controller configuration view open, which hides the jog controls, the arrow and Page keys do not jog and Space does not resume, also when the view opens between the key going down and the focus answer; with it closed they do again (`keys_do_not_jog_or_resume_while_the_setup_view_hides_the_jog_controls`).
- The A axis on the Control page (LIO-185, simulated UC100): A neither steps nor holds a jog before Set machine A; then a 15° step turns it 15° whatever the mm step, a held A jog outlives the driver's 400 ms through the keepalive, Zero A sets G54's A offset and leaves G55's, `G0 A90` by MDI ends at work A 90°, and the page shows 90.000°, A+/A−, the degree steps and Zero A; an E-stop clears A, after which neither a jog, a calibration move nor `G0 A0` moves it and the page and the calibration's Move say to set machine A; a reconnect keeps G54's A offset and where A was left; without `[axes.a]` the page shows nothing of A (`a_jogs_and_zeroes_in_degrees_and_never_moves_before_set_machine`). A state file of X, Y and Z only loads with every offset (`a_state_file_without_a_loads_with_its_offsets`, `control::tests::a_state_file_of_x_y_and_z_is_padded_to_six_axes_and_any_other_left`). Cycle Start lists A among the axes without a machine position from connecting and after every E-stop, and alone once X, Y and Z are set; Set machine zero here sets A too (`cycle_start_names_a_while_it_has_no_machine_position`). Home and End are A's jog keys (`jog_keys_only_on_the_control_page_and_not_while_typing`).
- Work zero: Zero X and Zero all; G55 and back to G54; nothing in E-stop (`work_zero_and_the_coordinate_systems`).
- Spindle and coolant: CW at an RPM and Stop; CCW without a direction output and a reversal are not sent; a bad RPM starts nothing; flood only when configured; CCW with a reverse output (`spindle_and_coolant_follow_the_profile`).
- Overrides: feed override 150 %, clamped at 200 %; limit override only with limit switches (`feed_and_limit_override`).
- MDI: a line runs and the field clears; an empty line sends nothing; a line in E-stop stays in the field (`an_mdi_line_runs_and_the_field_clears`).
- The machine model: the tool tip is the machine position, the beam moves with Y only, the carriage with X, the table never; the moving parts change colour until the position is set; back faces are culled and faces drawn far to near, table first (`machine_view::tests`). The Control page renders with soft limits before and after machine zero, with switches, and while jogging (`the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`).
- Inspected on Windows (11 October 2026, debug build with `AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`, driver commit 36885a1, the driver's `jy5800-investigation.toml`, simulated, isolated workspace; not a test). Real input to the window: Set machine zero here (log "X/Y/Z declared at machine 0.000", "Soft limits on: X 0…390, Y 0…280, Z -55…0 mm"); → held 1.5 s by keyboard (no key repeats from synthetic input) jogged X to 12.3 mm with X+ highlighted and stopped on release; Y+ held with the mouse jogged and stopped on release; pressing X+ and moving off the button with the mouse button still down stopped the jog; ↑ with the project-name field focused did not jog; ← held 3.2 s stopped at X 0.000 ("Soft limit reached") and did not restart; an MDI line typed with Enter moved the machine to X 200, Y 150, Z −20 and the model followed; closing the window saved that position in the simulator's state file. On that PC `cargo test` gave 785 passed and 2 failed (the two STEP tests, as before).
- Not yet validated: any of this on the real machine. The travel (390 × 280 × 55 mm) in the profile's soft limits and the model's dimensions are assumed, not measured.

## UC100 machine control (part 3: running setups)

- Driver (UC100-driver, branch `axis-cam-run`, `tests/simulated.rs` and `controller::tests`, through the real controller and the simulated UC100): a job of actions reaches its targets, reports each action's tag as the current line and finishes ok (`a_job_runs_its_actions_and_reports_their_tags`); it is refused before anything moves outside the soft limits, with a NaN, a zero feed, a negative dwell, a probe, Override limits on, or while another job runs (`a_job_is_checked_before_anything_moves`); Hold stops it, Resume continues, Stop ends it (`a_job_holds_resumes_and_stops`). A feed hold keeps the rest of a dwell (`feed_hold_keeps_the_rest_of_a_dwell`). After a pause, Resume returns to the pause point Z up, across, down (`resume_after_a_jog_returns_to_the_pause_point_from_above`), moves the rest of a job with a work zero change (`a_work_zero_change_in_a_pause_moves_the_rest_of_the_job`), stays paused when the rest would leave the soft limits (`resume_is_refused_when_the_rest_would_leave_the_soft_limits`), refuses a spindle turning the other way and restarts a stopped one (`resume_restores_the_spindle_and_refuses_a_reversed_one`), and a G-code line after a jogged M0 keeps the program's own X and Z (`a_gcode_program_jogged_in_a_pause_continues_from_its_own_position`). A failed PWM spindle command fails the job, at the start and at the end (`a_failed_spindle_command_fails_the_job`). The simulator's home switch reads active while its axis is on it (`homing_from_the_switch_references_the_axis`). A rotary A axis (LIO-182) jogs in degrees at its own speed (`a_jogs_in_degrees_at_its_own_speed`), a move of A alone takes the time `axes.a` gives whatever its feed (`an_a_only_move_takes_the_time_its_limits_give`), a job that indexes A between XYZ cuts is refused until A is declared and then ends with A at the commanded angle (`a_job_indexes_a_between_xyz_cuts`), and moves past A's soft limits are refused (`a_moves_past_the_a_soft_limits_are_refused`); in AXIS CAM, `control::tests::a_job_indexes_a_between_xyz_cuts_on_the_simulator` sends such a job through the Control page's connection and finds A at 90°, with Z retracting to its soft maximum, Z 0. A move that reverses onto a soft limit exactly finishes (LIO-200): `control::tests::a_retract_to_the_z_soft_limit_finishes_on_the_simulator` runs Z down to −2, a cut along X, a rapid back up to the Z 0 limit and a rapid along X to the end, which used to stop with "Soft limit reached". The driver's simulator now moves a reversing record by what the encoder counts for it (`sim::tests::records_move_the_counters_to_the_encoder_position`, exact) and counts whole steps against the limits: on the limit, or less than half a step past it after the host re-anchors, does not trip, a step past does (`sim::tests::a_move_reversing_onto_a_soft_limit_stops_there_and_one_step_past_trips`, `sim::tests::a_counter_less_than_half_a_step_past_a_limit_reads_as_on_it`, `tests/simulated.rs` `a_retract_that_reverses_onto_the_z_soft_limit_finishes`); a limit between two steps goes to the outer step (`config::tests::soft_limits_between_steps_go_to_the_outer_step`).
- Lowering: a tool run's blocks become machine moves at plan − datum + work offset, feeds in mm/s, rapids without a feed, spindle, coolant, dwells and the end, zero-length moves dropped, the first retract at the start X/Y, each action tagged with its block, and a block maps back to its plan move (`run::tests`).
- A two-tool setup (the fixture block's Facing and Drilling) on a simulated JY5800-like machine: checked into two tool runs; tool run 1 runs to the end with progress by operation and block and ends at the clearance plane with spindle and coolant off; Cycle Start waits for the drill's Z touch-off; Record reference and Set Z from reference move work Z by the new tool's length; a moved X zero holds Cycle Start back until it is put back; tool run 2 runs at the new Z offset (`a_setup_runs_tool_run_by_tool_run_with_a_manual_tool_change`). After a tool run started, a setup whose work zero moved in the project is refused once the Control page has checked it again, naming the old and the new zero and the distance (its origin moved to a corner; the same origin on a stock grown 10 mm to one side, 5 mm), Cycle Start does nothing, putting the setup back lets it run, and picking it again starts over (`a_work_zero_moved_in_the_project_between_tool_runs_is_refused`).
- Cycle Start waits, saying why, for: a work zero that keeps the job inside the soft limits (checked once X, Y and Z have a machine position), the setup's work system, a check after an edit, the AXIS CAM travel agreeing with the profile, a spindle direction output for CCW, a mist output for mist (`cycle_start_waits_for_what_the_run_panel_lists`). Without a machine position (none set yet, or cleared by an E-stop) Cycle Start does not wait: the Run panel says it runs with the soft limits off and without checking the job against the machine's travel, and with only a work zero tool run 1 runs to the end at the work zero's clearance plane, as a tool run moves only in work coordinates (`cycle_start_runs_without_a_machine_position`). Hold, Resume and Stop of a tool run; an E-stop ends it with the driver's reason (`a_tool_run_holds_resumes_and_stops`). A spindle left running by hand the other way, on a profile with a reverse output, holds Cycle Start back before anything moves, saying to stop it, and the machine stays Idle with its position; stopped, or running the tool run's way, the tool run starts (`cycle_start_refuses_a_spindle_running_the_other_way`, issue #41).
- The 3D view's live tool is at machine − work offset + datum with the running tool's size. While it runs, planning is locked: Play, Generate, an edit and the AI planner are refused and playback does not move; Cycle Start waits for a background check under way (`the_3d_view_shows_the_machine_and_planning_waits_for_it`). Machine zero is offered only for axes without a home switch, Home for switched ones (`the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`).
- Inspected on Windows (11 October 2026, debug build with `AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`, driver commit fac396e, the driver's `jy5800-investigation.toml`, simulated, isolated workspace with the fixture block's Facing and Drilling at 1000 mm/min, coolant Off, 5 mm clearance; not a test). Real input to the window: after Set machine zero here, picking the setup listed two tool runs (T1 Flat 10: Facing; T3 Drill 4: Drilling) and kept Cycle Start grey, the work zero still at machine zero putting X, Y and Z outside the soft limits; an MDI line to X195 Y140 Z−8 and Zero all enabled it. Tool run 1 ran with "operation 1 of 1: Facing · block 10 of 36" and a progress bar; Hold held it and Resume continued; on the Work page the machine's tool was drawn in red and moved over the part. At its end the panel marked it done, showed the tool change and kept Cycle Start grey ("touch off its Z first") until Set Z from reference; tool run 2 then drilled at the new Z offset (work Z = machine − (−5)) and both were done. Found on the way: the first drawing of the live tool was hidden behind the part (fixed in ff212cf); and pressing Generate & play while a tool run cut made this debug build starve the driver, which E-stopped with "Motion buffer ran empty mid-move (USB or computer too slow)" while the Run panel showed that reason and asked for machine zero again (the profile keeps 40 ms of motion queued). Generate & play only computes and plays the on-screen preview; its stock meshing on every core starved the driver's thread, which has no raised priority on Windows. Since then planning is locked while the machine moves, and the simulation no longer follows the machine (the red tool shows it). After merging part 4, `cargo test` gave 802 passed and 2 failed (the two STEP tests, as before).
- Not yet validated: any run on the real machine. The checklist below is for the first attended runs on the Mac (LIO-54).

### First runs on the real JY5800 (LIO-54, attended, macOS)

A hand on the hardware E-stop for every step; the mushroom's power cutoff is not yet verified.

1. Measure the real travel and correct both the profile's soft limits (ASSUMED 390 × 280 × 55 mm) and the AXIS CAM machine's X/Y/Z travel to match; Cycle Start refuses while they differ.
2. Connect over USB: the identity line shows firmware 1041; Reset reaches Idle; the E-STOP button and Esc each give E-stop (Esc's message is `smoke::tests::escape_sends_the_estop_from_anywhere_even_while_typing`; this step checks it reaches the real window).
3. Jog each axis at 10 % and check the directions against the model; jog X to its left end, Y to its front end, Z to the top; Set machine zero here; "Soft limits on" appears; jog slowly into each limit and the jog ends at it.
4. E-stop at rest: the machine position is cleared, the soft limits go OFF, and the Run panel says Cycle Start runs with them off until machine zero is set again.
5. Air cut: a two-tool setup on a scrap block, with work Z zeroed 20 mm above the stock top so that nothing touches. Check the order: the tool rises (or drops) to the clearance plane where it stands, the spindle starts (the commanded RPM is uncalibrated) and waits the start dwell, then approach and descent; Hold and Resume mid-move; feed override; Stop ends the tool run with the spindle off.
6. Watch the log for "Motion buffer ran empty mid-move": the profile keeps only 40 ms of motion queued (`motion.buffer_ms`; the driver's default is 150 ms). On Windows a debug build starved it while AXIS CAM generated a preview during a run; planning is now locked while the machine moves, so check that the banner shows in MANUFACTURE and that the AI planner, Generate and Play wait. The driver's controller thread asks for user-interactive QoS on macOS and time-critical priority on Windows (LIO-292: at normal priority on Windows, two busy test runs beside it delayed it 100 to 160 ms and emptied the buffer); the log's "Motion thread priority" line says what it got. If it still happens on the Mac, raise `buffer_ms` (feed hold then reacts later).
7. Tool change: with T1 zeroed, touch a fixed surface and press Record reference; change the tool; touch the same surface with T2 and press Set Z from reference; check a known height; Cycle Start for tool run 2 checks it at the new Z.
8. First real cut: a shallow facing pass in soft material at a conservative feed (the profile allows 1000 mm/min), spindle running. Compare the cut's depth and position with the plan and record the results here.

## UC100 machine setup (part 4)

- Profile form: an edit changes only its own line and keeps trailing and preceding comments; an integer stays an integer; a decimal point being typed stays on screen (`setup::tests::an_edit_changes_its_line_only_and_keeps_the_comments`). Pins change, appear in a table the file did not have, and go; a pin with two roles is the driver's error and blocks Save (`pins_change_appear_and_go`). An emptied optional field removes its key; a field that is not a number, or a missing required one, blocks Save (`an_empty_optional_field_removes_its_key_and_bad_numbers_block_save`). Save writes the file and refuses one changed on disk since it was opened (`save_writes_the_file_and_refuses_one_changed_on_disk`); a CRLF file opens unchanged and keeps CRLF (`a_windows_file_opens_unchanged_and_keeps_its_line_endings`); a file that is not TOML is not opened (`a_file_that_is_not_toml_is_not_opened`).
- From the page: edits survive switching between Control and Controller configuration, Save, Revert, a refused profile, a broken file (`setup_edits_and_saves_the_profile`). Saving while connected is refused while jogging; once Idle it reconnects with the new steps per mm, in E-stop, with no axis position set (`saving_while_connected_reconnects_with_the_new_profile`).
- Inputs: the log keeps the last 20 edges with their time and counts changes per pin (`control::tests::the_input_log_keeps_the_last_edges`).
- Outputs: a free pin forced High shows in the controller's readback and Release brings it back; step and PWM pins are not forced; E-stop releases a forced pin and nothing is forced in E-stop (`outputs_are_forced_only_while_idle_and_on_free_pins`).
- Calibration: 10 mm commanded at 200 steps per mm gives 2000 pulses; 9.95 mm measured gives 201.005 steps per mm, which Use in profile puts in the form and Save applies; no move in E-stop or with a travel that is not a number (`calibration_measures_steps_per_mm_and_fills_the_profile`).
- Inspected on Windows (11 October 2026, debug build with `AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`, a copy of the driver's `jy5800-investigation.toml` at commit 36885a1, simulated, isolated workspace; not a test). The form showed the profile, accepted by the driver, with Save and Revert greyed until a change (this found the CRLF bug fixed in 2bef27e). Connected, with real clicks: pin 14 High read HIGH with High marked, Release brought it back to LOW; Move on X counted 32000 pulses live for 100 mm; 99.5 mm typed as measured gave 321.608 steps per mm; Use in profile, then Save and reconnect: the app reconnected in E-stop, and the file differed from the original in exactly one line (`steps_per_unit = 320` → `321.608`), with its 40 comment lines and CRLF line endings kept. `cargo test`: 796 passed, 2 failed (the two STEP tests, as before).
- A axis (LIO-183): Add the A axis puts `[axes.a]` after the last axis with the file's comments and other axes intact, Remove gives the file back and drops A from the homing order (`adding_the_a_axis_keeps_the_comments_and_the_other_axes`, `removing_a_leaves_the_comments_of_the_others_and_drops_a_from_the_homing_order`); a step or direction pin another role uses is refused (`adding_a_is_refused_on_a_pin_that_is_already_used`, `an_a_pin_taken_by_another_role_is_refused_by_the_driver_check`). Adding A unticks `commissioning.pinout_verified` and `commissioning.steps_per_unit_verified` and turns `required` on, so the driver refuses Reset until both are ticked again, and X, Y and Z keep their other checks (`adding_a_withdraws_the_pinout_and_steps_checks_so_reset_is_refused`, `a_profile_without_a_gate_is_gated_once_a_is_added`). The rotary machine's profile is made that way, its assumed A values unverified (the pins and polarity tick is refused until A's direction is ticked, jog A+ and the top of the part moves toward −Y, and reversing A or unticking its direction unticks it, LIO-210), and an older unedited copy is replaced (`the_rotary_machines_profile_shows_a_in_degrees_and_its_assumed_values_are_not_verified`, `a_rotary_profile_copy_written_before_the_gate_is_replaced_but_an_edited_one_is_kept`). Steps per degree is 200 × microsteps × ratio / 360 (35.556 for 16 microsteps and 4:1, 53.333 for 6:1) and a measured turn gives pulses ÷ degrees (`steps_per_degree_follows_the_drive`). The pin list names pins 8 and 9 A step and A direction once A is in the profile (`the_pin_list_names_a_step_and_direction_once_a_is_in_the_profile`). On the simulator the rotary profile refuses Reset until the checks are ticked in the form and saved, then 90° commanded counts 4000 pulses, 89° measured gives 44.944 steps per degree (`the_rotary_profile_refuses_reset_until_a_is_checked_and_then_calibrates_a_in_degrees`). The speed field of A is shown in °/s; the file and the driver hold °/min.
- Not yet validated: any of this on the real machine, and the input monitor with a real switch (the simulator's inputs do not change).

## The driver's egui app retired for daily use (LIO-58)

- Gap check (11 October 2026) of the driver's `src/gui.rs` against the Control page. Covered: Connect and the identity line; E-STOP, Esc and Reset with the E-stop reason; Override limits; the position readout (machine, work, referenced); Zero per axis and all, G54–G59; Home; declaring machine zero without switches; feed rate and feed override; spindle CW/CCW/Off with the same CCW guards; flood and mist; continuous and step jogs, jog speed, keyboard jogs and the stop on focus loss; MDI and the log; inputs, E-stop input report, stop latch and limit flags; output readback and the output test; the profile editor (as a form, Save reconnects); macOS App Nap. Added: Space holds a job from any page and resumes it on the Control page (the egui app toggled hold and resume with Space). Left: running a G-code file made elsewhere, with its preview and listing (LIO-111); editing the profile as raw TOML (still a text editor); Go to XY zero (an MDI line); the run's elapsed seconds.
- Space: in MANUFACTURE it holds a running tool run and does not resume it; on the Control page it resumes (`a_tool_run_holds_resumes_and_stops`), but not while the Controller configuration view is open (`keys_do_not_jog_or_resume_while_the_setup_view_hides_the_jog_controls`). A focused text field takes Space as a character (iced's text field captures it, so the key reaches AXIS CAM as captured). Tab and Shift+Tab move between fields while a tool run is going, as the Control page's fields stay editable: they are not refused with the planning-locked status (`smoke::tests::tab_moves_the_focus_while_the_machine_moves`).
- Driver commit 7a834e1 (`gui` no longer a default feature; the library is unchanged): `cargo test` and `cargo test --features gui` pass on Windows (82 unit and 16 simulated tests each); `uc100-gui` and `guitest` build with `--features gui` and are refused without it. AXIS CAM pins it with `default-features = false`; `cargo test` there gave 802 passed and 2 failed (the two STEP tests, as before). One earlier run also had `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state` time out waiting for the simulator under the suite's load; it passed alone and in the rerun.

## Probing Z with a touch plate (LIO-124)

- Driver commit bec0ff1 (LIO-123): `Command::Probe` (machine target, mm/min), `Snapshot::probe_result`, and `Command::SimBlock` for the simulator's block. Every probe ends with a program outcome, a refused or stopped one too; AXIS CAM probes with G38.3 so that a miss is its own message, and shows the driver's message for the rest.
- Settings: the plate thickness has no default and must be set; feed, maximum distance and retract default to 100 mm/min, 20 mm and 5 mm, also on machines saved before; bad values are listed (`control::tests::probe_settings_leave_the_plate_and_tip_to_measure_and_need_sane_values`). The example's machine library still merges without duplicates (`explore_example_merges_a_legacy_library_without_duplicates`).
- The work Z given to where the machine stopped puts the contact at the plate's top (`control::tests::work_z_puts_the_contact_where_it_belongs`); the simulator's block sits on the middle of the table, its top halfway down Z, and needs soft limits on X, Y and Z (`control::tests::the_simulated_block_sits_on_the_middle_of_the_table`).
- Through the simulated UC100 (a fast JY5800-like profile with a probe input on pin 13; block X 145…245, Y 90…190, top at machine Z −40; 10 mm plate): Probe Z is refused until Z has a machine position; a 20 mm probe from Z −10 misses, sets nothing and says so; from Z −30 it touches at Z −40, work Z there becomes 10 (offset −50) and the tool lifts 5 mm; planning is locked while it probes and unlocked after (`probe_z_sets_work_z_at_the_plate_and_lifts`). It is offered only with a probe input, not while the tool is inside the block (the probe input active), not with a plate thickness removed and saved while connected, not in E-stop; beside the block at the bottom of Z the driver refuses ("the target is where the machine already is") and the panel says so (`probe_z_waits_for_a_probe_input_a_plate_and_a_free_input`). E-stop during a probe ends it with the driver's reason and leaves work Z as it was (`e_stop_stops_a_probe`). With a setup to run, Probe reference records the surface under the plate (work Z −30, the stock top being at machine Z −20), and after a wrong Zero Z, Probe Z from reference puts the work offset back to −20 and counts as the touch-off (`a_tool_change_probes_the_reference_surface`).
- Inspected on Windows (12 October 2026, debug build with `AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`, driver bec0ff1, the driver's `jy5800-investigation.toml` with a probe input added on pin 13, simulated, isolated workspace, 10 mm plate; not a test). Real clicks: after Set machine zero here the Work zero panel offered Probe Z with its settings and the simulator's block (X 145…245, Y 90…190, top at Z −27.5). From machine Z −20 it touched at Z −27.5 (log "Probe touched at machine X195.0000 Y140.0000 Z-27.5000", "Program Probe finished") and lifted to Z −22.5, with work Z 15.000 (the plate's top at 10). With the setup picked, the Run panel showed Probe reference and Probe Z from reference beside the by-hand buttons; Probe reference recorded the surface at Z 0.000 and enabled Probe Z from reference. `cargo test`: 809 passed, 2 failed (the two STEP tests, as before).
- Not yet validated: a real plate on the JY5800 (LIO-126). Its probe input is found (pin 13, active-low, 17 October 2026: see the section at the end), but no probe has moved the machine, and the trip position the board reports is not hardware-verified (the driver's PROTOCOL.md).

## Probing X/Y edges for the work zero (LIO-125)

- Settings: the probe tip diameter (0 to 50 mm) has no default, like the plate's thickness; either left empty is not set (Probe Z needs the plate, Probe corner and Probe centre the tip), and bad values are listed (`control::tests::probe_settings_leave_the_plate_and_tip_to_measure_and_need_sane_values`). A plate removed and saved while connected still blocks Probe Z (`probe_z_waits_for_a_probe_input_a_plate_and_a_free_input`).
- The routine, run against a box without the driver (`control::tests::probing_touches_each_face_at_its_spot_and_finds_the_zero`), on a 124 × 84 × 40 mm stock with a 4 mm tip: every final touch lies in its spot from `post::touch_spots` (the band 1 to 10 mm below the top measured at the ball's equator, 5–15 % of each side from the corner or 45–55 % for the centre, the top's spot), each side's contact is the tip's centre plus or minus the radius, and no guarded descent lands on the box. Corner started 2 mm in from both edges: top, −X at the start's Y, −Y, then −X again at its spot (4 touches); centre started 3 mm off: 5 touches, measured 124 × 84; corner started 15 mm in: the top is touched again on its spot. The simulator's block takes a checked setup's stock X/Y size (`the_simulated_block_sits_on_the_middle_of_the_table`).
- Through the simulated UC100 (FAST with a probe input on pin 13, the fixture's top setup, its ~124 × 84 mm stock as the block round X195 Y140 with its top at Z −40, 4 mm tip, 1000 mm/min): Probe centre from 3 mm and 2 mm off the middle sets the work offset to X195 Y140 Z−40 within 0.15 mm in 5 touches, locks planning while it runs, backs off past +Y and up 10 mm, and counts as the touch-off (`probe_centre_sets_the_work_zero_on_the_middle_of_the_block`). Probe corner from 2 mm in from the corner (as the point probe finds it) touches −X twice and sets the corner a tip radius inside the block's, as a point probe should (`probe_corner_sets_the_work_zero_on_the_front_left_corner`). Refused without a setup picked, for Model origin ("touch it off by hand"), without a tip, after the project changed until it is checked again, and with G55 active for a G54 setup; started beside the block, the top is missed; started 20 mm off the middle, the guarded descent beside −X touches the block and stops it; E-stop during a side ends it; none of these sets anything (`probing_the_edges_needs_a_stock_zero_a_tip_and_its_work_system`).
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 813 passed, 2 failed (the two STEP tests, as before).
- Inspected on Windows (12 October 2026, debug build, driver bec0ff1, the driver's `jy5800-investigation.toml` with a probe input on pin 13, simulated, isolated workspace, 2 mm tip at 100 mm/min; not a test). Real clicks: once Top was picked and checked, the block became X 133…257, Y 98…182 (top at Z −27.5) and the Work zero panel offered Probe centre with the header's touch-off words. From X198 Y138 Z−15 it touched the top at Z −27.5, −X at X133 and +X at X257 at Y138, −Y at Y98 and +Y at Y182 at X195, all at Z −34, and said "Work zero set on the centre of the stock top at machine X 195.000 Y 140.000 Z -27.500, from 5 touches. Measured 122.000 × 82.000 mm across; the setup's stock is 124.000 × 84.000 mm" (the point probe, less the 2 mm tip). Planning stayed locked while it ran. The Machine library's Probing section shows the tip field and its help.
- Not yet validated: a real probe on the JY5800 (LIO-126): the trip position, the overtravel at the probe feed, and every move in toward the stock at the probe feed. The approach assumes the operator starts within the clearance (10 mm, less on small stock) of the top's spot.

## Two workspaces, Manufacture and Machine (LIO-127)

- While a tool run cuts, the Manufacture workspace does not open: the Work and Tools pages, Edit setup, picking a setup and picking an operation are refused with the locked message, the Control page stays and the selected operation is kept; the Control page with its Job view renders (`the_3d_view_shows_the_machine_and_planning_waits_for_it`). The switcher goes back to the Manufacture page left for the Machine workspace, and picking Machine again keeps it (`the_machine_workspace_goes_back_to_the_manufacture_page_left`).
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 814 passed, 2 failed (the two STEP tests, as before); `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state` timed out once under the full suite's load and passed alone.
- Inspected on Windows (12 October 2026, debug build, the driver's `jy5800-investigation.toml`, simulated, isolated workspace with the fixture block's Facing and Drilling; not a test). Real clicks: the switcher at the left of the toolbar opens a list of MANUFACTURE and MACHINE, its help card beside it; MANUFACTURE shows Start, New project, the project name, Import model, Open and Save project in the dark bar, the libraries, AI planner and Operation support in the toolbar and the ribbon on the Work page; MACHINE shows Control and Setup and the E-STOP. Picking 1. Top in the Run panel showed it in the Job view beside the panel. During tool run 1 the switcher read "MACHINE · Manufacture opens when the machine stops", a click on it did nothing, and the Job view drew the machine's red tool moving over the stock. After Stop, MANUFACTURE opened on the Work page it was left from.

## Contrast of small text and toolbar tiles (#82, #88)

- `muted_text_meets_contrast_on_every_surface` computes the WCAG contrast of the secondary-text colour `MUTED` against white panels, the application background and the selected, setup, hovered and active-button fills it is drawn on, and asserts at least 4.5:1 on each. Before (0.43, 0.50, 0.57): 4.09 on white, 3.74 on the application background, 3.36 on the selected operation row. After (0.30, 0.37, 0.44): 6.65, 6.07 and 5.45; the lowest of the seven surfaces is 5.45.
- `toolbar_glyphs_meet_contrast_on_their_tiles` reads every `assets/tb-*.svg`, takes the first shape as the tile and checks each other fill and stroke colour against it for at least 4.5:1 (and that no glyph uses opacity). White on the tile before and after: Materials 2.27 to 4.95 (#e0a100 to #946800), Tools 3.58 to 5.18, Operation support 4.35 to 5.59, Control 3.45 to 5.31 (new tile #2b7a3d); the others were already above 4.5 (Workholding's and Manufacture's translucent shading is now solid white and separated by strokes in the tile colour).
- `the_control_tile_is_a_panel_not_a_play_triangle` (the Control icon is a panel with a dial and a button) and `an_unavailable_strategy_says_why_on_hover` (the hover help of each unavailable strategy names the reason and Operation support).
- Not tests: operational texts of 10 and 11 px (browser details, the playback lines, the status bar, the toolbar and ribbon labels) are now 12 px; ribbon captions 10 px; the inspector tab labels (11 px, one row of tabs), the "NATIVE PROTOTYPE" and "CAM STUDIO" brand text and the help card's concept-illustration note stay as they were. Unavailable strategies in the ribbon are drawn at 45 % opacity with the label in the muted colour. Toolbar tiles are drawn at 26 px instead of 28 so the solid tiles weigh less against the light operation icons. None of this was inspected in the window: this machine's renderer screenshots are unreliable, and the layout was not checked at the minimum window width (1120 px). No UI-scale preference was added.
- Linux (16 October 2026): `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=6`: 939 passed, 0 failed after the rebase onto #76, #77 (the four new tests included).

## Stock pictures in the setup browser (LIO-132)

`the_browser_draws_the_stock_each_setup_starts_from_and_leaves_in_its_jaws` covers the contoured fixture. Both setups are in a vise. Setup 1 faces, pockets and contours the top 5 mm of the walls. Setup 2 has the part turned over and faces it.

- **Before the job is simulated**
  - Each setup starts from its block: no green (cut) pixels, and over 100 grey jaw pixels.
  - Neither setup has an after picture.
  - Each start picture is drawn once.
- **After the job is simulated**
  - Setup 1 still starts from its block.
  - Setup 2 starts from what setup 1 left it. The cut band is green where the jaws, which grip it, leave it in sight (over 20 pixels).
  - Each setup's after picture has over 1,000 green pixels and over 100 grey ones.
  - Setup 2 starts from the very stock setup 1 leaves (the same `Arc`), so the job's stock is meshed twice, not three times.
- **Other jobs**
  - A one-setup job gets its after picture.
  - The example, which is not simulated, keeps its block and has no after picture.

Other checks:

- `the_setup_editor_says_the_stock_is_the_original_until_the_earlier_setups_are_simulated` now expects a one-setup job to be simulated, for its after picture.
- The stock run no longer starts on the Control page. While it ran it held Cycle Start back between tool runs, which `a_setup_runs_tool_run_by_tool_run_with_a_manual_tool_change` caught once one-setup jobs were simulated. With the run kept to the MANUFACTURE workspace, all smoke tests pass, apart from `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state` timing out under the full suite's load (LIO-131); it passes alone. The smoke suite takes about 65 s instead of 42 s, because more test apps simulate and mesh their stock.
- Not a test: inspected on Windows (12 October 2026, debug build). Setup 1 had Facing, Contour (bottom 35) and Drilling, a flipped setup faced the other side, and both were in the starter vise. Each picture showed the stock in the vise:
  - Top started as its block and ended faced and contoured, with its five holes as dots.
  - Flipped started from what Top left it, turned over; the contoured band at the bottom was behind the jaws.
  - Flipped ended with that side faced.
  - The pictures were ready within about 20 s of opening MANUFACTURE.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 814 passed, 3 failed: the two STEP tests, as before, and `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`, which timed out under load and passes alone (LIO-131).

## The owner's machine by default (LIO-138)

- `the_owners_machine_runs_on_the_profile_it_writes_and_keeps_an_edited_one`:
  - The Jingyan CNC4030 has no Machine library issues and passes the UC100 run checks (`PostSettings::for_uc100`).

## NC review leads a UC100 setup to MACHINE (LIO-142)

- `nc_review_runs_a_uc100_setup_on_the_machine`:
  - The export check refuses a UC100 machine with "G-code export is LinuxCNC only. … run this setup on it from the MACHINE workspace."
  - The NC review page renders with that setup refused and its **Run on the machine** button.
  - Run on the machine from the NC review page, with another setup active and the machine disconnected, opens the Control page (MANUFACTURE goes back to the NC review page) and does not connect.
  - Connect then picks that setup in the Run panel and makes it the active setup.

## The NC page is worded for the controller (#79)

- `the_nc_page_is_worded_for_the_machines_controller`, on the page's description (`ui::nc_page`) that `post` draws, so no screenshot:
  - A UC100 job's page is titled "Run on the machine", its intro and note say the setups are checked for a run and take no G-code file, and a setup whose run checks pass has the Run button and no error text. The export check still refuses it as before (`result` is unchanged; `SetupPost::run` carries the run checks).
  - A UC100 setup whose run checks fail (a spindle speed above the profile's) lists the problems and keeps the button.
  - A LinuxCNC job's page keeps "NC review", "Check each setup, then export LinuxCNC G-code.", the export note and no Run button; a LinuxCNC setup that fails its export checks is still an error.
  - The page builds in each state.
- Found in the second review round (#188): `JobPost::ready()` and `write_job` required every setup's `result` to be ok, and a UC100 setup's is always the refusal of a G-code file, so a job of one LinuxCNC and one UC100 setup never showed Export although the page said it would once every setup passes. A UC100 setup is now judged by its run checks and writes no file (`post::tests::a_job_of_a_linuxcnc_and_a_uc100_setup_exports_the_linuxcnc_program`): with both passing the job is ready and `write_job` writes the one LinuxCNC file; a failing run check holds the export back and is the reason `write_job` gives; a job of UC100 setups alone is not ready, having no file.
- Not covered: the mixed-controller wording, and how the page looks in the window.
- The step strip's "04 Review & run" label for a UC100 setup and the connected path (Run on the machine picks the setup at once, as clicking it in the Run panel does) are not covered by a test.
- Not inspected in the real window: the window is shared with other work on this PC.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 813 passed, 6 failed: the two STEP tests, as before, and four simulated-UC100 smoke tests that timed out under the load of parallel builds (`the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`, `a_jog_into_a_soft_limit_ends_at_the_limit`, `e_stop_stops_a_probe`, `keep_the_last_position_declares_where_the_machine_was_left`); each passes alone.

## Detours that come back (LIO-147)

- `smoke::tests::part_then_machine_then_setup_without_hidden_operations`, on an imported part in an isolated workspace with no machine and no tool:
  - Create first setup opens the machine wizard, whose Save reads "Save and create first setup". Saving creates Setup 1 in MANUFACTURE, with no operation.
  - Face opens the Tool library and adds nothing. After New tool, Save reads "Save and continue to Facing", and the page builds. Saving adds the Facing operation and returns to MANUFACTURE, with no action left pending.
  - Face again with no tool, then MANUFACTURE and back to Tools: saving a tool adds nothing and stays on Tools.
  - With one machine in the library, Create first setup creates the setup with it, without the Machine library.
- Not a test: the window was not driven or looked at; the labels and the empty Tool library's text ("Facing needs a cutter. Create one: saving it adds Facing.") come from the same code.
