## Planning for a UC100 machine (LIO-141)

- Why the planner refused the UC100: its checklist and its export stage called the LinuxCNC post (`PostSettings::from_machine`), which refuses every other controller. Nothing else in planning depends on the controller, so the planner now checks a UC100 machine's setups with the checks a UC100 run already makes (`post::checked` with `PostSettings::for_uc100`, then the profile's outputs), not with the post.
- `plan_check::tests::a_uc100_machine_is_planned_for_with_its_runs_checks_and_its_profiles_outputs`, on the example block's machine set to UC100 with the owner's `jy5800.toml` profile:
  - The checklist is met, and the part report's `coolants` is only Off: the profile has no flood or mist output, and Air uses the mist output.
  - The good plan, whose operations use Flood, is refused at the export stage, by operation, with the run's message ("uses flood coolant, but the UC100 profile has no flood output…"), and no problem names LinuxCNC.
  - With every operation's coolant Off, it passes every stage.
- The run's checks before Cycle Start keep their messages (`output_problems` is shared), as the run tests in `src/smoke.rs` and `src/run.rs` show.
- Not covered: what needs the connected machine (the work offset, machine positions, the soft limits at the work zero, the profile's soft limits against the machine's travel) is checked when the setup is run, not when it is planned. A live planning run needs the owner's ChatGPT sign-in and was not made; the test checks the plan directly, with no model and no request to OpenAI.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 813 passed, 7 failed: the two STEP tests, as before, and five simulated-UC100 smoke tests that timed out under the load of parallel builds (`a_jog_into_a_soft_limit_ends_at_the_limit`, `cycle_start_waits_for_what_the_run_panel_lists`, `keep_the_last_position_declares_where_the_machine_was_left`, `probe_centre_sets_the_work_zero_on_the_middle_of_the_block`, `saving_while_connected_reconnects_with_the_new_profile`); the five pass alone.

## One meaning of "Setup", and the setup editor in steps (LIO-149)

- Names. "Setup" now means only a position of the part on the machine: Setup 1, **+ Setup**, **Edit setup** and the step 01 Setup.
  - The MACHINE toolbar's **Setup** is now **Controller configuration**. Its verdict reads "Reset is refused until commissioning is verified", and the probe warning on the Control page says "add it under Inputs in Controller configuration."
  - The machine wizard is the **Machine profile wizard**. **Set up a machine** is now **New machine profile**, on the start of a blank project, on a part with no setup and in the Machine library.
  - Earlier sections keep the names of their day: their Setup on the MACHINE toolbar is Controller configuration.
- The setup editor has four steps, as tabs under the setup name: **Machine** (machine, work offset, origin), **Stock** (project material, stock), **Position** (flips, rotation, translation) and **Holding** (modelled workholding with its Checked gap to jaws and clamps and the Fixture note, then, under the heading Setup notes · recorded, not checked, the Requested fixture clearance, Reposition action and Setup instructions). The setup's issues and Duplicate, Move up, Move down and Delete follow every step, so they stay in sight.
- `smoke::tests::the_setup_editor_opens_each_step_and_marks_what_is_done` (the example job and starter library):
  - The editor opens on Machine; each step opens in one click and builds.
  - Machine and Position are ✓.
  - Holding is ○ with no modelled workholding, ✓ with the suggested vise, and ○ again with the jaws on the X sides, where the stock is wider than the vise opens.
  - Stock is ○ with no material, ✓ once one is chosen, and ○ again for a 10 mm fixed box the part does not fit in.
  - Holding is still the step shown after opening an operation and going back with Edit setup.
  - The tool editor builds with **Save tool** and **Delete tool** under its scrolling fields, outside the scroll, so they stay in sight at any window height.
- The renderer's smoke run opens the Stock step for screens 22–25 and 92 and the Holding step for screen 88, where it scrolled the inspector before. It drives a real window, so it was not run here.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 818 passed, 3 failed: the two STEP tests, as before, and `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`, which timed out waiting for jogging under the full suite's load and passes alone.

## Hover help gets out of the way of a click (LIO-148)

- `help::tests::a_click_shuts_hover_help_until_the_pointer_comes_back` drives the Face command's illustrated help, built by the shared `help::wrap` that every hover card goes through, with mouse events in a headless tiny-skia renderer and the real 0.6 s delay:
  - A control that appears under a resting pointer shows no help.
  - Once the pointer has been off it, hovering opens the help.
  - Pressing the control shuts the help, and the release still sends the button's message.
  - The help stays shut while the pointer rests or moves on the control.
  - Moving off and back opens it again.
- Not inspected in the real window: the screen is shared with other work. The review's cases (Face opening the Tool library, the Geometry and Heights tabs, the Run panel's setup button) all go through `help::wrap`.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 818 passed, 4 failed: the two STEP tests, as before, and two simulated-UC100 smoke tests that timed out under the load of parallel builds (`probe_z_waits_for_a_probe_input_a_plate_and_a_free_input`, `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`); each passes alone.

## Hover cards: a short hover text, the rest under More… (#89)

- Every card is split into a hover text (what the button or the selected value does and one caveat, about three sentences) and a More… text (the procedure, the limits and the conventions). `help::tests::every_hover_text_is_under_the_limit` builds every card the app makes from its own texts: the commands (a list of the messages, the Control page's and the Run panel's actions among them), the operation buttons and the dimmed ribbon tiles (#88: the reason and the click stay on hover, what the strategy does goes under More…) for each strategy, every field key for each of the six targets (the keys read from the match arms of `help::field_help`), and every choice (the key and value pairs read from the match arms of `choice_help::selected`, plus the work offsets, the 19 work zeros, the controllers, the connections, the arrangements, the playback speeds, the workholding kinds and the material groups that its fallbacks word, each with a counterclockwise spindle and a one-way pattern where the text reads them): 606 cards, and the planner-pictures card; each hover text is under 350 characters.
- The split moved whole sentences (the three hover texts that needed a new sentence, the work zeros, the modelled workholding and Probe corner/edge/centre, keep the old sentence under More…); the one-time test that every sentence of the 606 earlier texts survived verbatim is gone (#200), so a card can be reworded, and `every_hover_text_is_under_the_limit` still checks that no hover text is empty or a copy of its More… text.
- The card is a widget of its own (`help::HoverHelp`, in place of the tooltip wrapper): `help::tests::the_card_stays_open_under_the_pointer_and_more_expands_it` drives the requested fixture clearance's card with mouse events in the headless tiny-skia renderer, to the card's overlay first and then to the control as the runtime does: hovering opens the card after the 0.6 s delay and starts the animation's ticks; the pointer resting on the card past the 0.25 s linger keeps it open; More… makes the card wider and taller and sends the app nothing; Pause stops the ticks; off both the card lingers, then closes and forgets More… and Pause; opened again it is the hover text alone. `help::tests::a_click_shuts_hover_help_until_the_pointer_comes_back` (LIO-148) passes unchanged through the new widget.
- A card with no room on its usual side opens on the opposite side of its control, and one with room on neither side is kept in the window over the control, which stays live (#184): `help::place` flips Bottom/Top and Left/Right when the preferred side lacks the card's size and the gap, then clamps into the window; `CardOverlay::mouse_interaction` returns None, not Idle, over a card that intersects its control (except over its More… and Pause links), so iced leaves the pointer to the control. `help::tests::the_card_takes_the_side_with_room_and_stays_in_the_window` checks the placement arithmetic (a row near the bottom edge → above it, a field near the left edge → to its right, Top and Right the same way, a window too short for either side → clamped over the control); `help::tests::a_control_under_a_clamped_card_can_still_be_clicked_to_close_it` opens the requested fixture clearance's card over its control in a 420 px window, checks the control keeps the pointer, and clicks it: the press shuts the card and the release reaches the button.
- The tests on the texts look in the hover text or the More… text as the sentence moved: `help::tests::workholding_commands_and_fields_have_specific_help`, `the_holding_steps_gap_fields_say_what_each_does`, `speed_and_feed_help_says_what_the_material_check_does`, `planner_commands_describe_the_live_planner`, `choice_help::tests::choice_help_says_what_the_workholding_check_covers_and_what_it_does_not` and `post_settings_have_choice_specific_help`; the pictures box's "a run keeps the choice it started with" is in `ui::PICTURES_HELP_MORE` (`smoke::tests::the_pictures_choice_is_fixed_while_a_run_goes`).
- The field-help gallery (screen 15, `--help-only`) draws its first card as More… and Pause leave it. Rendered headlessly through Iced's tiny-skia renderer at 1240 × 980 and inspected (not a test, and the picture is not committed): the requested fixture clearance's card is expanded, wider, with its More… text, Less and Play under the animation's still frame; the five other cards have Pause under their animation; nothing is clipped or overlapping. The unreachable Stock detail command text (the choice's card is returned first) is now the More… text of the three stock-detail choices.

## Help texts match the controls (issues #34, #35, #36)

- The machine wizard's UC100 profile file field has its own help card (path of the driver profile, Browse…, the Control page's Setup view), not the generic "Saved parameter" text (`choice_help::tests::choice_help_keeps_implemented_and_saved_preferences_distinct`).
- The Hole order card says what the code does: Optimize travel drills the nearest remaining hole next, a greedy route that is not a global optimum; By X then Y drills in setup X then Y order. "Selection order" is no longer accepted by `cam::drill` or described; a project saved with it loads as By X then Y (`storage::tests::a_saved_selection_order_loads_as_by_x_then_y`).
- The help cards and the message handler for a stock-source control are deleted; no control sends it, and each later setup's stock source is set by `Document::sync_stock_sources`.

## What the job leaves uncut (LIO-146)

The fixture block (120 × 80 × 40 mm) has 4 mm of stock on top, 3 mm below and 2 mm on each side. Each face is sampled 0.5 mm outside itself against the exact cuts of the stock the last setup leaves.

- `faces_left_under_stock_are_found_until_a_later_setup_cuts_them`:
  - **Setup 1 faces the top.** No face of the top is under stock, but every face of the bottom and of the −X side is.
    - The largest region is reached from −X, +X, −Y, +Y and the bottom. The through holes join it there.
    - The next region is the pocket: "5868 mm² reached from the top: X 16–88, Y 20–60, Z 28–40".
    - Between 30% and 100% of the surface is under stock (85.5% here).
  - **Setup 2, flipped, faces the underside.**
    - The bottom is no longer under stock, and less of the surface is (72.1%).
    - The sides still are.
    - The four through holes are regions of their own, each reached from the bottom and the top.
- `a_narrow_uncut_strip_in_a_large_flat_face_is_found` (issue #201): a 120 × 120 mm face (two triangles with edges up to 170 mm) over stock cut everywhere but a 4 mm strip.
  - The share under stock is between 1% and 10% (the strip is 3.3%). Sampled in 15 mm pieces, as a triangle over 40 mm used to be, no sample fell in the strip and the share was 0.
  - With the cut over the strip's side left out, every face is under stock.
- `a_face_is_split_into_at_most_the_samples_a_run_can_afford` (issue #201): a 120 mm right triangle is halved into triangles with no edge over 40 mm that add up to its area, in at most 2⁶ of them; a triangle 5 m across stops at 2⁶, which bounds a face to 4096 samples.
- `completeness_is_not_checked_without_a_cut_or_after_a_failure`:
  - A job without an operation says "No operation has cut the stock yet."
  - A job whose operation has lost its tool names that operation.
- `the_job_says_what_it_leaves_under_stock_once_it_is_simulated` (smoke), on the same two setups. The line under the 3D view:
  - says "Completeness not checked yet." until the stock run;
  - then says "Magenta faces are still under stock after the whole job: …", with more than one region;
  - says "Completeness not checked: Flipped / Facing could not be generated." once setup 2's operation loses its tool.

  MANUFACTURE renders each time. The example part says nothing.
- `the_viewport_draws_the_same_pixels_after_the_split`: the pinned pixels do not change with no face marked. With every face marked uncut and the stock hidden, over 10,000 pixels are magenta.
- `faces_under_stock_are_magenta_over_the_simulated_stock_when_shown` (issue #75): a simulation of the block job's first operation with the stock shown. With every face marked uncut and Show uncut faces on, some pixels are magenta although the part itself is not drawn; with it off, or with no face marked, none are; and the toggle is in the raster's cache key.
- `a_selected_face_under_stock_is_striped_in_blue_and_magenta` (issue #75; the outline it replaced ran along every triangle of a face's mesh, across the face, and looked broken): with every surface selected and the faces marked uncut, at least 30 % of what was magenta is the selection's blue and at least 30 % stays magenta, and along the fullest row the two take turns at least 6 times; with Show uncut faces off, the selection fills the faces as before.
- `the_3d_view_has_a_show_uncut_faces_toggle_and_a_legend_that_match_the_line` (smoke, issue #75), on the two-setup job: no toggle before the stock run (the legend is tan and green only), then the checkbox in the widget tree and a longer legend that names magenta; the line says "Magenta faces …". While an edited job is simulated again the line says "Completeness not checked yet." and the checkbox stays, so the Toolpath box beside it does not move. Toggled off, the line says "… Show uncut faces is off." with no "Magenta", the legend drops its magenta key and the checkbox stays. With the stock hidden as well there is no legend. The example part has no toggle.
- `the_raster_is_drawn_again_when_the_uncut_result_changes`: the 3D view draws its raster again only when the key it is cached under changes, and that key now holds the uncut result's identity (where its faces are and how many). It is the same for the same inputs, and differs with no face marked and every face marked, with the result gone and with the result worked out again. Found in the project-wide review (issue #8): the key left the result out, so the magenta faces appeared, or went away after an edit, only once the camera moved or the stock was toggled.
- Not a test, seen in a debug test build with a temporary print:
  - **Fixture job, one setup** (Facing, Pocket, Contour, Drilling, Parallel, Waterline): 14 regions are left. These include:
    - the bottom;
    - the slot on −Y ("1231 mm² reached from −Y: X 48–72, Y 0–14, Z 8–32") and the one on +X;
    - the four side holes;
    - the top edge's chamfers;
    - the walls of the Ø6.6 holes, which the Ø4 drill does not reach;
    - two 2–3 mm² pocket-floor corners the Ø10 cutter does not reach.
  - Slivers under 2 mm² are left out.
  - The check took 0.65 s for its 10,676 faces and 18,726 cuts, and 0.22 s for the facing job's 200 cuts. It runs in the background stock run.
- The real window was not opened: the screen is shared with other threads. The tint and the lines were checked through the tests above.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 814 passed, 11 failed. The failures:
  - the two STEP tests, as before;
  - nine simulated-UC100 smoke tests that timed out under the load of parallel builds.

  Eight of the nine pass alone. `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state` timed out waiting for jogging even alone while the PC stayed loaded. Its app has no part or setup, so the new check never runs in it. `cargo fmt --check` flags only lines of LIO-149's.

## Live machine state and Run checks (LIO-150)

- `the_status_line_shows_the_machine_state_once_connected`: after Reset the status line reads "Connected to JY5800: Idle", no longer the E-STOP it connected in. Once another message has replaced it, an E-stop leaves that message alone.
- A connection lost after connecting reads "Lost the connection to <machine>; see the log on the Control page." The simulator does not drop its connection, so no test covers it.
- `cycle_start_waits_for_what_the_run_panel_lists`: an edit marks the run's check out of date. A tick before the edits pause leaves the check alone; once they pause, the Control page checks the setup again on its own, as Check again does (a new check under way, no longer out of date).
- The review's case, clamp errors still listed after switching to a vise, goes through the same flag: a failed check is redone the same way. It is not tested on its own.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`), after rebasing on LIO-146: 819 passed, 6 failed: the two STEP tests, as before, and four simulated-UC100 smoke tests that timed out waiting for the simulator's machine zero, with the CPU at 100 % from the parallel builds (`a_tool_run_holds_resumes_and_stops`, `probe_z_sets_work_z_at_the_plate_and_lifts`, `probe_z_waits_for_a_probe_input_a_plate_and_a_free_input`, `the_3d_view_shows_the_machine_and_planning_waits_for_it`). Under that load each one passed alone at least once in three runs. That wait comes before any edit, and so before the check that is redone. Before the rebases, on a quieter PC: 816 passed, 2 failed (the STEP tests).

## From the soft-limits errors to a work zero on the stock (LIO-140)

- `the_soft_limits_errors_lead_to_a_work_zero_on_the_simulated_block`, through the simulated UC100 on FAST without a probe input, with the fixture's top setup (Stock top center, G54):
  - Before machine zero, Work zero on the stock says to set it.
  - After Set machine zero where the machine starts, as in the blind review, X, Y and Z each fall outside the soft limits. Each error ends "Put the work zero on the stock: see Work zero on the stock below".
  - The guide names Stock top center in G54, says the axes have a machine position, says to jog over the centre of the stock top and press Zero all, and says nothing about a probe. It gives the block and the work zero on it: machine X 195 Y 140 Z −40, the top centre of the block sized to the stock.
  - Move there brings the tool tip to that point. After Zero all, nothing stands before Cycle Start. Once the first tool run starts the guide is gone.
  - The test presses Set machine zero again while an axis stays unset. About one run in three, the driver dropped the Y or Z declaration sent while it re-read the position after the one before (LIO-163). `probe_z_sets_work_z_at_the_plate_and_lifts` fails the same way. With the second press the test passed 8 runs in a row alone.
- `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state` and the other Control page tests render the guide and the machine model with the block and the work-zero ring.
- Not checked in the real window (it is shared with other work on this PC): the layout of the guide and the machine model's ring and block.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`), with about twelve parallel builds on the PC: 818 passed, 9 failed. Two are the STEP tests, as before. The other seven are simulated-UC100 smoke tests that timed out under the load, and six of them pass alone.
- The seventh, `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`, times out waiting for jogging when run alone too. origin/main without this change does the same (LIO-131).
- In earlier runs, `calibration_measures_steps_per_mm_and_fills_the_profile` and `probe_corner_sets_the_work_zero_on_the_front_left_corner` each failed once alone, at their machine-zero wait (LIO-163).

## Coolant that follows the machine (LIO-145)

- `new_operations_take_the_coolant_the_machine_has`: a Facing added on a UC100 machine starts with Flood when the profile has a flood output, Mist when it has only mist, and Off on the owner's `jy5800.toml`, which has neither. The default is the first of `control::coolants`, the choices the machine has outputs for, which the planner's part report also gives the model (LIO-141), so `a_uc100_machine_is_planned_for_with_its_runs_checks_and_its_profiles_outputs` covers that list.
- `cycle_start_waits_for_what_the_run_panel_lists`: with the drill on Mist and no mist output, **Set coolant to Off** lists only the drill, in tool run 2, not Facing's Flood, which the profile has. Pressing it sets the drill's coolant to Off and checks the setup again, and the "no mist output" reason is gone.
- The Run panel was not inspected in the window. Twelve threads shared the PC, so the view and smoke tests stood in for it.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 823 passed, 5 failed: the two STEP tests, as before, and three simulated-UC100 smoke tests that timed out under the load of parallel builds, waiting for machine zero. Rerun alone, each passed, two of them only after two or three failed tries.

## Workholding says how a setup is held; clamps a row each (LIO-144)

- `the_setup_header_follows_the_modelled_holding_and_clamps_are_edited_by_row`, on the example with the starter library:
  - A new setup's header says "No workholding modelled"; with the fixture note Low profile clamps it says "Low profile clamps, not modelled".
  - With Vise it says "100 mm machine vise", and the setup sheet no longer mentions Low profile clamps. With Clamps it says "4 × Toe clamp 60 mm".
  - The clamps are read as rows. A side and an offset changed and a clamp removed save "-X:0;-Y:12;-Y:0", and the header says "3 × Toe clamp 60 mm".
  - An offset being typed ("-") stays in its row unsaved, with "A clamp offset must be a number." under the rows. Separators typed into an offset never add a clamp.
  - Back to None, the header shows the note again. The setup editor builds in each state.
- The NC header and the setup sheet print `Fixture:` only for a setup with None and a note; a modelled vise or clamps replace it (`post::tests::the_header_and_the_setup_sheet_say_what_holds_the_stock`, `model::tests::the_setup_sheet_describes_modelled_workholding_in_place_of_the_fixture_note`). A new setup has no note, so the golden program has no `Fixture:` line.
- Not inspected in the real window: the window is shared with other threads, so this was checked with tests only.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 824 passed, 4 failed. Two are the STEP tests, as before. The other two, `the_edge_test_is_quick_where_long_open_edges_meet` and `probe_z_waits_for_a_probe_input_a_plate_and_a_free_input`, ran late under the full suite's load and passed alone.
- `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state` ("timed out waiting for jogging") passed in that run, but fails now and then alone while other builds keep the PC busy. Main without this change failed it 3 times in 3 at such a time. (Fixed under #9: the test pressed the jog while the driver was still homing; see Controller tests without wall-clock timing.)

## Browser marks that match the Run checks (LIO-143)

`the_browser_marks_an_operation_ready_only_when_the_run_checks_pass` replays the blind review's case on a simulated UC100 machine like the JY5800: the fixture block's Top setup with Facing and Drilling.

- **Suggested toe clamps**
  - Generated and not yet checked, Facing has the grey … and Drilling ○. The simulation panel says "Checking Top as run will…".
  - Once checked, the lines the app keeps are exactly `run::programs`' refusal, the list the Run panel shows. Both operations get the amber !, because the clearance plane is below the clamps.
  - The panel's heading is "Workholding problem: Run will refuse Top" ("Workholding in the way" until LIO-167 added the cutting forces). Its first line is Facing's own clamp hit, and every line Run lists is there.
- **The suggested vise instead**
  - The check is out of date as soon as the holding changes. While it is checked again the panel keeps the clamp lines, in their amber box, under "Checking Top as run will…", so it keeps its height and the 3D view above it does not move.
  - Checked again, `run::programs` passes and both operations get the green ✓. The panel says "Ready to run: Run's checks of Top pass.".
- **Facing on mist coolant**, which the profile has no output for: until it is checked the panel keeps the ready setup's two lines, the heading grey ("Checking Top as run will…"). Then both operations go back to the grey ✓·. The panel says "Run will refuse Top" and lists the coolant line first. A check that comes back after the project was replaced is dropped, so the new project shows nothing of the old one's, and the check the project started is taken.

- **Glyphs and hover help (#74)**: a grey and a green ✓ differed by colour only. `each_browser_mark_has_its_own_glyph_and_hover_help_that_names_the_state` checks that the six marks (— ○ … ✓· ! ✓) have six different glyphs and six different state names, and walks the same job: generated and unchecked reads "…" with "Generated, being checked", Drilling "○" with "Not generated", the toe clamps "!" with "Workholding refused" and the Run panel's "Run will refuse Top" lines, the vise "✓" with "Ready to run", mist coolant "✓·" with "Generated, will not run" and the coolant line, and a switched-off operation "—" with "Suppressed".

Other checks:

- The check is `run::plan_problems` (LIO-141) for a UC100 machine: what a run refuses before Cycle Start, without the connected machine. For any other machine it is export's `post::checked`.

- `the_simulation_waits_for_the_edits_to_pause_and_runs_one_at_a_time` now covers the new check too. It starts after the same pause as the stock's run, one at a time, and the app's blocking work ends only once both have answered.
- Not inspected in the real window: the window is shared with other work on this PC.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 827 passed, 3 failed: the two STEP tests, as before, and `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`, which timed out under the load of parallel builds and passes alone (LIO-131). In earlier runs under heavier load, other tests missed their time limits too, among them the two that run a setup through a tool change and `the_edge_test_is_quick_where_long_open_edges_meet`; each passed alone. (The jogging timeout had a cause of its own, found and fixed under #9 on Linux: see Controller tests without wall-clock timing.)

## Does the workholding hold against the cut? (LIO-167)

The estimate is a model, not a measurement: every number it assumes is named at the top of `src/forces.rs`, and the clamping force (the library entry's) and the spindle power (the machine's) can be typed in.

- **The force.** `forces::tests::a_slot_pushes_with_the_stock_it_removes_up_to_what_the_spindle_drives`:
  - A Ø10 slot 4 mm deep across a 100 × 60 × 40 mm stock at 600 mm/min, replayed on the voxel stock, removes 400 mm³/s in its full stretches (within 1 %), at 600 mm/min, its force halfway up the 4 mm the flutes are in.
  - In aluminium with 3 flutes at 8000 rpm that is kc 1971 N/mm² and 188 N tangential: 282 N sideways and 94 N up, on a 5 kW spindle.
  - An 800 W spindle that tops out at 12000 rpm drives at most 127 N at the edge of a Ø10 tool, so the same cut pushes 191 N sideways, in aluminium as in steel, which a job without a material counts as.
- **A job that holds, and a shallow grip under a heavy cut.** `post::tests::the_pocket_holds_in_a_vise_gripping_8_mm_and_tips_the_stock_out_of_a_1_mm_grip`:
  - The fixture block's Pocket, in the starter vise on ±Y at grip 8, exports.
  - At grip 1, `post::held` (the Run checks' and the plan check's workholding list) pins one problem on the Pocket, and export refuses the setup with exactly that line: "Pocket: the cut could tip the stock out of the vise: about 191 N, 45 mm above the top of the jaws' contact, which is 1.0 mm high and 98 mm long (×0.5, under the ×2 wanted). Grip deeper, take a smaller stepdown or stepover or a lower feed, or choose another holder."
- **Earlier machining that takes the grip.** `forces::tests::earlier_cuts_that_lower_the_jaws_contact_leave_a_later_setup_unheld`, the starter vise gripping 8 mm on ±Y against 500 N sideways 1 mm under the top:
  - On the whole stock it holds ×2.5 against tipping, and no issue is said.
  - After an earlier outside contour down to 5.5 mm above the bottom, over the faces the jaws close on, **Can it be held?** still passes: 75 % of its points have material.
  - The jaws touch only the lower 4.5 mm, though: ×1.3, and the issue gives the contact's height (4.5 mm) and length (98 mm).
  - The contact is sampled from where the stock stands, as the jaws are placed (#159, below): `forces::tests::the_jaws_contact_is_measured_from_where_the_stock_stands`.
- **Clamps.** `forces::tests::one_clamp_lets_the_stock_tip_over_its_own_edge_and_a_second_across_holds_it`:
  - A single toe clamp on −X, its bite's centre 6 mm in, holds 2000 N × 6 mm against 300 N at 39 mm: ×1, the stock tipping over its −X bottom edge, and the issue says to add a clamp on +X.
  - With a second clamp on +X, friction is the least: "Pocket: holds ×5.2 against sliding."
- **The setup editor and Run.** `smoke::tests::the_setup_editor_and_the_run_checks_say_whether_the_vise_holds_against_the_cut`, on a simulated UC100 machine:
  - With the suggested vise the editor lists, under "Against the cutting forces", "Facing: holds ×5.5 against tipping." and "Drilling: no cutting force sideways or up to hold against.", and no issue. Facing gets the green ✓.
  - At grip 1 the editor lists the Facing's issue (×0.5) and its margin reads "Facing: only ×0.5 against tipping; ×2 is wanted.". Facing gets the amber ! and Drilling the grey ✓·, since Run will refuse the setup.
- **A later setup on the walls an earlier one finished.** `smoke::tests::the_setup_editor_shows_what_the_earlier_setups_left_the_vise_to_grip`:
  - Turned over, setup 2 grips 3 mm of the walls setup 1's Contour finished. The jaws have material there, but with the vise's typical 5000 N they do not hold the stock against setup 2's facing 39 mm higher (a contact 2.9 mm high, ×1.9).
  - With the vise's clamping force set to 10 kN in the library, the line goes, without simulating the earlier setups again.
- **Existing tests.** Three collision tests hold the fixture block with one toe clamp on −X. With the starter's 2000 N the Pocket would tip the block over that edge (×1.3), so `post::tests::pocket_job` gives its clamp 10 kN: those tests are about where the clamp sits.
- **Limits of the model**, by construction and not tested:
  - One peak per operation, pushing in whichever direction is worst.
  - The removal rate is read from the simulation's voxels (the longest stock side divided by 96), so a pass lighter than a voxel can count a whole layer. The slot cap keeps that within reason. Until #60 (below) a pass that swept none of the voxels' centres counted nothing, and passed.
  - Drilling, and any cutter's twist about the spindle, are not counted.
  - Friction, the clamping forces and kc are typical values: measure or estimate yours.
- Not inspected in the real window: the window is shared with other work on this PC, so this was checked with tests only.
- `cargo test` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`): 834 passed, 3 failed. Two are the STEP tests, as before. The third, `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`, timed out waiting for jogging under the load of other builds and passed alone; its app has no part or setup, so this change does not reach it.

## A jog the driver ended is not restarted by the keepalive (#40)

The Control page sends a held jog again every 100 ms, because the driver ends a jog it has not heard about for 400 ms. The resend was decided from the last snapshot alone: whenever it said Jog and 100 ms had passed, `JogStart` went out. When the driver ends a jog itself (a soft limit, its own 400 ms timeout) it takes a few of its 4 ms loops from the end to Idle, with Jog in the snapshots between; a tick that read one of those and sent the jog again could reach the driver already Idle, where `JogStart` starts a fresh jog. At a soft limit the controller stops it again at once (a stutter at the limit, where README promises a new press); after a stalled tick it would run the axis on. Now `Connection::refresh` sends the jog again only while it is still the jog the press started: the driver has logged nothing since the press (`Held::log_seq` against the snapshot's `log_seq`; the driver logs "Soft limit reached" when a limit ends a jog, before it goes Idle) and the last send is within the driver's 400 ms (`JOG_KEEPALIVE`), so a jog the driver timed out is not restarted either. A held jog kept alive every 100 ms is unaffected. The hold still ends when the snapshot leaves Jog, as before.

- **The race, made deterministic** (`control::tests::a_jog_the_driver_ended_is_not_restarted_by_the_keepalive`, on a simulated UC100 with the JY5800-like limits): X set to machine zero and moved to X 3, then −X held until the driver ends the jog at X 0 with one "Soft limit reached". The test then puts the connection where the race puts it: the snapshot the driver published while stopping (Idle's log with the limit line, the state still Jog), the key still held and the resend due, and runs the part of `refresh` that acts on a snapshot (`observe`, split out of `refresh` for this). Over the driver's next 75 loops the state stays Idle and the log grows by nothing; then a +X press jogs and a release stops it. The live driver cannot be caught in the 4 ms window from a test, so the race state is set by hand rather than waited for.
- Before the fix the test fails at the log check: the resend reached the driver Idle and it logged "Soft limit reached" a second time (9 lines against 8).
- The other jog tests pass unchanged, `a_held_jog_keeps_moving_and_stops_on_release` (a hold past the driver's 400 ms) included; the new test passed 5 times in 5 alone.
- Not inspected in the real window: checked with tests only.
- `cargo test --locked` on Linux (16 October 2026, other builds sharing the machine), rebased on #60: 870 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored in 342 seconds; 869 at main plus the test above. `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked`: 52 warning lines, none on a changed line (the one in `control.rs` is `to_sim_zero`, as at main). `cargo fmt` changed nothing outside the two files of this change.

## A cut too light for the voxels still pushes (#60)

A pass thinner than about half a voxel (a 0.3 mm underside skim, a wall's finishing allowance) sweeps none of the voxels' centres. `forces::bites` dropped such runs, so the operation had no push, its margin line read "no cutting force sideways or up to hold against" and it passed on any grip. Now a run whose cutter touches material (grown by half a voxel all round, it sweeps the centre of a voxel that still has material: `Volume::touches`, the read-only half of `Volume::cut`; a whole voxel since #158, below) but removes no voxel counts as a skin a voxel thick along the run, as wide as the cutter or as tall as its depth in the stock, whichever is more (the most that can hide between the centres), at most the slot: for a skim that is exactly the slot, 2r × depth × length; for a finishing pass along a deep wall, a voxel's thickness over the wall's height. A run in the air is still nothing, whether beside the stock or over what the setup's earlier operations cut away, and drilling is still not counted.

- **The force.** `forces::tests::a_skim_thinner_than_a_voxel_still_pushes_and_a_shallow_grip_refuses_it`, on the 100 × 60 × 40 mm stock whose voxels are about 1 mm:
  - A Ø10 skim 0.3 mm deep across the stock at 1000 mm/min, its tip above the top voxels' centres, removes 50 mm³/s in every stretch (10 × 0.3 mm² at 16.7 mm/s), its force 0.15 mm under the top. The same move 10 mm beside the stock gives no bite.
  - In steel with 3 flutes at 8000 rpm that is 50 N tangential, 75 N sideways. The starter vise gripping 1 mm holds that ×1.6 against tipping: the line reads "Facing: only ×1.6 against tipping; ×2 is wanted." and the issue names the 75 N, 39 mm above the 1.0 mm contact.
  - Before the fix the test fails at its first assertion: the skim gives no bites.
- **A setup whose only cut is the skim.** `post::tests::a_skim_too_thin_for_the_voxels_is_still_held_against_and_a_1_mm_grip_refuses_it`: the fixture block's Facing stopped 3.7 mm above the part (a 0.3 mm skim; the block's voxels are 1.3 mm) exports in the starter vise at grip 8, and at grip 1 `post::held` pins one problem on the Facing, which export refuses with exactly that line: "Facing: the cut could tip the stock out of the vise: about 75 N, 46 mm above the top of the jaws' contact, which is 1.0 mm high and 98 mm long (×1.4, under the ×2 wanted). Grip deeper, take a smaller stepdown or stepover or a lower feed, or choose another holder." Before the fix the setup exports at grip 1 too.
- The three LIO-167 tests and the smoke tests are unchanged: a cut that sweeps voxels is counted as before, and "Drilling: no cutting force sideways or up to hold against." still stands for drilling.
- **The planner's good plan still passes its check**, held in a vise, with its Top setup's Pocket at a 1 mm stepdown (`smoke::tests::apply_brings_back_a_workholding_entry_deleted_after_the_preview` and `undo_brings_back_a_workholding_entry_deleted_after_apply` apply it). Since the pocket stays down from loop to loop at the programmed feed, its first loop past the boss slots the narrow corridor beside it at 1000 mm/min, not at the 25 % entry feed that every loop's first lap used to take: 2 mm deep that is a capped 191 N, ×1.8 in the 2.9 mm grip; 1 mm deep it passes. A first version of the skin also allowed a voxel's advance of the cutter's whole cross-section, which gave a pocket's link moves of 2 to 4 mm, in cleared stock but beside a wall, a capped 191 N: the Top setup's Pocket, gripped 3 mm, was refused at ×1.9. The skin's rate does not depend on the run's length.
- Not inspected in the real window: checked with tests only.
- `cargo test --locked` on Linux (16 October 2026, three other builds sharing the machine): 843 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored in 328 seconds; 841 at main plus the two tests above. `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked`: the same 53 warning lines as at main, none in the changed code. `cargo fmt` was run on the changed files; five files main leaves unformatted were left as they are.

## The Drill editor's holes, the spindle cap and the plane clusters are not worked out again for nothing (#156, #176, #180)

- **#156.** The Drill editor listed the holes with `cam::holes` inside `view`, which passes over every triangle per opening and ran on every redraw (the planner page ticks twice a second, playback every 33 ms). `App::drill_holes` keeps the last answer, errors too, keyed by a hash of the mesh fingerprint, the setup's `rx`, `ry`, `rz`, `tx`, `ty`, `tz` and the operation's selected surfaces (their mesh fingerprint and ids). `smoke::tests::the_drill_editor_works_out_its_holes_once_per_part_placement_and_selection` counts the calls through a test seam (`DRILL_HOLE_RUNS`, per thread): with nothing selected three views run `holes` once; after a surface is selected five views run it once; a second surface, then a change of `tx`, run it once each over three views; an edit of the feed (not an input) runs it not at all. Not a before/after timing: the seam is new, so the test cannot run on the old code; the old code called `holes` on every view by construction.
- **#176.** `material::check` read and parsed the UC100 profile through `control::spindle_cap` on every call. `spindle_cap` now keeps the cap of each profile file by path and modification time (`control::CAPS`): the file is read and parsed again only when its time changes, and a missing file or an empty path is read as before (so it is refused, not kept). It still asks the file system for the file's modification time on each call, one `stat`, and does not read or parse the file; storing the cap on the `Machine` was not chosen because the profile path is edited in several places (the wizard, the Setup view, a plain field) and a stored value could go stale. `control::tests::the_spindle_cap_is_read_once_per_profile_file_and_modification_time` (five calls read once; a rewrite with a later time reads once more; another path is its own entry) and `control::tests::a_profile_that_is_missing_is_refused_not_kept` (a missing profile is an error, written afterwards it is used at once; a machine that is not a UC100 reads no file, though its profile path does not exist) count the reads through a per-thread seam (`CAP_READS`).
- **#180.** The plane clustering merged faces while each was within `PLANE_TOLERANCE` (0.01 mm) of the previous, so a chain of steps merged over any height, and the plane's depth came from the face with the lowest id, which also decided "top" or "floor". A cluster now closes when a face is more than 0.01 mm above its lowest (heights sorted, ties by id), and the depth is the area-weighted height of the cluster's faces. `analysis::tests::a_chain_of_faces_each_under_the_tolerance_apart_does_not_merge_across_the_tolerance`: three 40 × 30 mm tops at 10, 10.008 and 10.016 mm give two planes, the 10.016 face alone as "top" (depth 0) and the other two as one floor 0.01 mm below, in each of four orders of the surface ids with the same areas; the old code gave one plane of three faces whose kind and depth followed the first id. `coplanar_faces_a_few_microns_apart_are_one_plane_whichever_side_of_a_bucket_they_fall` still passes.

## A Pocket stepover over 50 % is reported in the editor (#31)

`Operation::issues` checked the stepover of Face, Indexed and Pocket against 0.01–100 %, but the pocket generator refuses anything over 50 % (offset contours must overlap), so a Pocket at 60 % showed no warning and failed only at Generate or export.

- Pocket now has its own bound, 0.01–50 %, with the generator's words: "Pocket stepover must be between 0.01 and 50% for overlapping offset contours." Face and Indexed keep 0.01–100 %. The stepover help says Pocket accepts up to 50 %.
- `model::tests::a_pocket_stepover_over_50_percent_is_an_issue_but_a_facing_one_is_not`: a Pocket at 50 % has no stepover issue, at 60 % and at 0 % it has one; a Face at 60 % and 100 % has none and at 101 % has one. Before the fix the Pocket at 60 % gave no issue and the test failed.

## Checks at their fields, and the keyboard into the form and its help (#80, #81)

- Each check of the selected operation is placed at the text field its message names (`model::issue_field`; `model::tests::each_check_message_names_the_field_it_is_about` covers the messages of `Operation::issues`, the speed and feed lines and the ones about no field). The field gets a red border for an error or an amber one for a warning, with the message under it after its label, Error or Warning; the list under the form keeps the checks no field on the open tab shows, in a red box for the errors (what stops Generate and the export) and an amber one for the warnings (a speed or feed outside the material's cutting data, which stops nothing), each line labelled, with a count of those the fields above show. `smoke::tests::a_pocket_stepover_over_50_percent_is_shown_under_the_stepover_field`: a Pocket at 60 % on Passes has its message right after the stepover field's label in the widget tree, labelled Error, and not in the list; on Tool the list has it. `smoke::tests::a_warning_and_an_error_have_different_labels`: a feed of 1 mm/min is a Warning at the feed field, a stepover of 0 an Error in the list, headed "1 error stops Generate and export".
- The operation audit's rows are buttons that open their operation at the tab the setting its line names is on (`ui::issue_tab`). `smoke::tests::an_audit_row_opens_its_operation_at_the_tab_its_line_names`: a Facing at 0 rpm fails its row with "rpm must be greater than zero."; the row opens it on Tool, in its setup.
- Tab with nothing focused enters the inspector's first field (`control::focus_first`), else the open page's first form field, else the first text field; on the Control page while a machine is connected it does nothing, so the arrow keys keep jogging (`tab_move`). The focused field's border is 2 px wide. `smoke::tests::tab_with_nothing_focused_enters_the_form_unless_the_keys_jog` checks the decision and lays the Work page out in a headless tiny-skia renderer: nothing is focused, the operation runs over the widget tree, and the operation's name field has the focus, which `help::focused_field` names.
- F1 pins the focused field's help card in the lower right corner (`help::focused_field`, `Message::HelpKey` and `HelpFor`), open to its More… text (#89); the card's Less, More… and Pause toggles reach the app there (`Message::HelpMore`, `HelpPause`), since no hover overlay takes them; F1 again, the card's Close button, Esc or leaving the page puts it away. Esc stays the E-stop while a machine is connected, and on the Control page (`Message::EscapeKey`). `smoke::tests::f1_pins_the_focused_fields_help_and_esc_puts_it_away_where_it_is_not_the_estop` (the card's text is in the widget tree while pinned), `smoke::tests::escape_is_the_estop_while_a_machine_is_connected` (a simulated UC100 reaches E-stop and the pinned help stays), `smoke::tests::escape_sends_the_estop_from_anywhere_even_while_typing` (the Esc and F1 key messages, typing or not).
- The pinned card's hint names the key that puts it away (#185): "Esc closes it" where Esc does (`App::esc_closes_pinned_help`: no machine connected, off the Control page), else "Esc is kept for the E-stop · Close puts it away". `smoke::tests::f1_pins_the_focused_fields_help_and_esc_puts_it_away_where_it_is_not_the_estop` reads both hints from the widget tree on the Work and Control pages, and `escape_is_the_estop_while_a_machine_is_connected` reads the E-stop hint with a simulated UC100 connected and the Esc hint once it is disconnected.
- A pinned card with an animation gets the 33 ms tick (#186): `App::ticks_fast`, the subscription's predicate, is true while a pinned card has a diagram and is not paused, as it is for a playing simulation, an animating hover card or a connected machine. `smoke::tests::a_pinned_card_with_an_animation_ticks_fast_until_paused`: a text-only pinned card ticks slow; the requested fixture clearance's card ticks fast, slow on Pause, fast on Play, and fast again when pinned anew after a Pause.
- Not inspected in the real window: the Linux renderer's screenshots are unreliable here; checked with the tests above.
- `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=6` on Linux (16 October 2026, rebased on #152, other builds sharing the machine): 949 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored in 359 seconds; 942 at main plus the seven tests above. `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` passes and `cargo fmt` was run.
- `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=6` on Linux (16 October 2026, for #184, #185 and #186, other builds sharing the machine): 958 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored in 367 seconds; 955 at main plus the three tests above (`help::tests::the_card_takes_the_side_with_room_and_stays_in_the_window`, `a_control_under_a_clamped_card_can_still_be_clicked_to_close_it`, `smoke::tests::a_pinned_card_with_an_animation_ticks_fast_until_paused`). `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets -- -D warnings` passes and `cargo fmt --check` is clean.

## Import from the start screen, closing during an import, a model path that is not UTF-8 (#45, #46, #48)

- **Import model** on the start screen archived the resumable project and made a blank one before the file dialog opened, so cancelling left a blank project. The new project is now started when a file is chosen (`FileImported(Some(Ok))` on the start screen). `smoke::tests::importing_from_the_start_screen_keeps_the_project_until_a_file_is_chosen`: with a project to continue, Import then a cancelled dialog leaves it on the start screen, resumable, with nothing in History; importing a file then opens the new project in MANUFACTURE and archives the previous one. Before the fix the cancelled import had already archived it and left MANUFACTURE.
- `blocking_work` now counts a model import, so closing the window during one chooses `Exit` (the import runs under `spawn_blocking`, which the runtime waited for, up to 120 s for a STEP file, with no window), and `Close` when a G-code export is also running. `smoke::tests::a_model_import_in_flight_ends_the_app_on_close`; before the fix the decision was `Close`.
- `ModelFile::path` is a `PathBuf`, written as a JSON string when it is valid Unicode (so older project files, where the field was a string, load unchanged) and as the raw bytes otherwise. `model::tests::the_model_path_is_read_from_a_string_and_written_as_one_when_it_is_text` (an old-format string loads and is written back as a string) and `model::tests::a_model_path_that_is_not_utf8_survives_a_save_and_load` (Unix: a path built with `OsStr::from_bytes` that is not UTF-8 round-trips). serde's own `PathBuf` cannot be written when the path is not UTF-8, hence the small `path_json` module. The example project has no imported part, so it has no path to check.

## The work zero on any stock, and the probe that finds it (LIO-166)

- Choices: `post::ORIGINS`, a corner, an edge's middle or the centre of the stock's top or bottom (18) and Model origin. "Selected point (planned)" is gone; a project saved with it is refused with "… is not a work zero: choose one under Work coordinate origin." (`unsafe_or_unsupported_jobs_are_refused_with_reasons`). A stock-based zero is a point of the box the setup starts from (`simulation::leftover_box` for a later setup), and each zero is found from the stock top and the sides it names (`post::references`; Model origin from the front-left corner).
- The probe against the real in-process material (`control::tests::the_probe_sets_the_programs_zero_on_every_stock_state`): every one of the 19 zeros, on three stock states of the fixture block (setup 1's raw stock; a setup turned 90° about Z, and one flipped about X, after setup 1 faced and pocketed). The routine (`control::Edges`, as the Control page runs it) runs against the original block in the setup's frame less what `rest::Removed` says earlier setups can have cut, to 1e-5 mm, with a 4 mm tip; no guarded descent may land on material. The work zero it sets equals `post::datum` within 0.01 mm for every zero the check accepts, the bottom zeros and Model origin included. The check refuses exactly three: Stock top center and Stock bottom center on the turned setup (setup 1's pocket is in the middle of the top, where both are touched off) and Stock bottom center on the flipped one (the pocket is under it).
- Refusals name the spot and the zeros still on the stock: a contour that took the +X side refuses Stock top center with "Choose one that is, Stock top front-left, Stock top left, Stock top back-left, Stock bottom front-left, Stock bottom left and Stock bottom back-left, or Model origin for this setup." (`a_stock_datum_is_refused_when_an_earlier_setup_cut_the_side_it_needs`, all four sides; `side_touch_off_spots_lie_just_below_the_stock_top`). The other refusal tests keep passing with the old spots and messages.
- Words: the header and program 1 say where every zero is touched off; front-left now names each side's stretch from the front and from the left, and a zero on the bottom is "Touch off Z47 on the stock top … (Z0 is the stock bottom, 47 mm below it)" (`a_stock_based_work_zero_says_where_to_touch_off`).
- A jaw or clamp in the probe's way (`a_jaw_or_clamp_in_the_probes_way_is_named`): a +X jaw up to 8 mm below the top of a 40 mm stock is named for Stock top center and not for front-left; 11 mm below it is clear; a clamp over the top's spot is named for Stock top left and not for Stock top right. The probe is refused with that line; export and Run are not.
- Through the simulated UC100 (`probe_edge_and_the_touch_off_set_a_zero_on_the_stock_bottom`): Stock bottom back on the fixture's top setup offers **Probe edge**; from over the top's spot it sets the work offset to the block's middle across X, Y a tip radius inside the block's back side (the simulator's probe is a point) and Z the stock's 47 mm below the block's top, within 0.15 mm. By hand, the Run panel's step 4 is "Set X0 Y0 Z47": Move there puts the tool tip on the block's top over the zero and the button sets the work offset to the block's back edge, 47 mm below the top. Model origin is offered Probe corner (`probing_the_edges_needs_a_stock_zero_a_tip_and_its_work_system`). Probe centre, Probe corner, Probe Z and the soft-limits guide pass as before.
- The 3D view's X0 Y0 Z0 marker is `post::datum` on the box the view knows for a later setup, and none is drawn on the original block (`a_later_setups_known_box_is_drawn_as_its_stock_and_otherwise_none_is`).
- Not inspected in the real window: the PC was in use. Not validated on hardware (LIO-126).
- `cargo test --release` on Windows (`AXIS_SKIP_STEP=1`), rebased on LIO-167: 837 passed, 3 failed. Two are the STEP tests, as before. The third, `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`, timed out waiting for jogging under the suite's load and passes alone (LIO-131; fixed under #9, below).

## Controller tests without wall-clock timing (#9)

- **The cause of the jogging timeout** (`the_control_page_renders_in_every_state`, SWITCHED profile) was in the test, not in the app or the driver. The driver marks the last homed axis referenced two of its 4 ms loops before it is back in Idle (one loop for its position re-anchor, one for `pending_idle`), and the test waited for the three axes to be referenced only, so its jog press reached `Connection::act` while the snapshot still said Homing, where `manual()` rightly drops it: a jog must not start during homing, and the app does not queue a press. With 20 ms between the test's ticks the press landed in that 8 ms window in 3 of 4 logged runs on Linux (`state=Some(Homing) manual=false referenced=[true, true, true, ..]`, then no held jog) and in the one that passed it saw Idle. The test now waits for Idle and the references, as `home_needs_home_switches_on_every_axis` already did. The LIMITED profile passes either way: a declared machine zero keeps the driver in Idle.
- **Waits are measured in the driver's time.** `tick_until` and `run_until` count the simulated controller's `timer`, which the simulator advances once per driver loop (4 ms when the PC keeps up, later under load, while the simulated machine still moves in real time), so a limit of `secs` is `secs × 250` loops, with a wall-clock cap of ten times that and a minute at least should the driver stop. The 111 waits keep their numbers.
- **`mdi` no longer sleeps 60 ms** for the driver to take its line. A line that runs may leave Idle for a single driver loop, too short to be sure to see, and a refused one never leaves it; so a second line follows it, `G999`, which the driver refuses in every state (`MDI: machine is busy` while the line runs, `MDI: unsupported G999` otherwise) and takes in order: that refusal in the log says the line has been taken up, and Idle from then on says it is done.
- **The five wall-clock assertions** are counts or orderings now: the open-edge split's count of ends looked at (`Closure::looked`, about 30 per open edge on the fan cylinder against 170 million for every end of every open edge); the partial proposal's summary read up to the 256 KiB limit, not the 4 MiB; the fake OpenAI server's new `answered()` and `closed()` counts, so a token endpoint stalled 10 s is given up on (Retry) before the fake answers, and a completed reply is returned while the fake still holds the connection; and the callback listener's wait limit (10 s) as the only bound on three idle connections, each of which the listener gives 5 s.
- Not fixed here: a declaration (`SetMachine`) the driver drops when two arrive within one of its loops (LIO-163), which `work_zero_steps`' test still presses again for.
- On Linux (Rust 1.99, 24 cores, 14 GB, other worktrees building and testing at the same time, load average 35 to 75): the single test passed 10 times in 10, about 1.0 s each; `cargo test --locked` four times in a row: 839 passed and 2 failed in the first (1,748 s, under a load average above 100 with the 4 GB swap full: `a_setup_runs_tool_run_by_tool_run_with_a_manual_tool_change` by the driver's own E-stop "Motion buffer ran empty mid-move (USB or computer too slow)", and `calibration_measures_steps_per_mm_and_fills_the_profile` at its wait for E-stop after reconnecting, whose new driver thread had published nothing within the 60 s wall cap), then 841 passed in each of the next three (457 s, 159 s and 292 s). Before the change the test failed 3 times in 4 alone and once in 1 in the full suite here. `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked`: no new warning in the files touched (the seven there are on untouched lines). rustfmt 1.10 (Rust 1.99) reformats lines main has not been formatted with, so only this change's own hunks were formatted.

## The Run panel keeps to its setup (#3)

- Found in review (issue #3): a run kept the index of the setup it was picked with. Setups moved or deleted in MANUFACTURE only marked it out of date, and the check once the edits pause, as Check again, used the old index: after a move it installed another setup's programs under the run's name, from which the touch-off button, Probe Z and Probe corner, edge and centre then set a work zero; after a delete it indexed past the end and the panel read "The check failed: task … panicked".
- Now the run names its setup by id and its index follows the project (`run::Run::follow`, at every edit and before every check): with the first setup picked, a copy of it without operations moved before it moves the run to the second index, and the check after the edits pause finds the setup's two tool runs, not the copy's refusal. Deleted, the run reads "This setup was removed from the project." in place of its programs at once, after the check once the edits pause and after Check again; Cycle Start's problems are that line alone, and the touch-off button, Probe Z, the edge probes and the work-zero steps have nothing to act on. `run::programs` refuses an index past the end with the same line instead of indexing it (`smoke::tests::the_run_keeps_to_its_setup_when_setups_are_moved_or_removed`).
- Found in the second review round (#190): `follow` kept the old index when the setup was deleted, so the Run strip showed whichever setup took that index as picked, under the old name. The index is now out of range then, so no setup is picked (`run::tests::a_removed_setup_leaves_no_setup_of_the_project_picked`: after a delete and another setup in its place, `follow` answers None and no index of the project is the run's).
- Found in the second review round (#189): removing the only UC100 machine from the library while connected left the connection live with no Disconnect on the Control page. Remove is refused for the connected machine with "Disconnect before removing the connected machine.", and the page draws Disconnect whenever a connection is live, also with no machine in the library (`smoke::tests::the_connected_machine_is_not_removed_and_disconnect_stays_on_the_control_page`).

## The 3D view drawn right-handed, not mirrored (#5)

The projector's screen right was (cos yaw, −sin yaw, 0) with the viewer at (sin yaw · cos pitch, cos yaw · cos pitch, sin pitch), so right × up pointed away from the viewer: every view was a mirror image, and the planner's pictures flipped their frame to undo it. `viewport::Camera` now has the machine view's frame: the viewer at (sin yaw · cos pitch, −cos yaw · cos pitch, sin pitch), yaw 0 from the front, screen right (cos yaw, sin yaw, 0) and screen up +Z projected on the screen.

- The camera buttons' views project the axes as a right-handed frame: TOP shows +X right, +Y up and +Z toward the viewer; FRONT +X right, +Z up and +Y away; RIGHT +Y right, +Z up and +X toward the viewer; ISO, the default camera, +X right and down toward the viewer, +Y right and up away, +Z up (`viewport::tests::the_named_views_project_the_axes_as_a_right_handed_frame`).
- The pixels agree with the projector: in a top view every pixel a box beyond the part's +X side changes is in the right half of the frame, and every pixel of one beyond +Y in the upper half (`viewport::tests::a_top_view_draws_what_is_beyond_x_on_the_right_and_beyond_y_above`).
- The planner's pictures are no longer flipped. Their nine angles keep their viewpoints (each yaw is π minus the old one), a top view still has +X to the right and +Y up and a front view +X to the right (`look::tests::a_top_view_keeps_x_right_and_y_up`), the tags' depth lookups read the frame as drawn (`look::tests::no_tag_covers_the_triad`, `look::tests::the_pocket_floor_is_tagged_where_it_can_be_seen`) and the canvas no longer mirrors (`look::canvas::tests::composite_fills_the_background`).
- Picking goes through the same projector and depth (`viewport::tests::picks_visible_floor`, `viewport::tests::imported_picker_uses_visible_surface_and_setup_transform`). The setup browser's stock pictures, the Control page's job view and the planner preview draw with the same projector, so they agree with the machine view, whose frame was already right-handed. A drag now turns the model the way the mouse goes, as in the machine view (`smoke::tests::the_preview_view_has_its_own_camera` still steers both cameras).
- The pinned digest of `the_viewport_draws_the_same_pixels_after_the_split` was pinned again: the drawing changes by design.
- Renderer screens are not conclusive on this Linux machine (the capture repeats a stale frame from screen 39 on and loses most labels after the first screen, with every adapter); screen 01 was inspected before and after. Not inspected in the real window.
- `cargo test --locked` on Linux (15 October 2026, in the worktree's own target dir, rebased on main at bff3946): **848 passed, 0 failed**. Before the rebase, 843 ran and one failed: `analysis::tests::the_edge_test_is_quick_where_long_open_edges_meet`, a 2-second timing assertion that took 2.9 s under the suite's load on a machine building several worktrees at once and passes alone in 0.55 s. `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked` adds no warning in the files touched; `cargo fmt --check` passes.

## Cycle Start, Hold, Resume and Stop in sight on the Control page (#71)

- The connected Control page is now a fixed part over a scrolling one. Fixed: the machine's state, SIMULATED, Reset and the E-STOP, the E-stop reason, and the **Run** strip (`run_strip` in `ui`, replaced by `ui::run_bar` in LIO-274): the setup picked, the tool run Cycle Start starts ("Next tool run: 2 of 12, T3 Drill 4"), **Cycle Start**, **Hold**, **Resume** and **Stop**, the Space note, the progress of the tool run being cut or the "Before Cycle Start" reasons with **Set coolant to Off**, and how the last tool run ended. Scrolling: the identity line, the soft limits, the **Tool runs** panel (`run_panel` in `ui`, removed in LIO-274: the list, Check again, the tool change, the reference surface and probe rows, Work zero on the stock) beside the Job view, then the position, jog, model, work zero, spindle, overrides and MDI as before. Every message, action and guard is as it was; only where it stands changed.
- `the_run_bar_keeps_cycle_start_and_stop_now_beside_a_long_operations_list` (then the_run_strip_keeps_cycle_start_and_stop_above_a_long_tool_run_list, renamed by LIO-274): the fixture job's two tool runs on the simulated machine, then the same checked as twelve. The page builds with both. Counted by their state in the widget tree, the strip holds the setup's button and the four run buttons with two tool runs as with twelve, and the Tool runs panel holds ten buttons more. `the_control_page_renders_in_every_state` and the run tests build the page as before.
- Not inspected in a window: the Linux renderer smoke screenshots are unreliable on this machine. Not validated on hardware (LIO-126).
- `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked` adds no warning (the nested `if` at the E-stop reason, which the fixed part's code touched, is collapsed). `cargo test --locked` on Linux (16 October 2026, rebased on #16, the machine shared with three other builds): 865 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored, in 669 s.

## Sidebars that fold and resize, Fit, and an inspector that keeps its tabs and Generate in sight (#76, #77)

- The Work page is the browser, a handle, the 3D view, a handle and the inspector (`ui::work_page`). Each sidebar has a ‹ › button at its top that folds it to a 28 px strip holding the Show button; the handle between a sidebar and the view is a 7 px canvas (`ui::Grip`) that drags the sidebar's width, the browser from 170 to 420 px and the inspector from 320 to 560 (`ui::BROWSER`, `ui::INSPECTOR`). The widths, whether each is open and whether the inspector's About is open are `settings::Layout`, saved in `settings.json` when a drag ends or a button is pressed. What is drawn comes from `ui::fitted`: the saved widths cut to their ranges, then, so that the 3D view keeps 500 px (`ui::VIEW_MIN`) in the window at its least width (1120, `MIN_WINDOW`), the browser narrowed first and the inspector after it; the window's width comes from its resize events (`Message::Resized`). **Fit** beside ISO/TOP/FRONT/RIGHT is `Message::Camera("FIT")`: the zoom and the pan go back, the direction stays.
- The inspector (`ui::inspector`) is a pinned head, a scrolling body (the only scrollable, still `"inspector"` for the smoke run's scrolls) and a pinned foot (`ui::Panel`). For an operation: the icon and the strategy's name with the Hide button, and the tabs in one row of `FillPortion(1)` buttons with centred 11 px labels (two rows of three for the six-tab Orientation strategies); the body: the name field, **About ‹strategy›** (`Message::ToggleStrategyHelp`, closed by default, the choice saved), which holds the strategy's explanation and its availability line, and the tab's parameters; the foot: the configuration checks, the workholding caution, Generate, Duplicate, Suppress, Delete, Move up and Move down. The setup editor pins its title and steps and, under the body, its checks, Duplicate setup, Move up, Move down and Delete setup…. Every message, action and parameter is as it was.
- `settings::tests::the_work_page_layout_is_saved_and_a_file_without_it_reads_as_the_defaults`: the layout round-trips, and a settings file without it, or with part of it, reads as the defaults for what it lacks.
- `ui::tests::the_3d_view_keeps_its_least_width_beside_the_sidebars`: the arithmetic of `fitted`, `view_width` and `clamped`: the defaults leave the view over 500 px at 1120; both sidebars at their most are cut, the browser to its least first, to exactly 500; a wide window gives both their widths; a closed sidebar is its strip; saved widths outside the range are cut to it; a drag stops at the range and at the view's least.
- `smoke::tests::the_sidebars_fold_and_resize_and_the_layout_is_saved`: on the example, each sidebar folds to fewer widget nodes and back, and both choices are saved at once; a drag's live width is not saved and its end is; past the range a drag stops at the range's end; at 1120 the browser as drawn gives way and the view keeps 500; the inspector dragged wide there pushes the browser to its least and stops at the view's least, while the browser's saved width is kept for a wider window; the page builds at both widths; a new app on the same workspace starts with the saved layout.
- `smoke::tests::fit_refits_the_scene_and_keeps_the_views_direction`: after an orbit, a zoom and a pan, Fit sets the zoom to 1 and the pan to 0 and keeps the yaw and the pitch; TOP still sets the pitch; the help card names what it does.
- `smoke::tests::the_wheel_zooms_toward_the_pointer_far_in`: a wheel step aimed at a corner of a 140 × 100 × 12 box zooms the 3D view in 1.5× with that corner's screen point unchanged (within 0.01 px); forty more steps aimed at it reach the 100× limit (was 4×) with it still in place (within 0.5 px), and eighty steps out stop at 0.3×. On the TOTEM case (not a test, 21 October 2026), zooming on a screw boss kept it at the same screen point at 1×, 10× and 100× and each frame drew in 4–16 ms.
- `viewport::tests::turning_a_zoomed_in_view_keeps_the_surface_at_its_centre` (found in review: at 100× a 5 px drag swung the zoomed-in point 700 px, off the view, as the view turned about the scene's centre): with a point of the fixture part brought to the view's centre at 100×, the point the view turns about is back at the centre through `Projector::at_depth`; a drag through the canvas's own `update` publishes a turn whose pan shift keeps that point within 0.5 px of where it was, where the turn alone would move it more than 100 px. Three more moves handled before the view is drawn again keep it within 0.5 px too: each turn starts from the camera the earlier ones left (found in review: computed from the view's camera, two to four moves a redraw drifted it 130–930 px). `viewport::tests::a_long_line_through_the_frame_is_drawn_without_gaps`: a line from x −500 000 to 500 000 px across a 200 px frame lights every pixel of its row, in both line drawers, since only its part in the frame is stepped along (at 100× a toolpath move over 20 mm drew as dashes, the steps being capped at 10 000); a segment off the frame draws nothing.
- `smoke::tests::the_inspector_pins_the_identity_tabs_and_generate_and_scrolls_the_parameters`: counted by their state in the widget tree, the inspector of the example's Facing has one scrollable, and outside it exactly Hide, the five tabs, Generate, Duplicate, Suppress, Delete, Move up and Move down, on every tab; the About disclosure is closed at first, opening it adds nodes without moving a pinned button, the choice is saved and a new app starts with it; the setup editor pins Hide, the four steps, Duplicate setup, Move up, Move down and Delete setup…; with no setup, the inspector still has its one scrolling body.
- Not inspected in a window: the Linux renderer's screenshots are unreliable on this machine. The drag itself (the canvas's press, move and release) is read in the code, not driven by a test.
- `smoke::tests::the_browser_builds_with_a_long_operation_and_setup_name_and_keeps_its_count_fixed` (#78) now expects one button outside the browser's scroll, Hide, where it expected none.
- `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` pass. `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=6` on Linux (16 October 2026, rebased on #83/#86, #84/#85 and #14 at 5f1417e, in the worktree's own target dir, the machine shared with other builds): **935 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored** in 295 s; on #90/#91 before that, 929 passed. The run before the browser test's count was corrected had that one failure.

(`control::Connection::run_program`), but the touch-off button under Work zero on the stock ("Zero all", "Set X0 Y0 Z47") and Move there were live: the panel still showed the step from the old check, and the button set the work offset for it.

The new test fails at Move there without the fix.

## The renderer smoke captures the frame after each step, checks each capture, and has MACHINE screens (#90, #91)

- **What was wrong (#90, reproduced).** Iced's `window::screenshot` does not read the window: it renders again the primitives the window last drew, and the window draws only on a `RedrawRequested`, which winit on Wayland delivers only after the compositor's frame callback for the previous frame (GNOME sends none to a covered window or to any window while the session is locked). The smoke took its screenshot on the next tick, whether or not a frame had followed the step, so a window that drew no frame gave the previous screen again, and with most labels gone: a text widget's paragraph belongs to the widget tree, which every message rebuilds, and the old primitives hold only weak references to the paragraphs the rebuilt tree replaced (canvas text, which is cached by content, and the pick lists stayed). The run's own checks (the model in the motion samples) did not see it, and it exited 0. Measured on the owner's runs of 15 October (`/tmp/smoke-NVIDIA`, `-AMD`, `-llvmpipe`, `-x11`, four windows at once): 54 to 56 files each, 9 or 10 distinct; a labelled screen measures 28 to 30 sharp luminance edges per 1,000 drawn pixels, the stale frame 3.8.
- **Now** (`src/smoke.rs`): a subscription on the window's frames (`smoke::frames`, raw events, only while the run waits, since each message has Iced draw again) counts the frames drawn after a step (frames timed before the step, during a window resize or while the stock surface is still being meshed do not count), and the second one is captured (`App::smoke_frame`); a step whose frame has not come in 15 s of an unblocked main thread fails the run naming the screen (`App::smoke_overdue`; a tick more than 2 s after the one before says the thread was busy with a tick's in-line work, and the wait starts again). Each capture is checked (`App::smoke_check`): its hash differs from the capture before it (screen 91 excepted, which is captured twice by design), over 2 % of its pixels are drawn, and it has over 12 sharp edges per 1,000 drawn pixels at the window's scale; each failure names the screen. The two resize steps wait for the window's new size first.
- **Measured, 16 October 2026.** On the desktop (GNOME 50 Wayland, NVIDIA Vulkan, session locked, 1.25 scale): screen 01 captured 2 frames after its step, then `Screen 02-geometry: the window drew 0 frames after its step in 15 s …` and exit 101. Under Xvfb (1920 × 1200, llvmpipe), on the branch rebased on 5981d41: exit 0 in 14 minutes, 122 files, 121 distinct hashes (screens 59 and 62 are the same picture by design: the flipped setup at its start, before its playback and after the rewind; an earlier run of the branch had all 122 distinct), every one of the 123 captures 2 frames after its step, the window 1440 × 930 → 1120 × 720 → 1440 × 930 as the steps ask; text 15.9 (screen 07, Tools) and 17.8 (screen 94, the Control page before Connect) to 120 (screen 72) edges per 1,000 drawn pixels, drawn share 9 % (screen 72) to 79 %.
- **#91, screens 94 to 98**, the MACHINE workspace with the owner's machine (`control::owners_machine`, the JY5800 profile written to the smoke's workspace, connection Simulated) assigned to the setup; inspected in the Xvfb run: 94 the Control page before Connect (the machine's button, "Connection: Simulated", Connect, "Not connected."); 95 connected, E-STOP with SIMULATED, Reset, the Run strip ("Pick a setup…"), "Soft limits are OFF", the position table with no position set and the log; 96 Idle after Reset and Set machine zero here (X, Y and Z "Yes", "Soft limits on: X 0…390, Y 0…280, Z -55…0 mm", the log's three "declared at machine 0.000" lines) with the machine drawn in the Position panel; 97 setup 1 picked in the Run panel, checked in line, "This setup cannot run" with its reasons (the program's 65 mm Z span over the 55 mm travel, the clamps the job was given at screen 89) and Check again, the Job view under it; 98 the Setup view: the profile's path, "The driver accepts this profile.", the inputs, the outputs and the calibration. The status line reads "Connected to Jingyan CNC4030: E-STOP" and "…: Idle" (the tick puts it there; the steps wait for the driver's state with the connection's own refresh, up to 10 s). The steps leave the tasks they return alone, as the other smoke steps do, and the run disconnects before it closes.
- README: Tests and renderer checks says the window must stay visible, `WGPU_BACKEND=vulkan` on Linux, how to run under Xvfb, the per-capture checks and screens 94 to 98; the platform table's Linux row no longer calls the screenshots unreliable.

## Work zero as on a milling machine: DRO lines, Probe from, and a probing screen

- **What was asked.** Every way of setting the work zero reviewed, a probe able to reach the zero from a corner one can get at, and the DRO operations a milling machine's display has, such as ½.
- **What was missing.** The Control page had Zero X/Y/Z/all, G54–G59, Probe Z and one Probe corner / edge / centre button that probed only the spot the setup's zero names: a jaw at that corner left the probe refused, with "touch it off by hand". There was no ½, no value to set an axis to, no way back to X0 Y0, and no probe without a setup picked.
- **Now.** The Work zero panel sits across the page under Position and Jog: per axis the work position, **Zero**, **1/2** (half, `Action::WorkHalf`: SetWork to half the position shown) and a value with **Set** (`Action::WorkSet`, Enter too), then **Zero all**, **Go to X0 Y0** (`Action::ToWorkXY`: one rapid of X and Y, Z where it is; Idle, X and Y referenced) and G54–G59. Setting Z by any of them counts as the touch-off. **Probe from** (`Action::ProbeFrom`) is a 3 × 3 grid of the stock top's spots, back at the top: the check of a setup lists, for each, why a probe cannot find the zero from it (`post::probe_from`: a jaw or clamp in the way, by `in_the_way`; a face it touches cut by an earlier setup, by `stock_zero_cut`; a stock not square to the setup). The routine (`control::Edges`) already found the setup's datum from the faces it touches, so from another spot it places the zero by the stock's size and says so ("placed from the back-right corner by the setup's … mm stock"). A new pick of setup in the Run strip resets it. **Probe a feature** (`control::Feature`, `control::Routine`), without a setup, from where the tip is: Z top, an edge X+/X−/Y+/Y−, the four outside corners (a guarded step of `CORNER_REACH`, 15 mm, along each side, then a probe of the other) and Bore centre (−X, +X, then −Y, +Y at the middle found); the feature becomes work zero on its axes, the tip's radius allowed for, and the tip goes back where it started (the bore's centre for Bore centre). A guarded step that touches, a miss, Stop or E-stop sets nothing.
- **Tests.** `control::tests::every_zero_is_found_from_every_spot`: each of the 18 stock-based zeros on a 124 × 84 × 40 mm box, probed from each of the nine spots, is found within 1e-9 mm, and only a spot not its own says "placed from". `control::tests::each_feature_is_found_from_where_the_tip_is`: on a box, Z top, the four edges and the four corners within 1e-3 mm, each ending where it started; a Ø30 bore found on its centre from 5 mm and 3 mm off it, "30.000 mm across Y"; no rapid or guarded move touches material.
