Through the simulated UC100: `the_setups_zero_is_probed_from_the_corner_picked` (a front-left zero probed from the back-right corner lands on the front-left corner within 0.15 mm; nine spots offered; a new setup pick goes back to the zero's own), `a_feature_is_probed_where_the_tip_is_and_becomes_work_zero` (Z top, X+, X− and the front-left corner on the simulator's block, other axes untouched, the tip back where it started; a corner started in front of the block but not left of it stops at its guarded step with nothing set), `half_set_and_go_to_x0_y0_work_as_on_a_dro` (½ of X40 is X20 with the machine still; Set refuses "abc" and takes " -5 "; Go to X0 Y0 moves X and Y only; nothing in E-stop). `the_control_pages_typed_fields_and_sliders_have_help` covers the Set field's card.
- **Renderer smoke, 16 October 2026, macOS (Apple Silicon, 2× scale).** `--smoke-test` exit 0: 123 PNG files (99 screens, 24 motion samples), 122 distinct (screens 59 and 62 alike by design). New screen 99, the owner's simulated JY5800 Idle with no setup picked: the Work zero panel across the page under Position and Jog, X, Y and Z each with its position, Zero, 1/2, a Set field ("-5" typed for X) and Set; Zero all, Go to X0 Y0, G54–G59; Probe Z and every feature button greyed with "The UC100 profile has no probe input" (the JY5800 profile has none). The Probe from grid is not on a smoke screen: the smoke project's setup 1 cannot run (screen 97), and the grid needs a checked setup; `the_setups_zero_is_probed_from_the_corner_picked` renders the page with it (`a.view()`). Screen 96 now scrolls 970 px to show the machine under the Work zero panel.

- **The setup's zero had no place of its own (the owner could not find it).** It was a 19-entry list, "Work coordinate origin", under the setup editor's Machine step. Now the editor has a **Work zero** step (`ui::SETUP_STEPS`, `setup_zero`): Stock top / Stock bottom / Model origin, a 3 × 3 grid of the stock seen from above (back at the top) for the corner, edge middle or centre, and the work offset, with the note on how it is touched off and probed. The 3D view's heading reads "Work zero: <zero> · Change G54 / holding" (the zero first, so a narrow view cuts off the rest), the link opening that step from wherever the inspector is (`Message::SetupStep` opens the setup editor). The step's ✓ needs a work offset and a zero on the stock; Machine's needs the machine only (`the_work_zero_step_picks_the_zero_on_a_grid_of_the_stock`, `the_setup_editor_opens_each_step_and_marks_what_is_done`).

## A drill's point in the stock (#155)

- **What was wrong.** Since #6 a blind hole's `bottom` is the apex of the cone a 118° drill point leaves, and the drill is sent there; but a drill simulated as a flat cylinder (`CutterShape::Flat`), so the simulation removed the ring of part between the CAD cone and the cylinder, up to r·tan 31° tall (2.4 mm for Ø8). On the example block's 1.29 mm cells that ring held cells well inside the part, over the coarse gouge check's threshold of 3, and the plan was refused with "This operation cuts into the part". The uncut faces and the removed volume read the cone as cut too.
- **Now.** `CutterShape::Drill`: flat flutes over the cone of a 118° point (`simulation::POINT_RISE`, tan 31°, the slope `cam::holes` takes a blind hole's floor to have), with the programmed tip at the apex. The drill cycle's plans sweep it (`cam::drill`) in the three sweeps: the voxel estimate and every reader of `volume::swept` (the coarse gouge check, the uncut faces, the stock summaries), the continuous field the stock surface is meshed from (`stock_surface::Cut::field`) and the top-down height grid (`simulation::StockGrid::cut_shape`). A plunge leaves the cone over the tip; along a ramp the cone is lowest where the tip's descent balances the cone's rise, or at an end of the move when the move is steeper than the cone. The flat and ball shapes are untouched: their branches are the expressions they were, and their pinned tests pass unchanged. The drawn tool is still a cylinder down to its tip, and the evidence for cutting-start heights (`rest.rs`, constant-height flat sweeps only) does not use drill sweeps, as it did not before.
- **Tests.** `plan_check::tests::a_plan_that_drills_a_blind_hole_with_a_drill_point_floor_passes_the_gouge_stage`: the cam tests' Ø24 cup on a base whose Ø8 bore ends in a drill-point cone (`cam::tests::revolved`, the fixture of #6), drilled by a Ø8 drill (T4, 8000 rpm, 1000 mm/min) to the apex, 14.4 mm under the stock's top, passes every check; before the change it was refused with `gouge 1/Some(1): This operation cuts into the part: about 61 mm3 near X 8-16, Y 8-16, 8-10 mm below the top (setup 1 axes, from the model's -X/-Y corner).` `plan_check::tests::a_drills_point_cone_under_a_blind_hole_is_not_a_gouge` reproduces the issue's estimate: the same hole in a Ø120 × 40 mm disc in a 124 mm stock (1.29 mm cells), swept as a flat cylinder to the apex, removes 20 cells well inside the part (the issue estimated 4–21), and swept as a drill, none. `volume::tests::a_drills_point_is_a_cone_under_its_flat_end` pins `POINT_RISE` to tan 31° and checks a plunge against the cone's height at several radii, and two ramps, a horizontal stroke and a move steeper than the cone against the lowest cutter sampled at 4,000 instants; `volume::tests::swept_shapes_are_invariant_to_playback_subdivision` now covers the drill. `stock_surface::tests::continuous_cutter_fields_match_analytic_swept_solids` and `continuous_cut_field_follows_rotated_and_translated_setup` compare the drill's field with `volume::swept` as they do the flat and ball ones, and `a_drills_mesh_keeps_its_point_cone_and_closed_surface` meshes a Ø6 drill's plunge at Standard detail: watertight, the floor within 0.03 mm of the cone and the wall within 0.01 mm of the radius. `simulation::tests::a_drills_sweep_leaves_its_point_cone_in_the_stock_grid` checks the height grid's cone under a plunge exactly, and a ramp against the sampled lowest cutter, whatever the playback's chunking.

## Planner analysis and checks (no AI)

- Part report on the example block: the top plane with five holes (four Ø6.6 through with Ø11 openings, one Ø6 blind), counterbore rings, the 72 × 40 mm pocket at 12 mm depth with its fitting cutter, and the features of the other five orientations. A face is listed only when at least half of it is visible from above (a 40 × 20 mm face of 80 triangles under an overhang is listed whole at 85% and 60% visible, and not at 40% and 15%), and no surface is listed in two orientations. Faces whose heights differ by no more than 0.01 mm from their neighbour in height order are one plane, wherever a fixed bin edge would fall: two 40 × 30 mm block tops 0.0002 mm apart, at every step of 0.0001 mm across 0.002 mm boundaries (20 pairs from 10.0000 and 10.0002 mm), are one plane of kind top in the report (`analysis::tests::coplanar_faces_a_few_microns_apart_are_one_plane_whichever_side_of_a_bucket_they_fall`), and a plan listing the surfaces of both finds that plane (`proposal::tests::a_plan_may_list_surfaces_of_coplanar_faces_a_few_microns_apart`). A plane's holes are listed completely or not at all, and a part that is not a closed solid is refused.
- Proposals: the schema is strict at every level (every field required, nothing extra) and lists exactly the fields of the Rust types. The parser rejects unknown fields and a missing non-nullable field.
- Job building: setup 1 keeps its name and work zero (the plan's are ignored), fixture and stock; later setups share its machine and fixture, get the next free work offset (G55) and the orientation's rotation, and inherit stock. The option combinations the generators require are fixed by the app, surface IDs are deduplicated, and control and format characters are removed from names and notes.
- Reference problems (an unknown tool, a wrong tool type, surfaces from another orientation or plane, a cutter that does not fit a floor, speed and feed limits, too many operations, a drill marked as going through a blind hole, an oversized breakthrough) are each reported with their place: the setup and operation where one applies, the setup or the whole plan otherwise.
- A hand-written two-setup plan passes all stages with its six operations timed, and a built job survives saving and reopening. A reference problem stops before generation, a job the app cannot save stops at structure, and a generation failure and an export refusal are each reported at their own stage. A failing plan returns no job.
- Tool axis: a Ø4 mm drill with a 15 mm breakthrough, which goes 12 mm below the stock, is refused. A Ø1 mm drill marked through on the Ø6 × 8 mm blind hole never reaches this stage: the drill cycle refuses the blind hole at generation, with its setup and operation, and the same case in a second setup is refused in that setup's frame without flagging the first (`plan_check::tests::a_drill_sent_past_a_blind_holes_bottom_is_refused`, `plan_check::tests::a_blind_hole_in_a_later_setup_is_refused_in_that_setups_frame`). The good plan and a plan with Parallel and Waterline finishing added raise no problem; the 0.1 mm limit is the same for every strategy.
- Later setups: in the flipped setup, a Ø4 mm drill through the Ø6.6 holes with a 3 mm breakthrough (set on the built job) is refused for going 3 mm below the part's underside, although the flipped stock box reaches 4 mm lower (setup 1's Face removed that stock); with no breakthrough the job passes.
- Drilling through in a later setup: a plan with `through` true in the flipped setup is refused at the structure stage (the part rests on the exit face); with `through` false the same plan passes every check.
- Closed surface: copies of a vertex within the tolerance are one vertex. The tolerance covers float rounding and text with 7 significant digits: 8 f32 steps at the part's largest coordinate, or, if that is more, √3 times the last of 7 significant digits there (with the coordinate taken as at least 1 mm). Copies are welded only in groups that fit in a box whose diagonal is at most twice the tolerance; the points of a wider group stay distinct. A triangle written twice with the same winding counts once, each open edge is split at the ends of other open edges within the tolerance of it, and the open edges left refuse the part with their number in the message; a part that encloses no volume is refused with its own message, and a part more than 1,000,000 mm from its file origin with another. A test ray whose only solid spans are of zero length (a crossing facing down and one facing up within 0.0001 mm of one height, with nothing solid on either side) is inconsistent and reads as solid from the model's bottom to its top. Refused: a box with 12 × 12 mm squares missing from both its top and its bottom (which no vertical ray sees; 8 open edges), the same openings placed at (412.3, −287.6, 95.1) mm with each opening's corners joined across it in pairs by rows of zero-area triangles whose points are 0.0003 mm apart (8; the tolerance there is 0.00049 mm), a 40 × 3 mm slot through the top and bottom at the origin whose ends are bridged by collinear zero-area triangles 0.000055 mm apart (8), a box missing one wall triangle (3), a single triangle, a UV sphere with T-junctions and every tenth triangle removed, a box beside an open cup whose 10,000 rim vertices are 0.0006 mm apart, and a UV sphere whose vertex copies are up to 0.0002 mm apart (still refused at its 30 mm size, where the tolerance is 0.000017 mm); a triangle, a hemisphere and a flat grid each written with both windings enclose no volume; a 100 × 100 mm OBJ sheet of 1 mm quads on a gentle saddle, each quad written in both windings so that its two sides use opposite diagonals, has no open edge and encloses about 0.08 mm³, and is refused by the ray test (every ray inconsistent); a box at 1,100,000 mm gets the far-from-origin message. In a unit test, copies of a vertex 0.00001 mm apart in a row joined by zero-area triangles are welded while the row spans 0.00003 mm and stay distinct at 0.00004 mm (the tolerance there is 0.000017 mm). Accepted: a closed box, a box with a T-junction, a UV sphere and one whose vertex copies are up to 0.00001 mm apart, a fan-capped cylinder with T-junctions, 4 mm (256 segments) and 1 mm (128 segments) cylinders whose caps are fans from a rim vertex with every other triangle split, a 1,024-segment fan-capped cylinder with every other top spoke split into 100, the example block with every third triangle split at full size and scaled to 10, 15, 6 and 4.7 mm long, with one triangle written twice, placed at (412.3, −287.6, 95.1) mm and at (1012.3, 687.6, 0) mm with every 10th triangle's longest edge split into 30 pieces, split every 10th into 120 and then turned 30°/30° and placed at (412.3, −287.6, 95.1) mm, at the origin with every 50th split into 100, and with setup 1 moved by (500, −300, 80) mm, two boxes sharing an identically triangulated face (stacked and side by side), a box at 990,000 mm, and all five example models. Written as ASCII STL with `%.6e` (7 significant digits) after T-junctions computed in f64 and read through the app's STL reader, also accepted: the example block placed from the origin to 120 mm with every 10th longest edge split into 30, and the block scaled to 101.6 mm (4 in) with every third triangle split and with every 10th split into 30. Found in review (not a test): with 6 significant digits (`%g`) the same meshes are refused (8,740 open edges for the first, 168 and 1,373 for the 101.6 mm ones), because 6 digits round coordinates of 100–999 mm to 0.001 mm; binary STL, 3MF, STEP or text with at least 7 digits are accepted. Found in review (not a test): the 7-digit coverage is for files in millimetres or another power-of-ten unit; text in inches or feet with 7 digits, or with 4–5 fixed decimals on parts under 100 mm, is not covered when the mesh has T-junctions. Found in review (not a test): dense T-junctions (vertices within about 0.002 mm of each other or of an edge) on parts more than about 1 m from their file origin are refused, also as binary STL. Openings narrower than twice the tolerance (0.002 mm or less up to 1 m from the origin) are welded shut. These limits come from constructed meshes in review; no realistic export tried was refused. A closed box whose top faces inward passes the edge test and is refused by the ray test, with the message that has no count. A void whose top lies on the block's top face reads the same whether its triangles are listed before or after the block's, and is still accepted: its zero-length span at the top lies above solid, so it is not on its own. Measured (not a test), release build, about 200,000 triangles: 0.17 s for the fan-capped cylinder with T-junctions, 0.13 s for the T-junction sphere with every tenth triangle removed (refused), 0.42 s for a sphere whose vertex copies are 0.00012–0.00027 mm apart (refused); the debug-build test of the 20,000-triangle cylinder takes 0.4 s. Measured (not a test): the whole part report takes at most 3.3 s (release) on the realistic meshes tried up to 200,000 triangles, the slowest being the example block with every triangle split into 16 (170,816 triangles), so no triangle limit is set. Known limit: part analysis can take tens of seconds on meshes with tens of thousands of long sliver triangles in one plane (synthetic fan or T-junction slivers; in review, a 100 mm disc whose caps fan 20,000 rim segments from one rim vertex, 79,996 triangles, took 21 s in release); realistic meshes up to 200,000 triangles take a few seconds.
- Open meshes behind the report: with the report's refusal bypassed, a box whose top lacks a 12 × 12 mm patch is refused at the gouge stage when a Waterline cuts 38 mm into the patch, by both the tool axis and the coarse check (the check's report carries the coarse "This operation cuts into the part: about …" message for the Waterline, so dropping the coarse problems fails the test); a plunge where the underside lacks a patch is refused; a pocket open from below under a missing top patch passes. The closed box passes. The example block has no inconsistent ray, and a mesh no test ray hits (two closed 0.1 mm cubes at opposite corners of a 100 mm square) is refused as not a closed solid.
- Proposal numbers: a negative `bottom_offset` (an outside Contour 3.9 mm below the model in the flipped setup) is refused at the structure stage, as are negative allowances and numbers that are not finite (a JSON 1e39).
- Coarse gouge: a synthetic cut into the part (a 10 mm cutter plunged 8 mm into solid material beside the pocket) is detected, and the good plan removes no cell well inside the part. Three removed cells in all are needed, and then every operation that removed some is named, including one that removed 1 or 2 cells before the total was reached (`plan_check::tests::every_operation_that_removes_deep_cells_is_named_once_the_total_is_reached`: an operation removing 2 and one removing 1 are both named). A drill sweeps the cone of its point, so a blind hole with a drill-point floor drilled to its apex is not a gouge: see **A drill's point in the stock (#155)**.
- Coverage: the exposed share rises as operations are added, planes the report lists are not counted again among the sides, and leftover stock is split into above, around, inside the part's bounding box and below. The finished good plan leaves none above or below.

## Flat parts from sheet: DXF import, Sheet stock, tabs and hold-down (LIO-250)

- **DXF reader** (`dxf::tests`, 57 tests): each entity (LINE squares chained in any order and direction, an ARC and LINE slot, a CIRCLE, two semicircle ARCs, a closed LWPOLYLINE, an R12 POLYLINE with its VERTEX and SEQEND); bulges (a positive bulge goes anticlockwise and a negative one clockwise, bulge 1 between two points is a circle centred between them, a closed polyline's last bulge is its closing segment, the real tray's −3.439 bend reliefs); `$INSUNITS` (inches ×25.4, cm, missing or unitless read as mm with a note, an unknown code refused by name); chaining (ends 0.005 mm apart join, 0.5 mm apart is the open-outline error naming both ends and the gap, three ends at one point the branch error); nesting (holes found whatever the entity order, an island inside a hole, two separate outlines and no loops at all refused); layers (BEND, frozen and off layers, annotation layers, DEFPOINTS and paper space skipped with notes; text and dimensions ignored; a SPLINE on a cut layer refused, on BEND ignored); a self-crossing bow-tie and two crossing circles refused; a circle drawn twice, or a polyline drawn backwards over another, merged; a negative extrusion mirrors X; CRLF and leading spaces; a binary DXF refused. The flat solid is watertight (every edge used twice, in opposite directions), its normals point out and its volume is the area × the thickness within 0.1 %, its walls carry one CAD face per line or arc (1 top, 2 bottom), and no triangle is degenerate, on random star plates with holes, an L-shape with holes and a slot, 256 holes in a row and the real files. The power feed's mains tray (`examples/mains-tray-flat.dxf`) reads as 197.535 × 295.134 mm with 9 holes (2 × r2.2, 2 × r1.7, 4 × r2.5 and a 47.2 × 28.2 cut-out) and its BEND layer (5 lines) skipped; its lid as 104 × 93.134 mm with 5 round holes.
- **Sheet stock and the spoilboard** (`sheet::tests`): the sheet lies at the part's bottom, centred on X0 Y0, as thick as the part when empty, thicker when set, and a thinner one is refused; the allowance is 0.5 mm when empty, any other stock has none, and −1, 5 and a word are refused. On the tray (`a_cut_deeper_than_the_spoilboard_allowance_is_refused`), a contour 0.3 mm below the sheet and a drill's 0.4 mm breakthrough are refused with a 0.2 mm allowance and pass with 0.5 mm. The plan check's floor is the allowance below the sheet (`plan_check::axis_problems_over`).
- **Tabs** (`cam::tests::tabs_go_on_straight_sides_evenly_spaced_never_on_corners_short_segments_or_tight_arcs`): on a 100 × 40 rectangle 4 tabs 6 mm wide each sit on one side clear of the corners by the margin, within 6 mm of the even spacing and at least 1 mm apart; on a side with a notch of 2 mm steps none lands on the notch; a Ø10 circle takes none ("Only 0 of 2 tabs…"), a Ø400 one takes 6; more tabs than the sides hold are refused with how many fit. The existing tab test (`contour_tabs_keep_the_part_joined_to_the_stock_around_it`) passes unchanged with the new placement.
- **The tray, cut and exported** (`sheet::tests::the_dxf_tray_is_cut_from_a_sheet_with_its_holes_and_a_tabbed_outline_and_exports`): the DXF at 1.5 mm on a 260 × 360 sheet held by 4 corner screws; the Ø4.4 drill, the Holes contour and the tabbed outline each go 0.3–0.4 mm into the spoilboard; the Holes contour (smallest hole 5 mm) goes round the cut-out and the four Ø5 holes and not the Ø4.4 or Ø3.4 ones; the outline rises over its 4 tabs to 0.6 mm above the sheet's bottom at both levels below that; the LinuxCNC program has the tabs' Z and names the sheet and the screws in its header.
- **Hold-down** (`sheet::tests`): a sheet without a hold-down is an issue; tape puts no boxes in the way; screws default to the corners, a screw off the sheet or whose text does not read is refused; on the tray (`a_hold_down_screw_in_the_cutter_path_or_on_the_part_is_refused`) a screw 7.2 mm beside the outline is refused by the export ("Outline: … hold-down screw 1 …") and by the UC100 run checks, the other screw is not named, and a screw in the middle of the sheet is refused as on the part in the editor and at export; an outline cut through without tabs is refused on screws and exports on tape, and a sheet held by nothing is refused at export (`an_outline_cut_through_without_tabs_is_refused_on_screws_but_not_on_tape`). A plan with tabs and a Contour `inside_holes` passes the plan check and builds those settings; a `bottom_offset` past the allowance and an outline without tabs on screws are refused (`a_plan_for_the_tray_with_tabs_and_holes_contours_passes_the_plan_check`). A project file saved before DXF import loads with no thickness (`model::the_model_path_is_read_from_a_string_and_written_as_one_when_it_is_text`).
- **Part report** (`sheet::tests::the_part_report_lists_the_trays_holes_cut_out_sheet_and_screws`): the tray's `sheet`, the screws as `Hold-down screws` boxes, the top plane's 8 through holes by diameter and its 47.2 × 28.2 opening.
- **Headless** (by hand, 2026-10-24, projects in /tmp from `examples/bearing-housing.axis.json` with Aluminium 5052-H32, the 3-axis Workshop Mill, T1 Ø3 flat end mill, T2 Ø3.4 and T3 Ø4.4 drills, a Sheet 260 × 360 (lid 160 × 150) held by 4 corner screws): `--part-report` on `mains_tray_flat.dxf` at 1.5 mm gives 197.53 × 295.13 × 1.5 mm, as the matching `mains_tray_flat.step` (its points span 197.535 × 295.134 × 1.5; its cylinders 2 × Ø4.4, 2 × Ø3.4, 4 × Ø5, plus the outline's Ø12 and Ø5.432 arcs), the top plane's 8 through holes (2 × Ø3.4, 2 × Ø4.4, 4 × Ø5) and its 9 openings with the 47.2 × 28.2 cut-out; the lid gives 104 × 93.13 × 1.5 with 4 × Ø8.5 and 1 × Ø10 (its STEP: 104 × 93.134 × 1.5, 4 × Ø8.5, 1 × Ø10). A plan of Drill Ø3.4, Drill Ø4.4 (through, breakthrough 0.4), Contour `inside_holes` from 5 mm and an outside Contour with 4 tabs 4 × 0.6 mm, both 0.3 mm below the part, passes `--check-plan` (4 operations, 9.7 min; the lid's two contours 4.4 min) on the LinuxCNC machine and on a UC100 with the owner's profile; `--export-gcode` of the saved job writes one program whose outline rises 8 times to Z-0.900 (4 tabs at the two levels Z-1.6 and Z-1.8 below their top) and goes no lower than Z-1.9 (the drills' 0.4 mm into the board). With screw 1 at 24,250 the plan check fails with "Outline with tabs: the tool hits the hold-down screw 1 at X29.7 Y244.0 Z-0.8 (within the 2 mm gap)." (also on the UC100) and the export writes nothing; a screw at 130,180 is refused as on the part.

## AI planner

- Verified with a scripted stand-in for the model, and for the OpenAI backend with the loopback stand-in; no test contacts OpenAI. A live planning run needs the user's ChatGPT sign-in and is not part of this evidence.
- The OpenAI backend sends the whole conversation (system, user, assistant, user) with the plan schema, and once the plan route has refused structured output it asks for plain JSON at once.
- A good first proposal gets one coverage round, and the passing plan with the larger exposed share is kept; a first plan that leaves nothing covered makes one request. A failed check goes back to the model with the check report, and the corrected proposal is accepted. Output that is not a proposal counts as a request and is answered with the parse error; a proposal inside a Markdown code fence or between sentences is read.
- Four failures end the run with the last problems (the last reply's parse error, not the first reply's check problems) and no job. A plan that already passed survives a later request that fails, cannot be parsed or does not pass; the outcome counts the requests made, names the request whose plan won and keeps a follow-up API error. Of two different check failures, the last is reported. An API error, or a check that fails to run, after a failed check keeps that check's problems (bounded); a check that fails to run first leaves none, and one after a plan passed ends the run with that plan.
- An API error or an unmet precondition ends the run at once; a part with setup 1 rotated by 30° is refused before any request.
- Each run's stage sequence is pinned (Analyzing, Asking, Checking, Repairing after a failed check or an unreadable reply, Extending for the coverage round); every stage has its own page text, and the running text names the model the run was started with, not one picked later.
- The Plan task's body sends each stage and each check's result with the run's epoch, ending with Previewing and a preview that has its 3D view, or with the failure, and the app shows that result. Blocking work is counted until it ends, even after the run waiting for it is cancelled, and Plan is refused while an earlier run's blocking work is still running. Dropping a run while its part report, a plan check or the 3D view is held running keeps the count at 1 until that stage ends, then 0. With one blocking thread held by a first stage, a second stage queued behind it already counts (2) before its closure starts, so the count is taken when the work is queued, not inside the closure (mutation-checked).
- The first request carries the instructions and the part report, and none of the project title, the file path, the file name or the mesh fingerprint. Correction rounds after a generation failure and after an export failure carry none of the project title, setup 1's name, the file name or path, a program name (`.ngc`) or the mesh fingerprint, and the plan returned keeps setup 1's own name.
- The instructions mention each limit the builder and the generators enforce that the model can act on (checked by key words), and each of their numeric statements holds on the example block (120 × 80 × 40 mm, Ø10 flat end mill), with every operation generated after the stock the ones before it leave, as the check does: Parallel with a 0.6 mm stepover and tolerance 0.02 generates, 0.5 mm exceeds the 30,000-sample limit; Pocket at a 0.5 mm stepdown generates, and with the tool staying down from ring to ring still does at 0.25 mm and exceeds the 30,000-move limit at 0.2 mm, outside Contour 0.35 and 0.3 mm, Waterline 0.45 and 0.4 mm (all three generate at 0.5 mm); Pocket at 0.5 mm generates with 3 finish passes and with a 1° ramp and exceeds the move limit with 4 finish passes or a 0.1° ramp, and an inside-floor Contour after it generates with 4 finish passes and exceeds it with 5, while no allowed ramp angle (down to 0.1°) takes it over, as the instructions' finish-pass and ramp sentences and the qualified 0.5 mm guide say; Pocket at 0.5 mm generates at stepover 12 % and exceeds it at 10 %, while an inside-floor Contour's stepover stays 40 %; Face at 5% stepover generates at a 0.4 mm stepdown and exceeds 2,000 strokes at 0.38 mm, as the stated levels × passes formula predicts; at 12.5% stepover 0.165 mm generates and 0.155 mm does not, a pair only the formula's "+1" separates (26 levels × 76 rows is 1,976 strokes, × 77 is 2,002); with `facing_area` `model` 0.38 mm generates (the stock's width fails there) and 0.36 mm does not; Waterline on a 60 × 40 × 20 mm box generates 300 layers in 17,701 moves and refuses 301 layers; each of these three was mutation-checked (dropping the "+1", facing the stock for `model`, allowing 301 layers); a peck 2% above the drilling depth ÷ 1,000 generates and 2% below exceeds 1,000 pecks per hole; a later setup without operations is refused.
- Model text: a refusal ends the run with one clean line of bounded length; a 5 MB summary is kept as 600 characters, 15 warnings as 12 and 25 unmachined entries as 20 (the counts left out are kept), and names, reasons and workholding notes are cleaned and bounded; setup and operation names keep only letters and digits of any script, combining marks on a kept letter and - _ . , / ( ) # + : & % ~ < > = ± ° ⌀ ≤ ≥ before the check builds the job, after fullwidth forms are read as ASCII, dashes and minus signs as -, ～ and 〜 as ~, ٫ ٬ ٪ as . , %, ⁄ and ∕ as /, ∅ as ⌀ and × as x, and after format characters (soft hyphen, U+200B–200F, U+2060–2064, U+FEFF) are dropped without a space, so quotation marks (Pi and Pf, " ' „ ‚, primes, fullwidth and ornamental quotes, ＂ and ＇ also between letters), check or cross marks (including ✅ ❌ ❎ ✕ ✖ √) and look-alikes outside any list (the modifier letters ʺ ʻ ʼ ˮ, geresh and gershayim, the ditto mark, corner brackets, ⹂, 🗴–🗹, ᐟ ᐠ ᐥ, ŉ and the Duployan affixes) are removed, and each removed character leaves a space, so words and numbers never run together ("a«b»c" becomes "a b c", "Pocket→drill 1✓2" becomes "Pocket drill 1 2"); "Face top", "Pocket Ø8 (left)", "Drill 4x M6", "Waterline 0.5 mm", "Kontur außen", "粗加工 1", "Slot <6 mm", "Fillets R≤2", "Chamfer 45°", "Drill ⌀6.8", "Bore Ø10 ±0.02", halfwidth katakana with their voiced sound mark and Devanagari and Thai names with their marks are unchanged, while "Holes 1–4" becomes "Holes 1-4", "Face to Z−0.5" "Face to Z-0.5", "10*10 pocket" "10x10 pocket", "10 * 10" "10 x 10" (a * elsewhere is removed: "**Roughing** pass" becomes "Roughing pass", "Face *top*" "Face top"), "穴あけ 1～4" "穴あけ 1~4", "タップ M6〜M10" "タップ M6~M10", "مته ۳٫۵" "مته ۳.۵", "گام ۵۰٪" "گام ۵۰%", "Drill ∅10" "Drill ⌀10", "1⁄4 in drill" "1/4 in drill" ("M6~M10 tap" is unchanged), a Persian word written with a zero-width non-joiner stays one word, "Bohrung" written with a soft hyphen loses only the hyphen, "paral·lel" "parallel", "드릴·탭 M6" "드릴 탭 M6", "Drill·Tap M6" "Drill Tap M6" and "Face · top" "Face top", "Pocket—left" "Pocket-left", "粗加工（左）" "粗加工(左)", "フェイス・トップ" "フェイス トップ" and fullwidth "Ｆａｃｅ １０＊５" "Face 10x5", and an operation named "… holes 1–4 ⌀6.8" reaches the built job as "… holes 1-4 ⌀6.8"; an apostrophe between a letter or digit and a letter stays ("Bob's", "rock'n'roll", a decomposed "José's", "Op 2's") and × becomes x ("10x10 pocket") (the summary keeps its quotes); every text an API error carries, OpenAI's own messages included, is shown as one clean line of at most 300 characters; a failed run's problem messages are at most 300 characters. Cleaning stops reading once it has kept enough characters: clipping a 5 MB text to 11 characters fifty times takes well under a second.
- In the app: Plan starts only when the checklist is met, and setup 1's own issues (a blank name, a negative clearance) are an unmet item, so a job that passes the check on the anonymized copy is valid with the user's setup 1. Progress and results of an earlier run are ignored; Cancel and an edit abort the run's task and move its epoch on, so a late result of the first run is not the next run's preview.
- Cancel, Discard and (while running) Sign out drop the plan and leave the job unchanged. An edit to the job drops it too, and the status bar then says that planning stopped or the preview was discarded because the job changed; when the message has a status of its own (saving a tool, Undo with a preview on screen), that comes first and the drop sentence follows. Sign out, or a sign-in reported gone, during a run stops it with "Planning stopped because you are signed out.", so its late failure cannot sign out a later sign-in.
- Apply keeps the previous job in History without changing what Recover previous project reopens, and changes nothing when that copy cannot be written. It replaces setup 1's operations, removes later setups (also when a later setup is active) and activates setup 1. Apply adds the planner's setup-1 workholding note to setup 1's notes on the same line ("… · Planner: …", or "Planner: …" alone; line breaks become spaces; at most 500 characters), and setup 1 keeps that note in its `planner_note` parameter, which survives saving and loading the project. A later Apply first removes the last " · Planner: <that note>" followed by the end of the notes or by white space, or else the last "Planner: <that note>" at the start of the notes or after white space with the same ending, and replaces it: text typed after the planner's note ("Soft jaws. · Planner: Vise. Torque 40 Nm." becomes "Soft jaws. Torque 40 Nm. · Planner: Clamps.") or before a note Apply wrote alone ("Use soft jaws. Planner: Vise on the long sides." becomes "Use soft jaws. · Planner: Clamp on the short sides."), notes the user starts with "Planner: ", "Planner: <that note>" right after other text ("XPlanner: Vise."), a planner note the user changed and one the user went on with mid-word or mid-number ("Vises on both ends", "on the long sides, 40 Nm", in either form) stay, and of two whole copies the last one is replaced. A note that does not end in . ! ? or 。 counts as whole only before the end of the notes or a line break: "Planner: Vise grips here" keeps "Vise" and gets the new note added, while "Soft jaws. · Planner: Vise\nTorque 40 Nm." becomes "Soft jaws.\nTorque 40 Nm. · Planner: Clamps."; a note ending in ! ? or 。 is whole before a space too. Undo first keeps the applied job, with the edits since, in History, and changes nothing when that copy cannot be written. It restores only the setups and the active setup (so the note and its parameter go too): a tool, a machine, the title and the material changed after Apply stay, and a tool deleted since comes back, named in the status with its number: it keeps its own number when no tool in the library has it, and only a tool whose number a newer tool has taken is renumbered, to a number neither used nor kept by another re-added tool (tested with two re-added tools: one keeps T4, the other gets T5 rather than T4; a deleted tool that two restored operations use comes back once, and the job still validates). Importing another part, reloading the part, New project and Open end the Undo; New project and Open also drop a preview or a running plan.
- The preview's operation rows show the strategy, the planner's name, the tool and the time, the values that will run for each of the six strategies (an inside-floor Contour with its entry, ramp angle and fixed 40% stepover), and the planner's reason. Later setups are titled "Setup n · named by the planner: “…”", and setup 1's workholding note says it goes at the end of setup 1's notes on Apply, replacing an earlier planner note if it is unchanged. The value rows include the coolant, and Face's facing area. A Model-origin work zero is located from the part's −X −Y corner and the stock top, or from the part's top when an earlier setup cut the stock top. The second check line names the machine rpm and feed limits, and a note under the check lines says what is not checked (cutting loads, tool deflection, fixtures, clamps and the machine body) and that speeds and feeds are checked against the material's cutting data, which are starting points (`smoke::tests::the_preview_and_the_program_page_say_what_their_checks_leave_out`). The check lines, that note, each setup's work-zero line and the NC review page's note come from one function each that the pages use, and tests assert their output, including the good plan's setup 2 line ("… X0 Y0 at X+60 Y+40 mm from the part's −X −Y corner (part 120 × 80 mm), Z0 3 mm below the stock top"). It lists the walls and curved surface still covered and how coverage is measured. Its 3D view has its own camera, which starts framed on the part and never moves the machining viewport's camera.
- A failed run lists its problems (twelve, then a count) and has nothing to apply. Every API error reaches the failed panel, the status and the account note; a usage limit also shows the banner, and a sign-in that is gone signs out. A refusal reaches all three as one clean, bounded line.
- A follow-up request's usage limit or sign-out reaches the account panel and the preview, but not from a run whose client has been replaced. A plan that finishes clears the usage-limit banner and an account note that starts with the usage-limit message, also when the fallback-model sentence follows it. Counts read "1 setup", "2 setups" (and likewise for operations and problems).
- Without a model list (empty, or the request failed) the saved model is kept, or `gpt-6.1-sol` is used, and the panel says so; a model list loaded later replaces that note, whatever it began with. A sign-in that is gone gets no model.
- Renderer screens inspected (4 October 2026, before the final fix wave's text changes; not a test): a passing plan with Apply plan and Discard after the check lines, totals that read "3 operations in 1 setup", labelled planner text (setup 1's workholding note saying it goes at the end of setup 1's notes on Apply, replacing an earlier planner note, on one line) and each operation's values; its stock view with the Apply note; a failed run with its problem; a running plan asking for a correction, naming its model; and ready to plan, where the smoke run asserts the checklist is met. On these screens and on the not-granted screen, the checklist agrees with the account panel. The final fix waves' changes to these screens (coolant in the value rows, the Model-origin location, the second check line and the Not checked note) were not inspected in the renderer; tests assert the texts the pages take and build the pages.

## Materials and speed/feed checks

Each statement names the test behind it (in the module it is in; `smoke::tests::` are the application tests in `src/smoke.rs`), unless it is marked as inspected or measured. No test runs an Iced task through Iced's runtime (the bodies of the Plan and sign-in tasks are run directly as futures), touches the workspace on disk (they use temporary folders) or contacts OpenAI.

- The twenty-five starter materials each pass the material's own validation, have complete cutting data, start their notes with the starting-point sentence, use a listed group and have distinct names; Aluminium 6061-T6 holds Vc 150–300 m/min and 0.020–0.050 mm for Ø3–6 mm (`material::tests::starters_are_valid_and_complete`). The second set is six common aluminium alloys (Aluminium 6082-T6, 6063-T6, 5083-H111, 5052-H32, 1050A-H14 and Cast aluminium plate (MIC-6)), all in the Aluminium group, after the first nine, which keep their order (Aluminium 6061-T6 first, MDF ninth); 6082-T6 holds Vc 150–300 m/min, 5083-H111 120–250 m/min and 1050A-H14 0.010–0.025 mm for Ø3 mm and under (`material::tests::the_second_starter_set_is_six_common_aluminium_alloys`). The first nine rows (group, hardness, Vc, the five chip load ranges and the note) were compared with the design table by a script when this section was written (not a test); the six added rows are the values of their plan (not compared with a source). The third set is seven woods (Pine, Oak, Hard maple, Beech, Walnut, Cherry and Birch plywood), all in the Wood and composite group, after the first fifteen, which keep their order; pine holds Vc 100–1200 m/min, oak 100–1000 m/min and hard maple 0.050–0.130 mm for Ø3–6 mm, and `material::added_since` names what a library given set 1 or 2 is still missing (`material::tests::the_third_starter_set_is_seven_woods`). A library given the alloys but not the woods gets the seven once, after its own entries; a deleted wood does not come back and a wood the user already has under any capitals is not doubled (`model::tests::a_library_given_the_alloys_gets_only_the_woods_and_metals_once`). The wood rows' chip loads are conservative values in line with the MDF row and common router chip-load charts, and their surface speeds are wide enough for a router's usual speeds (not compared with a single source): on a 24000 rpm, 1000 mm/min router a 1/8" two-flute bit in oak at 18000 rpm (180 m/min), a Ø6 bit in hard maple slowed to 10000 rpm (188 m/min), a 1/2" bit in walnut at 18000 rpm (718 m/min) and Ø25 surfacing bits in birch plywood at 12000 rpm and pine at 15000 rpm are in range, while a chip thinner than cherry's minimum on a machine that could feed faster is still out (`material::tests::common_router_cuts_in_wood_are_in_range`).
- Cutting data count as present only when all twelve numbers are there, each positive and each minimum not above its maximum (equal is allowed); a material with none is valid, and partial data, a blank name or notes over 500 characters are issues, as is a name over 60 characters (after trimming, counted in characters; `material::tests::a_name_is_at_most_60_characters`) (`material::tests::cutting_data_must_be_complete_positive_and_ordered`, `material::tests::the_ordering_message_needs_two_positive_numbers`). A bad value is named in words ("Chip load up to 6 mm, minimum, must be a positive number."), and the ordering message appears only when both numbers are positive (`material::tests::a_bad_value_is_named_in_words_without_a_false_ordering_message`).
- Diameters 2.9 and 3 mm fall in the first band, 3.1 and 6 mm in the second, 10 mm in the third, 16 mm in the fourth, and 16.1 and 50 mm in the last (`material::tests::bands_follow_the_tool_diameter`).
- A Ø6 mm three-flute tool at 8000 rpm and 1000 mm/min (Vc 150.8 m/min, 0.042 mm per tooth) is in range for Aluminium 6061-T6; 20000 rpm (377 m/min) is above the range and 200 mm/min at 8000 rpm (0.0083 mm per tooth) below it; a Ø1 mm tool at the machine's 12000 rpm and 300 mm/min (0.0083 mm per tooth, inside the 0.003–0.010 mm a Ø1 takes) is in range although its Vc is under 150 m/min, and at 11000 rpm it is not; 7900 rpm is in range by the slack; a material without cutting data and a tool with 0 flutes are reported as not checked (`material::tests::surface_speed_and_chip_load_are_checked_with_machine_limits`).
- On a UC100 machine the "at the machine's maximum" exception uses the maximum the model is told: with a saved rpm of 30000 and a profile whose spindle maximum is 24000, a Ø1 mm two-flute tool at 24000 rpm and 320 mm/min (0.0067 mm per tooth, inside the 0.003–0.010 mm a Ø1 takes) passes the cutting data stage in Aluminium 6061-T6 although its Vc (75 m/min) is under 150 m/min, and at 20000 rpm (63 m/min) it is refused for its surface speed only (`plan_check::tests::a_uc100_plan_at_the_profiles_spindle_maximum_passes_the_minimum_surface_speed`).
- The machine's maximum feed excuses a chip load below the minimum, in the machine's own units: 300 mm/min at 8000 rpm is accepted on a machine whose limit is 300 mm/min (or 11.811 in/min) and flagged at 299 mm/min (`material::tests::the_machine_feed_limit_counts_in_its_own_units`).
- Every limit has 1% of slack and no more. 0.5% above the surface speed maximum, or above the chip load maximum, is in range and 2% above is flagged, each alone (`material::tests::the_upper_limits_have_one_percent_slack`); 1.5% above either maximum, and 1.5% below either minimum, is flagged (`material::tests::a_value_between_one_and_two_percent_past_a_limit_is_refused`); 0.5% below either minimum is in range and 2% below is flagged (`material::tests::the_lower_limits_have_one_percent_slack`). Changing the slack of each of the four limits (1.01 to 1.02 on either maximum; 0.99 to 0.98 and to 0.999 on either minimum, and to 0.97 on the chip load minimum) made at least one of these tests fail, tried when they were written and not repeated by a test. The material's name in a message is one line of at most 60 characters, also in "has no cutting data" (`material::tests::the_materials_name_in_a_message_is_one_line_of_at_most_60_characters`).
- A project saved before the library loads with no materials and no material id, and keeps its material text (`material::tests::documents_saved_before_the_library_still_load`).
- **The fourth starter set** is a bronze and two steels for the mill power feed's parts: Bronze CuSn12 (Brass and copper, 95 HB, Vc 100–200 m/min), Steel 1045 (C45) normalised (Steel, 190 HB, 70–130 m/min) and Silver steel 1.2210 annealed (Steel, 220 HB, 50–90 m/min), after the woods. Each is valid, has complete cutting data and a hardness in HB; 1045 is slower and takes lighter chips than 1018 and its chip loads stay under Harvey Tool's carbide figures for 10XX steel at Ø6.35 and Ø12.7 mm; silver steel is slower than 1045 and under Machining Doctor's 95 m/min for O1; bronze runs at lower speeds and chip loads than Brass C360 (`material::tests::the_fourth_starter_set_is_a_bronze_and_two_steels_with_hardness`). Their sources are in the README's Materials section; the numbers are conservative starting points between the sources and were not cut on a machine. A library given the woods (`starter_materials` 3) gains the three once, after its own entries, keeps its user's edits (an edited 1018's speed and hardness) and its deleted entries deleted (Brass C360 stays out), and a deleted new one does not come back (`model::tests::a_library_given_the_woods_gains_bronze_and_two_steels_without_losing_edits`); `material::added_since` names what a library of each earlier set is missing (`material::tests::the_third_starter_set_is_seven_woods`).
- **Hardness in the holding check.** A hardness written as a number of HB or HBW is read (`material::tests::hardness_is_read_in_brinell_only`) and raises kc1.1 above the group's reference hardness by (HB ÷ reference)^0.4, at most 1.5 times; a softer material or another scale keeps the group's value. 1018 (126 HB) keeps 1700 N/mm²; 1045 (190 HB) gets ×1.18, silver steel (220 HB) ×1.25, bronze CuSn12 (95 HB, reference 80 HB) ×1.07, brass C360 (78 HRB) stays 700, 60 HB and 60 HRC keep 1700 and 900 HB stops at ×1.5, plastic is never scaled; the same bite pushes 1045 1.18 times as hard as 1018, and 70 % of an 800 W spindle takes a shallower stepdown in silver steel than in 1045 than in 1018 (`forces::tests::a_harder_material_pushes_harder_and_a_softer_one_keeps_its_groups_force`, `forces::tests::a_stepdown_in_a_harder_material_is_shallower_for_the_same_power`). The 0.4 exponent is an assumed, rounded rule, not a table value.
- **Bronze is finished conventionally.** The bronze's `finish_milling` field is Conventional and no other starter has one (`material::tests::only_the_bronze_asks_for_conventional_finishing`). A Pocket or Contour with at least one finishing pass and the milling direction Climb gets one line naming the material (`finishing::tests::a_climbing_finish_in_bronze_is_named_and_a_conventional_one_is_not`); Facing, Drill and surface strategies, zero finishing passes and materials without the field have nothing to check, and applying the rule sets Conventional once (`finishing::tests::only_a_pocket_or_contour_with_finishing_passes_has_a_finish_to_check`, `finishing::tests::applying_the_rule_sets_the_direction_once`). The planner's plan check lists it as a cutting data problem on that operation and fails the plan, and none in aluminium (`plan_check::tests::a_climbing_finish_in_bronze_is_a_cutting_data_problem_naming_the_operation`); a plan built in bronze takes Conventional for its operations with finishing passes and leaves those without (`proposal::tests::a_plan_in_bronze_finishes_conventionally_and_in_aluminium_it_does_not`). The operation editor shows the same line as an amber warning (not covered by a test).
- A material text that matches a starter in another case is linked, and the project takes the starter's spelling; calling it again changes nothing; an unknown name ("Inconel 718") is added as a twenty-sixth material without cutting data; a document with no material gets the twenty-five starters and none chosen; a stale id is cleared (`model::tests::adopting_materials_adds_starters_and_links_legacy_names`). The starters are added whole only to an empty library: a library with one entry that has already been given the current starter set gets none, and a legacy name still links to it or is added without cutting data (`model::tests::a_library_with_entries_gets_no_starters`). A library saved before the second set (`starter_materials` 0) gets the six aluminium alloys, the seven woods and the three metals once and none of the first nine (a library of one entry becomes seventeen), and records the current set; an alloy deleted afterwards does not come back; a name the library already has, in another case, is not added twice; an empty library gets all twenty-five and the current set (`model::tests::an_existing_library_gets_the_new_alloys_once`). A deleted new alloy stays deleted through New project and Explore example, which carry the set with the library (`smoke::tests::a_deleted_new_alloy_does_not_come_back_in_a_new_project_or_the_example`). Choosing a material sets its id and its name, and an unknown id is refused (`model::tests::selecting_a_material_sets_its_id_and_name`).
- A name is taken regardless of case and spaces at its ends; a material keeps its own name, and a blank name is no clash (`model::tests::a_material_name_is_taken_regardless_of_case_and_spaces`). One rule, `material::same_name` (trimmed, Unicode lower case), serves this check, the link of a legacy name and Explore example (`material::tests::names_match_ignoring_case_and_surrounding_spaces`): a legacy name that differs from a library entry only by a capital outside ASCII links to it and adds no second entry (`model::tests::a_legacy_name_differing_only_by_a_non_ascii_capital_links_to_the_entry`), and Explore example keeps one of two library entries that differ so and does not announce a starter's spelling variant as the user's own material (`smoke::tests::explore_matches_material_names_like_the_library_does`). A long legacy name is shown cut to one line of 60 characters in the note, and the library keeps it whole (`model::tests::a_long_legacy_name_is_clipped_to_one_line_of_60_characters_in_the_note`). Duplicate or empty material ids are refused when the project is checked and when it is loaded (`model::tests::a_project_with_duplicate_or_empty_material_ids_is_refused`, `storage::tests::loading_refuses_materials_that_share_an_id_or_have_none`).
- Loading reports the tool-numbering note first and the material note second, and no notes once the project is saved with its numbers and link (`storage::tests::loading_reports_whether_tools_were_numbered`). `--export-gcode` ends its success and its refusal with the notes in that order (`post::tests::headless_export_announces_tools_numbered_on_load`), and adds none for a project that has no material text (`post::tests::headless_export_matches_the_ui_path`).
- The history folder keeps the newest 50 archives: with 55 older archives and one more written, 50 `*.axis.json` remain (the new one and the 49 most recently modified), the oldest are removed, and other files in the folder stay (`storage::tests::the_history_keeps_the_newest_50_archives_and_nothing_else_is_removed`).
- Opening a project whose material text is "Brass C360" links it to the library and says so in the status after Open, at workspace start-up and after Recover previous project, and it loads without the note once saved (`smoke::tests::loading_a_project_with_a_legacy_material_links_it_and_says_so`). A workspace whose start-up load returned notes is marked changed, so the autosave writes it once its edit delay has passed; the next start has no note, keeps the added material's identifier and does not write the file again; tools numbered on load are saved the same way, and a workspace that needed no migration, or a blank one, starts unchanged (`smoke::tests::a_workspace_migrated_on_start_is_saved_once_and_its_note_does_not_repeat`). The bearing-housing example, whose material is "Aluminium 6061-T6", links to the starter on load and adds no material without cutting data (`storage::tests::the_bearing_housing_example_links_to_a_starter_material`).
- A blank workspace starts with the twenty-five starters. A material the user added is carried to a new project and into Explore example; the example's material is linked to the user's renamed and edited copy of its starter, no name is repeated (ignoring case), the status names the user's own material and not the starters, and ends with the link note; opening the example again or a new project adds nothing; from a library without the user's edits the example links to the starter (`smoke::tests::new_project_and_explore_example_carry_materials_without_duplicates`). When the user deleted the starter, Explore example adds its material without cutting data and says so, and a later New project and Explore example keep that one entry (`smoke::tests::explore_example_adds_its_material_without_data_when_the_user_deleted_it`).
- The Materials page creates, edits, saves and deletes materials like the tool library: a duplicate name is refused, partial cutting data is refused with its issue, the project material cannot be deleted while it is chosen, and the page builds (`smoke::tests::materials_are_created_edited_saved_and_deleted_like_tools`). Saving trims the name, renames the project's material when it is the chosen one (a change of case is no clash), refuses a rename onto another material's name in any case, leaves the project material alone when another material is saved, puts the editor on the first material after a delete, and discards an unsaved draft without touching the library (`smoke::tests::saving_a_material_keeps_names_unique_trimmed_and_the_project_material_in_step`).
- The picker sets the id and the name, marks the project changed, ignores an id that is not in the library, and the page builds in every state: a chosen material, one without cutting data and none (`smoke::tests::the_material_picker_selects_by_id_and_every_state_builds`). Choosing the material that is already chosen changes nothing and does not drop a running plan; choosing another one does, and the status says what the new material did to the speeds and feeds, then that planning stopped (`smoke::tests::picking_the_selected_material_again_changes_nothing`). The note under the picker has three outcomes: none, "… has no cutting data: speeds and feeds are not checked." and "No material is chosen: …" (`smoke::tests::the_material_note_has_three_outcomes`).
- The material commands and every material field have their own help text, which claims no collision, gouge or cutting-load checking, and a cutting-data field names itself in full in its title (`help::tests::material_commands_and_fields_have_specific_help`). The name's help says a name has up to 60 characters. The spindle speed and feed help, the NC review page help and the Plan help say what the check does (`help::tests::speed_and_feed_help_says_what_the_material_check_does`).
- **Choosing a material sets every operation's speed and feed.** For every starter material, Ø1, 3, 6, 10 and 20 mm tools with 1 to 4 flutes and three machines (12000 rpm and 5000 mm/min; a 24000 rpm, 1000 mm/min router; a 40 in/min machine), 1,500 cases, the recommended speed and feed pass the check; Aluminium 6061-T6 with a Ø6 three-flute at 12000 rpm and 5000 mm/min gets 9980 rpm (the middle of 7958–12000) and 1050 mm/min (the middle of 599–1497); Pine with a Ø3 three-flute on the router 10611 rpm (its least surface speed, since 1000 mm/min cannot reach 0.04 mm a tooth) and 1000 mm/min, and with a Ø10 6000 rpm, a quarter of the router's maximum, not the 3333 the chip load would ask, as a Ø50 gets 6000 rather than the 4138 in the middle of its range; a Ø0.5 two-flute in aluminium gets a sixth of a Ø3's chip load (0.0016–0.0051 mm a tooth), and 0.015 mm a tooth on it is above "0.0017–0.005 mm for a Ø0.5 mm tool"; a material without cutting data, a tool without flutes, or a machine without a maximum spindle speed and feed gets none (`material::tests::a_recommended_speed_and_feed_pass_the_check_for_every_starter_tool_and_machine`). In a document on the router, choosing Pine sets the Facing at 12000 rpm and the Contour at 8000 both to 10611 and leaves an operation without a tool, saying "1 operation not set: the operation has no tool."; again it changes nothing; Pine, Acrylic (PMMA), then Pine again ends at 10611 rpm as Pine directly does, the result astra's review found missing when only operations outside the data changed (Contour and Pocket then stayed at Acrylic's 16670 rpm, 0.020 mm a tooth in pine); Aluminium 6061-T6 then changes both and puts both in range; a material without cutting data changes nothing and says so; without a material nothing is said (`model::tests::choosing_a_material_sets_every_operations_speed_and_feed`). In the app, picking Pine for the fixture job (aluminium speeds) puts every operation but the drilling in range, names each changed speed old → new on the status line, keeps the drilling's and says so, and picking it again changes nothing (`smoke::tests::picking_a_material_sets_every_operations_speed_and_feed_and_says_so`).
- On the fixture job, a Pocket at 30000 rpm gives "Surface speed 942 m/min is above Aluminium 6061-T6's 150–300 m/min." and at 12000 rpm and 2500 mm/min 377 m/min; the NC review page lists "Setup 1 · Pocket: …" with the same text; a suppressed operation is not listed; with a material without cutting data every operation is listed as "Not checked. Unknown has no cutting data."; with no material nothing is listed; an operation whose tool is not in the library is listed as "Not checked. The operation has no tool in the library." (in the operation editor and on the NC page); and the program is still ready to export with the Pocket outside the material's data, so a warning does not block export (`smoke::tests::speeds_and_feeds_outside_the_materials_data_warn_and_never_block_export`). The list's heading is the problem heading when any line is a problem, and the not-checked heading when all are not-checked lines (`smoke::tests::the_nc_heading_follows_the_lines`). These tests build the Tool tab and the NC review page; no renderer screen shows a warning box or the NC list, so their layout was not inspected.
- Planner: the part report carries the material as an object (name, group, hardness, notes, the surface speed range and five chip load bands up to 3, 6, 10 and 16 mm and above) and every tool's flutes; the material's text is one cleaned line of at most 60, 40, 40 and 500 characters (`analysis::tests::the_report_carries_the_materials_cutting_data`, `analysis::tests::the_report_lists_tools_machine_limits_and_serializes`). Planning needs a chosen material with cutting data: the report and the checklist give "Choose a material on the setup page." or "Add cutting data to Inconel 718 on the Materials page.", and a material text with no library material behind it is not enough (`analysis::tests::a_report_needs_a_material_with_cutting_data`, `analysis::tests::preconditions_name_what_is_missing`).
- Plan check: a Pocket at 2000 rpm gives two problems at the "cutting data" stage, naming setup 1, operation 2 ("Surface speed 63 m/min is below Aluminium 6061-T6's 150–300 m/min." and "Chip load 0.167 mm per tooth is above Aluminium 6061-T6's 0.030–0.080 mm for a Ø10 mm tool."); they do not stop generation (six operations still timed, coverage still measured) or the export checks (a flipped setup's refused Stock top center is reported beside them), a generation failure and a cutting-data problem in another setup come back in the same report, a Ø4 mm drill at 280 mm/min is below the chip load range (0.0078 mm per tooth, which a check now says with a fourth decimal under 0.01 mm) because drills are checked like end mills (`plan_check::tests::speeds_and_feeds_outside_the_materials_data_are_refused_beside_the_other_problems`). An operation whose speed and feed cannot be checked is refused too, one problem each at the "cutting data" stage: a job with no material ("Speeds and feeds could not be checked: Choose a material with cutting data." for each of the six operations), a material without cutting data ("… Aluminium 6061-T6 has no cutting data.") and tools with no flutes ("… The tool or the operation has no positive speed data."); generation and the other stages still run, and a long name stays one clipped line (`plan_check::tests::a_plan_whose_speeds_and_feeds_cannot_be_checked_is_refused`). A problem's message is one line of at most 300 characters even when the material's name has a line break, a direction mark and 500 characters: the name is cut to 60 characters and the range numbers stay in the message (`plan_check::tests::a_cutting_data_message_is_one_clipped_line_whatever_the_materials_name`).
- The instructions give the two formulas, the bands, the report's field names, the machine-limit exceptions, the drill rule and the refusal with its "cutting data" stage (the 1% of slack is in the text, not asserted), and no longer tell the model to choose conservative speeds; their worked example (a three-flute Ø10 mm tool at 8000 rpm and 1000 mm/min: Vc 251 m/min, 0.042 mm per tooth) is what the check computes (`planner::tests::the_instructions_explain_the_speed_and_feed_check`), and they say the cutting-data problems come together with the generation, export, tool-axis and gouge ones (`planner::tests::the_instructions_say_which_problems_come_with_the_cutting_data_ones`).
- A scripted plan with a spindle speed of 2000 rpm is sent back to the model with the material's data in the first message and the "cutting data" problem naming setup 1, operation 1 in the correction, and the corrected plan is accepted (`planner::tests::a_speed_outside_the_materials_data_is_sent_back_and_the_repair_is_accepted`). The page beside Plan says the cutting data are sent, and the preview's note says speeds and feeds are checked against the material's cutting data, which are starting points (`smoke::tests::the_preview_and_the_program_page_say_what_their_checks_leave_out`).
- The planning fixtures run inside Aluminium 6061-T6's data: the good two-setup plan (Ø10 and Ø6 mm tools at 8000 rpm and 1000 mm/min, the Ø4 mm drill at 12000 rpm and 1200 mm/min) passes every stage with no cutting-data problem (`plan_check::tests::a_good_two_setup_plan_passes_every_stage`), and the Ø1 mm drill at 12000 rpm and 240 mm/min (0.0067 mm per tooth, inside the 0.003–0.010 mm a Ø1 takes) raises only the blind-hole refusal the test is about (`plan_check::tests::a_drill_sent_past_a_blind_holes_bottom_is_refused`).
- Not tested: that a ball end mill's nominal diameter is used (the code takes the tool's diameter whatever its type; the planner fixture's Ø6 ball end mill is in range), and the drill rule beyond the Ø4 mm case above. The ball end limit and the drill rule are documented in the README, not measured.

## The feed holds the force on each tooth (LIO-254)

- A Ø10 two-flute flat end mill at 600 mm/min and 6000 rpm in steel slots 4 mm deep, then goes back 2.5 mm into the slot's wall: holding the slot's force keeps the slot at 600 mm/min and feeds the side cut (F1 ÷ F2)^(1/(1 − mc)) faster, within the tenth it is rounded down by, the force there at the target or at most a tenth under it (`tooth_load::tests::a_light_side_cut_after_a_slot_is_fed_up_by_the_force_ratio`). With a 650 mm/min machine the side cut runs at 650; holding a tenth of the slot's force, which wants a twentieth of the feed, runs both at 150, a quarter of the programmed (`tooth_load::tests::a_held_feed_stays_between_the_machines_most_and_a_quarter_of_the_programmed`). A pass above the stock keeps 600 mm/min and its one move; going back along the slot it cut, over nothing, runs at 600 more than a cutter's reach from the slot's end (`tooth_load::tests::moves_in_the_air_keep_the_programmed_feed`). One lighter sample in thirty along a move is not fed up for its 0.5 mm; one twice as heavy slows its 0.5 mm piece only, rounded down to the tenth (`tooth_load::tests::a_short_fast_stretch_is_smoothed_away_and_a_short_slow_one_kept`). The split moves follow each other end to start, each timed at its feed, the plan's duration their sum, no more of them than the load has samples; allowed no more moves than the plan had, each move takes the slowest feed of its pieces (`tooth_load::tests::splitting_stays_within_the_move_count_and_retimes_the_plan`). The automatic target, worked out on a straight cut into a block, is within 3 % of the force measured along a 4 mm slot and within 5 % of a 1.25 mm side cut's (`tooth_load::tests::the_nominal_force_is_the_one_measured_along_that_cut`).
- The fixture job's Facing and Pocket: an operation saved without the choice gets the same moves and feeds as a new one, programmed; with the Pocket held, the Facing is unchanged, the Pocket's moves are split into at least three feeds, timed again, every one written as an F word in the exported program, seen by the workholding check's bites, and the load along the new moves comes with the simulation (`tooth_load::tests::a_held_pocket_exports_and_checks_the_feeds_it_was_timed_at`). In the app the choice is under the cutting feed, the force field and its automatic value appear when it is on, a negative force is an error at the field, and after Generate the SIMULATION panel says "Feed 287–3600 mm/min to hold … N per tooth" and the path is coloured at once (`smoke::tests::holding_the_force_per_tooth_is_chosen_with_the_feed_and_the_simulation_says_its_feeds`).
- A flute is measured in pieces a quarter of a millimetre tall however long it is (it was at most 40 pieces, 1.5 mm each on a 60 mm flute, so cuts 0.8 and 1 mm deep took the same force): with 60 mm flutes a 1 mm slot takes the analytic force within 3 % and a 0.75 mm one three quarters of it (`tooth_load::tests::a_shallow_cut_with_long_flutes_is_measured_at_its_depth`). The automatic target's depth is that of the levels a tooth cuts along, not of an Adaptive's travel half a millimetre over the level before (`tooth_load::tests::the_levels_depth_counts_the_levels_that_cut_not_the_travel_over_them`).
- Every strategy a CNC mill cuts with a milling cutter offers the choice (LIO-293): Facing, Adaptive, Pocket, Contour, Waterline, Parallel, Chamfer and Groove, none of them on a manual mill; Drill, Box, Turn, Scallop, Indexed, Rotary and 5-axis do not (`tooth_load::tests::every_cnc_milling_strategy_offers_the_held_feed`). A Parallel's, Chamfer's and Groove's automatic force is the median force along the path by length, where a tooth meets material: 10 mm at 100 N, 10 at 20 and 10 at 300 give 100; 10 mm at 100 N and 20 at 20 give 20; a path in the air none (`tooth_load::tests::the_paths_force_is_the_median_over_the_length_cut`). On the fixture job after its Facing, at 3 mm levels, a held Parallel and Waterline are split into more moves at several feeds; the Parallel holds the median force of its path as programmed, the Waterline at most a full slot's force 3 mm deep (`tooth_load::tests::a_held_waterline_holds_a_full_slots_force_and_a_parallel_its_paths`). The circlip plate's held groove feeds its second pass, the 0.1 mm band the first leaves, faster than its first (`groove::tests::a_held_groove_feeds_its_second_passes_thin_band_faster`). A held chamfer along a line on the fixture block plunges at no more than its F75, and its most force is within a factor of two of the force held (`chamfer::tests::a_held_chamfer_holds_its_paths_force_and_plunges_no_faster`). In the app a Waterline and a Parallel show the choice, and under the force field the Waterline's full slot and the Parallel's force along its path (`smoke::tests::a_waterline_and_a_parallel_offer_the_held_feed_with_their_automatic_force`).
- In an indexed setup (LIO-294): the Ø40 bar faced square, a side at each of A 90, 180, 270 and 0°, holds the force with its feed at several feeds, split into more moves, timed again, each piece keeping its move's A in the same order; each angle's moves are measured against the part as that angle poses it, the stock the angles before left carried on, so every angle's side takes at least half the force held (in the setup's frame alone the later angles would pass where the first had cut); the target is the Facing's nominal cut, no deeper than its stepdown, each angle's levels taken from the stock's top at that angle (`tooth_load::tests::a_held_indexed_facing_is_measured_at_each_angle_in_its_own_frame`). The same measure colours an indexed operation's path, and a rotary setup's round bar along X counts as material, not its box's corners.
- Measured, release build, 26 October 2026, with other builds on the machine (`smoke::tests::render_the_tooth_load` with `AXIS_STRATEGY=adaptive AXIS_STEPDOWN=1`, and `AXIS_HOLD=1` for the hold; the force measured again along the moves as held): the fixture's Adaptive clearing at 1 mm levels (26,189 feed moves, 59,698 samples) measures in 2.5–3.1 s and generates in about 6.3 s with the hold or without. Its automatic target is 134 N, its 1.5 mm optimal load 1 mm deep at 1200 mm/min. The most force over each 5 mm stretch of level cutting was 71–134 N (10th to 90th percentile) at the programmed feed and is 127–133 N held, 96 % of the stretches within 15 % of the target; the feeds run 287–3600 mm/min and the operation takes 38.2 min instead of 44.1. At 12 mm levels the stretches go from 167–617 N to 367–606 N held at 608 N (86 % within 15 %); a Pocket at 3 mm levels from 10–537 N to 22–522 N held at 522 N, its light stretches (finishing loops, a wall's skim) at three times the feed. The helical ramps stay under the target, as a ramp is never fed faster than its own feed, and so do cuts too light to reach it at three times the feed.

## Planner progress

- A run reports its stage starts and each check's result in order: with a first plan that fails, a corrected one that passes and a coverage round, Analyzing, Asking(1), Checking(1), the failed result with its problem line, Repairing(2), Checking(2), passed, Extending(3), Checking(3), passed (`planner::tests::a_run_reports_each_check_result`). A reply that is not a plan is reported as unreadable before the next stage, with the parse error as one line of at most 300 characters, also after a plan has passed (`planner::tests::an_unreadable_reply_is_reported`). A reply that completes with no text and no calls is reported as unreadable with "The reply was empty.", and the correction round sends the model "(empty reply)" as its own message, never an assistant message with empty content, which the API may refuse (`planner::tests::an_empty_reply_is_not_echoed_back_as_an_empty_assistant_message`). A check with eight problems lists the first five, each as "Setup 1, operation 1: …" with the message clipped to 300 characters, and counts the other three, while the failure keeps all eight; exactly five problems count none (`planner::tests::a_check_result_lists_five_problems_and_counts_the_rest`). A request that ends in an API error, and a check that fails to run, end the events at their own stage with no result after it, on the first request, after a failed check and after a plan that passed (`planner::tests::a_request_that_errors_or_a_check_that_fails_to_run_ends_the_events_without_a_result`).
- The client keeps the text of the reply as it streams, in place of the character count the timeline showed before the planner pictures; those tests are under Planner pictures (`openai::tests::respond_streaming_holds_the_streamed_text_and_each_call_starts_empty`, `openai::tests::the_stream_grows_while_the_reply_is_still_arriving`, `planner::tests::the_openai_backend_sends_the_conversation_and_remembers_plain_json`).
- The Plan task sends each event, each check's result included, with the run's epoch, and the run's timeline stays with its preview with every step ended (`smoke::tests::the_plan_task_sends_its_stages_and_its_result_with_the_runs_epoch`). An event of an older run changes nothing (`smoke::tests::progress_and_results_belong_to_the_current_run`).
- A stage ends the open step and starts its own; a check's result joins its request's step and ends it; a result for a request that has no step goes nowhere; the finished run keeps its timeline with every step ended, and a late event does not touch it; Discard, Cancel and an edit to the job clear it (`smoke::tests::the_timeline_follows_the_runs_events_and_stays_until_the_plan_goes`). A failed run keeps its timeline for the failure view, and Try again starts with an empty timeline and a new text (`smoke::tests::a_failed_run_keeps_its_timeline_and_the_next_run_starts_clean`). The App leaves the streamed text alone when a stage starts, a check's or a request's; a new request's row starts at "Thinking" because the client's attempt clears the text, shown by running the real backend against the loopback fake after those stages, and an older run's event leaves the text as it is (`smoke::tests::a_new_requests_row_starts_at_zero_because_the_client_resets_the_count`).
- While running, an ended step reads in the past tense with its seconds and the open one as it runs; a check with problems and an unreadable reply get the warning mark and the others a tick, and so does a step that ended without the result it should have: a check that did not finish ("did not finish"), a request that no check follows ("no reply") and an analysis that nothing follows ("did not finish"), under the row while the run goes and at the end of the row's line when it is over (`smoke::tests::a_step_that_ended_without_a_result_is_not_ticked_and_says_why`); the problems (up to five and "and N more") or the parse error are listed under their row, "did not pass" when a check gave none; only the open request shows what the reply has so far (see Planner pictures), and an open check shows nothing yet (`smoke::tests::running_rows_list_each_steps_problems_and_the_draft_under_it`). When the run is over, each step is one line ending in "passed", "N problem(s)" or "reply could not be read" (and, after the planner pictures, in what a refused request was sent again without and the number of looks), with nothing under it, and the failed and the preview pages build with that timeline (`smoke::tests::a_finished_timeline_is_one_line_a_step_with_a_short_summary`).
- Renderer screens inspected (5 October 2026; not a test). Screen 84, the running timeline: ticks for the finished steps, an amber "!" on the failed check with its two problem lines and "and 2 more" under it, the open correction row with "received 1840 characters" (replaced by the plan's own lines in the planner pictures work, see Planner pictures), the Cancel button; nothing is clipped or overlapping. The open row repeats the sentence of the heading above it, and the "!" is narrower than the tick, so the text of the warning row starts a few pixels to the left of the others (cosmetic). Screen 87, the finished timeline above a plan preview: "How the plan was made" with six one-line rows (the failed check ends "2 problems", the second check "passed"), the preview's check lines and the new Not checked note below it; nothing is clipped or overlapping. Before this section was written, screens 86 and 87 kept the scroll position of screen 82 and showed the end of their pages (the finished timeline was above the visible part); the smoke run now scrolls the planner's result back to the top after screen 82, and 87 shows the timeline. Screen 86, the Materials page: the library list with the starters and their Vc ranges, and the editor with name, group, hardness, notes and cutting data; before the scroll reset it showed the five chip load bands, "Configuration checks passed" and the Save and Delete buttons, also without clipping. Its fields run under the editor's scroll bar and the group drop-down's arrow is half under it, exactly as on the Tools page (screen 07), and the list's selected row runs under its own scroll bar. Screen 10, the 1120 px minimum window: the document bar's Materials button fits between Tools and AI planner with nothing clipped; "1 setup" at the top of the browser column is partly under the column's scroll bar, as in earlier smoke runs. Screens 85 and 87, the planner page's left panel: the "Sent to OpenAI" sentence now reads "… the project material (its name, group, hardness, notes and cutting data), stock size, and …" and wraps inside the panel; the panel scrolls, and, as before the change, the end of the sentence (what is not sent) is below the visible part until it is scrolled.
- The running page keeps the stage, the seconds since the run started, the waiting note and Cancel before the timeline's own scrollable, in the widget tree laid out on the software renderer without a window (`ui::probe`): the overview picture and every timeline row come after the scrollable, and Cancel keeps its place with a timeline of 3 rows or of 40 (`smoke::tests::the_running_page_has_its_stage_time_and_cancel_before_the_scrolling_timeline`).
- A finished run puts its outcome and actions before the timeline: a failure shows "No plan this time", Try again and Dismiss, then "What the planner tried"; a preview shows "Plan ready to review" and Apply plan, then "How the plan was made" and its rows in a scrollable of their own; Apply plan and Discard are still on the page twice, at the top and the bottom (`smoke::tests::a_finished_page_has_the_outcome_and_its_actions_before_the_timeline`).
- Clicking at every eighth point of the running page sends a toggle from the overview and from a look's thumbnail; the toggle opens the picture in a scrollable that holds it at its natural size (a 300 × 200 picture scrolls a 300 × 200 content), under Cancel and with a Close button that sends the same toggle; another picture replaces it, the same one again closes it, a picture the timeline no longer has shows nothing, and a new run starts with none open (`smoke::tests::a_thumbnail_click_opens_the_picture_at_full_size_and_a_second_click_closes_it`).
- A summary, workholding note, operation reason and warning of more than their limit show clipped with "Show full text" (four links); each opens on its own to its last word with one "Show less" and closes again; a click on the page sends the toggle; a short text has no link, and Discard forgets what was open (`smoke::tests::show_full_text_opens_a_clipped_note_reason_and_summary`).
- Not tested: how the pinned part and the scrolling timeline share a short window on a real display (the Linux renderer's screenshots are unreliable here); the layout is reasoned from Iced 0.14's rules (the timeline's scrollable fills what the pinned part and the enlarged panel leave, and the enlarged panel is a fixed 420 px high).

## Planner pictures

Each statement names the test behind it (in the module it is in; `smoke::tests::` are the application tests in `src/smoke.rs`), unless it is marked as inspected or not a test. No test contacts OpenAI: requests go to the loopback stand-in or to a scripted model. Whether the user's ChatGPT plan route accepts tools and images for their model is not established here; a real run is needed, and the fallbacks below cover a refusal.
