- **Drawing to pixels.** Splitting `Viewport::pixels` into `render` leaves the pixels of the 3D view unchanged: a 400 × 300 view of the post fixture's job gives the same 600 × 450 frame as before the split, pinned by a digest (`viewport::tests::the_viewport_draws_the_same_pixels_after_the_split`; pinned again when the mirrored projection was fixed, #5). `render` tints marked surfaces with the selection tint, keeps the tint under the stock's box, draws the same pixels as without marking, and returns a depth buffer that is finite exactly where a pixel was drawn, for the part and for the simulated stock and tool (`viewport::tests::render_tints_marked_surfaces_and_returns_the_depths`).
- **The font and the canvas.** Every character the tags and captions use has a glyph (digits, capitals, space, + - . , : / ( ) Ø × % … ·); lower case is drawn as capitals and any other character as "?" (`look::canvas::tests::every_character_a_tag_or_caption_uses_has_a_glyph`, `look::canvas::tests::text_draws_the_glyph_pixels`). A tag is drawn centred on its point with its text inside its box, and refused near the picture's edge (`look::canvas::tests::a_tag_is_drawn_centred_and_refused_at_the_edge`). A hairline has no gaps, horizontal or at a slant (`look::canvas::tests::a_hairline_is_drawn_without_gaps`). Compositing fills the background and writes a PNG (`look::canvas::tests::composite_fills_the_background`).
- **The overview.** It is a 768 × 768 PNG; its description starts "+Z up, isometric front-left, whole part", says "stock shown" and lists the top plane's surface ID among the tags (`look::tests::the_overview_is_a_labelled_768_pixel_png_of_setup_1`). The pocket's floor is tagged where it can be seen and is not tagged in a front view, where the pocket's wall hides it (`look::tests::the_pocket_floor_is_tagged_where_it_can_be_seen`). A picture is framed to fill it: the 3D view's fit, scaled and moved so that the projected corners of each framed box (the part, the stock, each jaw or clamp and, for an operation, the simulated stock and the reach of its moves; or the surfaces zoomed on) fill 90% of the width or the height above the caption strip; the 3D view keeps its own fit, which its digest test pins. Without its tags, the overview of the block, and of the block with 36 mm of stock and a clamp on each side in X, spans at least 80% of the picture's width or height, with a margin on every side (`look::tests::the_overview_fills_the_picture`). A tag goes to its plane's next point rather than cover the triad's corner, reach past any edge of the picture, overlap a tag already drawn or stand where something nearer hides its point (`look::tests::a_tag_goes_to_its_next_point_rather_than_cover_the_triad_or_leave_the_picture`), and with the part drawn small around the triad no tag is drawn over it (`look::tests::no_tag_covers_the_triad`). The pictures have the 3D view's own frame, right-handed and no longer flipped since #5: in a top view +X is to the right and +Y up, and from the front +X is to the right (`look::tests::a_top_view_keeps_x_right_and_y_up`; the 3D view's frame is pinned under **The 3D view drawn right-handed**). A scene can move between threads (`look::tests::a_scene_can_move_between_threads`).
- **The tool.** Its schema requires every argument the views take and offers the nine angles (`look::tests::the_tool_requires_every_argument_the_views_take`). Highlighting a surface adds the selection tint to the picture, and focusing on it makes it fill more of the picture (the pocket floor, most of the part's top, gets more than 1.5 times the pixels of the whole-part view, which already fills the picture) and says "zoomed on" in the description (`look::tests::highlighting_and_focus_change_the_picture`). A view of an operation with the stock shown draws the stock left after it and not the part's faces, so highlighted surfaces cannot be tinted there; the description says "highlight of surfaces … not drawn" and names them, and says nothing of the kind with the stock hidden or without highlights (`look::tests::highlights_are_reported_as_not_drawn_when_the_stock_after_an_operation_is_shown`). Arguments that are not JSON, an unknown angle or orientation, a surface the part does not have, more than 200 surface IDs, an operation before any proposal has been built and an extra argument each give one sentence of at most 300 characters and no picture (`look::tests::bad_arguments_get_one_sentence_errors`). An operation of the latest proposal is drawn with every pass of its toolpaths (at least 2,000 pixels of them where the viewport draws about 600) and described "after setup 1, operation 1"; an operation that does not exist is refused in a sentence (`look::tests::an_operation_of_the_latest_proposal_is_drawn_with_its_toolpaths`). The model's purpose is cleaned and cut to 120 characters (`look::tests::the_purpose_is_cleaned_and_clipped`). The plan check keeps the job it built for a plan that fails, so an operation of a failing proposal can be drawn, and `check` itself still returns none (`plan_check::tests::evaluate_keeps_the_job_of_a_failing_plan`).
- **Requests with images and calls.** A message with an image is sent as `input_text` and `input_image` parts (a base64 PNG data URL, detail `high`), a lone text as a plain string, a call and its output as `function_call` and `function_call_output` items without an item id, the tool as a strict function with `tool_choice` `auto` or `none`, and a request without a tool has neither key (`openai::tests::images_tools_and_calls_are_sent_as_items`). A message's text is its text parts joined by line breaks (`openai::tests::a_messages_text_is_its_text_parts_joined_by_newlines`). Function calls are read from the stream's `response.output_item.done` events, or from `response.completed` when nothing was streamed (`openai::tests::function_calls_are_read_from_the_stream_or_the_final_event`). An HTTP 400 without a known code is `ApiError::BadRequest` (`openai::tests::an_http_400_without_a_known_code_is_a_bad_request`), and its message is as clean and bounded as the others (`openai::tests::every_message_from_openai_is_one_clean_bounded_line`).
- **The streamed text.** The client keeps the text of the reply being streamed in a shared string that each attempt clears first, in the structured and the plain route, and `respond` is unchanged (`openai::tests::respond_streaming_holds_the_streamed_text_and_each_call_starts_empty`); the text grows while the reply is still streaming, read from outside before the reply is complete (`openai::tests::the_stream_grows_while_the_reply_is_still_arriving`). The planner's backend shares that string: after a structured attempt that was refused and streamed nothing it holds the plain retry's reply, a later request starts it again, and the tool is offered only when asked for, with its choice (`planner::tests::the_openai_backend_sends_the_conversation_and_remembers_plain_json`).
- **The tool loop.** The first request offers the tool, carries the PICTURES instructions and the overview as an image after the part report; calls are answered with their outputs and one user message holding that round's pictures (none when every call failed), and the rounds are not planner requests (`planner::tests::the_model_gets_an_overview_and_the_pictures_it_asks_for`). Six pictures are allowed in a request: of eight calls, two get "No more pictures for this request; reply with the proposal.", later sends of that request still offer the tools with `tool_choice` `auto` (only `view_model` is refused; see Leftover stock and planner tools), the overview and six pictures go as images (seven, within the eight kept), and the next planner request may look again (`planner::tests::pictures_are_limited_per_request_and_the_next_request_may_look_again`). A view that cannot be drawn counts: six of them use up a request (`planner::tests::pictures_that_cannot_be_drawn_count_against_the_limits`). After the run's twentieth picture a call of `view_model` gets "No more pictures in this run; reply with the proposal." and is not counted, while another tool called in the same round is answered (`planner::tests::after_the_runs_last_picture_view_model_is_refused_and_the_other_tools_stay_open`; it was `tool_choice` `none` for every tool before the planner tools). Of eighteen calls in one reply the first sixteen are run and the last two are kept, not dropped: each gets the sentence "Only the first 16 calls of a reply are run; this one was not. Call it again in your next reply." as its output, so the model sees an answer for every call it made (`openai::tests::calls_past_the_limit_are_all_kept_for_an_answer` for the stream, and a reply of more than 64 calls fails with "The reply made more than 64 function calls." instead of being answered call by call, the calls' ids and names counting toward the 4 MiB reply limit (`openai::tests::a_reply_with_too_many_calls_fails_instead_of_being_answered`, `openai::tests::the_ids_and_names_of_calls_count_toward_the_reply_limit`, issue #177), `planner::tests::calls_past_the_limit_each_get_a_sentence_and_are_not_run` for the loop), and the TOOLS instructions state the limit of 16 calls per reply, with pictures and without (`planner_tools::tests::the_instructions_state_the_limit_of_calls_per_reply`). A model that goes on calling tools through sixteen rounds fails the request with "kept calling tools" (`planner::tests::a_model_that_never_stops_looking_fails_the_request`; it was eight rounds, "kept asking for pictures", before the planner tools). Of ten images in three messages only the last eight are sent again and the first two become "(Earlier picture, not resent: …)"; with pictures off every image becomes "(Picture not sent: …)"; with the tool off a call and its output become plain text (`planner::tests::only_the_last_eight_images_are_resent_and_the_rest_become_text`). The plan task hands each checked proposal's job to the pictures, passed or not, for the operation views; that line has no test of its own (the loop's tests build their scene without a job).
- **Refusals.** A request refused with a 400 and then with Unsupported is sent again without its tools (since the planner tools, every tool, `view_model` among them, not `view_model` alone) and then without pictures, each drop reported once for request 1 and in that order, and the run remembers: the next request has no tool choice and no PICTURES section (`planner::tests::a_refused_tool_then_refused_pictures_are_dropped_in_order`). After the tools are refused, later requests are sent without them but their instructions still explain the overview, without `view_model` or the limits, and the overview goes with its description and its image; no drop of pictures is reported (`planner::tests::once_the_tool_is_refused_the_instructions_no_longer_offer_it`). After the pictures are refused too, the conversation has no image part and no call or output item: calls and outputs are text, pictures their descriptions (`planner::tests::refused_pictures_are_dropped_from_the_whole_conversation`). The third fallback, plain JSON without structured output, is the one that existed before pictures (`planner::tests::the_openai_backend_sends_the_conversation_and_remembers_plain_json`).
- **The instructions.** The PICTURES section stands before RULES and only with pictures; without it the text is the one pictures-off runs get. The tool, its fields and its limits (6 per request, 20 in the run) are explained only while the tool is offered, and the overview only when the conversation carries it (a run whose overview could not be drawn is not promised one); the stock's and the workholding's colours, the triad's and "the report's numbers are right" always are (`planner::tests::the_pictures_section_comes_before_the_rules_only_with_pictures`). A run that drew the overview promises it in its first request (`planner::tests::the_model_gets_an_overview_and_the_pictures_it_asks_for`). With pictures off a run sends the system text and the part report only, with no `view_model` and no PICTURES section; the other tools are offered since the planner tools (`planner::tests::without_pictures_the_tools_are_still_offered_and_nothing_else_changes`; before them, no tool at all).
- **Reading a reply that is still streaming.** A real proposal cut at every character boundary never loses what has been read: the summary only grows and is always a start of the real one, setups and operations never disappear, a tool, speed or feed once shown never changes, and the whole reply reads as the whole plan (`proposal::tests::a_reply_cut_anywhere_reads_as_far_as_it_has_come`). Text before the JSON and a code fence are skipped; text with no JSON gives an empty draft (`proposal::tests::text_before_the_json_and_a_fence_are_skipped`). Only objects count as setups and operations, also against 5,000 stray elements (`proposal::tests::only_objects_are_setups_and_operations`). `partial` is bounded and does not panic on 100,000 open brackets, a cut escape, a cut `\u` escape or a 4 MiB summary (read up to its 256 KiB limit, not whole) (`proposal::tests::partial_is_bounded_and_never_panics`). A 500-number array, cut anywhere inside it, keeps the summary, the setup and both operations read before it (`proposal::tests::a_long_array_of_numbers_keeps_everything_before_it`). A number shows at its comma or at white space after it, one still being written hides only itself, and spaces inside a string are kept (`proposal::tests::a_finished_number_shows_at_its_comma_and_one_being_written_hides_only_itself`).
- **The timeline.** Only the open request shows what its reply has so far, "Thinking: the model has not started writing yet." until the reply has JSON and then the plan's lines, and an open check shows nothing yet (`smoke::tests::running_rows_list_each_steps_problems_and_the_draft_under_it`). A draft reads as "Plan: …" (cleaned, one line) and one line per setup with its operations, the one being written marked "writing: …" with its tool, rpm and feed so far (`smoke::tests::the_draft_reads_as_plan_and_setup_lines`); a summary whose quotes have arrived with no word is still "Thinking", and a setup shows without an empty plan line (`smoke::tests::a_summary_not_yet_written_is_still_thinking`); six setups are listed, then "and N more setups" ("and 1 more setup") (`smoke::tests::a_draft_shows_six_setups_and_counts_the_rest`). A look is one line, "Looked: description — "purpose"", or without the purpose, and a look with no picture reads as its bare error sentence (`smoke::tests::a_look_reads_as_one_line`). While the run goes, the open request lists its looks (the picture ones with a thumbnail, the error ones without) and a refusal as a warning line that says what the request is sent again without and no cause, since any HTTP 400 counts ("OpenAI refused the request; sending it again without pictures.", "… without its tools."; the second said "… without the picture tool." before the planner tools), an event for a request that is not on the timeline joins nothing, a refusal alone marks the finished step with the warning mark, the overview is kept for the page and a new run starts without it (`smoke::tests::the_running_request_shows_the_draft_looks_and_drops`). A finished run is one line a step: the request's line says what it was sent again without and then, last, the pictures looked at ("· sent again without tools · 1 look", "· 2 looks"; error lines are not counted), nothing is listed under it, and both finished views build (`smoke::tests::a_finished_line_says_what_was_refused_and_how_many_pictures_were_seen`).
- **The setting and the disclosure.** Pictures are on unless turned off, the choice is saved, and settings saved before the field read as on (`settings::tests::pictures_are_on_unless_turned_off_and_the_choice_is_saved`). The checkbox's choice is saved to the settings file, survives reopening the app, and the planner page builds with it on and off (`smoke::tests::the_pictures_choice_is_saved_and_shown_on_the_planner_page`). The "Sent to OpenAI" text names the pictures the app draws only while they are on, always says the CAD file is not sent, and is the base text exactly when they are off (`smoke::tests::the_disclosure_names_pictures_only_when_they_are_on`). The base text also says, with or without pictures, that the sizes of every vise and clamp in the workholding library are sent, sizes only and never their names or notes, since the planner sends them (same test; added on 9 October 2026 with the planner's library), and that the app's check reports and tool answers are sent while the model works, where it said "on correction rounds" (same test; corrected in the final review's fix wave: `check_plan` and the other tools send their answers whenever the model calls them). While a run goes the box is disabled and its message changes nothing, the setting and the file stay as they were, and once the run is over the box works again; its hover help says what is sent, that the CAD file is never sent and that a run keeps the choice it started with (`smoke::tests::the_pictures_choice_is_fixed_while_a_run_goes`). `Plan` builds its backend with `App::plan_backend`, which carries the choice into the run: against the loopback stand-in, a whole run started with the box unticked sends no `input_image` part, no `view_model` and no PICTURES section but the five other tools in every request, one started with it ticked sends the overview and `view_model` with the five in every request, and unticking or ticking the box after the backend is built does not reach the run (`smoke::tests::a_run_sends_pictures_and_the_tool_only_as_the_box_was_when_it_started`). That `Plan` itself calls `plan_backend` and hands the backend to `run_plan` is read in the code, not tested: the `Plan` update returns an Iced task that no test runs (the task body, `run_plan`, is run directly with a scripted backend in `smoke::tests::the_plan_task_sends_its_stages_and_its_result_with_the_runs_epoch`).
- **Renderer screens inspected** (8 October 2026, release build with the final review's fixes; not a test). Screen 84, the running timeline, has no picture and is as before: the open correction row lists "Plan: Face the top, rough the pocket with T3, then turn the part over." and "Setup 1 · +Z up · writing: Face "Face top"" where it said "received 1840 characters"; the failed check keeps its two problem lines and "and 2 more"; nothing is clipped or overlapping, and the open row still repeats the sentence of the heading above it (cosmetic, as before). Screen 87, the finished timeline: the first request's line ends "· 2 looks" and no look is listed under it; the other rows are as before ("2 problems", "passed"); nothing is clipped. Screen 91, the first request going (`planner = Running(Asking(1))`; step 114, captured twice because Iced draws a picture of 2 MiB or more only from its second frame, so the second file replaces the first, scrolled 90 px so the overview sits at the top of the visible part): the overview now fills its picture: the stock, the part and the setup's four clamps (see-through grey boxes, one at the middle of each side of the stock, all four in view) span about nine tenths of its width and four fifths of its height above the caption, where the run before the fix left them about two fifths of the width; seven tags, none over another or over the triad (335,3107; 2570; 2562,2563,2566…; Ø6.0; Ø6.6 three times), where that run drew five; the caption with a 20 mm scale bar, and the description listing the same seven tags; the finished "Analyzed the part · 2 s"; the open row "Asking gpt-6.1-sol for a plan (1 of 4)… · 40 s" with the plan's two lines; a look with its 120 px thumbnail (a top view, the stock and clamps across its width) and its "Looked: … — "Which face is the pocket floor"" line; "Surface 999999 is not a surface of this part." without a thumbnail; and the amber "OpenAI refused the request; sending it again without the picture tool." (the wording of that run; it now reads "… without its tools.", see the inspection under Leftover stock and planner tools). Nothing is clipped or overlapping. The see-through clamps lie over the stock's edges and parts of the part, and one hole's tag sits where the hole is seen through a clamp; the thumbnail's caption and tags are too small to read at 120 px; at that scroll offset the line "This can take a few minutes. Nothing changes until you apply a plan." is cut through by the bottom of the visible part, and Cancel is below it. The fixture composes a run in which the tool was refused after the two looks. A picture here decodes to 2.25 MiB (768 × 768 × 4 bytes), over the 2 MiB from which Iced uploads images after the first frame (see `viewport.rs`); the first capture had their place empty, which is why the screen is captured twice. The overview's and the looks' pixels are drawn by `look` and checked above; what the model makes of them is not checked here.

## Workholding (vise and clamps)

Each statement is backed by the unit test it names, unless it is marked as inspected or as a limit of the model.

- **Library.** The two starters are valid entries ("100 mm machine vise": jaw width 100, jaw height 30, jaw thickness 20, maximum opening 120, grip 8, clamping force 5000 N; "Toe clamp 60 mm": length 60, width 20, height 25, bite 12, clamping force 2000 N), both noted "Measure yours"; a clamp whose bite is not shorter than its length and a negative size are refused (`holding::tests::starters_are_valid`). A name is bounded to 60 characters and notes to 500, like a material's (`holding::tests::a_holders_name_and_notes_are_bounded_like_a_materials`), and a name is taken whatever its capitals and the spaces at its ends (`model::tests::a_holder_name_is_taken_regardless_of_case_and_spaces`). The starters are added only to an empty library (`model::tests::adopting_holders_adds_the_starters_only_to_an_empty_library`); a project saved before the library existed gets them on load without a migration note, and a library with entries is loaded as it is (`storage::tests::a_project_without_a_workholding_library_gets_the_starters_silently`, `storage::tests::a_workholding_library_with_entries_is_loaded_as_it_is`). A project with a duplicate or empty entry id is refused (`model::tests::a_project_with_duplicate_or_empty_holder_ids_is_refused`).
- **The Workholding page.** Entries are created, edited, saved, refused when invalid or when the name is taken, and deleted like materials (`smoke::tests::holders_are_created_edited_saved_refused_and_deleted_like_materials`); the page starts with the starters and keeps a user's edits (`smoke::tests::the_workholding_library_starts_with_the_starters_and_keeps_a_users_edits`); an entry that a setup uses cannot be deleted (`smoke::tests::a_holder_a_setup_uses_cannot_be_deleted`); a new project carries the library (`smoke::tests::new_project_carries_the_workholding_library`); Explore example merges the user's entries by name without duplicates (`smoke::tests::explore_merges_workholding_without_duplicate_names`); Undo applied plan brings back an entry deleted after the plan was applied (`smoke::tests::undo_brings_back_a_workholding_entry_deleted_after_apply`), and Apply brings back an entry that has left the library since the preview, with its own identifier, once, naming it in the status, and with "(restored)" after its name when another entry has taken it; an entry that is there is not added again (`smoke::tests::apply_brings_back_a_workholding_entry_deleted_after_the_preview`). Through the Workholding page a deletion discards the preview first, so the test removes the entry from the library directly: it covers an entry lost any other way.
- **The setup's keys.** A setup without them is None and has no issues (`model::tests::a_setup_without_holding_keys_is_none_and_has_no_issues`). A vise reads its axis, offset, grip and gap; clamps read one to eight `side:offset` parts; an unknown side, no clamp, nine clamps and a negative grip are errors (`holding::tests::layouts_are_read_from_the_setup`). Numbers are trimmed, the vise axis must be X, Y or empty, and a bad gap is ignored while the setup is None (`holding::tests::layouts_trim_numbers_check_the_axis_and_ignore_the_gap_without_workholding`); clamp text is read whatever the setup's other keys say (`holding::tests::typed_clamp_positions_are_read_whatever_the_setups_other_keys_say`); the gap is 2 mm unless the setup says otherwise (`holding::tests::the_gap_is_two_unless_the_setup_says_otherwise`). Choosing Vise or Clamps points the setup at an entry of its kind, starts a vise at the entry's grip and the gap at 2 mm, and replaces a deleted entry (`model::tests::settling_a_setups_holding_picks_an_entry_of_its_kind_a_grip_and_a_gap`, `smoke::tests::choosing_a_vise_writes_the_setup_keys_and_the_layout_follows`); picking what the controls already show changes nothing (`smoke::tests::picking_the_chosen_holder_again_changes_nothing`); clamp positions are saved only when every clamp reads, and what was typed stays in the field with its error (`smoke::tests::clamp_positions_are_written_only_when_they_read_correctly`, `smoke::tests::clamp_positions_need_a_setup`, `smoke::tests::a_replaced_project_forgets_the_typed_clamp_text`).
- **What is wrong with it.** The setup's issues come in this order: the library entry (none chosen, gone), the layout, the entry not fitting the layout, then, from the stock the setup starts from, a vise opening less than the stock is wide across the jaws (`model::tests::holding_issues_name_the_layout_the_entry_and_the_opening`), jaws that do not overlap the stock along their length (an offset of half the stock's length plus half the jaw width or more) and a clamp whose offset is beyond half the length of its side, each said in millimetres, a clamp exactly at the end of its side being allowed (`model::tests::an_offset_must_leave_the_jaws_on_the_stock_and_each_clamp_on_its_side`), and last a later setup that is not square to setup 1 (turned from it by other than a multiple of 90 degrees), which cannot model a vise or clamps because a vise turns with the stock and a box does not: "This setup is not square to setup 1, so its vise or clamps cannot be modelled. Choose None, or square the setup.", worked out from the rotations alone, so with or without the part; quarter turns, a half turn and none (0, 90, 180, 270, -90 and 360 about Z) are fine, 30 about Z and a 45 degree tilt are refused, a setup is compared with setup 1 and not with the world, None says nothing, and the stock-based checks are not made for such a setup (`model::tests::a_later_setup_not_square_to_setup_1_cannot_model_its_workholding`); a library without an entry of the kind says to add one on the Workholding page, ahead of any number error (`model::tests::a_missing_entry_comes_first_and_says_where_to_add_one`, `smoke::tests::a_setup_with_no_entry_of_its_kind_says_so_before_any_number_error`). The setup editor lists them (`smoke::tests::the_setup_editor_shows_what_is_wrong_with_the_workholding`; for a setup turned 30 degrees, and fine at 90 and 180, with no 3D boxes drawn for it: `smoke::tests::the_setup_editor_says_a_later_setup_not_square_to_setup_1_cannot_model_its_workholding`) and the page and the editor build in every state (`smoke::tests::the_workholding_page_and_the_setup_editor_build_in_each_state`). The opening is checked against the stock the setup starts from, which for a later setup is setup 1's carried into its frame as the simulation hands it over (`model::tests::a_later_setups_stock_is_the_first_setups_carried_into_its_frame`, `model::tests::the_opening_check_of_a_later_setup_uses_the_stock_it_really_starts_from`; that is the box the editor uses until the stock the earlier setups left is worked out, see Leftover stock and planner tools), and it says nothing when that stock cannot be told (`model::tests::the_opening_check_is_skipped_silently_when_the_stock_cannot_be_resolved`). The boxes are refused with the first of those lines (`model::tests::holding_boxes_refuse_with_the_first_line_the_editor_shows`, `holding::tests::the_refusal_is_what_boxes_refuses`, `holding::tests::boxes_refuse_a_holder_of_the_wrong_kind_or_with_issues`); a vise too narrow for the stock says how wide the stock is across the jaws and how far the vise opens, in millimetres (`holding::tests::a_vise_too_narrow_for_the_stock_says_so_in_millimetres`).
- **The stock on every redraw.** The editor's lines, the 3D boxes, the operation notes, the playback reason and the NC page's note each ask for a later setup's stock several times per redraw, and each asks for the part's bounds turned by setup 1's angles and by the later setup's. The part keeps the bounds of its last eight angle sets, so the two alternate without a pass over the mesh each time; a cached answer is what a fresh mesh computes, over more angle sets than are kept, and a mesh keeps its own (`mesh::tests::rotated_bounds_keep_several_angle_sets_and_stay_correct`, `mesh::tests::a_mesh_keeps_its_own_rotated_bounds`). The saving itself was not timed (by construction, not measured). The stock is told for clamps too, since their offsets are checked against it.
- **Where the boxes are.** On a 100 × 60 × 40 mm stock centred on the origin, a vise on the Y sides at offset 5 and grip 8 has its -Y jaw at Y -50 to -30 and X -45 to 55, from far below the stock bottom (at least 100 mm) up to Z 8, and a toe clamp on -X at offset 10 spans X -98 to -38 (12 mm onto the stock, 48 mm out), Y 0 to 20 and Z 40 to 65 (`holding::tests::vise_jaws_and_clamps_sit_where_the_spec_says`); on a stock away from the origin both vise axes and a clamp on each side follow it (`holding::tests::boxes_follow_a_stock_away_from_the_origin`). The 3D view draws them from the stock's bottom up, without the gap (`holding::tests::the_view_draws_jaws_down_to_the_stock_bottom_only`), only when the stock is shown and the setup can be modelled, round the preview's stock when one is shown (`smoke::tests::the_3d_view_gets_the_boxes_only_when_the_stock_is_shown`), as solid neutral grey blocks, changing only the jaw's own pixels (`viewport::tests::workholding_is_drawn_as_solid_grey_blocks`); a block in front of the part hides it and takes its place in the depth buffer, so a planner tag behind it is left out, and a block behind the part is hidden by it (`viewport::tests::a_block_in_front_of_the_part_hides_it_and_one_behind_is_hidden`); since the planner pictures the viewport draws whatever boxes it is given and the 3D view passes none while the stock is hidden (the smoke test above), so the pictures can show the vise without the stock; the camera fit contains them, those round the setup's own stock whether they are drawn or not, so hiding the stock does not move the view (`viewport::tests::the_camera_fit_contains_the_workholding_drawn_or_not`; 22 October 2026, see **The view stays put**).
- **The check.** A move crossing a box hits although both its ends are clear (`holding::tests::a_move_crossing_a_box_hits_even_when_both_ends_are_clear`); a 3 000 mm move through a 1 mm clamp is not missed (`holding::tests::a_long_move_through_a_thin_clamp_is_not_missed`); a Ø6 mm tool clipping a jaw's corner hits at a closest approach of 2.98 mm and does not at 3.08 mm, with no gap (`holding::tests::a_diagonal_clip_of_a_jaw_corner_hits_only_within_reach`); slopes and plunges are exact (`holding::tests::slopes_and_plunges_are_checked_exactly`). A Ø6 mm tool passing 4 mm from a clamp is clear with the 2 mm gap and hits with 5 mm; a rapid high above is clear; the holder starts at the stickout, so a short stickout lets a Ø32 mm holder hit where the tool would not; there is one hit per box and part, not per sample (`holding::tests::the_check_finds_tool_and_holder_hits_and_respects_the_gap`). The margin the suggestion ranks by is positive exactly when there is no hit, for every combination of seven toolpaths, a clamp and the jaws, and gaps of 0, 2 and 5 mm (`holding::tests::a_positive_margin_is_exactly_no_hit`). That the cutter is checked at its cutting radius from the tip up to the stickout and the holder above it at the holder radius is what these tests exercise; a shank wider than the cutter or a reduced neck is not modelled, because the tool has no shank diameter (a limit of the model, not tested). The machine profile's path blending tolerance (G64 P) is added to the gap in export's check, the UC100 run's check and the operation preview, since the controller may leave the programmed path by that much at a corner; the lines still name the setup's gap (`post_check::tests::the_profiles_blend_is_added_to_the_gap`, `post::tests::the_profiles_blend_is_added_to_the_gap_at_export_and_on_a_uc100_run`; see **LinuxCNC post**).
- **The suggested vise.** With no toolpaths the jaws go across Y, where a 100 × 60 mm stock is narrower; contours near ±X leave them there, contours near ±Y send them to the X sides, and every operation counts, not only the first (`holding::tests::the_vise_goes_on_the_sides_the_toolpaths_leave_alone`). A floor at Z 7.5 near all four sides lowers the grip to 5 mm; a floor at Z 5 is reached even at 3 mm, so the grip stays 3 and the axis whose jaws are farther from the moves (X, 4 mm against 3) is kept; the reduction ends at 3 mm, not 3.5 mm for a grip of 8.5 (`holding::tests::a_deep_pocket_on_every_side_lowers_the_grip_and_keeps_the_clearer_axis`); a grip of 100 000 mm comes down to the 5 mm that clears (`holding::tests::a_tall_grip_is_reduced_to_the_largest_that_clears`); the grip starts at the entry's (12 stays 12, 2 stays 2, 4 goes to 3 and no lower) (`holding::tests::the_vise_grip_starts_at_the_holders_and_is_never_raised`); when only one axis fits the opening it is the one used, even where the moves hit it (`holding::tests::the_vise_axis_must_fit_its_opening_when_one_axis_does`); the fallback counts the holder as well as the cutter (`holding::tests::the_vise_fallback_counts_the_holder_too`); grips are kept to a hundredth of a millimetre, as the setup stores them (`holding::tests::the_grip_is_a_hundredth_of_a_millimetre_the_setup_can_store`).
- **The suggested clamps.** With no moves there is one clamp at the middle of each side, and a rapid high above changes nothing (`holding::tests::clamps_are_four_one_per_side_and_the_middles_with_no_moves_nearby`). A clamp goes only where its bite keeps the stock for the whole setup: a Ø20 facing over the whole top leaves no spot, and the suggestion is no clamps and "Toe clamps cannot hold this setup: on every side the 12 mm bite loses its stock or comes within 2 mm of a toolpath. Leave about 35 mm of stock beside the part on two opposite sides and face only its outline, or use the vise." (12 + 2 + 20 = 34); a Ø10 facing inside the bites leaves the middles, which the export's checks then find nothing wrong with; and a rim the earlier setups cut under the X clamps' bites leaves only -Y and +Y (`holding::tests::a_clamp_goes_only_where_its_bite_keeps_the_stock_for_the_whole_setup`). Without two opposite sides no clamps are placed, and the sides without a spot are named ("on +X and +Y"); with the part known, the stock to leave counts how far a tool cuts past the part's outline at the clamps' height, the holder's 16 mm radius where it comes down to them (a contour 5 mm outside the part: 35 mm needed, so about 40 mm), and without it, or for a facing, the cutter's diameter (about 25 mm); facing only the outline is advised for a facing that cuts 20 mm past it, or with the part unknown, and not for one kept to 5 mm past it nor for a contour; a stock narrower than two bites, where opposite clamps would overlap, says so (`holding::tests::without_two_opposite_sides_no_clamps_are_placed_and_the_sides_are_named`). The stock advised is the next 5 mm above what is needed, since a clamp holds only where it is strictly clear: Ø6, Ø10, Ø11 and Ø16 facings (20, 24, 25 and 30 mm needed) are advised 25, 25, 30 and 35 mm (`holding::tests::the_stock_to_leave_is_more_than_the_bite_the_gap_and_the_reach`; found in review: a requirement that was a multiple of 5 was advised as exactly the failing value). In a later setup, stock the earlier setups cut free from the part holds nothing: with setup 1's contour round the part down past the bottom, the frame left keeps material under the X bites and the Y clamps at the ends of their sides, which a suggestion blind to the part would place and the export refuses, every one, as cut free; knowing the part there are none, and the reason says to leave material joining them; with a skin left under the contour they hold again (`holding::tests::a_spot_whose_stock_the_earlier_setups_cut_free_from_the_part_holds_nothing`; found in review). Before any toolpath a bite must stay beside the part's outline when the part is known: 10 mm of stock beside it gives no clamps and "…the 12 mm bite reaches over the part. Leave about 15 mm of stock beside it on two opposite sides, and more for the cutters' reach, or use the vise."; 20 and 15 mm give the middles; 20 on X and 10 on Y the X middles and the Y clamps past the part's ends, across the diagonal; without the part the stock alone decides; the status then ends "The clamps are on the stock beside the part; suggest again once the operations are generated." (`holding::tests::without_toolpaths_a_clamp_bites_only_beside_the_part`; found in review). A pair of opposite clamps kept off the middle goes to opposite ends, -Y at -20 and +Y at +20 (`holding::tests::opposite_clamps_away_from_the_middle_go_to_opposite_ends`; found in review: both went to the -X end). A clamp must also keep its stock joined to the part to the end of the setup, its own operations done: the stock the setup leaves is its feed moves cut from the stock it starts from as the simulation cuts them, the same voxels and cuts as the simulation's after the example block's Face, Pocket and Contour (`holding::tests::the_stock_after_a_setup_is_what_its_simulation_leaves`); a contour round the part down past the stock's bottom leaves every bite its material but cuts it free from the part by the end of the setup, so no clamp is placed ("Toe clamps cannot hold this setup: on every side its operations cut the stock under the 12 mm bite free from the part. Leave a skin or tabs joining them until the last setup, or use the vise."), the export refuses the X clamps placed without that check ("… setup 1, operation 3 would cut the part free from the stock under its bite. Leave a skin or tabs joining them until the last setup, or hold this setup in the vise."), and the preview finds them cut free; stopped 3 mm above the bottom the contour leaves a skin and the clamps hold (`holding::tests::a_spot_whose_stock_the_setup_itself_cuts_free_holds_nothing`; found in acceptance review). Every reason, and the refusals of a clamp the setup's own cuts take the stock from, end their advice within 240 characters at worst (three sides named, a 12.5 mm bite, a 2.5 mm gap, setup 12, operation 12, an offset of -12.5) (`holding::tests::the_reasons_keep_their_advice_within_240_characters`; found in review). Each side tries every 5 mm and keeps its clearest spot: a lead through the -X clamp's middle moves it to the end of its side (offset 20 of a 60 mm side, where its whole width stays on the side; it was the quarter point, 15, before the search went every 5 mm) and a cut along Y moves the Y clamps to 20 mm of 100, the first spot clear by 3 mm beyond the gap (`holding::tests::clamps_leave_a_side_whose_middle_the_toolpaths_cross`); a cut low in the stock counts for the clamps above it, its holder keeping the Y clamps 35 mm out along their 100 mm sides (25 before the search went every 5 mm) (`holding::tests::a_cut_low_in_the_stock_counts_for_the_clamps_above_it`); the holder counts as it does in the exact check (offset 20, clear by 2.4 mm, over 15, clear by 0.49) (`holding::tests::clamps_count_the_holder_as_the_exact_check_does`); a spot clear by 3 mm or more beyond the gap is as good as any, so the middle stays (`holding::tests::a_spot_clear_by_three_millimetres_is_as_good_as_any_other`; 10 mm before the acceptance review, which kept clamps off middles clear by 4.65 mm and crowded two into one corner of the six-operation block on 40 mm all round); of the spots as good as any, a side takes one that crowds no clamp placed already (within a clamp width of it): with leads through all four middles, -X at -20, +X at +20, -Y at +20, +Y at -20, one at each corner (`holding::tests::clamps_off_the_middles_spread_to_the_four_corners`; found in acceptance review); clamps on neighbouring sides never overlap, a side whose only clear spots would meet a clamp already placed going without (`holding::tests::clamps_on_neighbouring_sides_never_overlap`). A stock shorter than three clamp widths (60 mm) on both axes gets two clamps on opposite sides (50 × 50 and 59.9 × 59.9 mm: two; 60 × 50 mm: four), and a lead through the -X middle takes the Y pair (`holding::tests::a_small_stock_gets_two_clamps_on_opposite_sides`, `holding::tests::two_clamps_take_the_pair_of_sides_that_is_clearer`). A stock away from the origin is followed (`holding::tests::the_suggestion_follows_a_stock_away_from_the_origin`), and the operations that clash are counted per plan (`holding::tests::the_clashes_are_counted_per_plan`).
- **Suggest layout in the app.** The first Vise or Clamps on a setup, and another library entry, place the jaws or clamps; later edits of the offset, grip, gap and axis move nothing else, and Suggest layout always suggests (`smoke::tests::choosing_a_vise_the_first_time_suggests_it_and_later_edits_are_kept`, `smoke::tests::choosing_clamps_the_first_time_suggests_them_and_the_typed_text_is_dropped`); it needs a setup, workholding and a usable entry (`smoke::tests::suggest_layout_needs_a_setup_workholding_and_a_usable_entry`), reads the generated toolpaths and changes none (`smoke::tests::suggest_layout_reads_the_generated_toolpaths_and_changes_none`), looks at the active setup's own toolpaths (`smoke::tests::suggest_layout_looks_at_the_active_setups_own_toolpaths`), writes and marks the project changed only when something changes (`smoke::tests::suggest_layout_writes_and_touches_only_what_changes`), leaves out and counts the operations that cannot be generated (`smoke::tests::operations_that_cannot_be_generated_are_left_out_and_counted`), says in the status when the stock is too wide for both vise axes (`smoke::tests::a_stock_too_wide_for_every_vise_axis_is_said_in_the_status`), writes nothing without a stock (`smoke::tests::without_a_stock_nothing_is_suggested_or_written`) and leaves another setup's typed clamp text alone (`smoke::tests::suggesting_clamps_leaves_another_setups_typed_text_alone`). On the example block faced over its 2 mm of stock and pocketed, choosing Clamps places none: the status is the reason with "about 25 mm", the setup editor lists it first and then "Place 1 to 8 clamps.", Suggest layout says it again and keeps positions typed by hand (the old middles, which the export refuses: their bites reach 10 mm over the part, and which are still checked: the reason is not counted against them), and the line goes once the setup changes (`smoke::tests::clamps_are_not_placed_where_the_facing_takes_every_bite_and_the_editor_says_why`). With 25 mm of stock beside the part on ±X and the Face on the model's outline the clamps go on that rim, at -X 0 and +X 0, and past the part's ends at -Y -75 and +Y 75, every operation clear of them, nothing listed under the controls and nothing for the export to refuse; with 16 mm, none and the same reason without the facing advice, the Face being on the outline already; with all six of the block's operations the reason says about 40 mm, 35 mm places none and 40 mm places the rim clamps, which export (`smoke::tests::with_25_mm_of_stock_beside_the_part_the_clamps_go_on_it_and_export`). With Ø6, Ø11 and Ø16 facing cutters the reason advises 25, 30 and 35 mm; the outline facing keeps every cutter off the rim, so 16 mm places none and 16.5 mm already places the rim clamps, as does the stock advised, and they export: the advice is on the safe side, and the suggestion and the export agree at the edge (`smoke::tests::the_advised_stock_beside_the_part_holds_the_clamps`). Turned over after setup 1 contoured the block on 40 mm of stock down to the stock's bottom, setup 2 gets no clamps and the reason that the earlier setups cut the stock under the bites free from the part; clamps typed there anyway are refused by the export as cut free (`smoke::tests::a_later_setup_gets_no_clamps_on_stock_the_earlier_setups_cut_free`). With setup 1's Contour down to the part's underside only, setup 1's clamps hold and export, but setup 2's Face takes the 3 mm left under the part, which joined it to the stock round it: setup 2 gets no clamps and the reason that its operations cut the stock under the bites free; the four clamps placed before this check, typed there, are refused by the export, each naming setup 2's Face; the preview of that Face finds them holding at its start and cut free (red) at its end (`smoke::tests::clamps_are_not_placed_on_stock_the_setup_cuts_free_and_the_preview_turns_them_red`; found in acceptance review). In setup 1 itself a Contour down to the stock's bottom gets no clamps and typed ones are refused, naming the Contour; down to the part's underside the clamps go on the rim and export (`smoke::tests::clamps_are_not_placed_on_stock_setup_1_cuts_free_and_a_skin_holds_them`; found in acceptance review). A refusal goes once a suggestion places clamps, even for the setup as it was, and once an earlier setup changes (`smoke::tests::a_refusal_goes_once_clamps_are_placed_or_an_earlier_setup_changes`). The example (5 mm of stock beside its part, no toolpaths) gets no clamps: its bites would reach over the part; with 20 mm beside it, the middles and the note to suggest again once there are toolpaths (`smoke::tests::choosing_clamps_the_first_time_suggests_them_and_the_typed_text_is_dropped`). When a later setup's first simulation is missing, Suggest layout falls back to the earlier cuts and bands worked out for the job (by construction: with the sequence as it is, an operation after one that fails does not generate either, so the case is not reached in a test). The status line reads "Suggested: vise jaws on ±Y, grip 8 mm. Every operation is clear of them." or says how many operations come within the gap, how many could not be generated and that without toolpaths only the stock decided (`holding::tests::the_summary_says_what_was_chosen_and_how_the_operations_fare`), and what is written is read back in the setup's own terms (`holding::tests::a_suggested_layout_is_written_and_described_in_the_setups_own_terms`); the status says clamp offsets in millimetres as the other texts do, "1.23" for 1.234 and "0" for -0 (`holding::tests::a_suggestion_says_its_offsets_like_the_other_texts`). A setup counts as placed by one rule everywhere, in choosing Vise or Clamps and in the plan check's layouts for later setups alike: a vise by its axis (the grip alone is not one, since choosing a vise fills it in from the library entry), clamps by their positions (`holding::tests::a_setup_is_placed_by_the_key_the_first_vise_or_clamps_writes`). The suggestion keeps the moves clear by the gap and the machine profile's path blending tolerance, as export checks them (#162, below: `holding::tests::the_suggestion_keeps_the_moves_clear_by_the_blend_the_export_adds_to_the_gap`). The suggestion does not look at the links, approaches and retracts that export adds (by construction, not tested): export can refuse one that the suggestion left.
- **The operation preview.** Nothing is said before an operation is generated; then a clamp 40 mm onto the stock gives the Pocket one line, "Pocket: the tool hits the clamp on -X (offset 0) at X-22.0 Y… (within the 2 mm gap).", and after it the line that the Pocket would cut away the stock under its bite, "which reaches 38 mm over the part", under "Too close to the workholding (export will refuse this)". The lines are worked out once and served from a cache, again when the gap, the library entry or the work zero changes (in the setup frame, and the heading says so, when the work zero cannot be told), on every tab; the warning never blocks the preview, goes away when the geometry is picked, another operation is shown or the operation is edited, and a setup that cannot be modelled says "Workholding cannot be checked; export will refuse this setup until it is fixed" with the editor's line (`smoke::tests::the_operation_preview_warns_when_the_toolpath_comes_within_the_gap`).
- **Export.** Without workholding nothing is checked, even with an entry that is gone; a clamp 12 mm onto the stock exports and one 40 mm onto it is refused with exactly the line the check finds, "Pocket: the tool hits the clamp on -X (offset 0) at X-22.0 Y… Z15.0 (within the 2 mm gap)."; moved along the side (to 42 mm, on the 84 mm side of the stock), it exports again (`post::tests::workholding_over_the_toolpath_refuses_the_setup_and_clear_workholding_exports`). The headless export, the code `--export-gcode` runs (`export_gcode`, which calls `export_document`, without starting a process), refuses the same hit with "Nothing was written. Top: " and exactly that line, writes no folder, and exports once the clamp's bite is 12 mm (`post::tests::headless_export_refuses_a_clamp_hit_with_the_export_message`). A later setup not square to setup 1 is refused with "Turned: " and the not-square line only, exports at 90, 180 and 270 degrees and, with None, at 30 (`post::tests::a_later_setup_not_square_to_setup_1_is_refused_for_its_workholding_at_export`). A setup that cannot be modelled is refused with the editor's line: a deleted entry, none chosen, a clamp text that does not read, a stock too wide for the vise (`post::tests::workholding_that_cannot_be_modelled_refuses_the_setup_with_the_editors_line`). Every move counts: a feed through a clamp is one problem for the tool in work coordinates (`post_check::tests::a_feed_through_a_clamp_is_one_problem_for_the_tool_in_work_coordinates`); a link between two operations (`post_check::tests::a_link_between_operations_of_one_tool_is_checked_in_either_retract_mode`), the descent from the retract height (`post_check::tests::the_descent_is_checked_even_when_every_move_is_clear`), the final retract, which belongs to the last operation (`post_check::tests::the_final_retract_is_checked_even_when_every_move_is_clear`), the retract before a tool change, which is the old tool's (`post_check::tests::the_retract_before_a_tool_change_is_the_old_tools`) and the approach after it, across at the retract height (`post_check::tests::a_tool_change_approach_sweeps_the_retract_height`) are checked even when every move is clear; the first approach is a point, not a sweep from the origin (`post_check::tests::the_first_approach_is_a_point_not_a_sweep_from_the_origin`). Each operation is checked with its own tool (`post_check::tests::each_operation_is_checked_with_its_own_tool`), and there is one problem per operation, box and part of the tool: a tool and holder that come within the gap of two clamps are four problems, where before issue #61's fix the list was keyed on the operation and the part alone and dropped the second box the same part hit (`post_check::tests::one_problem_per_operation_part_and_box`). The workholding problems the plan check reports are the messages export refuses the setup with, each with the operation it names (`post::tests::held_finds_the_problems_prepare_refuses_a_setup_with_and_the_operations_they_name`). The NC review page lists them for a modelled setup and leaves other setups alone (`smoke::tests::the_nc_page_lists_what_a_modelled_setup_hits_and_leaves_other_setups_alone`).
- **Clearance plane and machine home.** At the clearance plane the program's first rapid starts wherever the tool was, so the plane must be above every box and its gap: exactly at the top and gap is refused, a half millimetre above it is not, the highest box is the one named, the first when tops are equal, and every program of a touch-off job is checked (`post_check::tests::at_the_clearance_plane_it_must_be_above_every_box_and_its_gap`); through the whole export, a plane of 15 mm over clamps that reach 25 mm above the stock is refused, so are 26.5 mm, and 30 mm exports (`post::tests::at_the_clearance_plane_clamps_above_the_plane_refuse_the_setup`); a touch-off setup says it once, not once per program (`post::tests::a_touch_off_setup_reports_the_clearance_plane_once_not_once_per_program`); the message is in work coordinates and says how far over the stock top the operations' clearance must be raised, that field being a height above the stock top, which differs from the Z of the limit when the work zero is not the stock top ("… Raise the operations' clearance above 27.0 mm over the stock top (Z27.0), or retract to machine home.": `holding::tests::a_plane_not_above_the_box_and_its_gap_reads_in_work_coordinates`, and 27.0 mm over the stock top for a limit of Z30.0 in a setup whose zero is 3 mm below it, `plan_check::tests::the_clearance_plane_refusal_is_reported_once_per_setup_as_workholding`). Retracting to the machine's Z home goes over everything: no problem is found for a rapid across at that height (`post_check::tests::a_tool_change_approach_sweeps_the_retract_height`); that the home height clears every jaw and clamp is an assumption of the model, not a check. The motion of a tool change is not modelled (a limit, not tested); the program header, the NC review page and the plan preview say that it is not checked and that retracts to machine home are assumed to clear everything (`post::tests::the_header_says_what_was_checked_of_the_workholding`, `smoke::tests::the_preview_and_the_program_page_say_what_their_checks_leave_out`).
- **The header, the sheet and the comments.** A modelled setup's header has a `Workholding:` comment in place of `Fixture:` and the setup's notes follow as `Setup notes:`; the setup sheet has a Workholding paragraph in place of the fixture note, which only a setup with None prints (`post::tests::the_header_and_the_setup_sheet_say_what_holds_the_stock`, `post::tests::planned_setups_carry_setup_1s_fixture_and_their_workholding_note`, `model::tests::the_setup_sheet_describes_modelled_workholding_in_place_of_the_fixture_note`; LIO-144). The header says what was and was not checked: without workholding one comment, "Not checked: fixtures, clamps and the machine body. LinuxCNC soft limits check absolute position. Preview in LinuxCNC and air-cut first."; with a vise or clamps three, "Not checked: parallels, bolts, the table, the machine body and tool-change motion. Vise jaws and clamps were checked as boxes with a clearance gap.", "Retracts to machine home are assumed to clear everything." and "LinuxCNC soft limits check absolute position. Preview in LinuxCNC and air-cut first.", none longer than 202 characters (the limit of LinuxCNC is 255), and back to the one comment with None (`post::tests::the_header_says_what_was_checked_of_the_workholding`). Every comment still begins with one of the post's fixed starts, the soft-limits and machine-home sentences being two of them, in the test that sweeps a header with workholding text at every offset (`post::tests::no_comment_begins_with_text_from_the_user_or_the_model`). The setup's requested fixture clearance, which nothing checks, precedes the notes in the header as a `Requested fixture clearance, not checked:` comment, a fixed sentence with the app's number, and follows the reposition on the sheet as a paragraph, "not set" when the field is blank (`post::tests::the_header_and_the_setup_sheet_say_what_holds_the_stock`, `smoke::tests::the_holding_step_builds_with_the_gap_fields_named_for_what_they_do`; issue #72).
- **The plan check.** A proposal through setup 1's clamps is refused once for each clamp, at its own stage and operation, for the tool and for the holder; setup 2, hits Face the four clamps from the app and one workholding problem, "Underside: Toe clamps cannot hold this setup: … about 25 mm …", and nothing else about its workholding: not the hits or the "Place 1 to 8 clamps." the export would say of it (`plan_check::tests::a_proposal_through_setup_1s_clamp_is_refused_once_at_stage_workholding`). Clear clamps pass, setup 2 gets the same entry and a layout, and that layout is what the suggestion makes of setup 2's own toolpaths, setup 1's Contour stopping 5 mm above the part's underside so the part stays joined to the stock round it (`plan_check::tests::clamps_placed_clear_pass_and_the_returned_job_lays_out_the_later_setups`); with the Contour down to the underside, setup 2's Face cuts the part free from that stock: the plan check does not pass, its one workholding problem says so for setup 2, and the four clamps the app laid out before this check, typed on setup 2, are refused by the export, each naming setup 2's Face (`plan_check::tests::clamps_on_stock_that_setup_2_cuts_free_from_the_part_do_not_pass`; found in acceptance review: the earlier test passed with every bite cut free after setup 2's Face); a vise in setup 1 gives setup 2 jaws on the axis its stock fits (`plan_check::tests::a_vise_in_setup_1_gives_later_setups_jaws_on_the_axis_the_stock_fits`); a placement a later setup already has is kept (`plan_check::tests::a_later_setup_keeps_a_placement_it_already_has`). The clearance-plane refusal is reported once per setup, as workholding, and a plan that raises every clearance above the clamps and the reported height passes (`plan_check::tests::the_clearance_plane_refusal_is_reported_once_per_setup_as_workholding`); an entry that is gone is one workholding problem and no export problem (`plan_check::tests::a_workholding_entry_that_is_gone_is_one_workholding_problem_and_no_export_problem`); a clearance that takes the program past the machine's Z travel is refused as export refuses it (`plan_check::tests::a_very_large_clearance_is_refused_by_the_exports_z_travel_check`).
- **Clearance cap.** A proposal's `clearance` above the machine's Z travel (the report's third `travel` value; 300 mm when it is 0; never below the default of 15) is refused with the cap in the sentence: 300 is accepted and 500,000 refused with unknown travel, 450 and not 450.5 with a travel of 450, and a travel of 10 leaves the default (`proposal::tests::clearance_is_capped_by_the_machines_z_travel_or_300_mm`); the instructions state the cap (`planner::tests::the_instructions_explain_the_workholding_boxes_and_the_gap`, issue #178).

Screen 89: the four clamps are solid grey bars lying across the stock's edges, shaded by the side each face faces, with the half-played Facing preview between them; nothing shows through them. Screen 92: the -Y jaw is a solid bar along the turned-over stock's lower front edge, and the +Y jaw is hidden behind the stock but for a sliver at the right, where it was drawn through the stock before. Screen 91: the overview's four clamps are solid bars on the stock's edges, so they no longer look sunk into the part; the tags are not covered. Screens 88 and the rest are otherwise as inspected before.
- Renderer screens and pictures inspected after clamps were placed only where they hold (9 October 2026, release build of this work's smoke run and pictures drawn by `look::draw` and `Viewport::render` from a test that was not kept; not a test). Screen 89: choosing Clamps on the example (facing over the whole stock top) places none, and the status line reads "Toe clamps cannot hold this setup: on every side the 12 mm bite loses its stock or comes within 2 mm of a toolpath. Leave about 25 mm of stock beside the part on two opposite sides, or use the vise." (its facing cutter is Ø6: 20 mm needed; that run said "about 20", which the review found fails, and this is the text after the fix); the four middles typed anyway are drawn, the facing preview at 5 % has cut a green strip under the -Y clamp, which is red, and the other three are grey on uncut stock; the inspector's warning lists the hits and, for each clamp, that the Facing would cut away the stock under its bite, "which reaches 12 mm over the part" (10 for +X). Screen 91: the overview's clamps stand on small amber pads, the tags uncovered. Screens 88 and 92 (vises) are as before. Pictures: on the example block with 25 mm of stock on ±X, the Face on the model's outline and the Pocket, Suggest layout places -X 0 and +X 0; before machining they stand on amber pads with a dark contact line, the part and its tags visible through the box; after the Face (front, left and isometric views) they rest on the uncut 25 mm rims, grey, the faced area between them. The old layout (four middles on 2 mm of stock) after the Face: four red clamps above the faced top, and the description names each ("; clamp on -X has no stock under its bite" …); in the 3D view at 30 % of the Face three are red over the cut stock and the +Y one grey on the uncut part, at 50 % and 100 % all four red.

The example block faced over its outline with a Ø6 cutter: on 2 and on 20 mm of stock beside the part the suggestion places none and advises 25 mm; on 25 mm it places -X 0 and +X 0, the export finds nothing, and after the Face (front and isometric) both clamps stand grey on uncut rims beside the faced area. On 35 mm beside it, the Y clamps go past the part's ends at -Y -85 and +Y +85, across the diagonal (top view). The later-setup probe (40 mm of stock, setup 1's contour down to the stock's bottom, setup 2 turned over): the suggestion places none and says the earlier setups cut the stock under the bites free from the part; the view after setup 1's contour shows the stock round the part cut through to the bottom, and the front view after setup 2's Face shows the part standing apart from the two blocks of stock the old four clamps would rest on, which the export refuses as cut free. Clamp on stock beside the part, on two opposite sides, or use the vise."). Screens 88, 91 and 92 as before.
- Pictures and screens inspected after the acceptance review's fixes (10 October 2026; release smoke run and pictures from a test that was not kept; not a test). The flipped probe (40 mm of stock on ±X, both Faces on the outline, setup 1's Contour down to the part's underside): setup 1's suggestion places -X 0 and +X 0; setup 2's places none and says its operations cut the stock under the bites free; with the four clamps placed before typed on setup 2, the front view after its Face shows the part standing apart from the two blocks of stock they rest on, the clamps red, and the description says each holds stock cut free from the part. Setup 1 with its Contour down to the stock's bottom: no clamps; typed ones are red after the Contour, on a frame standing apart from the part. With the Contour down to the part's underside: the clamps stay grey after it, the 3 mm under the part joining it to the rim. Suggest layout took 0.53 s on that block and 0.06 s on the flipped probe's setup 2 in a release build (5.8 s and 1.1 s in a debug build), the generation of the setup's toolpaths included. Every operation is clear of them."), with pads under them and no clamp said to bite over the part. - **What the planner is told and sent.** The report carries setup 1's clamps as boxes in the model's frame, the gap and the 28 mm they need above the stock top, no name, note or id of the entry, and nothing at all without workholding (`analysis::tests::the_report_carries_setup_1s_clamps_gap_and_the_clearance_they_need`); a vise is two jaws from the stock's bottom and needs no clearance (`analysis::tests::a_vise_is_reported_as_two_jaws_from_the_stock_bottom_and_needs_no_clearance`); workholding that cannot be modelled stops the report and unmeets the checklist item (`analysis::tests::workholding_that_cannot_be_modelled_stops_the_report_and_the_checklist`); the first request and the workholding problems carry no entry name or note (`planner::tests::the_report_and_the_workholding_problems_carry_no_entry_name_or_note`). Plans copy setup 1's workholding kind, entry and gap to later setups without a placement (`proposal::tests::later_setups_copy_setup_1s_workholding_without_a_placement`). A proposal's `clearance` can only raise the default of 15 mm: 15 is accepted, 14.9, 5 and 0 are refused, an older proposal without the key still parses, and a new operation starts at the same constant (`proposal::tests::clearance_is_the_proposals_to_raise_and_never_below_the_default`, `proposal::tests::a_proposal_without_the_clearance_key_still_parses_with_none`). The instructions say what the boxes are, that moves within the gap are refused, that later setups get the same kind laid out by the program, that the default clearance of 15 already covers a height below 15 and that it is to be raised only when the reported height is 15 or more (`planner::tests::the_instructions_explain_the_workholding_boxes_and_the_gap`). The plan card shows each setup's modelled workholding, says the app laid out the later ones, and the note under the checks says jaws and clamps are checked in every setup (`smoke::tests::the_plan_card_shows_each_setups_modelled_workholding_and_what_is_checked_of_it`).
- **Texts.** A setup counts as modelling its workholding with Vise or Clamps and not with None, an empty value or anything else (`holding::tests::only_a_vise_or_clamps_count_as_modelled_workholding`). The status after an operation preview, the notes under the operation's tabs (facing, pocket linking and passes, entry and separate-paths links), the label over the simulation and the strip under the viewport say for such a setup that its toolpath is checked against the vise jaws or clamps as boxes with a clearance gap, or that collision checks beyond the modelled vise jaws and clamps are not implemented, and keep the old wording for a setup with None or with workholding that cannot be modelled; the app builds every one of them through the same function, which the test calls (`smoke::tests::the_status_and_the_notes_say_what_is_checked_only_where_workholding_is_modelled`). The plan preview's note and the NC review page's note have a wording for no modelled workholding, for every setup modelling it (each then says that tool-change motion is not checked and that retracts to machine home are assumed to clear everything) and, on the NC page, for some (`smoke::tests::the_preview_and_the_program_page_say_what_their_checks_leave_out`); the NC page counts a setup as modelling it only when its workholding can be checked, so a deleted entry or a clamp text that does not read counts as none (`smoke::tests::the_nc_page_note_counts_only_workholding_that_can_be_checked`). The help for the setup's workholding, gap, fixture, clearance and clearance-plane fields, the NC page, the Workholding page and the facing, linking, breakthrough and face-mill choices says what is checked and refused and what is not, including that the preview covers that operation's own toolpath and export every program move, links, approaches and retracts included (`help::tests::workholding_commands_and_fields_have_specific_help`); each of the five Fixture choices says it only describes the setup and points to Modelled workholding (`choice_help::tests::choice_help_says_what_the_workholding_check_covers_and_what_it_does_not`). The Holding step's two gap fields are named for what they do, **Checked gap to jaws and clamps · mm** and **Requested fixture clearance · mm · not checked**, and each card says which checks use it, the blend added, or that none does and where it is printed (`help::tests::the_holding_steps_gap_fields_say_what_each_does`; issue #72).
- Renderer screens inspected (7 October 2026, release build at commit 969dfcc; not a test). Screen 88: the example block's stock (122.0 × 80.0 × 53.0 mm) with the vise's two jaws drawn as grey boxes, the -Y jaw in front of the part and the +Y jaw behind it, only a sliver of it showing at the right, both from the stock's bottom up to the 8 mm grip; the setup editor, scrolled to its Workholding section, shows Modelled workholding: Vise, the library vise, Jaws face: Y sides, Jaw offset 0, Grip 8, Clearance gap 2, Suggest layout and Open workholding library, then Fixture, Fixture clearance and the start of Reposition action; the status line reads "Suggested: vise jaws on ±Y, grip 8 mm. Every operation is clear of them." and the simulation panel's label "Adaptive stock · jaws and clamps checked as boxes". Only the Y sides are chosen because the stock is 122 mm across X and the vise opens to 120. Nothing is clipped or overlapping. Screen 89: four clamps drawn round the stock, small in the viewport because the camera fit contains them, with the half-played Facing preview and its toolpath; the inspector, scrolled to its end, shows "Configuration checks passed", the amber box "Too close to the workholding (export will refuse this)" with four lines, one for each clamp ("Facing: the tool hits the clamp on -X (offset 0) at X-60.7 Y-14.0 Z-2.0 (within the 2 mm gap).", and the same for +X, -Y and +Y), then Generate operation preview and the four buttons below it; the status bar holds the long preview sentence on two lines, which fit. Nothing is clipped or overlapping. Screen 90: the Workholding page with the two starters listed with their sizes ("Vise · jaws 100 wide, 20 thick · opens to 120 mm", "Clamp · 60 long, 20 wide · bite 12 mm") and the vise definition (name, five sizes, notes); the form scrolls, so Save and Delete are below the fold at 1440 × 930, and the scrollbar covers the right end of the fields, both as on the Materials page (existing pattern, left as it is). Screen 10 (the 1120 × 720 window): with the new Workholding button the document bar's "Operation support" wrapped onto two lines; with the project name field narrowed from 185 to 140 points every button sits on one line (screen 10 of the final run was inspected after that change). The Browser's "1 setup" count is cut at its right edge by the scrollbar in that window; that is not new.

## Leftover stock and planner tools

Each statement names the test behind it (in the module it is in; `smoke::tests::` are the application tests in `src/smoke.rs`), unless it is marked as inspected, by construction or not a test. The tests that need a part use the example block of the post fixture (`post::fixture`), the planner's `block()` or the application tests' `leftover_app`. No test contacts OpenAI: requests go to the loopback stand-in or to a scripted model. Whether the user's ChatGPT plan route accepts the tools and the larger schema (each setup's `holder`, a whole proposal inside `check_plan`'s arguments) for their model is not established here; a real run is needed, and the fallbacks cover a refusal. What a model makes of the tools, and whether it plans better with them, is not established either.

- **Cuts remember their operation.** A cut carries the setup and operation (both from 0, in document order) whose move made it; consecutive collinear moves of one operation merge, and the next operation's first move starts a new cut even when it continues the same line (`volume::tests::cuts_record_their_operation_and_never_merge_across_operations`, `stock_surface::tests::cuts_merge_only_within_one_operation`). Through the sequence, the cuts after each operation are in order and the newest belong to that operation, the three operations of the fixture leave the sources (0, 0), (0, 1) and (1, 0), and a preview of the last operation starts from the inherited cuts and adds its own (`simulation::tests::cuts_remember_the_setup_and_operation_that_made_them`). `Removed::by` names the operation whose cut removed a point, the first when two did, and gives the same answer from a flipped setup; `at` is the same test without the name (`rest::tests::removed_says_which_operation_cut_a_point_first`).
- **The box a later setup starts from.** A facing over the whole top lowers the box's top to the facing's depth, and seen from a flipped setup raises its bottom by the same 2 mm (`rest::tests::a_facing_pass_over_the_whole_top_lowers_the_top_to_its_depth`). Contours through the whole height with their centres on the ±Y faces move those sides in by the width they removed (`rest::tests::full_depth_side_contours_move_those_sides_in_by_the_removed_width`); contours that stop above the bottom leave the sides (`rest::tests::side_contours_stopping_above_the_bottom_leave_the_sides`); a ball cut, a ramped cut and an exhausted work budget never trim (`rest::tests::ball_cuts_ramps_and_an_exhausted_budget_never_trim`); the box never reaches into the part (`rest::tests::the_leftover_box_never_reaches_into_the_part`); with no earlier cuts it is the original box (`rest::tests::with_no_earlier_cuts_the_leftover_box_is_the_original_box`).
- **The band a vise closes on.** Contour passes down to 2 mm above the stock's bottom keep the box's width and, for a vise in the same setup's frame, the band's at grips of 2 and 4 mm; turned over, the band at a grip of 8 mm loses 2.5 mm a side (less the 0.01 mm it stops short of a cut face) on ±Y, keeps X and the box's heights, and one of 11 mm, which reaches the 2 mm left, is the box; a grip of nothing and an exhausted budget leave the box (`rest::tests::the_band_a_vise_grips_loses_the_sides_cut_through_all_its_heights`). The band stops at the part, and ball-end passes never narrow it (`rest::tests::the_band_never_reaches_into_the_part_and_ball_cuts_never_narrow_it`). The sides the jaws close on are proven only over the jaws' reach along them: passes that leave the stock's corners uncut narrow the band for jaws 10 mm wide in the middle or moved 1 mm along, not for jaws as long as the stock or moved to reach a corner, and jaws beside the stock get the box; the sides along the jaws keep the box's length (`rest::tests::the_jaws_close_on_what_lies_within_their_reach_along_the_sides`). Found on the smoke run's screen 92 in the final fix wave: a Ø6 Contour round 4 mm margins leaves the corners, which held the whole side. The jaws stand on the band's faces, the opening is checked on its width (a vise opening 55 mm takes walls 50 mm apart, not the 60 mm box), the material checks pass on it, the suggestion fits the jaws on it, and a grip reaching the 3 mm the contour left uses the box (`holding::tests::a_turned_over_vise_closes_on_the_walls_setup_1s_outside_contour_finished`). On the reviewer's job (`post::fixture::flipped_after(3)` and `(4)`: setup 1 faces, pockets and contours the walls to the underside, with drills in the second; setup 2 turned over in the starter vise), the flipped box stays 124 × 84 mm and the band, for jaws on ±Y at grips of 8 and 30 mm and on ±X at 8 mm, is the walls with the Contour's 0.3 mm allowance, 120.7 × 80.7 mm; on ±Y the setup exports with a Model-origin zero and the plan check finds nothing but the fixture's slow drill, and on ±X it is refused for that alone: "Flipped: The stock is 120.71 mm across the jaws; this vise opens to 120 mm." (`post::tests::a_flipped_setup_in_a_vise_closes_on_the_walls_setup_1_contoured`). With the default "Stock top center" zero the ±Y export is refused for the zero instead (setup 1's Contour cut the sides where an edge finder touches them): a probe in the final fix wave, not a test. The planner's plan check passes the same kind of plan, setup 1's Contour stopped above its own jaws (`plan_check::tests::a_flipped_vise_setup_grips_the_walls_setup_1s_contour_finished`). Found in the final review: the vise closed on the box, so this job was refused at export and in the plan check ("removed the material the jaws grip", and on ±X "124 mm across the jaws"). Making the proof work on these walls took three changes to it, all of which keep it conservative: the candidate sides stop at the part's face (the box never moves into the part anyway), a box whose centre is material in no cut is given up before it is split, and a box is split along the axis where it is longest against the median of the cuts that reach it, not the longest one; with the old proof the band ran out of its budget on the rounded corners past a grip of 2 mm. The existing tests of the box and of the start heights pass unchanged.
- **The bottom the stock stands on.** Turned over after setup 1 faced the whole top 2 mm deep with a Ø10 ball end mill, which the box keeps, the jaws' band is the box, the highest the material's bottom can lie is 2 mm above the box's bottom, the jaws reach 10 mm above it for a grip of 8, and a Ø10 cutter along the +Y jaw's face 11 mm above the box's bottom hits them within the 2 mm gap, which jaws 8 mm above the box's bottom let through; without the earlier cuts, and after a facing over the middle of the top alone (the stock stands on its uncut ends), the jaws reach 8 mm above the box's bottom and the move is clear; the setup is holdable, at grips of 8 and 3 mm, since the material to grip and to rest on is looked for from where the stock stands (#159, below), and with nothing left between the jaws up to the stock's top it is refused outright (`holding::tests::jaws_reach_above_the_layer_a_ball_facing_took_off_the_bottom_a_turned_over_setup_rests_on`). The height itself: nothing cut, or the faced layer at the top, keeps the box's bottom; a ball facing 2 mm deep raises it 2 mm and a second one 2 mm deeper 4 mm; a flat facing the box follows leaves nothing to raise it past until a ball pass takes 0.5 mm more; a facing over the middle alone keeps it, also under a footprint that leaves 0.3 mm of the uncut ends, which the 64 fixed columns all miss and the proof finds exactly, while without a work budget the columns raise it 2 mm; cut through everywhere, it is the box's top (`rest::tests::the_bottom_rises_past_the_layer_cuts_of_any_shape_can_have_taken_off_it`).
- **The sequence.** A flipped setup after a facing of 2 mm starts from a stock 2 mm shorter whose bottom is 2 mm higher, with the other sides as carried over, and a vise grips from that bottom up (`simulation::tests::a_flipped_setup_starts_from_the_stock_the_facing_left`). Among more than 8,000 other cuts (a facing of 4 mm, a pocket, a contour to the part's underside and drills, with flutes that reach the faced layer) the facing still trims the flipped stock by 4 mm and the 3 mm of stock below the part keeps the sides (`simulation::tests::the_facing_still_trims_the_flipped_stock_among_thousands_of_other_cuts`). Every setup is told the box it starts from, those without operations too: none for the first, the box the facing left for a later one, the original block when nothing before cut, and none after an earlier operation that fails (`simulation::tests::each_setup_is_told_the_box_it_starts_from_with_or_without_operations`). A **Stock top** or **Stock top front-left** work zero is accepted on a later setup whose stock top an earlier setup faced whole (the setup text says "Stock: 124.000 x 84.000 x 43.000 mm.") and refused, naming the top face and Model origin, when the facing covered only the part's top inside a rim (`post::tests::stock_datums_sit_on_a_whole_faced_top_but_not_on_a_partly_cut_one`; it replaces the test that refused both).
- **A vise needs something to grip.** Jaws on ±Y with a grip of 8 mm after an outside contour that went down to 3 mm above the bottom: the band is the box (its lower 3 mm are whole), and the setup is refused, naming setup 1, operation 3 and its name, the band (from 0 to 8 mm above the stock's bottom) and what to do; a grip of 3 mm holds, jaws on ±X (where the contour did not go) hold, and a contour stopped 9 mm above the bottom leaves the band whole (`holding::tests::jaws_gripping_where_an_outside_contour_went_down_cannot_hold`). A stock 9 mm tall with a grip of 8.5 mm, or 12 mm, is refused for leaving less than 1 mm above the jaws, one with a grip of 8 mm is not, clamps and no holding are not, and setup 1 (no earlier cuts) has only that check (`holding::tests::the_stock_must_stand_a_millimetre_above_the_jaws`).
