A setup turned over onto a face where setup 1 pocketed away most of the bottom is refused ("removed most of the stock's bottom it would rest on"), and a pocket over the middle 18 mm leaves enough (`holding::tests::a_vise_needs_most_of_the_stocks_bottom_to_rest_on`).
- **Clamps need something to bite.** Clamps on ±X over a rim setup 1 faced off are refused, one on -Y where nothing was cut is not, and a clamp moved along its side past the rim's end holds (`holding::tests::each_clamp_needs_the_stock_under_its_bite`).
- **A part cut free cannot be held.** After an outside contour one tool wide down past the bottom, the jaws and the clamps hold a frame cut free from the part and are refused ("cut the part free from the material the jaws grip", "…from the stock under its bite"); not knowing the part, only the material is checked, and a contour stopped 3 mm above the bottom leaves a skin that joins them (`holding::tests::a_part_cut_free_from_what_the_jaws_or_clamps_hold_cannot_be_held`). A part with its corner cut away (an L of 20 triangles), contoured free along its own outline, is refused by the jaws and the clamps though the block its corner left, within the part's box but outside the part, still joins the frame, and a contour stopped 3 mm above the bottom joins them (`holding::tests::a_part_cut_free_along_its_own_outline_cannot_be_held_whatever_its_box_keeps`); found in the review of 15 October 2026 (#58): the fill was seeded from every voxel within the part's box, so the corner's block counted as part and the frame as joined to it. The stock is flood-filled through its occupied voxels from the part's own, those whose centres lie inside the model (`plan_check::PartGrid`, a vertical line through the model at each voxel column): material cut free is told from material joined to it and from a voxel cut away, with no answer without earlier cuts or without the part, the same seen from a flipped setup, a skin left under the ring joins, and a part where no stock is left cannot be told apart (`rest::tests::the_material_joined_to_the_part_is_found_through_the_stock_left`). A voxel is cleared when its centre lies in a cut, so a skin thinner than a voxel (about the longest stock side divided by 96) can count as removed, and a part held only by it can be called cut free; and a kerf narrower than a voxel can clear none, and a part cut free by it can be taken as joined. Neither has a test of its own; the README says so and to leave a web thicker than a voxel.
- **A clamp keeps its stock for the whole setup.** Setup 1, with nothing cut before it, faced across its whole top 1 mm deep: the clamps on -X and +Y are refused, each naming the facing ("…setup 1, operation 1 would cut away the stock under its bite. Keep those cuts off it, clamp on stock this setup does not cut, or use the vise.") when the part is beside the bites, with "(for example face only the outline)" only where the bite is farther from the part than the diameter of the cutters that took its stock (8 mm from it: for a Ø6 facing, not for a Ø20 one); when the bites reach over the part (by 2 and by 10 mm) no cut can be kept off them and the message says "…which reaches 10 mm over the part. Clamp on stock beside the part, on two opposite sides, or use the vise."; facing only inside the bites passes, and a vise is not checked against the setup's own cuts (`holding::tests::a_clamp_must_keep_the_stock_its_own_setup_would_cut_from_under_its_bite`). The plan check reports one such problem per clamp of setup 1 (the user's, 10 mm over the part, the advice whole within the 300 characters kept) when the plan faces the whole stock, before the hits, and for setup 2, which gets no clamps, the reason once (`plan_check::tests::a_proposal_through_setup_1s_clamp_is_refused_once_at_stage_workholding`); the export refuses the setup with it first and then the hit, and its message names the setup once (`post::tests::workholding_over_the_toolpath_refuses_the_setup_and_clear_workholding_exports`, `post::tests::headless_export_refuses_a_clamp_hit_with_the_export_message`, `post::tests::held_finds_the_problems_prepare_refuses_a_setup_with_and_the_operations_they_name`, `smoke::tests::the_nc_page_lists_what_a_modelled_setup_hits_and_leaves_other_setups_alone`); the operation inspector's warning lists it after the hits (`smoke::tests::the_operation_preview_warns_when_the_toolpath_comes_within_the_gap`); the planner is told so (`planner::tests::the_instructions_teach_planning_the_holding_before_any_operation`).
- **The message names the cause.** The operations behind at least a quarter of the failed samples are named, the most first, and one behind fewer is not (4 of 28 samples is too few; 12 of 36 is named after the larger) (`holding::tests::the_cause_names_the_operations_behind_a_quarter_of_the_failed_samples_most_first`). An operation reads as its setup number, its strategy and its name, "Contour \"Outside\"", and one the document does not have has no name (`holding::tests::earlier_operations_are_named_by_their_setup_number_and_strategy`).
- **The advice follows where the band lies.** The advice names `bottom_offset` when the band the jaws grip is at the bottom of the earlier setup's stock, and "Leave at least 8 mm of those sides uncut in setup 1, or cut them through all 8 mm there, or choose another holder." when it is at its top or on its side, as it is when the later setup is turned over or onto its side: setup 1's contour going only 5 mm down from its top cuts the lower 5 mm of the flipped setup's band and not the upper 3, so the jaws close on the box with half their face on air, and the advice names neither `bottom_offset` nor this setup; with the contour taken through all 8 mm the jaws close on the walls and the setup holds. `Removed::up_in` gives how far the later setup's up points up in the earlier setup's frame: 1 for the same way, -1 turned over, about 0 on its side, none for a source that cut nothing (`holding::tests::the_advice_for_the_jaws_band_depends_on_where_it_lies_in_the_earlier_setup`). The same through the plan check, for a flipped setup whose band setup 1's Contour cut in part (it stops 35 mm above the part's underside), within the 300 characters a message keeps (`plan_check::tests::a_flipped_vise_setup_whose_band_setup_1_cut_in_part_is_refused`), and through the export, for a setup facing the same way (`post::tests::a_vise_gripping_where_an_earlier_contour_went_down_is_refused_naming_the_contour`). Found in review of the third task of this work: the advice said `bottom_offset` for a flipped setup too; found in the final review: the flipped advice asked for cuts in this setup, inside its own jaws' band.
- **The grip fits a thin stock.** The suggested vise grip is the library's, lowered to the stock's height less 1 mm and never below 3 mm: stocks 40, 9, 6, 6.25 and 4.5 mm tall give 8, 8, 5, 5.25 and 3.5 mm, one 3.5 or 1 mm tall gives 3 mm, and an entry that grips less keeps its own grip (`holding::tests::the_suggested_grip_leaves_a_millimetre_of_a_thin_stock_above_the_jaws`). `holding_issues_with` checks the stock it is told: a box wider across the jaws than the vise opens, or too low for the grip, each with its sentence, and nothing without a box (`model::tests::holding_issues_with_checks_the_stock_it_is_told`).
- **Export refuses what cannot be held.** A job whose contour took the jaws' band is refused with the line the check finds, once, by the export and by `prepare`, naming the contour, and exports when the contour stops 6 mm higher (`post::tests::a_vise_gripping_where_an_earlier_contour_went_down_is_refused_naming_the_contour`); a job whose contour left a frame cut free from the part is refused with the cut-free line, and exports when 10 mm of stock under the part joins them (`post::tests::a_vise_on_a_frame_an_earlier_contour_cut_free_from_the_part_is_refused`).
- **The report's library and the schema.** `workholding_library` lists the usable entries by number from 1 with their kind and sizes, is present and empty without a library, skips an entry of a kind the planner cannot use without counting it, and never holds a name, a note, the project title or a setup's name (`analysis::tests::the_report_lists_the_workholding_library_by_number_in_sizes_only`). Setup 1's `workholding` carries the number of its own entry, which an unusable entry before it does not move, and no number when it models no holding or its entry is gone (`analysis::tests::the_reports_workholding_gives_the_library_number_of_setup_1s_entry`); the first request carries both (`planner::tests::the_report_names_setup_1s_library_number_in_its_workholding`). The schema asks every setup for its `holder` as an integer or null, a reply reads it back, and -1 is refused (`proposal::tests::the_schema_asks_every_setup_for_its_holder_as_an_integer_or_null`).
- **The holder a plan names.** A later setup built with a library number has that entry's kind and the user's gap and no position yet; null and setup 1's own number keep setup 1's (`proposal::tests::a_later_setup_builds_with_the_library_entry_it_names`); setup 2 can be held in a vise when setup 1 holds nothing or is clamped (`proposal::tests::a_later_setup_can_be_held_when_setup_1_is_not_and_can_switch_kind`). A number past the library, 0, and a number that is no entry are refused at the stage `structure` ("Setup 2: workholding 9 is not in workholding_library."), as is a setup 1 holder other than null or its own number ("Setup 1: holder must be null or 1: setup 1's workholding is the user's.") (`proposal::tests::a_holder_that_is_not_in_the_library_or_not_setup_1s_is_refused`). The plan check lays the named entry out for the setup's toolpaths: setup 1 keeps its clamps and setup 2 gets a vise with an axis and a grip the program chose (`plan_check::tests::a_holder_the_proposal_names_for_a_later_setup_is_laid_out_for_its_toolpaths`).
- **The stock before each setup in the check report.** Each setup has its size, its box from the part's corner in its own axes, how much was removed from each side and the operations that did it, by name: a facing of 2 mm off the top and a contour 3.5 mm in from -Y, with the operations by number only when the document has no names (`plan_check::tests::the_stock_summary_names_each_side_cut_away_and_the_operations_that_did`); in the check of the good two-setup plan, setup 2 is 124 × 84 × 43 mm with 4 mm removed from its bottom by setup 1, operation 1 (`plan_check::tests::a_good_two_setup_plan_passes_every_stage`). The feedback after a checked answer carries `stocks` (`planner::tests::the_planners_own_check_of_an_answer_becomes_the_latest_draft_for_the_tools`).
- **The instructions.** PLANNING THE HOLDING stands after WORKHOLDING and before SPEEDS AND FEEDS and tells the model to decide before any operation how each setup is held, that a later setup starts from the stock the earlier ones left, what a vise and clamps need, that a vise closes on the material in its grip band (walls an earlier setup cut through the whole band can be gripped, sides cut through part of it cannot), what a vise and clamps need (material under the band to rest on, a band no wider than `max_opening`), to cut the sides a turned-over setup grips through the whole band or leave them whole, and to stop an outside Contour above the grip with `bottom_offset` for a setup facing the same way and finish it later, to use `workholding_library` and `holder`, and that a failure comes back at the stage `workholding`; the advice not to cut the sides a vise grips, which the band contradicts, and the rule not to face away the top a flipped setup rests on, which was never one, are gone, and WORKHOLDING says to finish the walls below setup 1's jaws from the setup turned over (`planner::tests::the_instructions_teach_planning_the_holding_before_any_operation`; the last three found in the final review); it says what a null holder means (`planner::tests::a_null_holder_means_setup_1s_entry_or_no_modelled_holding`). The TOOLS section lists each tool offered with its limits, says what a request is (the part report, or a check report the program sends back) and counts every limit per request, never per reply, and neither it nor a tool's name appears when no tools are offered (`planner::tests::the_tools_section_lists_each_offered_tool_with_its_limits`; "reply" for both was found in the final review).
- **The tools are offered.** `view_model` first and the five others when pictures are on, the five alone when they are off, each with a description and a strict object schema, `check_plan` taking a whole proposal in the answer's own schema (`planner_tools::tests::the_tools_are_offered_with_view_model_only_when_pictures_are_on`). Calls are answered with JSON and each puts one line on the timeline, a call that cannot be answered a sentence, and tool rounds are not planner requests (`planner::tests::tool_calls_are_answered_with_json_and_each_gets_a_line_in_the_timeline`). A tool that does not exist is answered with "There is no tool named …", its name cut to 40 characters, and the run goes on (`planner_tools::tests::an_unknown_tool_is_answered_with_a_sentence`, `planner::tests::an_unknown_tool_is_answered_and_the_run_goes_on`). `view_model` goes through the same registry with its own limits, counted for a view that cannot be drawn too (`planner_tools::tests::view_model_goes_through_the_registry_with_its_picture_limits`).
- **The rounds.** Sixteen rounds of calls are allowed in a planner request, the send after the sixteenth may not call (`tool_choice` `none`), and a seventeenth round fails the request ("kept calling tools") (`planner::tests::sixteen_tool_rounds_are_allowed_and_a_seventeenth_fails_the_request`, `planner::tests::a_model_that_never_stops_looking_fails_the_request`); the count starts again with each planner request (`planner::tests::the_round_count_starts_again_with_each_planner_request`).
- **The planner's own check feeds the tools.** After the planner checks an answer, `stock_after` and `check_holding` read that answer, and a draft the tools checked next is the latest; an answer that does not build leaves no draft, and the tools say "Check a draft with check_plan first, or answer with a proposal." (`planner::tests::the_planners_own_check_of_an_answer_becomes_the_latest_draft_for_the_tools`; there `check_holding` of the starter clamp on setup 2 answers "Toe clamps cannot hold this setup" and "no place for clamps").
- **Refusals.** A request refused with a 400 or Unsupported is sent again without any tool, with no tool choice and without the TOOLS section, and the run remembers; with pictures on, `view_model` goes with the others and the pictures stay, and no drop of pictures is reported (`planner::tests::a_refused_tool_request_drops_all_the_tools_and_their_instructions`). A second refusal drops the pictures after the tools, in that order (`planner::tests::a_second_refusal_drops_the_pictures_after_the_tools`). When neither is left, a refusal is the request's error (`planner::tests::without_tools_or_pictures_a_refusal_is_the_requests_error`). Only Unsupported and a 400 whose message or parameter names tools, functions or images count as a refusal of them: a 400 on `max_output_tokens` ends the run with that error after one request and no Dropped row, with the fake server counting one request (`planner::tests::a_bad_request_that_names_neither_tools_nor_pictures_ends_the_run_at_once`, `planner::tests::only_a_bad_request_naming_tools_functions_or_pictures_is_a_refusal_of_them`, issue #25); a 400's `param` is part of the error's text (`openai::tests::a_bad_requests_parameter_is_part_of_its_text`). A 400 that names only images or pictures (`image_url` in its parameter) drops the pictures first, with one resend and no "without tools" row, and keeps every tool; naming both, naming the tools, or `Unsupported` drops the tools first (`planner::tests::a_refusal_naming_only_the_images_drops_the_pictures_and_keeps_the_tools`, issue #175). Once the tools are gone, a call and its output of any tool become plain text, as before for `view_model`, and while they are on they are kept (`planner::tests::calls_and_outputs_of_any_tool_become_plain_text_once_the_tools_are_gone`).
- **`check_plan`.** It answers the check report, with `stocks`, and its line is "Checked a draft: passed" (or "N problems"); the draft becomes the latest for the other tools and the operation views (`planner_tools::tests::check_plan_answers_the_check_report_and_the_draft_becomes_the_latest`). A draft that is not a proposal is one sentence of at most 300 characters, nothing is counted and the latest draft stays; a proposal sent as text, even in a code fence, is read as an answer is; arguments with another key are unreadable; a draft that does not build answers its problems ("T9 is not in the tool library."), counts, and leaves no latest draft (`planner_tools::tests::check_plan_answers_a_draft_it_cannot_read_or_build_with_its_problems`). At most 3 a request and 8 a run, each refused with "No more draft checks for this request; answer with the proposal." or "… in this run …", not run and not counted, the limits being `check_plan`'s alone (`planner_tools::tests::check_plan_is_limited_per_request_and_per_run`).
- **`stock_after`.** It answers the setup's `stocks` entry, with the line "Stock before setup 2: 124 × 84 × 45 mm; bottom cut 2 mm by setup 1" (and "Stock before setup 1: 124 × 84 × 47 mm" for the first); a setup the draft does not have is "The latest draft has no setup N."; nothing is counted (`planner_tools::tests::stock_after_tells_the_stock_before_a_setup_of_the_latest_draft`). Before any draft both it and `check_holding` say to check a draft first (`planner_tools::tests::before_any_draft_stock_after_and_check_holding_say_so`); after a draft whose generation failed, the setups it did not reach say the stock is not known (`planner_tools::tests::the_stock_before_a_setup_after_a_failed_generation_is_not_known`).
- **`check_holding`.** On the example block with a faced top, the starter clamp on setup 2 does not hold: setup 2's facing runs over the whole top, where clamps would sit, so none are laid out and the one problem is the plan check's reason, "Underside: Toe clamps cannot hold this setup: …", with the line "Clamp 2 for setup 2: 1 problem (no place for clamps)"; a vise opening 60 mm is refused for the width across its jaws, which is the walls' (80.1 mm: setup 1's Contour finished them through the band, so the jaws would close on them, on ±Y), not the 84 mm the box keeps ("opens 60, needs N" on the timeline), the starter vise holds there ("Vise 1 for setup 2: holds (opens 120, needs N)"), an entry that is not in the library is "workholding 0 is not in workholding_library." (and 9), a setup the draft does not have and a missing argument are sentences, and no answer holds an entry's name or notes (`planner_tools::tests::check_holding_tests_a_library_entry_on_the_stock_before_a_setup`). For setup 1, which keeps the user's holder, any entry other than setup 1's own is answered "Setup 1 keeps its own holder; check_holding is for later setups.", and its own entry is checked as the user placed it (a vise on ±Y at a grip of 6 mm, which no suggestion makes, holds: "Vise 1 for setup 1: holds (opens 120, needs 84)") (`planner_tools::tests::check_holding_checks_setup_1_only_with_its_own_entry_as_placed`; found in the final review: setup 1 was given a suggested layout). Its problems are the plan check's workholding problems for the same draft with that entry as setup 2's holder, and a vise on sides setup 1 left holds (`planner_tools::tests::check_holding_agrees_with_the_plan_checks_workholding_stage`).
- **`cutting_data`.** For a Ø10 flat mill (3 flutes) in the material's 150-300 m/min: 4,775-9,549 rpm, recommended 7,160, feeds from the chip-load band up to 10 mm at 7,160 rpm (0.030-0.080 mm per tooth), recommended 1,180 mm/min, the band reported; a tool over 16 mm is in the last band, with no upper diameter (`planner_tools::tests::cutting_data_reads_the_band_and_the_range_of_each_tools_diameter`). A Ø4 drill's range stops at the machine's 12,000 rpm and the middle, 11,968.7, is rounded to 11,970 (`planner_tools::tests::cutting_data_for_the_drill_clips_the_rpm_to_the_machine_and_recommends_the_middle`); a machine that stops at 6,000 rpm gives 6,000 only, and one that stops at 6,004 gives 6,004 because rounding to 6,000 would run below the least surface speed (`planner_tools::tests::cutting_data_runs_at_the_machines_maximum_when_the_material_wants_more`); a machine feed of 1,000 clips the feed range to 718-1,000 and recommends 860, and one of 500 gives 500 (`planner_tools::tests::cutting_data_clips_the_feed_to_the_machine_maximum`). A tool the report does not have, bad arguments, a material without bands or without surface speeds are sentences (`planner_tools::tests::cutting_data_answers_a_sentence_for_what_it_cannot_tell`). A Ø100 face mill at no more than 30 m/min would give 95 rpm, under the plan's 100 rpm floor: the answer is a sentence that the combination cannot be used, and just enough surface speed for 100 rpm gives a range of 100 (`planner_tools::tests::cutting_data_never_recommends_an_rpm_under_the_plans_minimum`, issue #181). The recommendation is `material::pick`'s, the one a material change sets: on a 24000 rpm, 1000 mm/min router the spindle runs more than 100 rpm under the middle of the range, at the speed `pick` gives, with a chip of the band's least within the check's 1 % (`planner_tools::tests::cutting_data_slows_the_spindle_when_the_feed_is_capped_as_a_material_change_does`); that it passes `material::check` is the property test of the material change.
- **`surface_info`.** The top's plane, with its depth 0, area, box and surfaces, the surface's own area in mm² and its mean normal (`planner_tools::tests::surface_info_of_the_top_lists_its_plane_and_the_surfaces_own_area_and_normal`); a floor's depth and the largest cutter that fits, line "Surface N: floor 12 mm deep in +Z" (`planner_tools::tests::surface_info_of_a_floor_says_how_deep_it_is_and_what_cutter_fits`); a surface two orientations list, in the report's order (`planner_tools::tests::surface_info_lists_every_orientation_whose_planes_name_the_surface`); a wall no plane lists, with its area and a note (`planner_tools::tests::a_surface_no_plane_lists_still_has_its_area_and_normal`); the normal in setup 1's frame, so a surface that faces up in the model faces down with setup 1 turned over (`planner_tools::tests::surface_info_gives_the_normal_in_setup_1s_frame_not_the_models`); a surface the part does not have and bad arguments are sentences (`planner_tools::tests::surface_info_answers_a_sentence_for_an_unknown_surface_or_unreadable_arguments`).
- **What the answers hold.** Over `cutting_data` for five tool numbers, `surface_info` for every surface and one past them, `stock_after` and `check_holding` before any draft, and `check_plan` of a draft that fails and of one that passes, each followed by `stock_after` for setups 0 to 3 and `check_holding` for every setup and library number 0 to 3, with setup 1 held in a library vise and a library whose entries, the project's title and setup 1 all carry a secret name and note, none appears (`planner_tools::tests::answers_carry_no_holder_name_note_project_title_or_setup_name`; the draft tools added in the final review's fix wave).
- **The setup editor's stock.** A later setup shows the original box and the note "Original stock size until the earlier setups are simulated." until the box is worked out, then the box setup 1 left (4 mm shorter in the fixture) and no note; setup 1 never has the note; with one setup, or without the part, there is nothing to work out (`smoke::tests::the_setup_editor_says_the_stock_is_the_original_until_the_earlier_setups_are_simulated`). An edit of an operation makes it out of date until it is worked out again (a facing 1 mm less deep leaves a taller box), and so do a tool's diameter, a setup's placement and another part, each undone by undoing it (`smoke::tests::an_edit_makes_the_known_stock_stale_until_the_simulation_is_done_again`); the vise's grip, offset and axis, the gap, switching to clamps and typing clamp positions do not, and a setup's rotation does (`smoke::tests::editing_the_workholding_keeps_the_known_stock`). The job starts only once the edits have paused for `EDIT_PAUSE`, 700 ms, the pause the project's save waits too (the test ticks right after an edit, which starts nothing, then with the last edit 800 ms back, which starts it), and one at a time (`smoke::tests::the_simulation_waits_for_the_edits_to_pause_and_runs_one_at_a_time`); the result of a job whose document was edited while it ran is dropped, the next job is for the edited document and its result is kept (`smoke::tests::a_result_for_a_document_that_was_edited_while_it_ran_is_discarded`). A setup after one that failed keeps the original box, the note, and is not worked out again for the same document (`smoke::tests::a_setup_whose_earlier_setups_failed_keeps_the_original_stock_and_the_note`); a later setup without operations has its box too, the original block when nothing before it cut, and none for any when an earlier setup failed (`smoke::tests::a_later_setup_without_operations_gets_its_box_from_the_job`).
- **The editor's holding checks.** The job that works out the box also makes each later setup's holding checks on the stock it left, and the editor lists them with the other workholding issues while they are current: on a job whose setup 1 Contour cuts only the top 5 mm of the walls, setup 2 turned over in the starter vise on ±Y lists "Setup 2 cannot be held in the vise: setup 1, operation 3 removed the material the jaws grip on ±Y …" (nothing before the job has run), exactly the lines export refuses the setup with, without its name; the NC review page then counts setup 2 as not checked. A grip of 3 mm, all of it cut through, makes the checks out of date without making the stock so; the next run keeps the stock (the same leftover volume, not simulated again) and checks again: nothing is listed, both setups count as checked, and the jaws are drawn on the walls, the band 80.7 mm across them inside the 84 mm box (`smoke::tests::the_setup_editor_shows_what_the_earlier_setups_left_the_vise_to_grip`). A job whose result comes back after the grip was edited keeps the stock but not the checks, and the next run is for the checks (`smoke::tests::a_result_whose_holding_was_edited_while_it_ran_keeps_the_stock_only`). Found in the final review: the editor listed only the height and opening rules, and the NC page counted such a setup as checked.
- **The editor's checks and layout use that box.** The jaws are drawn from the original box's bottom until the box is known and from the leftover's after it; a grip the original box is tall enough for and the leftover is not is listed in the editor once the box is known, with no jaws drawn, and is fine on the original box (`smoke::tests::the_workholding_checks_and_the_jaws_use_the_stock_the_earlier_setups_left`). Suggest layout takes the original box until the box is worked out (a vise opening 120 mm goes on ±Y for a block 124 mm across, grip 45 mm of the 47 mm) and then the leftover (grip 42 mm of 43 mm, "Suggested: vise jaws on ±Y, grip 42 mm.") (`smoke::tests::suggest_layout_uses_the_stock_the_earlier_setups_left_when_it_is_known`).
- **The editor draws that box as the stock.** For a later setup whose box is current, the view is given it (`ui::leftover_view`): none while it is not worked out, the box setup 1's facing left (4 mm shorter) once it is, with the jaws on it, none again after an edit of an earlier operation, still one after an edit of the vise, none for setup 1 and none for the example (`smoke::tests::the_editor_draws_the_stock_a_later_setup_starts_from_only_when_it_is_known`). A view given a box draws it as the stock, translucent, and fits the camera to it; one not given a box for a later setup draws no stock, as before, and one for the first setup draws its own (`viewport::tests::a_later_setups_known_box_is_drawn_as_its_stock_and_otherwise_none_is`; the label's wording, "Stock X × Y × Z" against "Remaining stock · generate preview", is in the same code and was checked on screen 92, not by a test).
- **A later setup's stock through an edit of its own operations.** Picking a face simulates the whole job again but cannot change what the setup starts from (found on the TOTEM case: every pick in setup 2 dropped its stock box for about a second, and the 3D view refitted, about 6 % closer). On the flipped two-setup job, after a note added to setup 2's operation the job is out of date (`left_boxes` and `current_checks` none) while setup 2's box is still known and real, `leftover_view` is the same box, `known_held` the same grip from the last checks, and the uncut line says "Checking completeness again …" until the job is simulated again, when it is the same line as before. An edit of setup 1's operation, or of setup 2's placement, makes the box unknown; a vise grip keeps the box but not the checks' grip. A project replaced by another keeps none of the old one's results (`smoke::tests::an_edit_of_a_later_setups_operations_keeps_the_stock_it_starts_from`). On a copy of the TOTEM project (inspected 20 October 2026, not a test), setup 2's Contour and a picked hole: the 3D view's scene bounds were the same before, while checking and after (min −73.4, −56.3, 3.8, max 73.8, 54.2, 17.2 mm), with the stock box, the magenta faces, both lines of the simulation panel and the browser's list (grey, "STILL UNCUT · CHECKING") kept.
- **The smoke screens' tool lines.** The five lines of screen 93 are what the tools themselves answer on the imported block, in the order a model might call them: a draft of two setups checked, the stock before setup 2, the starter vise on it, the cutting data of T1 and a floor surface (`smoke::tests::the_tool_lines_of_the_smoke_timeline_are_the_tools_own`; it checks their openings, "bottom cut" in the second and "floor" in the last, not the numbers, which depend on the document).
- Renderer screens inspected (9 October 2026, release build of the final review's fix wave; not a test). Screen 92, setup 2 turned over and held in the starter vise (step 116: setup 1, on stock 4 mm wider than the part all round, faces the 10 mm above the part and contours its walls with the document's Ø6 cutter down to the underside; setup 2 is "Flipped underside", turned about X; the editor at its Setup page; `leftover_view` asserted to be the box, `holding_lines` asserted empty, and each jaw asserted to stand on a face of the band): the log says "setup 2 starts from 128.000 × 88.000 × 43.000 mm, its original box is 128.000 × 88.000 × 53.000 mm" and "the jaws close on 80.668 mm of the box's 88.000 mm across them. Suggested: vise jaws on ±Y, grip 8 mm. Every operation is clear of them."; the panel "Stock before this setup · X × Y × Z" reads "128.000 × 88.000 × 43.000 mm" in blue, the label above the view "Stock 128.0 × 88.0 × 43.0 mm", and the status line is the log's suggestion. The view draws the see-through amber box round the part, as wide as the block for the 3 mm left at its top (setup 1's underside), and two grey jaws low on the ±Y sides: one along the part's lower front edge, its long bar running past the box's front-left corner as a jaw 100 mm wide does on a 128 mm box, and the other behind, showing at the right. That the jaws stand on the walls (80.7 mm apart: the Contour's 0.3 mm allowance and the band's 0.01 mm) and not on the box's faces 88 mm apart is pinned by the step's assertions and the log line; the 3.7 mm between them is not told apart on the picture. Nothing is clipped or overlapping, and the strip says the jaws and clamps are checked as boxes. Screen 93, the running planner (step 117): as in the earlier runs but for two tool lines, which are real answers on the smoke document as screen 92's step left it: "Stock before setup 2: 128 × 88 × 43 mm; bottom cut 10 mm by setup 1" and "Vise 1 for setup 2: holds (opens 120, needs 80.07)", the width the vise needs now being that of the walls the draft's Contour finished, not the box's 88; nothing clipped or overlapping. The earlier inspection of 9 October 2026 (before this wave) saw screen 92 with setup 1 only facing: the box 122 × 80 × 43 mm and the jaws on its faces; screen 91 then ended with the amber "OpenAI refused the request; sending it again without its tools."

## Continuous stock and adaptive meshing

- Flat, ball and drill-point cutter implicit fields (`stock_surface::tests::continuous_cutter_fields_match_analytic_swept_solids`) are compared against an independent swept-solid membership calculation for horizontal strokes, ascending/descending ramps and vertical entries. Tests include finite flute extent and rotated/translated setup frames.
- Adaptive octree / manifold dual contouring produces triangle surfaces directly from continuous cutter history; the old occupancy grid no longer supplies rendered voxel faces. Tests verify finite vertices and two incident triangles per undirected mesh edge for the cylindrical cavity, ball-bottom cavity and sloped-channel fixtures.
- A 3 mm cylindrical wall fixture verifies radius error below 0.01 mm at Standard detail, improved accuracy over Draft, and flat floor positions. Its surface vertex count remains below 1% of the corresponding 256³ virtual domain. This is fixture evidence, not a general machining tolerance guarantee.
- A ball-bottom fixture checks spherical radius within 0.03 mm at Draft detail. A ramp fixture checks the analytic inclined cut surface within 0.02 mm and verifies a closed mesh.
- Cutter history is shared between snapshots. Consecutive collinear playback fragments merge into one continuous sweep; adding a new cut does not mutate an inherited snapshot.
- Background result tests reject surfaces from before a rewind, operation edit or detail change. Orbit/pan/zoom reuse the current mesh. While a newer snapshot is being meshed, the simulation strip says so at normal size, "Updating the stock surface · 12 s, showing the last finished one", with the elapsed time, in place of the cell size and triangle count on the Detail line under the timeline; the details are back once the result is in (`smoke::tests::the_simulation_strip_says_the_stock_surface_is_updating_while_the_mesher_runs`, #83). During Play the strip's heading and the 3D view's hint line under the scene stay at the same height, at 1600 × 1100 and 1180 × 760, while the stock is meshed, through an update of 150 ms that says nothing, one of 12 s that is named on the Detail line, its result, and the next update, named at once after a slow one until one is quick again (`smoke::tests::the_view_stands_still_while_play_updates_the_stock_surface`, LIO-222).
- The workflow footer's 02 Operations and 03 Preview only navigate: 02 keeps the operation last selected (the first only when none is) and leaves a generated preview alone, and 03 shows the preview strip of the selected operation (the first when none is) without generating; Generate & play in the strip is what generates (`smoke::tests::the_footers_operations_and_preview_steps_navigate_without_selecting_or_generating`, #86). One name per destination and action is used in the labels, the help cards, the status lines and this README: MANUFACTURE for the workspace, NC review for the last stage, and Assign to current setup (`help::tests` check the NC review page in the speed and feed cards; `smoke::tests` the page title).
- The completed Fine pocket smoke used about 24,000 stored sweeps and 148,672 triangles at a 0.248 mm finest cell, taking about 2 seconds to mesh. The larger cumulative job reached about 48,000 sweeps; Standard updates took about 1.7–1.9 seconds. These are local measurements, not latency guarantees.
- Pocket completion, close-up Fine and Draft, rotated camera, Facing, Parallel, Waterline, inside/outside Contour, Drilling, flipped setup and restart screens were visually inspected. Flat faces, smooth pocket walls and tool-radius corners are visible. Fine sampling can still miss thinner features, and meshes are not certified collision/gouge results.
- Removed-volume readouts intentionally remain approximate: a separate bounded coarse occupancy grid supplies them. No dense fine-resolution volume is allocated for rendering.

## Cumulative operations and setup changes

- Pocket's starting stock exactly matches completed Facing stock. Restart returns to that inherited baseline; reverse scrub and incremental/direct playback agree.
- The next setup retains the complete prior cut history and empty occupancy cells after a 180° flip and translation. Its nominal stock offsets do not refill cavities. Frame roundtrips and finite-flute checks pass.
- Editing an earlier operation rebuilds downstream stock. Suppression skips the operation; an upstream generation failure blocks dependent previews with an explanation. Saved JSON regenerates the same baseline. Temporary stock bounds are not persisted.
- Setup reorder/delete relinks inherited stock sources. The first setup defines the initial blank; subsequent setups carry the same material. Deleting a setup leaves the next setup's notes as typed and says in the status line which setup it now takes its stock from, or that it starts from fresh stock (`model::tests::removing_a_setup_keeps_the_next_setups_notes`, `model::tests::removing_a_setup_reports_the_stock_source_change`).
- A renderer job with seven operations in the first setup and underside Facing in a flipped second setup verifies inherited start, partial removal, completion and restart. The whole-job report checks every enabled operation. The timeline remains per operation. New cap-coverage planning skips fully cleared top levels; general leftover-region rest machining is still unavailable.

## Automatic cutting start after earlier operations

- Facing → Pocket produces fewer depth levels and shorter estimated time, with the same completed stock occupancy as the original full-height path. Original XY clearance is retained; vertical approaches stop above the remaining top. The generated Pocket smoke shows Cutting start Z 43.43 mm and six levels from the faced stock.
- Tests verify partial facing keeps high regions, local coverage can lower a smaller region, finite flute lengths cannot erase an untouched cap, and a thin uncut strip cannot be ignored. Deliberately incorrect occupancy grids do not influence planning.
- Face bottom allowance, upstream edits, suppression and operation reorder rebuild the start. A flipped setup does not mistake the previous top for its new top. Temporary coverage state is not saved in project JSON.
- Outside Contour, drilling pecks and Waterline additionally check fewer levels, unchanged final targets, finite continuous motion and direct/incremental stock consistency after known cap removal.
- The optimization uses constant-height flat-cutter sweeps. Ramps, ball tools, partial regions and uncertain transformed boundaries conservatively retain passes when coverage cannot be proved.

## Operation coverage

Seven imported-mesh strategies have bounded generators: Facing, Adaptive clearing, Pocket, inside/outside Contour, circular-hole Drilling, Parallel and Waterline. Scallop, continuous Rotary and simultaneous 5-axis explicitly report unavailable; Indexed is a setup now (LIO-189). Legacy unsupported operations remain inspectable; unavailable new operations cannot be added to imported jobs.

- Facing: depth/spacing/feed-derived motion, continuous segments and times, clearance, known-volume removal, one-way/zigzag, alternating layer starts, keep-down/retract links and CW/CCW × Climb/Conventional. A depth from the cutting start to the target that is not less than the tool's stickout is refused, as the other kernels refuse it, instead of being simulated with the holder in the stock (`simulation::tests::a_facing_deeper_than_the_tool_stickout_is_refused`: 40 mm of stock above the target with a 35 mm stickout is refused, 34 mm generates).
- Pocket: actual selected-floor union, islands/disjoint regions, sampled wall/cutter clearance, outside/island preservation, floor depth, allowances, profile-ramp angle, direction reversal and bounded generation. Between loops the tool stays down: on the island fixture with a profile ramp and a finish pass, it enters fewer than half as often as it cuts loops, and every feed move (the travel along the loop just cut and each step across) keeps the cutter inside the walls and off the island, removing the same volume (`pocket::tests::a_profile_ramp_steps_over_at_depth_clear_of_the_walls_and_the_island`); a 70 × 70 mm floor with a 3 × 3 grid of 14 mm islands 5 mm apart, a Ø3 cutter and a 2° ramp generates with fewer entries than two thirds of its loops and leaves each island standing (`pocket::tests::a_plate_of_square_islands_ramps_in_at_2_degrees_without_ramping_every_ring`); the route goes back along the loop just cut the shorter way round (15 mm, not 25) and steps to the next loop's nearest point, goes another way round or not at all when a no-go area lies on the step, and none when the step is longer than 1.5 stepovers or leaves the allowed area (`pocket::tests::the_tool_stays_down_by_the_shortest_way_clear_of_islands_or_not_at_all`); a loop is cut only after the loops of the next ring inward beside it, each area finished (with its finish pass) before the next begins (`pocket::tests::inner_rings_come_before_the_ring_around_them`). The TOTEM keyboard's right case top (`examples/totem-case-top-right.step`, CERN-OHL-P, see `THIRD_PARTY.md`), pocketed to the plate 6.85 mm down between its 17 key openings with a Ø3 cutter, 40 % stepover, 2 mm stepdown, a 2° ramp, 0.3/0.2 mm allowances and a finish pass, generates in under 20,000 moves over 4 levels with fewer entries than two thirds of its loops (11 of 19 a level), and at every 0.25 mm of every feed move below the stock top, more than 10,000 points, the cutter's centre stays its radius plus the 0.3 mm allowance from whatever of each face of the part stands above the floor (`pocket::tests::the_totem_keyboard_case_top_pockets_at_2_degrees_without_touching_the_part`; mutation-checked: with the step's wall and island test removed and a 40-stepover reach, a step passes 1.73 mm from a face and the test fails). On the left case top, the same job exceeded 30,000 moves at about 63 min before the tool stayed down and now generates in 17,603 moves and about 24 min; the user's whole job on the right case top (Facing, Contour, Pocket) passes the plan check at a 2° and a 5° ramp and with Plunge (measured by hand, not tests).
- Adaptive clearing: the same selected floors, walls and islands as Pocket, cleared along a path that holds the cutter's radial engagement near the optimal load. Tests check that the walls and islands are never cut past the stock to leave; that the engagement replayed along the path in 0.05 mm pieces, at each move's feed, stays near the load: the 95th percentile at most 1.5 times it and no piece over 2.5 times it (the wall pass slows its feed in corners; a notch narrower than twice the cutter is slotted, as with Pocket); that pockets with a notch of any width in a wall generate; that the volume cleared is within 4% of the volume to clear; that an open side is entered without a helix; that a smaller cutter after a larger one (rest) cuts only the corners the larger one left; and that on a real STEP part, a keyboard case plate, the pocket is cleared at full depth without touching the part, with a Ø3 and a Ø2 cutter; and that the example block's pocket cleared adaptively posts to G-code and replays exactly. Measured on the ring test pocket (Ø2, 0.2 mm load): engagement 99th percentile 1.6 times the load, cycle 279 s against Pocket's 88 s at 10% stepover, whose corners take about ten times its stepover. The editor checks the optimal load (2% to 50% of the tool diameter), the minimum cutting radius (0 to the tool radius), the Helix or Plunge entry and the helix ramp angle (0.1° to 15°) with the generator's own messages.
- Contour: actual projected outside silhouette with radius/allowance compensation and final repeats; Inside follows only selected walls/islands. Tests distinguish inside contour from pocket clearing and verify floor targets and loop winding.
- Drilling: detected circular openings, counterbore opening versus narrowest bore, diameter filtering/rejection, four cycles, pecks/dwell, breakthrough below a hole that goes through, depth and travel order. A blind hole (the part goes on under its floor) is refused by name with Through = Yes, whatever the breakthrough, and drilled to its floor and no deeper with Through = No; the open cup whose floor is its lowest point counts as going through (`cam::tests::a_blind_hole_is_not_drilled_past_its_floor`). A blind hole whose floor is a 118°, 100° or 90° drill-point cone or has a 0.5 mm bottom fillet is detected at its full Ø8 bore, with its depth to the floor's lowest point, and a Ø7.95 drill is accepted; a Ø5 bore 3 mm deep under the floor, a Ø1 pin 2 mm off the centre, ledges 1 mm tall either side of a 2 mm channel across the floor and a blind Ø11 counterbore over a drilled Ø6.6 bore still narrow the drill to Ø5, Ø3, Ø2 and Ø6.6, and a drill 0.5 mm larger is refused (`cam::tests::a_blind_holes_drill_point_or_fillet_floor_does_not_narrow_its_bore`). The drill's sweep is its shape, a cylinder over the cone of its 118° point, so a blind hole's drill-point floor is left as it is (#155, above).
- Parallel: analytic flat/ball drop-cutter checks on plane interiors and edges, direction, spacing, slopes, allowances, translated geometry and bounded sample counts. This remains approximate surface finishing.
- Waterline: constant-Z projected contours, radial allowance, direction, layer limits and flat-cutter vertical access. Levels are the stepdown apart, and closer over a sloping face so that neighbouring loops lie at most the maximum stepover (`surface_stepover`) apart across it, with a level 0.01 mm under the top of each run of sloping faces; faces flatter than 1° are left. On a 21° wedge with a Ø4 flat mill, a 2 mm stepdown and a 0.5 mm stepover, the levels over the ramp are under 0.193 mm apart and the simulated stock leaves under 0.4 mm over it, while a 100 mm stepover (the old fixed levels) leaves ridges over 1.5 mm (`cam::tests::waterline_levels_close_up_over_a_gentle_slope`). On the owner's TOTEM aluminum bottom (left), the saved Waterline over the 21° ramp under the case (Ø3 flat mill, 2 mm stepdown, 0.5 mm stepover) went from 3 cutting levels, with stock 3 mm above about 61% of the ramp's sampled area, to 23 levels; with no stock to leave, none of 90 samples keeps 0.5 mm, and a denser check of 4,140 samples finds about 0.9 mm² still under taller stock, all within 0.04 mm of the wall above the ramp (the cutter's 0.02 mm clearance from the part), and with its 0.3 mm the only samples keeping more are within that allowance of the wall above the ramp. A new Waterline starts with a 1 mm stepdown and no stock to leave. Ball waterline and slope filtering remain unavailable.
- The preview polygon kernel's ±1,000,000 mm coordinate limit is checked on the placed part as well as on the stock box and the tool: a fixed-size box stays at the origin while the part follows the setup's translation, so the part's bounds alone can be out of range. The hole list refuses by itself for the Drill geometry list and the part report, which ask for it without the generator (`simulation::tests::a_part_translated_beyond_the_coordinate_limit_is_refused_in_a_fixed_box`: a fixed box with tx = 1e20 is refused with the limit message for all six strategies and by `cam::holes`, whose stock box is in range, and at tx = 0 the limit message is gone).
- All four newer strategies run on both packaged STEP and 3MF fixtures. Application tests cover creation, compatible tools, geometry, tabs, generation, playback, rewind, edit invalidation, saved parameters and whole-job failure reporting.

## Imports, rendering and existing workflows

- Native STEP reads the four-setup block as 120 × 80 × 40 mm (4,220 triangles, measured; the test needs more than 1,000 and runs only when the STEP helper is built). Tests cover source-unit conversion, translated/rotated assembly components and malformed input.
- Native 3MF reads the matching block as 120 × 80 × 40 mm (10,676 triangles, measured; the test needs more than 1,000). Tests cover package roots/units, multiple build instances, nested transforms, reflections, external Production references, bad indices, missing objects, cycles, singular/non-finite transforms and unsupported required extensions. Each import limit has a test one past it with the exact refusal message: `three_mf::tests::more_than_4096_package_entries_are_refused` (4,097 entries; 4,096 load), `more_than_64_levels_of_component_nesting_are_refused` (65 levels; 64 load), `more_than_128_model_parts_are_refused` (the root and 128 parts; the root and 127 load), `more_than_200000_triangles_are_refused` (200,001 in one mesh, and 100,001 instanced twice for the expanded count), `inflated_parts_count_as_read_not_as_declared` (the 100 MB expanded XML limit, counted as it inflates), and `mesh::tests::a_file_over_50_mb_is_refused_before_it_is_read` (a sparse 50,000,001-byte file). The STEP helper's two-minute timeout is tested through `step::convert` with a shell script as the stand-in converter and a 0.3 s limit (Unix only): `step::tests::a_converter_that_outlives_the_timeout_is_killed`, `a_converter_that_fails_without_a_message_is_reported_by_its_exit_status` and `a_converter_that_fails_with_a_message_is_reported_by_it`; none needs the real helper. The expanded-XML limit counts the bytes as the parts inflate, not the sizes the archive declares, and only the parts the root relationship reaches are read: a 2,420-byte archive whose four entries each declare 1 byte, with three model parts that inflate to 400 KB each (1.2 MB in all), loads under a 2 MB limit and is refused with the 100 MB message once the bytes read pass a 1 MB limit (`inflated_parts_count_as_read_not_as_declared`; the app's limit is 100 MB).
- Imported geometry selection stays attached to the real mesh and its fingerprint. Tests cover planar grouping, occlusion, placement, stale selection, replacement and isolation from sample geometry.
- Complete triangle rendering and 512-pixel image tiles with filtering gutters avoid the earlier async image-upload holes. Tests reconstruct the raster byte-for-byte and verify upload-size bounds. All 24 motion samples contain the model. Camera movement leaves fit scale/target stable.
- The 3D view's boxes (the stock, the jaws and the clamps) share one pair of nearest-surface buffers per frame, a depth `f32` and a tone `u8` per pixel, reset for each box, where each box allocated its own `f32` and `usize` pair; the pixels do not change (`viewport::tests::the_viewport_draws_the_same_pixels_after_the_split` keeps its digest, `viewport::tests::transparent_stock_preserves_part_occlusion_and_alpha` and `viewport::tests::workholding_is_drawn_as_solid_grey_blocks`).
- The help animations' clock is reduced to 60 s in f64 before it is cast to f32, so it keeps its precision after days of uptime. 60 s is a whole number of the 5 s phase, of the spindle's turns and of the chips' 41 px travel; the spindle's 2 rad/s became 19 turns in 60 s (1.99 rad/s) and the chips' 10 px/s 15 travels in 60 s (10.25 px/s), so nothing jumps at the wrap (`help::tests::the_animations_repeat_every_period_so_the_wrapped_clock_never_jumps`: the clock and the phase at t and t + 60 s, after six days of uptime, are the same, and the two speeds make whole turns and travels in the period).
- The browser's stock thumbnail of a meshed stock is cached by the job's fingerprint (`LeftStock::key`) with the setup, the end and the jaws, not by the stock's address, which a later stock can take once the old one is freed: a new `LeftStock` for an edited job whose volumes sit at the old address draws its own picture, and the same job asks for the same one (`smoke::tests::a_new_left_stock_result_does_not_reuse_the_picture_of_an_earlier_job`).
- Stock offsets, transparency, fixed/relative mode, rotation/translation, invalid parameters and legacy migration are covered. Hover descriptions and animated concept cards use the shared native renderer.
- Blank startup, explicit example loading, import before setup, first machine/tool/setup, last setup removal, project archive/recovery, atomic persistence, independent profiles, tool deletion protection, planner apply/undo and setup-sheet export remain covered.
- Explore example keeps the user's library without duplicates: from a legacy workspace (the user's own tool and the example's three tools from an earlier Explore example, all without numbers, and the example's machines without postprocessor settings), the example ends with four tools numbered T1–T4, the example's tools keeping the numbers their copies were given on load, with no number issues, three machine profiles and a job that validates, and the status names only the user's own tool; New project keeps that library. With a user library numbered T1–T3, the sample tools get T4–T6, and a second Explore example adds nothing.
- An unreadable `workspace.json` is moved aside to `workspace-unreadable-<id>.json` (kept byte for byte, its place named in the status), the recovery workspace is saved as `workspace.json`, and the next start resumes it without a new recovery file (`smoke::tests::an_unreadable_workspace_is_set_aside_and_the_recovery_workspace_is_resumed`).
- The tool editor's draft on an empty library has the next tool number, so the first Save is accepted.
- Closing: the workspace is saved first, and the window stays when it cannot be. A pending sign-out, sign-in save or refresh of the sign-in (from before its request until after its save) is waited for, at most 10 seconds (a repeated request keeps the first time; the window closes when signing out ends, when the sign-in save reports, saved or not, when the refresh ends, checked on Tick, or when the time is up). A sign-out still pending at the end of the wait starts deleting the saved sign-in on the blocking pool before the window closes, through the signing-out client so that its refresh cannot save the sign-in again; a delete started just before a Tokio runtime is dropped still completes. With nothing pending and a plan stage, the NC review check or the stock mesher running, the decision is to end the process, never while anything above is pending, and never while a G-code export runs (counted from Export until it reports, also when no folder is chosen): then the window closes. The app's close decision is one method that the close request carries out, tested case by case: a sign-out pending for 11 s gives delete-and-close, a sign-in save pending for 11 s gives close, an export with blocking work gives close and blocking work alone gives exit, and a refresh in flight gives wait when first asked and 9.5 s later, and close 10.5 s after the first request. After the wait, a refresh still waiting for OpenAI no longer counts as pending, so with blocking work the decision is exit; a refresh in its save is still pending, so with blocking work the decision is close and the save finishes. The delete a timed-out sign-out starts is one method of the app, run within the close request's update, which iced runs inside its Tokio runtime: in a Tokio test, with a signing-out client's refresh held on a /token answer delayed 300 ms, the method takes the client and starts the delete, the refresh then finishes and the store stays empty (checked in review: with the store's plain delete in its place, the test fails with the refreshed sign-in saved again). A close during a refresh shows "Closing when the sign-in refresh has finished (at most 10 seconds)…" and closes on the Tick after it ends. The close request's own call to that method is pinned too: in both timed-out close tests (a sign-out pending alone, and one whose client has a refresh in flight) a sign-in saved before the Close is gone once the entered runtime is dropped after the Tick, which joins the blocking delete (checked in review: with the call replaced by dropping the signing-out client, both tests fail with the sign-in still saved). The process exit itself is not run in a unit test, and neither is the process's lifetime after the wait: a sign-out or sign-in save still pending with blocking work gives close, so the window closes and the app ends when the plan stage, NC review check or stock mesher finishes, as the README says. Found in review (not a test): Cmd+Q (Quit in the macOS app menu) sends AppKit's terminate, which winit handles as the end of its event loop and Iced 0.14 does not pass to the app as a close request, so it skips this decision; the README says that only closing the window waits, and that Cmd+Q does not wait for a sign-out, a sign-in save or refresh, or a G-code export, may lose edits from the last moment and may leave an unfinished sign-out signed in at the next start.
- Save project writes the document when the file is chosen, not when the dialog opened: an edit made in between is in the file, the status names the file, a path that cannot be written reports "Save failed" and leaves the job unsaved, and a cancelled dialog changes nothing (`smoke::tests::an_edit_made_while_the_save_dialog_is_open_is_in_the_saved_file`).
- A failing automatic save of the workspace (a path that cannot be written) sets the status once, not again while the error stays the same, shows "Workspace not saved: …" in the header instead of "Saving workspace…", names a different error when it changes, and a later successful save clears it (`smoke::tests::a_failing_workspace_save_is_said_once_and_shown_in_the_header_until_it_works`).

## Selected-option help

- All shared choice controls pass the displayed value and current fields into their help. Legacy fallback values therefore match what the user sees. Tests verify method help/animation updates with CW/CCW, One way/Both ways, and selected entry, linking, cycle and containment options.
- Option-specific text covers operation, setup, machine, tool-type, coolant, postprocessor setting, playback-speed, visibility and stock-detail choices. Tests distinguish saved preferences from implemented functionality and check relative/fixed stock and detail-level explanations.
- Seven additional shared-renderer galleries cover Facing pattern/links, milling method with spindle context, ramp/plunge entry, all drilling cycles, three containment modes, stock modes/detail and spindle rotation. These exercise the same cards used by hover overlays.
- Native stock-detail verification exposed a tooltip obscuring the old drop-down. Detail choices were changed to three visible buttons; each button now explains its own resolution and cost.

Fixture-clearance follow-up: the setup field now uses a dedicated side-gap animation instead of the operation-height illustration. The shared field-help gallery was rendered and inspected, and the actual hover overlay was verified in the running app. `--help-only` renders this gallery in an isolated workspace.

## Packaging

The bundle is built from this source with `scripts/package-macos.sh`. Rebuild a bundle from the current source before using it: older bundles can drop the material and workholding libraries when they save. The last bundle check (4 October 2026, commit 06dbfd5) is recorded here and was not repeated since: the script bundled the STEP reader and 27 libraries (freetype 2.14.3, libpng 1.6.58, Open CASCADE 7.9.3, TBB 2023.1.0) and its deep/strict signature check passed; the app ZIP was extracted into a clean temporary folder, passed the same signature check, and its executable matches the bundle's.

The script builds the release binary, bundles the STEP helper with the non-system libraries it needs, the CAD resources, the icon and the license notices, signs the bundle ad hoc in a temporary staging directory and verifies its deep/strict signature, creates the ZIP when `AXIS_PACKAGE_ZIP` is set, and then replaces the destination bundle with it (the old one is removed, not merged into) and verifies the deep/strict signature of the bundle at the destination. The script refuses a host that is not Apple Silicon macOS and an `OCCT_PREFIX` outside `/opt/homebrew` unless `AXIS_PACKAGE_ALLOW_UNSUPPORTED=1`, and bundles the STEP helper that cargo reports for that build (`AXIS_BUILT_STEP_HELPER`), not the newest file found. This change to the script was checked with `bash -n` and `py_compile` only; it has not been run on a Mac yet. The earlier bundle had 27 relocated non-system dylibs; Fidget's MPL-2.0 license/source notice accompanied the unmodified dependency, and the Clipper and CAD notices were included.

Cargo still reports a future-compatibility notice in transitive `block 0.1.6` from native dialogs; builds and tests pass.

## Native project and final archive checks

Except where **Packaging** says otherwise, these checks were made with the earlier bundle and were not repeated for the G-code export and the AI planner.

- The rebuilt adaptive-stock core resumed the user's existing `AXIS_FourSetup_Block.3mf`. Pocket generated with stock after one earlier Facing operation. Live playback advanced, Pause worked, and forward scrubbing showed the machined pocket. The existing project, stock dimensions and machine/tool libraries were retained. In the follow-up, the user's current two-operation Facing/Pocket project is the workspace being preserved.
- Selected-option cards were inspected through the shared Iced renderer. After the Mac was unlocked, the corrected fixture-clearance tooltip was also verified as a live native overlay.
- The final app ZIP was extracted into a clean temporary directory. Deep/strict code-signature verification passed; its executable matches the installed bundle, and Fidget/Clipper license notices are present.
- Saved workspace JSON is compared with the pre-update backup. Source and application archives are regenerated for this update. The final remaining-stock build could not be reopened during the locked-Mac check; shared-renderer checks and package verification are completed independently.

## Library editors and the browser's layout (#87, #78)

- Library editors (#87): Tools, Materials and Workholding share one layout (`ui::library_editor`): the fields scroll, and under them, outside the scroll, are the unsaved-draft note and the Save and Delete buttons. `each_library_page_builds_with_save_and_delete_fixed_under_the_scrolling_fields` builds each page's widget tree, for the saved entries and for new drafts (a new tool, material, vise, clamp and an entry of another kind), and checks that it has two scrollables (the list and the fields) and, outside them, exactly three buttons: Back, Save and Delete. Before, Materials and Workholding had only Back outside the scroll.
- Labels: `a_workholding_entry_saves_and_deletes_under_its_own_name` checks "Save vise" / "Delete vise", "Save clamp" / "Delete clamp" and "Save entry" / "Delete entry"; Tools keeps "Save tool" (or "Save and continue to …") and Materials "Save material".
- Unsaved note: `a_library_editor_says_when_its_draft_is_not_what_the_library_holds` checks that a draft equal to the library entry has no note, an edit shows "Unsaved changes to this tool/material/vise", Save clears it, and a new material or clamp says it "is not in the library yet". Every message, validation and action of the pages is unchanged.
- Browser (#78): the "N setups" count and its rule are above the scrolling tree, and the tree has a 14 point right gutter for the 10 point scrollbar, which Iced draws over the content. Marks are in a 22 point column aligned right. Iced 0.14 text has no ellipsis, so names are cut to 20 (operations) and 14 (setups) characters with "…" (`ui::ellipsize`), the row is clipped as a net for wide letters, and the hover help still names the operation in full. `the_browser_builds_with_a_long_operation_and_setup_name_and_keeps_its_count_fixed` checks the cut (a name of 80 characters, a multi-byte name, a short name left alone) and that the browser's tree, with a long operation and setup name, has one scrollable and no button outside it.
- The Linux renderer's screenshots are unreliable here, so the look of the gutter and the marks column was reasoned from Iced 0.14's layout, not inspected; check it on the next renderer run.

## Closing during a STEP import, the Open CASCADE rebuild, foreign model paths and 3MF entry names (#171, #172, #173, #174)

- Closing during an import (#171): the running converter is registered in `step::RUNNING` (a `Mutex<Vec<Child>>`, a list rather than one slot because imports can overlap, as the tests do) from its start until it has exited, and the close path (`CloseAction::Exit`) calls `step::kill_running()` before `exit(0)`, which kills and reaps every registered converter; the waiting `load` then returns "STEP import cancelled: the application is closing." `step::tests::killing_the_running_converters_ends_the_child_and_cancels_the_import` (Unix, the `/bin/sh` stand-in running `exec sleep 60`, in a registry of its own) waits until the child is registered, calls `kill_all`, and checks the import's error, that the registry is empty and, with `kill -0`, that the child process no longer exists. The existing timeout test now passes through the same registry. Not covered: the temporary directory of an import that was cut short by `exit` is not removed (it holds only the converter's output and error text), and the close path itself was not run in a window.
- Open CASCADE upgrade (#172): `build.rs` now emits `rerun-if-changed` for `<prefix>/include/opencascade/Standard_Version.hxx` and `<prefix>/lib`, and `rerun-if-env-changed=CXX`. No test: it is cargo's rebuild decision, which a unit test cannot observe; the lines were read in the build script, and the build ran with them.
- Model path saved on the other OS family (#173): `model::path_json` reads a text, a byte array and a UTF-16 unit array on every platform; the array of the other family decodes lossily to a path that names no file. `storage::tests::a_model_path_saved_as_bytes_or_utf16_units_loads_on_every_platform` (no `cfg`) loads a project whose model path is a byte array holding a non-UTF-8 byte, and one holding a UTF-16 surrogate, and checks that the title, the model's name, size and format and the setups are kept.
- 3MF entry names (#174): `three_mf::read` skips an entry whose name `package_path` refuses instead of refusing the file; a reference to such a name is still refused where it is resolved. `three_mf::tests::entries_with_names_that_are_no_package_path_are_skipped` loads a valid 3MF with an extra `Metadata/my%20thumb.png` and the same with `:`, `?` and `#` in the name. Before, each failed with "Unsupported 3MF package URI".
- Linux (16 October 2026, Open CASCADE present): `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean, and `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=6` 945 passed, 0 failed.

## Soft limits on some axes, the check while the machine moves, a refused Cycle Start (#164, #167, #168)

- **Soft limits on only some axes (#164).** `Connection::soft_limits_configured` meant the driver's "any axis has both limits", so a profile limiting X and Y with Z's `soft_min` cleared showed "Soft limits on: X 0…390, Y 0…280 mm." in green while Z ran into its ends. It now means X, Y and Z all have both limits, as `MachineView::envelope` does, and `Connection::unlimited_axes` names the others. `ui::limits_off_warning` takes those axes instead of the flag: with all three it keeps the "This profile sets no soft limits" box, with some it says "Z has no soft limits in this profile, so nothing stops it at the end of its travel. Jog slowly and watch the machine." and "Add soft_min and soft_max for Z to the profile to turn them on.", and under that, when axes with limits still have no position, the machine-position sentences for those axes alone. `a_profile_with_no_soft_limits_gets_the_same_amber_warning_and_an_assumed_box` is extended with the LIMITED profile less Z's limits, connected: the driver's `soft_limits_configured` is true, the connection's false with `unlimited_axes` = [Z], the box reads as above (one axis, two axes, with X and Y unset too), and machine zero on X and Y leaves the amber box since the green line needs all three. Against the old code the extension cannot pass: the connection's `soft_limits_configured` was true for that profile, and the warning took a flag, not the axes.
- **The check while the machine moves (#167).** The Tick's recheck was gated on the edits pausing, the Control page, `stale` and no tool run running, not on `App::locked`, and `Run(Setup)` and `Run(Check)` pass `while_locked`, so `run::programs` simulated the whole job on the blocking pool during a jog, a probe or homing, against `locked`'s stated invariant. Now the Tick's recheck also needs `!locked()`, and `Run(Setup)` and `Run(Check)` (and Set coolant to Off's recheck) only mark the run `stale` while locked (`App::check_waits`), so the Tick checks it once the machine stops; the Run panel and `Run::problems` say "The setup is checked once the machine stops and the edits pause." for a run marked and not yet checked, and the "project changed" caution appears only for a run that was checked. `the_setup_check_waits_until_the_machine_stops`: on the simulated FAST machine with the checked fixture setup, a held jog; Check again returns an empty task and leaves the epoch, the programs and `stale`; 300 ms of ticks start nothing; picking the setup again returns an empty task with the waiting message; released, the tick that finds the machine Idle starts the check (a new epoch, no programs, not stale) and its answer lists the two tool runs. Before the change the test fails at Check again's task.
- **A Cycle Start the driver refuses (#168).** `Run::cycle_start` recorded `xy`, `zero`, `last` and cleared `touched` before any outcome, so a job the driver refused ("<name>: not started, the machine is not idle", sent in the few milliseconds after a work or machine zero while the driver re-anchors) counted as the first tool run: the "Work zero on the stock" steps, the touch-off button and Move there vanished and the X/Y-changed refusal armed, though nothing ran. `Running` now keeps what the start recorded over, and `Run::refresh` puts it back on an outcome that is not ok and says "not started"; a Stop, also not ok, keeps the record, since the tool run did cut. `a_cycle_start_the_driver_refuses_has_not_started`: zeroed and checked, a slow MDI move is sent and Cycle Start follows before any tick, so the app sees Idle and the driver refuses the job with "not started, the machine is not idle"; the run has no tool run done, is not started and shows the steps; the X work zero moved to where the machine stopped raises no "X/Y work zero changed"; a Cycle Start the driver takes starts the setup and hides the steps, and a Stop ends it not ok with the setup still started. Before the change the test fails at `started()` after the refusal.
- Not inspected in the real window: checked with tests only.
- Linux, 16 October 2026, rebased on a58c34d (#171 to #174), several other builds sharing the machine: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=6`: **957 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored** in 359 seconds (955 at main plus the two tests above; the three tests of this change passed alone first).

## The probe routines fly over the workholding, keep their setup, and set nothing after an E-stop (#163, #165, #166)

- Found in review (issue #163): between faces, Probe corner, Probe edge and Probe centre rose to 10 mm above the stock top and rapided straight across it, over lines the workholding check never looked at (it checked the columns beside the sides and over the top's spot only, up to 10 mm above the top). A clamp bar on the stock top more than 10 mm proud lay under the traverse, which is a plain rapid that the probe input does not stop.
- Now `post::Checked::holds` carries the setup's jaws and clamps (the boxes `zero_check` saw) to the routine, which travels at `post::probe_height`: `corner_area` above the stock top or above the tallest box, whichever is higher, bounded by the top of Z (`control::Edges::high`, also for the final retreat). When that bound would take a level traverse within 1 mm over a box, the tip's radius allowed for sideways, the routine stops before the move and names the box and the top of Z (`control::Edges::blocked`, checked in `edges_step` as each face is planned). `post::in_the_way` checks the columns up to that height and the line between every two faces' approach points at it (`post::segment_crosses`).
- `control::tests::a_clamp_bar_on_the_stock_top_is_flown_over_or_refused`: the 124 × 84 × 40 mm stock with a 30 × 24 mm toe clamp on its +X −Y corner, under the +X to −Y diagonal of Probe centre and clear of every column. 20 mm proud (10 mm more than the clearance), the routine's rise is 30 mm, every traverse is at the top of Z, 10 mm over the clamp, and the zero is found from one touch per face; the harness (`touch_box_round`) fails any level traverse whose tip would run into a clamp at or below its height. 35 mm proud, the clamp's top 5 mm above the top of Z, the routine refuses before the traverse to the −Y side with "cannot pass over the clamp on +X (offset 0), whose top is at machine Z 5.000 … Z ends at 0.000". Without the clamps, as before the change, the harness catches the traverse 10 mm above the top running into the clamp.
- `post::tests::a_jaw_or_clamp_in_the_probes_way_is_named` adds: `probe_height` is 50 mm for the bare 40 mm stock, 65 mm with a bar from 50 to 55 mm overhanging the +X side, which is now named for Stock top center (the column used to stop at 50 mm); the toe clamp of the test above is clear of every column and of the way at 70 mm, so the probe is offered, and `segment_crosses` finds its diagonal.
- Found in review (issue #165): the Run strip's setup buttons and `Run(Setup)` refused only a tool run, so another setup picked while a probe was under way took the probe's result: the work zero from the old setup's geometry, marked touched off on the new setup's panel.
- Now `control::Connection::setup_locked` (a tool run or a probe under way) greys the strip's setup buttons and makes the handler do nothing. `smoke::tests::picking_another_setup_in_the_run_strip_waits_for_the_probe`, through the simulated UC100: a copy of the first setup on the same machine, Probe corner on the first; the second picked while it probes leaves the run on the first and the probe running; it ends ok, the first setup is touched off at the block's front-left corner; then the second can be picked, untouched.
- Found in review (issue #166): `ended` accepted an ok outcome in any state but Run, Hold and Stopping. An E-stop between the probe's end and the next tick leaves the ok outcome in place (the driver has no program to abort) with the state E-stop; the app then sent `SetWork` and the retract, which the driver drops outside Idle and paused, and reported "Work Z set" with the run touched off.
- Now `ended` takes an ok outcome only Idle or paused; in any other state it ends the probe not ok with "The machine was E-stopped as the probe ended, so nothing is set: probe again." (a failed outcome keeps the driver's words), and `probe_done` and `edges_done` check `manual()` once more before `SetWork`. `control::tests::ended_takes_an_ok_outcome_only_idle_or_paused` (snapshots set by hand: Idle and paused ok with the probe's result, Run, Hold and Stopping not ended yet, E-stop, Jog and disconnected not ok, an older outcome not the probe's) and `control::tests::a_probe_that_ended_into_an_e_stop_sets_nothing`, through the simulated UC100: Probe Z onto the block from 10 mm above it; the driver's snapshot is watched directly, not by a tick, until the probe's ok outcome and trip are in and the machine is Idle, an E-stop is sent, then the tick: the probe ends not ok with that message, and after Reset the work offset is unchanged.
- A window of the driver's loop (4 ms) remains between the tick's snapshot and its `SetWork`: an E-stop the driver processes in it drops the set while the report stands. Closing it needs the next tick to confirm the offset the driver shows; not done here.
- Not inspected in the real window: checked with tests only.
- Linux, 16 October 2026, rebased on 6cf9a48, several other builds sharing the machine: `cargo fmt` changed nothing, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=6`: **991 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored** in 346 seconds; `scripts/check-validation-names.sh` exits 0. At a0f795b, before the last rebase, which touched none of the probe code, with the four rules disabled in place (`probe_height` ignoring the boxes, `Edges::blocked` always None, `setup_locked` without the probe, `ended` taking an ok outcome in any state and the `manual` guards off), the five tests above ran alone: 0 passed, 5 failed, each at the assertion its rule carries.

## Hole sizes read at the tessellation's vertices, and sharper drill-point floors (#154, #157)

- **#154.** `cam::holes` measured a bore's corridor to the chords of its tessellation, whose midpoints lie a chord's sagitta R(1 − cos(π/n)) inside the circle, so the STEP reader's 42-segment circles read small: Ø6.6 as 6.582, Ø6 as 5.983, Ø5 as 4.986, Ø11 as 10.969 and Ø18 as 17.952, the Ø6.6 drill was refused ("exceeds an opening Ø6.58") and the part report told the planner 6.58. Now `Hole` carries the opening's `sagitta` (from the loop's vertex count and its vertex radius, the mean distance of its vertices from the centre of a least-squares circle through them; the loop's box centre is off by up to half a sagitta with the polygon's phase, which showed as a 15.98 reading of a Ø16 bore); when the corridor is set by a chord whose triangle's own vertices lie within that sagitta past it, the bore is reported at those vertices; and `drill` accepts a drill up to the reported diameter plus twice the sagitta plus the 0.01 mm it allowed before (the report rounds to 0.01). A boss, a ledge or a smaller bore below still narrows the bore to its nearest point, since its triangle's vertices are not a sagitta past that point.
  - `cam::tests::a_step_bores_nominal_drill_is_accepted_and_the_bore_reads_its_nominal_size`: the STEP example turned over, its bottom plane selected: the four Ø6.6 bores read 6.60 (opening 6.60, 40 mm, through), a Ø6.6 drill with the 6.6 filter generates 4 holes and a Ø7.1 drill is refused; from the top, under their Ø11 counterbores, the same bores read 6.60 and take the Ø6.6 drill. Fails before the change on the 6.58 reading and the refused drill.
  - `analysis::tests::the_step_example_reports_its_bores_at_their_nominal_size`: the part report on the STEP example: the top plane's four through holes are Ø6.6 with Ø11 openings and 40 mm deep, its blind hole Ø6, the bottom plane's four Ø6.6 bores are Ø6.6, and every hole of every orientation reads a size that is a multiple of 0.1 mm.
  - The existing fixtures in `cam::tests::a_blind_holes_drill_point_or_fillet_floor_does_not_narrow_its_bore` (a Ø5 bore under the Ø8 bore, a Ø1 pin 2 mm off the centre, ledges either side of a 2 mm channel, a Ø6.6 bore under a Ø11 counterbore) still read their obstruction's size within 0.02 mm and refuse a drill 0.5 mm over it.
- **#157.** The clip that takes a blind hole's floor for what a 118° point leaves had a fixed slope of tan 31°, so a floor sharper than that crossed the clip within 0.1 mm of the centre and the hole was dropped ("No unobstructed circular holes…") or read as Ø0.4 to Ø1.1.
