Now the clip slope is the steeper of tan 31° and the slope of the floor's own cone at the apex: the gradient of the triangles that contain the centre at the floor's height, taken only when the floor has a vertex at the centre (a cone's apex), so a tilted floor plane keeps the 118° clip. The fan triangle's plane gradient is the cone's slope over cos(π/n), which is exactly the slope that puts the whole fan and the rings beyond it under the clip.
  - `cam::tests::a_blind_holes_drill_point_or_fillet_floor_does_not_narrow_its_bore` gains a 90° point (floor slope 1.0, apex 4 mm under the bore's wall) and a 100° point (0.84): each is detected at Ø8 with its opening Ø8 and its depth to the apex, a Ø7.95 drill is accepted and a Ø8.5 drill is refused. Both fail before the change with "No unobstructed circular holes…".
  - The drill of a sharper floor still stops with its point at the apex, so a 118° drill cuts the flanks of such a floor; the README says so.
- Linux, 16 October 2026, rebased on 0eb9623 (#218), several other builds sharing the machine: `cargo fmt` changed nothing, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=6`: **993 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored** in 355 seconds (991 at main plus the two tests above; the three tests named ran alone first), `scripts/check-validation-names.sh` exits 0. Before the rebase, at 6860dc6, the same three commands were clean with 944 passed, and the three tests failed before the change as described.

## Workholding, review round 2: a skim under an earlier cut's floor, the contact from where the stock stands, the suggestion with the blend (#158, #159, #162)

These checks are safety-relevant: a false pass of the cutting-force check can crash a machine, so each change keeps the conservative direction, and each has a test that fails before it.

- **#158, a skim over a surface an earlier cut left.** After an earlier operation faced to a height T, the highest voxel centre left lies anywhere from a hair to a whole voxel under T, so the material from that voxel's cube up to T is in voxels the facing emptied. A later pass with its tip in that layer removes real material, sweeps no centre, and the #60 probe, lowered by half a voxel, found nothing: the run was filtered as in the air, the operation had no bite, and the holding line read "no cutting force sideways or up to hold against". Now `forces::bites` grows the probe by a whole voxel (`thick`, the voxel's longest side) all round: the tip lowered by a voxel, the radius and the flute top a voxel out, which reaches the last centre kept from any tip above such a floor or beside such a wall. The skin estimate stays a voxel thick, and the original stock faces, half a voxel above the outermost centres, are reached as before.
  - `forces::tests::a_skim_under_the_floor_an_earlier_cut_left_still_pushes`, on a 300 × 60 × 40 mm stock whose voxels are 3.125 mm long and 3.08 mm tall: an earlier facing to a floor 0.8 voxel above the row of centres at 32.31 mm (34.77 mm); then a Facing in 1.5 mm layers from the box's top with a Ø10 cutter at 1000 mm/min, whose passes at 38.5, 37 and 35.5 mm are in the air and whose pass at 34 mm takes 0.77 mm under the floor, sweeping no centre. Only that pass bites, as a skin 3.125 mm thick and 10 mm wide (521 mm³/s, less than its 6 mm slot below the box's top), its force 3 mm above its tip. In steel with 3 flutes at 8000 rpm on a 5 kW spindle that is 524 N tangential, 786 N sideways; the starter vise gripping 3 mm holds it ×0.5 against tipping, and the issue reads "Finish: the cut could tip the stock out of the vise: about 786 N, 34 mm above the top of the jaws' contact, which is 2.9 mm high and 98 mm long (×0.5, under the ×2 wanted). …". Before the fix the test fails at its first assertion: no bites.
  - The #60 tests are unchanged: the 0.3 mm skim of the original top is still exactly its slot, the same move beside the stock is still in the air, and the fixture job's Facing skim is still refused at grip 1 (`forces::tests::a_skim_thinner_than_a_voxel_still_pushes_and_a_shallow_grip_refuses_it`, `post::tests::a_skim_too_thin_for_the_voxels_is_still_held_against_and_a_1_mm_grip_refuses_it`).
  - Cost, by construction: a finishing pass over a floor the same operation cut, or a pass up to a voxel in the air over an earlier cut's floor, can now count as a skin (at most the slot) where before it counted nothing in about half of the cases: the conservative direction.
- **#159, the material to grip and to rest on, from where the stock stands.** #59 placed a later setup's jaws from `Gripped::bottom` (the box's bottom raised past the layer the earlier cuts can have taken off it), but `holding::holdable` still sampled the jaws' contact from the box's bottom and the material to rest on 0.05 mm above it, and `forces::hold` the contact too. In the #59 geometry (a Ø10 ball facing 2 mm deep, which the box keeps; setup 2 turned over) every sample of the bottom lay in the faced layer, all were `Removed::at`, and the setup was refused with "setup 1, operation 1 removed most of the stock's bottom it would rest on", blaming the facing the stock in fact rests on. Now both sample from `held.bottom`: the jaws' contact from there up to the grip above it (at most the stock's top) and the resting bottom 0.05 mm above it; a point below where the stock stands is outside the band, no material, rather than something an earlier operation removed, so no message and no `cause()` blames the facing. When the earlier cuts went through everything between the jaws (`held.bottom` at the stock's top) the sample height is empty and the setup is refused outright: "Setup 2 cannot be held in the vise: earlier setups cut away the material between the jaws up to the stock's top, so nothing stands on the table for them to grip. Choose another holder." The height rule keeps reading the box.
  - `holding::tests::jaws_reach_above_the_layer_a_ball_facing_took_off_the_bottom_a_turned_over_setup_rests_on` now asserts the setup is holdable (no message) at grips of 8 and 3 mm, and the outright refusal with the bottom at the top. Before the fix it fails with the "removed most of the stock's bottom" message.
  - `forces::tests::the_jaws_contact_is_measured_from_where_the_stock_stands`: the same geometry, 500 N sideways 1 mm under the top; the contact is 7.84 mm high (8 mm less the sampling's insets) and 98 mm long, its top 9.92 mm above the box's bottom, 29.08 mm under the push; without the earlier cuts the contact starts at the box's bottom and the push is 31.08 mm above it. Before the fix the contact was 5.6 mm high and its top 7.92 mm above the box's bottom (the lower two rows of samples in the faced layer): conservative, but not the placed jaws.
- **#162, the suggestion and the blend.** `holding::Request` carries `reach`, the setup's gap plus `post::blend` of its machine profile, as `post_check::holding_problems` and the operation preview have counted them since #63; `Request::suggest_on` places the vise or clamps clear by the reach and stores the gap in the layout, and the status line's count of operations within the gap (`App::suggest_layout`, `holding::clashes`) uses the reach too, so what it calls clear is what export finds clear. `plan_check::laid_out` goes through the same request.
  - `holding::tests::the_suggestion_keeps_the_moves_clear_by_the_blend_the_export_adds_to_the_gap`: a Ø6 cutter 10.5 mm up along all four sides of the 100 × 60 × 40 mm stock is 2.5 mm above jaws gripping 8 mm, clear of the 2 mm gap and within it plus a 1 mm blend; the suggestion alone gives grip 8 at a gap of 2 and grip 7 at 3; the request for a setup on a profile with G64 P1 has gap 2 and reach 3, suggests grip 7 with gap 2 stored, counts no clash at the reach, and `hits` (what export runs) finds nothing, so the status reads "Suggested: vise jaws on ±Y, grip 7 mm. Every operation is clear of them."; at grip 8, as the gap alone suggested, the pass is clear of the gap and one clash at the reach, which export refuses. Clamps are placed by the same reach and store the gap, and the default profile's 0.01 mm blend counts (reach 2.01). Before the fix the suggested grip is 8.
- Before the change, with the three fixes reverted and the tests kept, the four tests above fail each for its issue's reason: no bites from the skim under the floor, the rest plane's message blaming the facing, the contact sampled from the box's bottom, and a reach equal to the gap.
- Linux, 16 October 2026, rebased on 138dd32 (#214), several other builds sharing the machine: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=6`: **996 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored** in 295 seconds (the four tests of this change passed alone first, 945 with them on the branch before the rebases).

## Fixture pins (16 October 2026)

Dowel pins in a plate on the table locate a part by holes in it, so that a setup on them, flipped or not, gets its X and Y work offset without probing, and a reference pin acts as the switchless machine's home for X and Y. Driver: UC100-driver at dae4a70 (`Command::SimBlocks`, several simulated probe blocks at once). Only the simulated UC100 was used; nothing moved a real machine.

- Settings: the pin list as X Y pairs (pin 1 first), at least two pins, no two closer than their diameter, diameter 1 to 50 mm and height 1 to 100 mm, both measured with no default; the recorded place of pin 1 (`pin_ref_x`, `pin_ref_y`) is written only by Record the pins' place, and one that does not read is refused; a machine's own issues list what is wrong (`control::tests::pin_settings_read_the_plate_and_refuse_what_does_not_place_a_part`).
- Pin centre and Boss centre: a ball tip of 4 mm on a Ø6 pin 8 mm tall, started on the centre and 1.5 mm and 2.4 mm off it, touches the top, goes out by both radii and 1 mm on each side, down 3 mm as a guarded move, and probes in at the tip's equator, 3 mm below the top: the centre is found to 0.001 mm with the tip's radius cancelling, the diameter across Y through the centre is 6.000 mm and across X the chord where the tip started; a pin 4 mm tall is probed 2 mm down; Boss centre without pins goes out by the maximum probe distance and finds a Ø30 boss (`control::tests::a_boss_or_pin_is_found_from_over_it_with_the_tips_radius`). The plate's squareness: pin 2 0.040 mm off the line over 100 mm is said, 0.070 mm is over 0.05 mm and asks to square the plate, pins along Y are measured off the line in X (`control::tests::squareness_measures_pin_2_off_the_line_from_pin_1`).
- On the simulated UC100 (a JY5800-like profile with a probe input, Ø2 tip, pins Ø6 × 8 mm 100 mm apart): the simulator's pins stand in front of its block; Record the pins' place from 0.6 mm off pin 1 keeps X145 Y45 to 0.01 mm, finds pin 2 on the line and ends over it; an E-stop clears the reference, and after a machine zero declared 1.3 mm and −0.7 mm off, the simulator's pins stand that far off in the new coordinates; Reference X Y from pin 1 declares X and Y moved by −1.300 and +0.700 mm, after which the pins and the tip over pin 1 are back where they were recorded; after another E-stop with only Z declared, Reference declares X and Y where they show, probes, and declares them from the pin; declaring X another way clears the reference (`control::tests::the_pins_place_is_recorded_and_references_x_and_y_again`).
- The holes are the part's own, chosen in the Work zero step (17 October 2026, after the user's "the pins holes should be already part of the original design, in the app we should need to select the holes in the part"): a flipped setup on the pins, about X and about Y, of `examples/dowel-block.3mf` (120 × 80 × 20 mm, two Ø4.1 dowel holes 8 mm deep 80 mm apart along X in its top face; faced and its holes drilled by an ordinary Drill on its top face with a Ø4.1 drill on G54, then flipped and faced on G55; pins Ø4 × 6 mm, 80 mm apart): the part opens exactly those two holes on setup 2's bottom, Ø4.1 and 8 mm deep, on the bottom of the stock it starts from; with both chosen, setup 2's pins are in them, pin 1 in the −X one, and pin 1's spot in work coordinates is its hole's; setup 1 plunges each hole, carried into its frame, at its centre at least 8 mm down; the check finds them drilled and warns of nothing; the pins are two Ø4 × 6 mm holding boxes from the stock's bottom. Without the drilling, setup 2 still exports, warned that no earlier setup drills holes for pin 1 and pin 2 (`post::tests::a_flipped_setup_on_the_pins_takes_x_y_from_the_holes_in_the_parts_design`).
- A hole locates the part only when it fits: a cut wider than the pin by more than 0.2 mm (a pocket, a larger hole) clears it but does not hold it, and at least half the pin's height must be held where no wider cut cleared round it. The first version of this check took setup 1's 15 mm deep Pocket, which a pin at X10 Y0 stood in, and a Ø4 drill through the air above that pocket's floor, for a pin hole; the test now sees that pin warned and the fixture's pins stand clear of the Pocket.
- Refusals: no hole chosen, and a machine without pins, are setup issues; the export refuses one hole for two pins ("Choose one hole for each of the machine's 2 fixture pins: 1 chosen."), a chosen place that is no hole of the part's bottom, and the setup turned onto its side (rx 90), where the holes are no longer on its bottom; pins 70 mm apart for holes 80 mm apart ("…from pin 1 the other pins are at X70 Y0, and from the hole at X… the other holes are at X80 Y0."); Ø5 pins ("is Ø4.1, smaller than the Ø5 pins"), Ø3.5 pins ("opens Ø4.1, 0.6 mm wider than the Ø3.5 pins: up to 0.2 mm over locates the part"), and 9 mm tall pins in the 8 mm deep holes (`post::tests::a_setup_on_the_pins_is_refused_until_its_chosen_holes_fit_them`).
- The pins as workholding: a plunge onto a Ø4 pin 6 mm tall to Z5 hits "the fixture pin 1 at X0.0 Y0.0", as a clamp would; to Z8.5, above the pin and the 2 mm gap, it is clear (`post_check::tests::a_fixture_pin_under_a_toolpath_is_hit_as_a_clamp_is`).
- End to end on the simulated UC100, through the app's messages (`smoke::tests::a_flipped_setup_on_the_pins_runs_with_x_y_from_them_after_an_e_stop`): the pins' place recorded and kept in the machine's profile; off to X1.3 Y0.7, E-stop, Reset and machine zero there; the flipped setup picked: the Run steps say to reference X Y from pin 1 and Cycle Start lists "X and Y are not referenced from pin 1"; Reference declares X and Y back by +1.3 and +0.7 mm; G55 selected (pressed again while the driver re-anchors after the declarations); Cycle Start lists the offset the pins give until Set X Y from the pins makes G55's X/Y offset pin 1's place less pin 1's hole in work coordinates; the part is put on the pins in the simulator once X and Y are referenced; Probe Z sets Z on its top without moving X/Y; no reason is left before Cycle Start, both pins of the setup, from its work coordinates through the offset, land on the machine where the simulator's pins stand, and Cycle Start starts the tool run.
- Pictures: `smoke::tests::render_the_fixture_pins` (ignored; `cargo test -- --ignored`) draws the window headless on tiny-skia to PNG: the Machine library's Fixture pins, the flipped setup's Work zero step with the pins drawn in the 3D view, the drill's pin holes, and the Control page's Run steps after an E-stop, once referenced, and ready. Looked at as a machinist would: the pins stand at the bottom of the flipped stock where X−40 Y−25 and X40 Y−25 from the top-centre zero put them, 6 mm tall; setup 1 drills them at Y+25, the flip about X; the steps after the E-stop name what is missing, Cycle Start is grey; ready, steps 1 to 3 read done and Cycle Start is green. Two things they found were fixed: the pins were hidden under the part (now outlined over the view, labelled Pin 1 and Pin 2, as the work zero's cross is), and the drill's Holes choice was not shown for a drill of pin holes.
- Choosing the holes in the part, in the 3D view (17 October 2026): with the flipped setup's Work zero step open and Pick holes on the part on, a click on a facet of the −X hole's wall chooses it, a click on the face the holes open on chooses nothing and says to click inside a hole, a click on the +X hole's end chooses it and pin 1 goes into the −X hole, a second click on the −X hole removes it, and away from the step or with picking off a click chooses nothing (`smoke::tests::the_work_zero_step_picks_the_parts_holes_for_the_pins_in_the_3d_view`). Rendered (`render_the_fixture_pins`, now also `2a-work-zero-choose-holes.png` and `3-drill-dowel-holes.png`) and looked at: before choosing, the two holes are ringed at the flipped part's bottom as Hole 1 (X−40 Y−25) and Hole 2 (X40 Y−25), as listed, each Ø4.1, 8 mm deep; chosen, Hole 1 reads pin 1 and Hole 2 pin 2 and the pins are drawn in them; setup 1's Drill of its top face finds the same two holes at X−40 Y25 and X40 Y25, Ø4.10, 8 mm deep, blind (the flip about X takes Y25 to Y−25).
- Not validated: the real JY5800 with a real probe and plate; the probe's trip position and overtravel on a pin (the simulated probe is a point, so the simulator gives a pin's diameter less the tip's); a plate not square to the machine beyond the warning (the pins are placed square to X and Y).
- Linux, 16 October 2026, on `t3/fixture-pins` with main (f85235d) merged in: `cargo test --locked -- --test-threads=6` gave **1024 passed, 0 failed, 1 ignored** (the pictures above) in 318 seconds, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt --check` clean, `scripts/check-validation-names.sh` exits 0.
- Linux, 17 October 2026, holes from the part's design: `cargo test --bin axis-cam` gave **1026 passed, 0 failed, 1 ignored** in 171 seconds, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt` applied.

## The JY5800's probe input (17 October 2026)

The owner wired a touch plate's lead to the control box's 對刀器 AUTO-CHECK posts (red: signal, black: common) and asked whether the signal reaches the computer. Read with the driver's `uc100 diagnose inputs` (dae4a70) on the real UC100 CD9090D3, firmware 1041, host E-stop held throughout; nothing moved.

- Before the posts were wired, pins 10, 11, 12, 13 and 15 read HIGH and no pin changed over 3 minutes.
- Wired, with the posts shorted, pin 13 read LOW and the others HIGH. Over 2 minutes of shorting and opening the posts by hand, pin 13 changed 14 times (HIGH open, LOW shorted) and pins 10, 11, 12 and 15 none. Some changes came 5 to 80 ms apart: contact bounce of a wire held by hand.
- `assets/jy5800.toml` gets `probe = { pin = 13, active_low = true }` under `[inputs]`, with the measurement; the owner's copy the app uses is changed the same way. `uc100 check-config` accepts it.
- Renderer smoke, macOS (Apple Silicon, 2× scale), `--smoke-test` exit 0: screen 98, the owner's simulated JY5800, lists pin 13 as the probe in the Inputs panel; screen 99 offers Probe Z refused only for the plate ("Touch plate thickness is not set: measure the plate and set it in the Machine library.") and the feature probes for the tip diameter, where on 16 October every probe said "The UC100 profile has no probe input".
- macOS, `cargo test --release`: **1034 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored**.
- Not validated: any probe that moves the real machine (LIO-126): the trip position, the overtravel at the probe feed, and how the controller takes the contact bounce. The plate's thickness is not measured yet.

## The project's material decides the touch plate (17 October 2026)

After the probe input was found, the owner: "we also need to add the probe thickness, if the material is not conductive", and X and Y are zeroed by hand. A touch plate kit has two leads: the clip stays on the tool, and the probe trips when the tool touches whatever the plate's lead is on, so that must conduct. Asked, the owner chose that the material decide on its own, and that the side probes be refused on non-metal. Only the simulated UC100 was used; nothing moved a real machine.

- A material conducts when its group is Aluminium, Steel, Stainless steel or Brass and copper (`material::CONDUCTIVE`). The app gives the connection the project's material after every message.
- On a metal, Probe Z, Probe reference and Probe Z from reference touch the stock itself: no plate thickness is needed or added. On wood, plastic, Other or with no material they touch the plate and add its thickness, as before, and wait for it to be set.
- Through the simulated UC100 (FAST with the probe on pin 13, no plate set): on wood, Probe Z says "Touch plate thickness is not set"; on Aluminium 6061-T6 it is offered, touches the block's top at machine Z −40, and work Z there becomes 0 (offset −40), saying "Work Z set: the surface touched is Z 0.000.", then the tool lifts 5 mm (`probe_z_on_metal_touches_the_stock_without_the_plate`). The plate tests without a material are unchanged (`probe_z_sets_work_z_at_the_plate_and_lifts`).
- Probe corner, Probe edge, Probe centre and every feature at the tip are refused without a material ("The project has no material: choose a metal one to probe the stock, or probe Z on the touch plate and zero X and Y by hand.") and on wood ("MDF does not conduct, so the probe cannot touch the stock: probe Z on the touch plate and zero X and Y by hand."), and offered on aluminium (`probing_the_edges_needs_a_stock_zero_a_tip_and_its_work_system`). Pin centre and the fixture pins are not refused: the pins are steel. The tests that probe the stock's sides now choose Aluminium 6061-T6.
- Pictures: `render_the_probes_by_material` (ignored) draws the Control page with a 10 mm plate on MDF and on Aluminium 6061-T6. On MDF, Probe Z reads "MDF does not conduct: touch plate on the stock top under the tool, the clip on the tool. … Work Z at the plate's top becomes 10 mm, the stock top Z 0". Probe corner and the feature buttons are greyed with the refusal. The Run panel's reference line names the touch plate, and the work-zero guide replaces its "…, Probe corner under Work zero does 3 and 4." line with the refusal and "With the touch plate on the stock top, Probe Z under Work zero sets Z0." On aluminium, Probe Z reads "Aluminium 6061-T6 conducts: no plate. The clip stays on the tool and the plate's lead goes on bare metal of the stock; touch the tool to the stock by hand first and see the probe input go active.", Probe corner is offered, and the guide reads "With the plate's lead on the stock and the clip on the tool, Probe corner under Work zero does 3 and 4." Found in review: the first wording, "probe clipped onto the stock", could be read as moving the tool's clip to the stock, which leaves the tool out of the circuit, so the probe never trips and the tool feeds into the stock the whole probe distance. Every text now names both leads.
- Renderer smoke, macOS, `--smoke-test` exit 0: screen 99, whose project is Aluminium 6061-T6, offers Probe Z with no plate set.
- macOS: `cargo test --release` gave **1035 passed, 0 failed, 4 ignored**. `cargo clippy --all-targets --release -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` are clean.
- Not validated: on the real JY5800, the plate's lead on metal stock, and the plate on wood. Anodised or painted metal does not conduct on its face: the README says the tool must touch bare metal.

## The manual mill: Box, the Steps page and what is not checked (17 October 2026, branch `t3/manual-milling-planning`)

Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-manual-milling-design.md`. Phase 1 only: you mark the manual work yourself. This section records the checks that exist as tests and, below them, what nothing checks. It records no test count; the full suite is run when the branch is reviewed.

**Checked by a test.**

- **The machine.** Only a machine of kind Manual is manual, and a machine with no kind is a CNC (`manual::tests::only_a_machine_of_kind_manual_is_manual`). The gears parse from a list or a range, and a variable drive rounds to ten rpm. A manual machine needs its gears, hand speeds and times (`manual::tests::a_manual_machine_needs_its_gears_hand_speeds_and_times`), and saving it sets its rpm to the top gear and its feed to the hand feed (`smoke::tests::saving_a_manual_machine_settles_its_rpm_and_feed`). A tool or holder with no machines fits CNC machines and no manual machine; the editors' check boxes edit that list.
- **What may run where.** Only Box and Drill on a manual machine, and Box not on a CNC (`model::tests::only_box_and_drill_are_allowed_on_a_manual_machine`, `model::tests::box_is_refused_on_a_cnc_machine`). A tool must fit the operation's machine (`model::tests::a_tool_must_fit_the_operations_machine`). The rpm must be a gear, and the feed at most the hand feed (`model::tests::the_spindle_speed_of_a_manual_operation_must_be_a_gear`, `model::tests::a_manual_operation_feeds_no_faster_than_the_hand_feed`). The box's sides must be in order, and its allowance, stepover and stepdown are checked.
- **Box paths.** Every move of a Box and of a Drill on the manual mill changes one axis (`cam::tests::manual_box_and_drill_plans_move_one_axis_at_a_time`). A pass stops at the allowance and cuts conventionally with M3 (`cam::tests::a_step_stops_at_the_allowance_and_cuts_conventionally`). Levels and rows are equal and never thin, and a box over the whole top faces it in one level. A closed pocket plunges at a quarter of the feed onto its floor plus the allowance (`cam::tests::a_closed_pocket_plunges_at_a_quarter_feed_onto_its_floor_and_allowance`). A box that reaches into the part is refused (`cam::tests::a_box_into_the_part_is_refused`).
- **Simulation.** A faced top on the manual mill lowers the next setup's stock (`cam::tests::a_faced_top_on_the_manual_mill_lowers_the_next_setups_stock`).
- **From selected face.** A floor grows to the stock's edges, a wall gives a step box open on its normal side, and a wall on the stock's edge, several faces and a slanted, curved or downward face are refused (`manual::tests`, the `a_floor…`, `a_wall…` and `a_slanted…` tests; `smoke::tests::the_box_tab_fills_its_fields_from_the_picked_face`).
- **Setups.** A manual setup put first takes what the old first setup had (`model::tests::a_manual_setup_put_first_takes_what_the_old_first_setup_had`), and a second one goes before the first CNC setup.
- **No G-code, and the minutes.** A manual setup makes no program and leaves the CNC programs as they were (`post::tests::a_manual_setup_makes_no_program_and_leaves_the_cnc_programs_but_their_air`). A job of manual setups alone has nothing to export. The setup sheet and the NC page name the mill (`model::tests::the_setup_sheet_names_the_manual_mill_and_points_to_the_steps`, `smoke::tests::the_nc_page_says_a_manual_setup_has_no_gcode`). The Run strip lists no manual setup (`ui::tests::the_run_strip_lists_no_manual_setup`). A manual setup still gets the generation and holding checks (`plan_check::tests::a_manual_setup_still_gets_its_generation_and_holding_problems`), and the plan check counts manual, CNC and moving minutes apart (`plan_check::tests::a_manual_setup_has_no_export_problem_and_its_minutes_are_counted_apart`). The stock's move time is added once for each change of machine (`manual::tests::the_stock_moves_between_machines_cost_the_manual_mills_move_time`).
- **The guide.** One section per operation between Set up and Hand over (`guide::tests::one_section_per_operation_between_set_up_and_hand_over`). A facing box is one step and a thin one-wall slab is one Square step (`guide::tests::steps_run_hold_zero_then_each_operation_and_a_face_is_one_step`, `guide::tests::a_squaring_box_is_one_square_step_cut_to_the_wall`). The targets replay the plan's moves in order (`guide::tests::targets_replay_the_plans_moves_in_order`). With the DRO's compensation on, X and Y read the tool edge on the compensation side, which is the centre plus or minus the radius (`guide::tests::x_and_y_read_the_tool_edge_on_the_comp_side_with_the_dros_compensation`). The holes are centres and depths in work coordinates, and a change of compensation sides alone is a Comp step. A pass shows the handwheel turns for its feed. The place is saved with the project and kept within the steps (`guide::tests::the_place_is_saved_with_the_project_and_kept_within_the_steps`).
- **The page.** The Face screen has the tool, the gear and the depth and no passes (`steps::tests::the_face_step_shows_the_tool_the_gear_and_the_depth_and_no_passes`). A pass lists its readouts in order, and Next and Back move the place and save it without an undo step. A section opens at its first unfinished step. The page shows without a controller and works while the CNC jogs (`smoke::tests::the_steps_page_works_while_the_cnc_jogs`), and screen 100 of the smoke shows it.

**Inspected, not asserted.** `steps::tests::render_the_steps` (ignored) draws one PNG for each kind of step at 1280 × 800 into `/tmp/manual-steps`. No test compares them; they are for looking at.

**Not checked.**

- No cut has been made on the real big mill from these steps. The conventional direction, the edge numbers with the DRO's compensation and the plunge of a closed pocket are checked against the app's own simulation and by reading the numbers, not at a machine. Whether the DRO's compensation sign and the app's agree on the real DRO is untested.
- The app does not read the DRO, so nothing checks that the machine is where a step says. A readout tapped lights the next number and proves nothing.
- The default gears, hand feed, hand rapid, handwheel mm per turn and the times are placeholders. The minutes are not compared with a stopwatch.
- The automatic stepdown (what the spindle's power takes) is a model, not measured on the mill. Backlash is not modelled.
- The holding check uses the library's vise sizes; the big mill's vise has not been entered or tried. A quill depth stop is not modelled.
- Old project files with no machine kind read as CNC by `is_manual`; no test opens a saved older project to show it.
- The step pictures were not tested on Windows or macOS. The planner (`proposal.rs`) does not know the manual machine, so the AI planner makes no manual setups. Suggested boxes and the PNG pages are not built.

## The manual lathe: turning, chucks and the dial step guide (17 October 2026, branch `t3/lathe-planning`, LIO-175)

Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-manual-lathe-design.md`; plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-17-manual-lathe-phase-1.md`. Phase 1 only: you add and size the Turn operations yourself. This section records the checks that exist as tests and, below them, what nothing checks. It records no test count; the full suite is run when the branch is reviewed.

**Checked by a test.**

- **The machine.** Only the mill is manual, only the lathe is a lathe, and both are hand machines that make no program (`lathe::tests::only_the_mill_is_manual_only_the_lathe_is_a_lathe_and_both_are_hand`). The defaults are valid and saving settles the rpm on the top gear (`lathe::tests::the_defaults_are_valid_and_settle_the_rpm_on_the_top_gear`). The dial numbers, divisions, speeds, feeds, pitches and `cross_reads` are checked (`lathe::tests::the_issues_of_a_lathe`). The wizard keeps the name and power when the kind becomes Lathe and saves the dial check's answer (`smoke::tests::choosing_the_lathe_kind_keeps_the_name_and_power_and_the_dial_answer_is_saved`); saving settles the rpm and the travel, refuses bad dials, and the library card says "Manual lathe" (`smoke::tests::saving_a_lathe_settles_its_rpm_and_travel_and_refuses_bad_dials_and_the_library_card_says_so`).
- **Dial readings.** Taking 6.00 off the diameter on a dial of 4 per turn is 1 turn and 2.00 on a diameter dial and 0 turns and 3.00 on a radius dial (`lathe::tests::six_off_the_diameter_is_a_turn_and_two_on_a_diameter_dial_and_three_on_a_radius_dial`). An unknown cross-slide dial gives no number and the other slides do (`lathe::tests::an_unknown_cross_slide_dial_gives_no_number_but_the_other_slides_do`). The carriage (65 mm is 3 turns and 5.0 on a dial of 20 per turn), the quill and the top slide convert with their own dials (`lathe::tests::the_carriage_quill_and_top_slide_convert_with_their_own_dials`). A reading is rounded to the dial's division and carries into the turns (`lathe::tests::a_reading_is_rounded_to_the_dials_division_and_carries_into_the_turns`). A negative or unusable distance or dial is refused (`lathe::tests::a_negative_or_unusable_distance_or_dial_is_refused`).
- **Round bar.** A round bar resolves to its box on the setup axis, and the relative bar from the part's largest radius (`stock::tests::a_round_bar_resolves_to_its_box_on_the_setup_axis`). The voxels of a round bar are inside its cylinder (`volume::tests::a_round_bar_has_material_only_inside_its_cylinder`). A revolve cut removes exactly its ring in its own frame (`volume::tests::a_revolve_ring_removes_exactly_its_ring_in_its_own_frame`, `stock_surface::tests::a_revolve_field_is_its_ring_in_its_own_frame`). The meshed surface of a bar is its cylinder and end faces (`stock_surface::tests::a_round_bar_meshes_to_its_cylinder_and_end_faces`), and the 3D view calls those faces stock and the turned ones cut (`viewport::tests::a_round_bars_cylinder_and_ends_are_block_and_its_turned_faces_cut`).
- **Chucks.** The starters are a 3-jaw and a 4-jaw with "Measure yours" notes (`holding::tests::the_chuck_starters_are_3_and_4_jaw_with_measure_yours_notes`), a chuck needs 3 or 4 jaws, a grip range, a jaw length and a body (`holding::tests::a_chuck_needs_3_or_4_jaws_a_grip_range_a_jaw_length_and_a_body`), and a chuck fits lathes only (`holding::tests::a_chuck_fits_lathes_only_and_a_vise_or_clamp_does_not_fit_one`). A lathe is refused a vise or clamps and a mill a chuck (`model::tests::a_chuck_setup_on_a_lathe_is_held_and_vise_or_clamps_are_refused_there`, `model::tests::a_chuck_is_refused_on_a_mill_and_a_chuck_entry_is_not_on_one`). The grip, stickout, range, swing and bar length are checked (`holding::tests::a_chuck_holds_a_bar_inside_its_range_grip_stickout_swing_and_length`); a stickout over four diameters is a warning and not an issue (`holding::tests::a_stickout_over_four_diameters_is_a_warning_not_an_issue`). A cut inside the grip is a problem, and a short stickout says how much to leave (`holding::tests::a_turn_inside_the_grip_is_a_cut_in_the_chuck_and_a_short_stickout_says_how_much_to_leave`). The chuck body is a box behind the jaws' end (`holding::tests::the_chuck_body_is_a_box_behind_the_jaws_end_about_the_axis`) and a move into it is refused (`holding::tests::a_move_into_the_jaws_is_refused_as_the_tool_hitting_the_chuck_body`). The mill's hit logic is unchanged (`holding::tests::the_mill_hit_logic_is_unchanged_for_a_plan_that_is_not_a_lathes`). Suggest chooses the 3-jaw for round work that fits and the 4-jaw, with "4-jaw: change the chuck", otherwise (`holding::tests::suggest_chooses_the_3_jaw_for_round_work_that_fits_and_the_4_jaw_with_the_chip_otherwise`). A library gains the chuck starters only when it has none and an older one gains none (`model::tests::the_library_starts_with_a_vise_a_clamp_and_two_chucks_and_an_old_one_gains_none`).
- **Lathe tools.** Choosing a tool type adds its defaults and keeps what was typed (`model::tests::choosing_a_tool_type_adds_its_defaults_and_keeps_what_was_typed`). A threading tool has a 55 or 60 degree form and a pitch range (`model::tests::a_threading_tool_has_a_55_or_60_degree_form_and_a_pitch_range`); boring bars, grooving tools and parting blades need their sizes (`model::tests::boring_bars_grooving_tools_and_parting_blades_need_their_sizes`). A lathe tool fits lathes only (`model::tests::a_lathe_tool_fits_lathes_only_unless_it_lists_machines_and_a_mill_tool_is_unchanged`) and a milling operation refuses one (`model::tests::a_milling_operation_refuses_a_lathe_tool`). The tool editor shows the fields of each type and none of a mill's (`smoke::tests::the_tool_editor_shows_the_fields_of_each_lathe_tool_type_and_no_mill_ones`).
- **What may run where.** Turn is a strategy with its name, help and tabs (`model::tests::turn_is_the_thirteenth_strategy_with_its_name_help_and_tabs`), refused on a mill, and a mill strategy is refused on a lathe (`model::tests::a_turn_is_refused_on_a_mill_and_a_mill_strategy_on_a_lathe`). A new Turn takes the defaults of its kind, and changing the kind brings its fields (`model::tests::a_new_turn_is_an_outside_turn_with_the_defaults_of_its_kind`, `model::tests::changing_the_kind_brings_its_fields_and_keeps_a_name_the_user_gave`). Each kind checks its numbers (`model::tests::each_turn_kind_checks_its_numbers`); a Turn needs the tool type of its kind (`model::tests::a_turn_needs_the_tool_type_of_its_kind`), a bore a bar that fits and reaches (`model::tests::a_bore_enters_with_a_bar_that_fits_and_reaches`), a groove its tool's width or more (`model::tests::a_groove_is_at_least_as_wide_as_its_tool`) and a thread a pitch the gearbox cuts and the tool covers (`model::tests::a_thread_pitch_is_one_the_gearbox_cuts_and_the_tool_covers`). The spindle speed is one of the gears (`model::tests::a_turn_spindle_speed_is_one_of_the_lathes_gears`). A Turn is checked against the bar it starts from (`model::tests::a_turn_is_checked_against_the_bar_it_starts_from`). A lathe Drill goes in on the centre to a depth and a drill off the lathe keeps selecting holes (`model::tests::a_drill_on_a_lathe_goes_in_on_the_centre_to_a_depth`, `model::tests::a_drill_off_the_lathe_keeps_selecting_holes`; `smoke::tests::a_lathe_drill_has_a_centre_tab_and_no_hole_selection`). The ribbon enables only Turn and Drill on a lathe setup (`smoke::tests::the_ribbon_has_a_lathe_group_and_enables_only_turn_and_drill_on_a_lathe_setup`), and the Turn editor has the kind chooser and the fields of each kind (`smoke::tests::the_turn_editor_has_a_kind_chooser_and_the_fields_of_each_kind`).
- **Pass rules.** The passes are equal, none deeper than the depth, and the finish pass comes last (`turn::tests::steps_are_equal_and_no_larger_than_the_depth_and_the_finish_pass_comes_last`, `turn::tests::an_outside_turn_takes_equal_passes_to_the_diameter_then_the_finish_pass`). The power feed is the machine's nearest to 0.1 mm per revolution, the finer of two as near (`turn::tests::the_power_feed_is_the_machines_nearest_to_a_tenth_and_the_finer_of_two`). A second Outside starts from the turned diameter and cuts no air (`turn::tests::an_outside_after_an_outside_starts_from_the_turned_diameter_and_cuts_no_air`), and a bore after a drill widens the hole in equal passes (`turn::tests::a_bore_after_a_drill_widens_the_hole_in_equal_passes_to_the_diameter`). A thread feeds in on the infeed table to its depth at the pitch (`turn::tests::a_thread_feeds_in_on_the_infeed_table_to_its_depth_at_the_pitch`). A Face, a Groove, a Part off, a Chamfer and the Drill on the centre are tested one by one (`turn::tests::a_face_takes_its_cut_off_the_end_as_it_is`, `turn::tests::a_groove_at_z_minus_20_is_cut_20_mm_from_the_free_end`, `turn::tests::parting_off_goes_past_the_centre_and_the_bar_left_in_the_chuck_goes`, `turn::tests::a_chamfer_breaks_the_end_its_hole_or_the_first_shoulder`, `turn::tests::the_drill_on_the_centre_goes_its_depth_at_full_diameter_and_its_point_beyond`). Z 0 is the free end, which is the Stock top center work zero (`turn::tests::work_z_is_zero_at_the_free_end_which_is_the_stock_top_center_work_zero`). A ring into the part is refused and one clear of it passes (`turn::tests::the_part_check_refuses_a_turn_into_the_part_and_passes_one_clear_of_it`), and no ring reaches into the part or its allowance (`turn::tests::rings_never_reach_into_the_part_or_its_allowance`). Turning and a lathe's centre drilling are planned by the lathe and not by the mill's generators (`cam::tests::turning_and_a_lathes_centre_drilling_are_planned_by_the_lathe`), and all fifteen strategies have a contract (`cam::tests::all_fifteen_strategies_have_an_explicit_executable_or_unavailable_contract`).
- **Lathe and mill.** A second lathe setup, turned end for end, starts from the first one's cuts (`turn::tests::a_second_lathe_setup_turned_end_for_end_starts_from_the_first_ones_cuts`), and a mill setup after a lathe setup starts from the turned diameter (`turn::tests::a_lathe_then_mill_job_hands_the_mill_the_turned_diameter`). A mill setup turned square to a lathe starts from the turned bar (`rest::tests::a_mill_setup_turned_square_to_a_lathe_starts_from_the_turned_bar`). **+ Add lathe setup** puts a round bar in the 3-jaw first, takes the old first setup's orientation, goes after the hand setups and before the CNC ones, and needs a lathe and a fitting chuck (`model::tests::a_lathe_setup_put_first_is_a_round_bar_in_the_three_jaw_chuck`, `model::tests::a_later_lathe_setup_goes_before_the_cnc_setups_and_after_the_hand_setups`, `model::tests::a_lathe_setup_takes_the_chuck_the_library_has_and_needs_a_lathe_and_a_fitting_chuck`; `smoke::tests::add_lathe_setup_shows_with_a_lathe_and_inserts_the_setup`).
- **No G-code, and the minutes.** A lathe setup makes no program and leaves the CNC programs as they were (`post::tests::a_lathe_setup_makes_no_program_and_leaves_the_cnc_programs_as_they_were`), and has no run, offset or CNC minutes (`smoke::tests::a_lathe_setup_is_hand_work_with_no_program_run_offset_or_cnc_minutes`). A clean lathe job has no problems at any stage and its minutes are the lathe's (`plan_check::tests::a_clean_lathe_job_has_no_problems_at_any_stage_and_its_minutes_are_the_lathes`); it gets the chuck's workholding problems and nothing about export (`plan_check::tests::a_lathe_job_gets_the_chucks_workholding_problems_and_nothing_about_export`). The split line names the lathe, the manual mill and the CNC and leaves out hand parts with no minutes (`plan_check::tests::the_split_names_the_lathe_the_manual_mill_and_the_cnc_and_leaves_out_hand_parts_with_no_minutes`), and a lathe before a CNC setup moves the stock once (`plan_check::tests::a_lathe_before_a_cnc_setup_is_counted_as_the_lathe_and_moves_the_stock_once`).
- **The guide.** The sample part's steps run Set up, each operation and Hand over (`guide::tests::the_sample_parts_steps_run_set_up_each_operation_and_hand_over`). The outside passes read off the touched diameter and stop from the new end (`guide::tests::the_outside_passes_read_off_the_touched_diameter_and_stop_from_the_new_end`), and a new tool or moved slides zero the carriage again on the end (`guide::tests::a_new_tool_or_moved_slides_zero_the_carriage_again_on_the_end`). Every dial number is `lathe::dial` of its distance (`guide::tests::every_dial_number_is_lathe_dial_of_its_distance`) and a radius dial reads half what a diameter dial reads for the same pass (`guide::tests::a_radius_dial_reads_half_what_a_diameter_dial_reads_for_the_same_pass`). The dial check comes first and only while the cross dial is not known (`guide::tests::the_dial_check_comes_first_only_while_the_cross_dial_is_not_known`). A measured diameter puts what is left on the last pass (`guide::tests::the_measured_diameter_puts_what_is_left_on_the_last_pass`), a bore reads out from the hole and measures the other way (`guide::tests::a_bore_reads_out_from_the_hole_and_its_measure_the_other_way`), the tailstock drill reads its depth and point on the quill (`guide::tests::the_tailstock_drill_reads_its_depth_and_point_on_the_quill`), a thread feeds the top slide at 29.5 degrees by the infeed over its cosine (`guide::tests::a_thread_feeds_the_top_slide_at_29_5_degrees_by_the_infeed_over_its_cosine`), and a chamfer feeds the top slide while one on a shoulder reads the carriage (`guide::tests::a_chamfer_feeds_the_top_slide_and_a_shoulder_one_reads_the_carriage`). The measurements and the place survive saving and loading, and a project saved without measurements loads with none (`guide::tests::the_measurements_and_the_place_survive_save_and_load`). A lathe setup needs an operation and a chuck (`guide::tests::a_lathe_setup_needs_an_operation_and_a_chuck`).
- **The page.** Each lathe screen shows its dials and short labels (`steps::tests::each_lathe_screen_shows_its_dials_and_short_labels`). Until the dial check is answered the cross slide shows no number (`steps::tests::until_the_dial_check_is_answered_the_cross_slide_shows_no_number`); the answer is saved to the lathe in the library and in its setups, and the numbers show (`steps::tests::the_dial_check_answer_is_saved_to_the_lathe_and_its_setups_and_the_numbers_show`). A measured diameter sets the finish pass's reading (`steps::tests::the_measured_diameter_sets_the_finish_pass_reading`). The page works with no controller and while the CNC jogs (`smoke::tests::the_lathe_steps_work_while_the_cnc_jogs`), and screen 101 of the smoke shows it (`smoke::tests::screen_101_shows_the_dial_steps_of_a_lathe_setup_put_first`).
- **A CNC machine from the lathe steps.** A CNC machine chosen on the lathe's Steps page shows its Control page, and "Manual lathe steps" goes back (`steps::tests::a_cnc_machine_on_the_lathe_steps_page_shows_its_control_page_and_back`).
- **Old projects.** An older library with entries but no chuck gains none (above) and a setup without holding keys is None with no issues (`model::tests::a_setup_without_holding_keys_is_none_and_has_no_issues`), and a project saved without measurements loads (above). No test opens a saved pre-lathe project file.

**Inspected, not asserted.** `steps::tests::render_the_lathe_steps` (ignored) draws one PNG for each kind of lathe step at 1280 × 800 into `/tmp/lathe-steps`. No test compares them; they were looked at by eye.

**Not checked.**

- **The thread form is not simulated.** A thread leaves a plain cylinder at the pitch diameter; its flank angles, crest and root are not drawn or cut, and the part check asks only that the part keep inside the major diameter (outside) or outside the minor (inside). Whether the infeed table gives a good thread in your material is untested.
- **Dial rounding to a division.** A dial reading is rounded to the dial's division, so a pass can end up to half a division away from the planned diameter (0.02 mm on the default cross-slide dial, whose division is 0.04 mm, and twice that on the diameter when the dial reads the radius). The Measure step before the finish pass is what takes that out; no test cuts a bar to see how the roundings add up. Backlash, spring of the work and wear of the tool are not modelled.
- **No real cut has been made** on the lathe from these steps. The dial check's two answers, the carriage and top slide readings, the 29.5 degree top slide for threads and the half-nuts rule are checked against the app's own simulation and by reading the numbers, not at a machine.
- **The dials are not read.** The app does not know where a slide is. A readout tapped lights the next number and proves nothing; a wrong zero is a wrong cut.
- **The default dial settings, speeds, feeds, pitches, swing, centres and the times are placeholders** (a small bench lathe's), not your lathe's. The chuck starters' sizes are typical, not measured. The minutes are not compared with a stopwatch, and the feed of a hand-fed cut is assumed to be 0.05 mm per revolution.
- **A later lathe setup from box stock falls back to the 4-jaw.** A lathe setup that starts from the box a mill left is not known to be round, so Suggest chooses the 4-jaw and says "4-jaw: change the chuck", even where the section in the jaws is still a turned diameter. Only a square bar and a round bar are tested.
- **Chamfers and grooves are drawn at true scale.** A 1 mm chamfer or a 3 mm groove on a Ø50 bar is small in the side view; nothing enlarges it, and the pictures were looked at only at 1280 × 800.
- **Round parts are held in the mill vise on their box.** After a lathe setup the mill's vise is modelled on the part's bounding box, not on the round part; a V-block, soft jaws or a collet block (LIO-249, below) model it round.
- The lathe's loads are not checked: cutting force, the chuck's grip against the cut, deflection and chatter of a long thin bar (only the four-diameter warning) and the tailstock's support. The planner (`proposal.rs`) does not know the lathe, so the AI planner makes no lathe setups, and the turned profile is not found from the part. The step pictures were not tested on Windows or macOS.

## Starting a setup's run from a chosen operation (LIO-177, 17 October 2026)

On the Run panel every tool run started at its first operation: operations already cut (in an earlier session, or before a Stop or E-stop) had to be cut again, or disabled in the project. Each operation card of the Tool runs panel now has **Start here**; Cycle Start then sends the tool run from that operation on.

- What is sent: for each operation after the first of a tool run, the run's check (`post::checked_to_start`) builds the program from it with `post::blocks` of the operations from it on, so it starts as after a tool change (retract, the operation's spindle speed and coolant, the start dwell, across at the clearance plane and down to its first point), and checks it as the whole program is checked (`post_check::check_setup` against the stock the operations before it leave, and the jaws and clamps). `Checked::from(k)` gives it; `run::lower(p, k, …)` lowers it. Nothing of the operations before it is in it.
- `a_run_starts_at_a_chosen_operation_with_a_safe_start_and_nothing_before_it` (the fixture job's Facing, Pocket and Contour, then its Drilling, on the simulated UC100): the program from the Pocket names no block of the Facing, begins Stop, Retract, Start (a tool change), Retract, Run with the spindle and coolant, Wait, Approach, Descend, holds every one of the Pocket's own moves and, from the Contour on, the whole program's blocks. Lowered where the machine stands: Z alone up to the clearance plane, the Pocket's S and coolant, the 0.2 s dwell, across at the plane over the Pocket's first point (and down to it unless it is at the plane); no action comes from a Facing block. The cards read Next, To come, To come; Skipped ×3 and Next when tool run 2 is picked; Skipped, Next, To come with the Pocket chosen. The Facing asks for mist, which the profile has no output for: refused from the first operation, not from the Pocket, since it is not sent. Cycle Start waits ("after operations taken as cut") until Z is touched off: the Facing took the top down 4 mm, so the touch-off is **Set Z-4** on that floor, with the tip 3 mm and 2 mm off the centre by eye it sets Z alone and leaves the X and Y work offset as it was, and Probe Z is refused (a plate laid elsewhere on the top could lie on the Pocket's floor). Then Cycle Start runs the job named "… tool run 1 of 2 from Pocket", the running blocks are the program from the Pocket, the cards read Skipped, Cutting now, To come, and it ends OK with tool run 2 next. Picking the Contour again in the tool run done marks it Next and the two before it Done.
- `a_partway_start_whose_surface_is_not_known_waits_for_the_work_zero` (the same job, its work zero on the stock top's front-left corner, which the Contour cut in part): tool run 2 picked, the check cannot tell one surface at the corner's touch-off spot, so Zero Z on the stock top is greyed and Touch off sets nothing, Probe Z says why it cannot set Z, the probes are refused from every spot, and the steps name Facing, Pocket and Contour as cut and T3 to load. Cycle Start waits until **Keep this work zero**; a new start, and a new check of the setup (which keeps the start), ask again; from the setup's first operation nothing changes.
- `the_surface_a_partway_start_touches_off_on_is_known_only_under_one_flat_floor` (`post::spot_surface`): the stock top uncut; under a Ø20 facing pass 1 mm deep, its floor, but only once that pass is before the start; not with a drill down into the spot after it, a pass whose edge crosses the spot, a ball end mill's floor, or flutes that do not reach the top; a deeper flat pass after the first, its floor. Grown by the radius of the tool touched off there (`spot_cuts`), the spot under the Ø20 pass holds for a 6.9 mm radius and not for 8 mm, whose tool would rest on the uncut top beside the pass.
- `each_operation_of_a_run_is_pictured_with_where_it_stands` and `the_run_bar_keeps_cycle_start_and_stop_now_beside_a_long_operations_list` (then the_run_strip_keeps_cycle_start_and_stop_above_a_long_tool_run_list, renamed by LIO-274; each card now holds a Start here button) pass as before.
- Pictures: `render_a_run_started_at_a_chosen_operation` (ignored; `cargo test -- --ignored`) draws the window headless on tiny-skia: the Pocket chosen before the touch-off, the Pocket being cut, and the Drilling chosen with the corner work zero whose surface the check cannot tell.

## Helical milling of holes, and floors overhung by a hairline (18 October 2026)

The TOTEM tenting bottom (`TOTEM_tenting_live_bottom_L.step`, the owner's, not shipped) has 28 blind holes 1.1 mm deep in its top floor, Ø3.9 and Ø1.75–1.9, with flat floors. Drill needs a drill bit for each size, and a 118° point is 1.2 mm tall on a Ø3.9 bit, deeper than those holes; a Pocket needs each hole's floor clicked. Its socket Pocket was also refused, so nothing after it could be simulated.

- **Helical milling** is a fifth Drill cycle (`model::HELICAL_MILLING`, `cam::helical`). Holes and the Hole diameter filter are found as for drilling (`cam::drill_holes`); a flat end mill narrower than each hole goes down a helix of Stepdown per turn to the hole's flat floor, once round there, to the centre and out. A hole leaving the cutter's centre more than its radius to travel is cut in rings from half its radius out, Stepover (% of its diameter) apart at most, innermost first. It climbs: counterclockwise round the hole with the spindle clockwise. Its flutes must cover the depth below where the material starts; the stickout covers the reach from the stock's top. A manual mill refuses it (`manual::HELICAL_ON_MANUAL`). The editor's Cycle tab shows Stepdown per turn, Ring stepover and Tolerance for it, and their checks open that tab (`ui::operation_issue_tab`). Its bites count for the holding check (`forces::bites`), and Fit speeds gives it an end mill's speeds. The planner may propose it (`drill_cycle` `Helical milling` with a flat end mill).
- **The size it lists is the size it cuts.** For a Helical milling operation `cam::holes` lists each hole as its flat end mill mills it (`cam::milled_bore`): its floor is the highest point of the hole's own sloping or flat faces inside the bore (on a drill-point cone or a fillet the flat end stops where it meets the wall), and its bore is measured above that floor. The Geometry tab, the diameter filter, the refusal of a cutter too wide and the cut all use that one size. A bore is read at its walls' corners where a face is a chord of it (its middle within 8 % of its corners' distance from the centre, spanning no more than 1.5 times the median angle of the hole's other wall faces, or rim faces for a rim face), else at its nearest point (`cam::corridor`), for drilling too. A D-flat's ends lie on the round as a coarse side's do, but it spans many sides, so it is read at the flat (the acceptance review's second finding: without the span rule a flat 0.1–0.3 mm deep in a Ø8 bore read Ø8, a Ø8 drill was accepted and the mill cut through the flat). this STEP's hole walls have coarser facets than their rims and corners 3.90–3.94 mm across, and read at the facets' middles (as the rim's sagitta allowed before) its Ø3.9 holes were milled Ø3.76–3.79 (the acceptance review's finding). Now each lists and cuts the circle through its nearest wall corner, Ø3.895–3.913, never past a corner, cutting the flat facets' middles away by up to their sagitta (0.08 mm here), as a drill of that size does.
- **Pocket floors.** A floor overhung past `pocket::LEAN` only in bands narrower than `pocket::SHALLOW_OVERHANG` (0.2 mm) is no longer refused: the cutter keeps clear of everything above it, so the band is left uncut. The tenting bottom's socket floor at its back edge ran about 0.06 mm under the wall over it, in slivers 0.01 mm wide (0.055 mm² against the 0.05 mm² limit).
- Tests: `helical_milling_mills_a_blind_hole_to_size_down_to_its_flat_floor` (the Ø8 × 8 cup with a Ø4 mill: its centre 2 mm out, six turns counterclockwise, reversed with the spindle; a Ø8 mill plunges, a Ø9 one and a drill are refused, as is a manual mill), `helical_milling_cuts_a_wide_hole_in_rings_from_the_centre_out` (Ø1.5 in the Ø8 bore), `helical_milling_cuts_a_coarse_walled_hole_to_the_size_it_lists` (a 64-sided rim over a 16-sided wall whose corners alternate 3.98 and 4.02 mm out: listed Ø7.96, cut Ø7.96 by a Ø4 and a Ø2 mill, a Ø8.2 one refused by that size; read at the facets' middles it fails), `a_shallow_d_flat_in_a_bore_is_not_read_as_round` (a 64-sided Ø8 bore with one flat face at X 3.7, 3.8, 3.9: drilling and milling list Ø7.4, Ø7.6, Ø7.8, a Ø8 drill is refused, the Ø4 mill's edge never passes the flat; without the span rule it fails), `helical_milling_goes_through_a_through_hole_and_stops_on_a_pointed_floors_rim`, `helical_milling_mills_the_totem_cases_top_holes_without_touching_the_part` (the shipped `examples/totem-case-top-right.step`: its Ø2 screw holes with a Ø1.5 mill, every cutting position at least the cutter's radius less 0.01 mm from every face standing over its tip; a 0.1 mm wider cutter fails it), `a_floor_overhung_only_in_a_hairline_band_is_pocketed_clear_of_it`, `helical_milling_takes_a_flat_end_mill_its_pass_settings_and_a_cnc`, `helical_milling_builds_a_drill_operation_with_a_flat_end_mill`, `helical_milling_pushes_sideways_where_drilling_does_not` and `a_drill_operations_pass_and_helical_checks_open_its_cycle_tab`.
- Not a test: the owner's tenting bottom job (its saved workspace, softwood, T4 Ø3) before the change stopped at Setup 1's third operation, with 55.8% of the part's surface uncut. After it the whole job simulates and 17.5% is uncut, no cell gouged. Helical milling of the top floor's Ø3.9 holes with T4 and of all 28 holes with a Ø1 mill (4 mm of flute), then a Ø1 rest Pocket on the floors 5.8 mm down for the pin pockets beside the sockets (not round, so Drill does not find them): 15.7% uncut, no cell gouged. For each of the 28 holes and both mills, the diameter listed and the diameter cut (the cutter's farthest centre plus its radius) differ by 0.000 mm.
- Pictures: `render_the_helical_milling_cycle` (ignored) draws the Cycle tab with the cycle's three fields and its preview ("Climb · helical"), and the Geometry tab listing the fixture block's holes as the end mill cuts them.
- macOS (Apple Silicon), 18 October 2026, on main (93bfef9) with these changes: `cargo test --release --locked` gave **1163 passed, 0 failed, 8 ignored**; `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets --release -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` are clean; `scripts/check-validation-names.sh` exits 0.
- Not validated: on the real machine.

## Pocket holes picked by their walls (18 October 2026)

A Pocket took only flat floors, clicked face by face. The TOTEM tenting bottom's holes have no floor to click when they go through, and their walls are 12 to 95 faces each; its top face is refused as a pocket floor, so a hole through the top could not be pocketed at all.

- **A click on a wall picks the hole.** In a Pocket or an inside Contour (Selected geometry), a click on a surface within 30° of vertical (`pocket::WALL`, `pocket::is_wall`) takes it as a wall of a hole (`pocket::hole_walls`): the part's faces are cut at a height every wall reaches (`level`), and the cut is followed round from that wall to where it closes (`Section::walk`), again from the walls found until they stay the same. The faces it passes through are the hole's walls, all added to the selection. The selection holds surfaces, as before: `pocket::holes` finds each hole again from its walls alone (`loop_of`: the loop at their level through them all and nothing else), trying each selected wall not in a hole found before, so a pocket's wall that goes on down the side of a slot in its floor (both holes' wall) does not stop either being picked; a wall whose hole is not wholly selected waits for one that is. A click on a picked hole drops it: on one of its walls, or on a wall of the same outline crossed at another height (`Hole::same_place`: their outlines differ by a hairline), keeping walls another picked hole shares.
- **Hole or not.** Just inside the outline, at the height the walls were picked at, a ray up crosses the part's faces an even number of times for a hole (none, or under and over a lip), odd for the outside of the part or of an island; eight places round the outline vote. The outside is refused ("not a hole"). A click on a face that is neither a floor nor a wall (a chamfer, a fillet, a screw hole's cone), or on a wall whose loop crosses one (`pocket::LEANING`: a screw hole's 0.18 mm lip), picks the nearest hole past it (`pocket::hole_near`: across the sloping faces sharing a corner with it, the nearest 8 walls they border tried in turn, up to 64 faces); before, such a face was added as a floor and the whole operation then failed "not perpendicular to setup Z".
- **How deep, where (`Hole::levels`).** The floors under the outline are each face's highest over the outline's inside (walls are not floors: a faceted wall's facets lean a few degrees). For each floor, the region of the outline nothing of the part is over down to it is cut to it; where nothing of the part is over or under at all, the hole is cut through, Breakthrough past the lowest corner on the outline (within 0.15 mm of it) of the faces running along it (two corners on it), whichever facets were picked; a face's corner off the outline does not count. A region only a hairline wider than the deeper one under it is cut with that one. A screw hole whose bore (Ø4.3) is wider than its opening at the top (Ø1.9) is cut through its opening, the bore left to a setup from below; a partly covered hole is cut where it is open. The cutter keeps inside the outline by its radius, the radial allowance and the margin, and clear of whatever of the part is over each level (`Level::over`), from where earlier cuts left material (`rest::start`; a level under a floor the same Pocket clears, or under the hole's level above it, starts where that one stops). A level too narrow for the cutter is left, as a corner is; a hole with none that fits is skipped when there is something else to cut, and each such hole or level is named in the operation's warnings (`Hole::fits`, `Hole::uncut`: "The hole at X-50.57 Y-28.03 (1.94 × 1.88 mm) is too narrow for the Ø3 cutter … left uncut") and marked "too narrow for Ø3: left uncut" in its Geometry list. The Geometry tab lists each picked hole (its centre, size, and each level's depth below its rim, to a floor or through, with a remove button using a wall it shares with no other) and shows Breakthrough when one goes through.
- **The acceptance review's first findings**, fixed: (1) every face under the outline counted as its floor, including the facets of the faceted socket walls leaning 1.4–6°, so 129 of 132 clicks on one through socket read blind, at 8.25–12.15, and cut 0.75–4.25 mm short of the underside recess (now: walls are not floors, and each region is cut to its own floor or through); (2) the four corner screw holes were refused as "the outside of the part" (now picked from their bore, cut through their opening); (3) one socket gave up to 13 results by the wall clicked (now one); the Geometry tab's "0 surfaces selected" above the hole list (now only floor surfaces are counted, and not shown as 0 beside holes). Found while fixing them: a through hole's depth came from the bottom of the walls picked, so a click on a long wall's upper facets would have stopped the cut where they stop (now the faces along the outline).
- **Dropping a hole.** A click on a picked hole drops it whether or not that wall's own outline picks a hole (`pocket::dropped`): each picked hole the wall is in, in the same place as the one the click picks, or (when all the click picks is picked) whose walls the click picks, with all the click picks; walls another picked hole shares stay. The Geometry list's remove button drops exactly its hole (`Message::DropHole`). The status line names the hole picked or dropped ("Picked the hole at X… Y… (W × D mm): its walls are blue."). A hole's rim (and the depth listed) is the top of what runs round its outline or round its opening, so a screw hole reads 10.90 mm deep from the part's surface, not 9.41 from its bore's top. The parity test runs before the floors are read, so a click on a sloping face far from any hole takes milliseconds.
- **The acceptance review's third findings**, fixed: (1) on two of the owner's through sockets (X15.66 Y-20.34, X2.69 Y43.70) the remove button and a second click did nothing (the handler worked out the clicked wall's own hole first and stopped on its error: 5 of 74 remove buttons, 492 of 3,348 re-clicks); (2) a click on the tray's walls 3297–3299 picked what lists as two holes, and a second click left both (their walls were kept as shared); also the screw holes' lip faces meeting the cone only at corners, the screw holes' depth from the bore's top, a sloping-face click taking up to 0.87 s, and a failed sloping-face click's message about a wall.
- **The acceptance review's second findings**, fixed: (1) with the owner's own T4 Ø3 the four screw holes (their Ø1.9 openings) were picked, listed "through, 10.74 mm deep" and left uncut without a word (now named in the warnings and the list); (2) a face with two corners on a socket's outline and its third 7 mm away at Z 3.8 took the socket at X15.66 Y-20.34 (and X56.1 Y-31.8) through to Z 2.8 for a 6.8 mm plate, 4.1 mm too deep (now only corners on the outline count: Z 6.9); (3) about 35% of clicks into a screw hole's opening landed on its cone and failed the operation with "Surface 1281 is not perpendicular to setup Z. Reposition the part", and 8% on its lip with "click a straighter wall" (now both pick the hole: of the faces within 3 mm of each of the four screw holes, every one picks it but one wall at X46.33 Y40.31, which says to click a straighter wall, 351 clicks in all through the app).
- Tests: `a_wall_picks_its_whole_hole_and_the_pocket_cuts_it_to_its_floor_or_through` (`pocket::hole_plate`: a 24-sided Ø6 hole 5 mm deep and a 6 mm square hole through a 10 mm plate; one facet picks 24 walls, one wall of the square picks 4; the round hole is cut to Z 1 inside its wall, the square through to 0.5 mm under the plate, both at once each to its own depth; a Ø8 cutter is refused), `a_hole_is_picked_whole_and_cut_only_where_it_is_open_from_above` (the plate's outside is "not a hole"; half a hole's walls are refused; a 2 mm shelf over the round hole's floor leaves 2 mm either side, refused for Ø2, cut round and under nothing of it with Ø1), `a_hole_under_a_lip_is_cut_through_its_opening` (a 6 mm bore under a 4 mm opening: picked from the bore, through, inside the opening), `a_through_hole_with_leaning_facets_goes_through` (an 8-sided Ø6 hole whose facets lean about 8°), `a_through_hole_beside_a_slope_is_cut_to_its_own_bottom` (a face with two corners on the square hole's top edge and its third 6 mm off and 5 mm under the plate: through to 0.5 mm under the plate, not under the slope; with every corner of such a face counted, it fails), `a_click_on_a_holes_chamfer_picks_the_hole` (`pocket::chamfered_hole`: a 45° chamfer facet picks the 24 walls; from the plate's outside wall nothing crosses its top or bottom to the hole: not a hole), `a_picked_hole_too_narrow_for_the_cutter_is_named` (the pocket and its 2 mm slot with a Ø3 cutter: the slot and the pocket's level down it are named, the pocket cut to its floor; with a Ø1 nothing is), `a_click_drops_the_picked_hole_its_wall_is_in_even_when_its_own_outline_fails` (`dropped` with no hole of the click's own: the picked hole the wall is in goes; a wall of none drops nothing; the pocket and slot picked by one click as two holes both go on a second; the pocket's own click keeps the slot whole), `a_hole_faceted_in_pieces_is_cut_through_from_either_piece` (`pocket::split_hole`: a Ø6 hole's wall faceted apart above and below Z 1; the upper facets alone cut through the plate; the halves are the same hole, the plate's two holes not; with the depth from the walls picked, it fails), `a_pocket_and_a_slot_down_its_wall_are_picked_and_cut_together` (`pocket::pocket_with_slot`, the shared west wall the lowest surface: the pocket cut to its floor and on down its slot, the slot to its floor, alone or together; with the hole found again from its lowest surface, as first written, or with a wall of an unselected hole refused at once, it fails), `a_click_on_a_wall_picks_or_drops_its_whole_hole_in_a_pocket` (the app: two clicks pick 7 walls; a click on the pocket again drops it but keeps the slot whole; a click on the outside changes nothing and says why) `a_click_on_another_facet_of_a_picked_hole_drops_it` (the split hole: a click on the other half drops it, then picks it; without `same_place`, it fails) `a_holes_remove_button_drops_that_hole_alone` (the app: the pocket's remove button leaves the slot whole, then the slot's leaves nothing) and `a_click_on_a_holes_chamfer_picks_it_and_the_pocket_names_what_its_cutter_leaves` (the app: a click on the chamfer picks 24 walls and another drops them; the pocket and slot with a Ø3 end mill give the two warnings in the operation's checks). Mutation checks also: with every face under the outline a floor, or the overhang test off, the plate tests fail.
- Not a test: the owner's tenting bottom (Setup 1, its saved workspace). A click on every one of its 2,910 wall surfaces: 63 outlines, each giving one cut whichever of its walls is clicked (the three through sockets "through, 68.9 / 68.9 / 75.4 mm² open"; the four corner screw holes through their Ø1.9 opening), the rest refused: the outside and its notches (not a hole), recesses the part covers in this setup, and chamfers, fillets and cones. One click on each of 74 holes, cut alone with a Ø3 and a Ø1 mill at zero allowances: 47 and 65 cut, the others too narrow; none deeper than its deepest level; three short of it where that level is too narrow for the cutter (a Ø3.9 hole's 0.6 mm deeper spot, one socket's opening for the Ø3); the sockets through to Z 6.9, 1 mm into the underside recess (the open notch at the edge, X56.1 Y-31.8, to Z 2.8: its outline runs along the part's outside wall down to Z 3.8, and nothing of the part is over or under its through part at any height, as the outside contour cuts it); no cutting position (ends and middles of every move) within the cutter's radius of any face standing 0.01 mm over its tip (the nearest 0.013 mm outside it). Through the app, for each of the 63 distinct holes (one per outline): its remove button drops it (63 of 63), a second click on the wall that picked it drops it (63 of 63), and a click on any one of its walls after picking it drops it (2,515 of 2,515); the tray's walls 3297–3299 pick 84, 65 and 63 walls and a second click leaves none. The slowest click on a sloping face takes 9 ms. One Pocket of the four screw holes and three sockets with a Ø1 mill generates (3.3 min at 2 mm stepdown); with the workspace's T4 Ø3 it generates the sockets (3.8 min) and its checks name the four screw holes as too narrow, left uncut; one of all 37 holes under 5 mm too (2.1 min with the Ø3, 5.9 with the Ø1); with every socket outline as well it passed the 30,000-move limit at 1 mm stepdown (that limit is gone since: see the next section).
