## Holes picked by their outline (19 October 2026)

On the TOTEM case top (`TOTEM_case_ble_top_L_V.08.step`) seen from above, the key openings go through a plate: their walls are edge on and the openings empty, so nothing could be clicked to pick one, and a picked one showed nothing (its walls are what turns blue).

- **The outlines.** While a Pocket or a Contour is being picked on an imported part, `App::refresh_part_holes` works out every hole a wall click would pick (`pocket::part_holes`: each wall not already in a hole found, its hole once, by `Hole::same_place` or `Hole::same_opening`, so a recess under a plate's opening, picked through it, is not outlined a second time over it) off the window's thread (`Message::PartHoles`, with the part's fingerprint and the setup's placement as its key; a result for another key is not shown, and a new placement starts another run). On the case top: 36 holes from its 1,048 walls in 0.9 s (59 before the openings were compared: each key opening and screw hole twice, once for the 16 mm recess under it). While they are worked out the Geometry tab says "Finding the part's holes". `App::hole_outlines` gives the view each hole's opening (`levels[0].open`) at its rim, its walls and whether the operation has it.
- **Under the pointer.** `Viewport::picked` returns a hole's first wall when the pointer is within `OUTLINE_REACH` (4 points) of its outline on the screen, before the part's faces; and, where no face of the part is under the pointer, when it is inside an outline (the smallest). The click then goes where a wall's click goes: it picks the hole, or drops it once picked. The marks layer draws the hovered hole's outline in amber (the pointer on its outline, a wall or its opening) and picked holes' outlines in blue.
- Tests: `a_holes_outline_under_the_pointer_or_its_opening_clicks_one_of_its_walls` (`viewport`: on the outline over a face, 3.5 points off it, not 30; inside it over a face the face; inside it off the part the hole; nothing far off or with picking off), `a_pocket_being_picked_outlines_the_parts_holes_and_a_click_on_one_picks_it` (the plate's two holes outlined, the square one at X 7–13 Y −3–3; a click on it picks it and only its outline is marked, another drops it; none once picking is finished; off the window's thread nothing is shown until the result for the current key arrives, and turning the setup starts another run), and `render_a_holes_outline_under_the_pointer` (ignored: pictures from above with the pointer on the outline and in the opening, and picked).
- **The acceptance review's findings**, fixed: the doubled outlines above (a click on the recess's blue outline picked the plate's hole too rather than dropping it); a 6-point reach covered the whole web between key holes at Fit zoom, now 4; nothing said the holes were still being found; two doc comments had moved to the wrong tests.
- Not a test: on the case top, from above, the pointer on the key opening at X18.2 Y13.3 outlines it in amber, on its outline and in its opening, and a click picks it ("Picked the hole at X18.20 Y13.28 (13.80 × 13.80 mm)", through) with its outline blue; seen from the ISO view the blue outline is on the opening's rim.

## Drill holes picked by a click, outlined with their diameters (19 October 2026)

A Drill took only horizontal rim faces: every round opening in a clicked face was drilled, and which holes those were, and their sizes, showed only in the Geometry list.

- **Holes by a click.** A Drill of the part's holes picks a hole as a Pocket does (`picks_holes`): a click on a wall, a chamfer, or the outline the view draws takes all its walls. `cam::holes` drills the round openings of the selected rim faces (the selected faces that are not walls) and each hole its walls pick read as a drill reads a rim's opening (`drill_hole`, through `round_hole`, the per-loop reading `round_holes` now uses), each centre once. `drill_hole` finds the walls' middle from their top edge in the part (the corners of their triangles at their highest), then looks up the part's flat faces facing up from there (`rims`, `rim_loops`) for the first with a loop round it, and on up while the next one's is round about the same centre: so a countersunk hole reads its opening at the face round the countersink, and a counterbore and its bore read as one hole from its top, whichever wall was clicked, exactly as a selected top face reads them; with no such loop, the walls' top edge itself. A hole that is not round is refused by name and nothing changes ("… is not round: a Drill drills round holes. Pick it in a Pocket."). The surface check takes a Drill's walls as a Pocket's. The Geometry list gives each hole to drill with its bore and opening, centre, depth and through or blind, and a **remove** button on those picked by their walls; a hole still drilled after a click dropped its walls (a selected rim face holds it) says so.
- **Every round hole outlined with its diameter.** While a Drill is being picked, the part's holes found in the background (`find_holes`: `pocket::part_holes` and each one's drill reading, `cam::drill_holes_of`) give the view the round ones, a counterbore and its bore once, each labelled with the largest drill in 0.1 mm steps it takes (`cam::largest_drill`: its bore reading plus twice its opening's sagitta and 0.01 mm, what the drill check allows), so a label never names a drill Generate refuses. Labelled outlines are drawn all the time, teal, blue once drilled (a centre among `App::drill_holes`), amber under the pointer, and inside one the pointer takes the hole before the faces of it (its floor, its bore). A label goes right or left of its hole where it covers no hole's outline, keeps `LABEL_GAP` (4 points) from every other hole's and covers no label placed before it (the drilled holes' first); else 20 or 32 points out in one of eight directions with a leader line to its hole that crosses no other hole and no other label; else it is left out, and the view says how many are and how to see them ("12 holes' sizes hidden where holes are close: zoom in, or point at a hole"); the hovered hole's label always shows, placed after the others so that pointing at a hole moves none of them, and drawn last, over any it must cover (`place_labels`, `label_spot`). A label, or the work zero's "X0 Y0 Z0", that would fall outside the 3D view is left out (the hovered hole's is moved into the view, with a leader line): text there is not clipped and was drawn over the panel below it.
- Test: `a_drill_outlines_the_parts_round_holes_with_their_diameters_and_picks_one_by_a_click` (the plate's round Ø6 hole outlined and labelled "Ø6", the square one not; a click drills it, "Picked the Ø6 hole at X-10.00 Y0.00", listed with depth 5 mm and blind, no issues with a Ø6 drill and Generate goes down to its floor at its centre; the square hole's wall refused with the selection unchanged; its remove button and a second click drop it; the pin holes' Drill outlines nothing).
- **The acceptance review's findings**, fixed: a clicked hole was read from the pocket's outline, cut from a section and offset a few hundredths inside the part, so it read about 0.07 mm small (a Ø3.9 drill refused on holes labelled Ø3.9), and six of the tenting bottom's pin holes failed the roundness test (not outlined; a click said "not round"). Now, on both parts, every hole a top face finds is found by a click with the same bore, opening, rim and floor (worst difference 0.0000 mm; the case top's countersunk Ø2 screw holes read their Ø4.3 opening at Z 16.30). Labels that were left out or stood beside a neighbour now always show, with leader lines. A label rounded to the nearest 0.1 could name a drill the check refuses; it is now the largest the hole takes.
- **The second acceptance review's finding**, fixed: at the default window (1440 × 930) and Fit on the tenting bottom, the last-resort rule printed 7 labels over others ("ØØ8"), labels sat on a neighbouring Ø4 ring and leader lines ran through other holes; now none does, 19 of the 31 are labelled and the note counts the 12 left out (all show at 2× zoom).
- Test: `hole_labels_never_cover_each_other_or_another_hole_and_crowded_ones_are_counted` (`viewport`: three holes apart all labelled beside them; on a 6 × 6 grid 11 points apart no label covers another or a hole but its own or comes within the gap of another, no leader line passes another hole, those left out are counted, and the hovered hole's label shows with no other label moved). The third review's notes, fixed: two leader-line labels stood 3 points from an unlabelled pin and could be read as its; pointing at a hidden hole moved or hid its neighbours' labels; the work zero's label was drawn outside the view at 2× zoom. The confirmation review's finding, fixed: the hovered label was drawn first, under the neighbours' it overlapped, for 14 of the grid's 24 hidden holes; the test now hovers each of the 36 and finds its label last.
- Test: `a_hole_picked_by_its_wall_reads_as_its_rim_face_reads_it_counterbored_or_not` (`cam`: the example block's Ø6.6 bores under Ø11 counterbores from the top and straight from the bottom; each reading of each hole, by the counterbore's walls or the bore's, equal to the selected face's to 0.01 mm, and a Drill of those walls drills it; mutation-checked: without the climb to the counterbore's top it fails; each reading's countersink equal too). ## A tool's number once, and the NC review of a UC100 job (19 October 2026)

Found recording the whole CAM flow on the TOTEM case top. A tool named with its number ("T4 Flat end mill 3mm", as the owner names theirs) read "T4 T4 Flat end mill 3mm" on the Run panel's tool runs, its tool-change note, the setup sheet's tools, the operation cards and the tool library's list, "T4: T4 …" in a LinuxCNC program's tool lines and "load T4 T4 …" in its touch-off note, and "… · T4" after the name on a manual mill's steps: `model::tool_label` gives the name alone when it already begins with its number and a space (`model::names_number`), and the program's tool line and the steps' chip leave the number off then (tests: `a_tool_named_with_its_number_is_not_numbered_twice`: "T4 …" for tool 4 once, but "T1 T4 …" for tool 1 and "T4 T45 drill"; `a_tool_named_with_its_number_is_numbered_once_in_the_programs`: the example job's tools renamed "T1 Flat 10" and so on, in touch-off mode, with no "T1 T1" or "T1: T1" in any program and "(T1 Flat 10 - diameter …)" and "load T1 Flat 10" in the first). And NC review of a job on a UC100, which writes no G-code file, said in its dark pane "Each setup's G-code appears here once its checks pass" beside setups that had passed; it now says that a UC100 takes no G-code file and each setup's program goes to the machine from MACHINE, through Run on the machine.

## Generation off the window's thread, and no move limit (18 October 2026)

Generate and Generate all ran the job in the window's message handler, so a long generation froze the window, and a 30,000-move limit (and others: 256 Pocket rings, 1,000 Pocket or contour levels and drill pecks, 2,000 facing strokes, 300 Waterline layers, 500,000 finishing samples, 60 million finishing evaluations) kept every toolpath short enough not to be noticed. On the owner's tenting bottom one Pocket of its 62 small holes and sockets was refused. Measured with the limit lifted (Ø1 mill): 61,000 to 269,000 moves generated in 0.4 s, played to the end in 5–19 ms and drew a frame in about 0.1 s; only the 3D stock mesh, rebuilt in the background from every cut, grew with them (7 s to 18 s, against 1.5 s for a 2,000-move plan). The UC100 driver streams moves through its 900-record buffer and limits nothing.

- **Off the window's thread.** `App::generate` hands the work to a blocking task with a shared `progress::Progress`, and its result comes back with the generation's epoch: `Message::Generated` (an operation's simulation), `GeneratedAll` (the audit) or `GeneratedLayout` (a setup's toolpaths, for where its vise jaws or clamps go: `App::suggest_layout` generates them, `place_layout` places the holding when they arrive). A result is used only while it is the generation under way, for the operation it was started for. An edit to the project (`touch`), opening or starting another (`replace_document`), choosing another setup or operation, another Generate and **Cancel** (`Message::CancelGenerate`) cancel it; Generate & play starts playback when it is done. The headless smoke run and the unit tests generate inline (`App::generate_inline`), as their other background work does.
- **Progress.** The generators report into the thread's progress without it being passed down (`progress::part`, `stage`, `expect`, `step`, `report`): each enabled operation the job replays up to the one asked for is an equal part, named "setup / operation"; within it a Pocket counts its moves against its estimate (levels × its rings' points), a Face its strokes, a Contour and a Waterline their levels, a drill or helical cycle its holes, a Parallel its grid's triangles, its outline's rows and then its passes. Every generator checks for Cancel (`progress::check`, every 256 moves or samples; each level, hole or triangle where those are long), and the replay of an earlier operation stops early once cancelled (`progress::stopped`). The simulation panel shows the operation, the percentage, a progress bar and Cancel.
- **One limit: memory.** `cam::CEILING` (5 million moves or samples) replaces every limit above; past it `cam::too_many` says to check the stepdown, stepover and tolerance. A move takes about 190 bytes with the stock cut it leaves and the copies the simulation keeps (a Pocket of 15.3 million moves peaked at 2.9 GB in the acceptance review), so the ceiling is about 1 GB; the largest real job seen, the tenting bottom's holes in one Pocket at 0.25 mm stepdown, is 270,000. The Pocket estimates its moves before making any and refuses past the ceiling then. The AI planner's instructions state the ceiling instead of the old limits and the stepdowns that kept within them.
- **Merged with Adaptive clearing and the manual lathe.** Both came with limits of their own (300,000 Adaptive moves; 30,000 lathe moves or rings); they use `cam::CEILING` and `cam::too_many` as well, and their move loops stop on Cancel (`progress::check`). Adaptive keeps its 1,000-depth-level and walker-step limits against runaway settings. Its time goes into walking each level, so the walk stops on Cancel at every step, and each level is a thousandth-steps share of the bar by the material it has cleared (`adaptive::tests::a_long_adaptive_moves_the_progress_bar_and_stops_soon_after_cancel`: the TOTEM plate with a Ø3 mill in one level, the bar past 20 % before any move is out, then stopped within 250 ms of Cancel; it fails with the walk's and the level's checks removed). Not a test: a Ø2 mill at a 0.3 mm load and 2 mm stepdown on that plate (73 s) reached 67 % at 54 s and stopped 1.8 ms after Cancel, where the acceptance review had measured a bar at 0 and 10–14 s.
- **The merge's acceptance review**, fixed: the above; a pin-hole Drill with a drill offered Helical milling on its Cycle tab, and drilled anyway (the tab now offers the four drilling cycles, as on a lathe, and one whose cycle already is Helical milling, switched to the pin holes or to a drill, is refused on its Cycle tab: `model::HELICAL_PIN_DRILL`, an end mill still milling them whatever the cycle), its note then saying what a drill does, as on a lathe (`a_lathe_drill_has_a_centre_tab_and_no_hole_selection`; it fails with the note left to the cycle); a lathe Drill with Helical milling (one copied from a mill) asked for a flat end mill and the lathe drilled anyway, and now says the cycle is for a mill (`lathe::HELICAL_ON_LATHE`, on the Cycle tab: `helical_milling_takes_a_flat_end_mill_its_pass_settings_and_a_cnc`).
- **Scrubbing.** The playback slider replays the moves between where the simulation stands and where it is moved (from the start when it goes back): 0.13–0.5 s for 200,000 moves, 3.9 s for 1.4 million in the acceptance review. A scrub that would replay more than `SCRUB_LIVE` (50,000 moves) waits until the slider is let go (`Message::ScrubDone`), and is then replayed off the window's thread on a copy of the simulation (`App::catch_up`; a copy takes a few milliseconds even at a million moves), put back (`Message::Replayed`) only into the simulation it was copied from and only if the slider has not moved since; the panel says so meanwhile. Play with a scrub to replay, or while one replays, starts playback when it is put back (the button reads Pause, and a second press takes it back), and the playback tick replays nothing meanwhile. A scrub the slider was not let go after (the arrow keys, the wheel) is replayed once the slider has been still for 0.4 s (`SCRUB_SETTLE`); a replay that fails leaves nothing waiting. Nothing of the stock is saved along the way.
- **The acceptance review's third finding**, fixed: Play pressed while the let-go was replaying off the window's thread started playback at once, so the next tick replayed the whole scrub on the window's thread (324 ms for 553,337 moves) and the replay put back was then dropped.
- **The acceptance review's second findings**, fixed: letting go of the slider still replayed on the window's thread (0.58 s at 628,600 moves, 3.9 s at 1.4 million, and a single click on the slider is a press and a let-go), and 200,000 moves took 0.13–0.5 s, not the tenth of a second the comment said; the operation audit page's "Check all enabled operations" showed no progress bar and no Cancel (`ui::generation_progress` is shown there too); an edit while a vise was being placed dropped the placement without a word (now the status line says to press Suggest layout).
- **The acceptance review's first findings**, fixed: a generation still running when another project was opened, started or the example opened applied its preview or audit to that project; picking a vise or clamps, or Suggest layout, generated the setup's toolpaths on the window's thread (8 s on a 0.05 mm Parallel); the bar stood still on the slow operations (a Builder's moves were counted against a Waterline's layers and filled the bar at once; Parallel, Contour and the drill cycles reported nothing); the memory figure (800 MB at 20 million) was four times too low; scrubbing a long toolpath stalled the window; another operation chosen during a generation left "Generating…" on the status line.
- Tests: `work_reports_its_parts_and_stops_when_cancelled` (`progress`: parts, stages, `report`, `check` and `stopped`), `generating_runs_off_the_window_with_progress_cancel_and_stale_results_dropped` (the app with `generate_inline` off: Generate returns at once with a generation under way and the fast tick; its result shows the preview; one returning after an edit, after Cancel or after New project is dropped; Generate & play plays when it arrives; choosing a vise starts the setup's toolpaths off the window and an edit drops them, saying so; a cancelled Face of 0.001 mm stepdown ends with "Generation cancelled."), `a_long_scrub_waits_for_the_slider_to_be_let_go` (live, let go inline, and as the window runs: the let-go replays a copy put back when done, dropped when the slider moved again; Play during the replay, or with a scrub still to replay, waits for it, a tick meanwhile moves nothing, and playback starts when it is put back; Play again takes it back; a failed replay clears; a scrub not let go is replayed after the slider is still), `pocket_rejects_stale_geometry_bad_targets_tools_and_parameters` (a 0.000001 mm stepdown is refused at the ceiling), and in the planner: `fine_stepdowns_the_old_move_limit_refused_now_generate`, `finish_passes_ramp_and_stepover_multiply_pocket_and_inside_floor_moves` (the finish passes and 10 % stepover the limit refused generate), `the_facing_peck_and_setup_rules_in_the_instructions_hold_on_the_fixture` (a 0.38 mm Face at 5 % and over 1,000 pecks a hole generate), `a_waterline_past_the_old_300_layers_generates`, `a_parallel_stepover_the_old_sample_limit_refused_now_generates`, `the_instructions_state_the_limits_the_program_enforces`.
- Not a test: on the four-setup block, a 0.01 mm Waterline (4,000 layers) and a 0.05 mm Parallel (0.001 tolerance) generated on another thread for 6 s: the bar went 80.6 % to 85.5 % and 80.8 % to 90.7 % (each the fifth of five operations; a Parallel's grid triangles, its outline and its rows are a third each); Cancel stopped the Waterline in 4 ms, and the Parallel in 0.5–1.8 ms whether pressed at 2, 4 or 6 s (before the outline was a stage of its own it took 0.58 s). A picture of the window part-way through the Waterline: the panel reads "Generating · Top side / Waterline finishing", 83 %, its bar and Cancel.
- Not changed: the 3D stock mesh is still rebuilt from every cut, in the background, so on a long job it takes seconds to catch up during playback.
- macOS (Apple Silicon), 18 October 2026, with the helical milling and hole picking changes, merged with main (pin holes, Adaptive clearing, the manual lathe, icon buttons, the planner chat): `cargo test --release --locked` gave **1395 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored**; `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets --release -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` are clean; `scripts/check-validation-names.sh` exits 0.

## Facing the part's outline (19 October 2026)

A Facing's Machining boundary "Model silhouette" faced the bounding rectangle of the placed model, so the owner's TOTEM case top, set to Model silhouette, had all of its 5 mm side stock cut to the part's top rather than only what is over the part. The owner asked for the stock to be faced down to the part's top only over the part's outline seen from above, a set distance past it, and for it to be set on the Geometry tab and cut in rows ("still zigzag, just not square but the model outline"). A first version as an Adaptive pass pattern was dropped for this.

- **Where (`Operation::faces_outline`).** A Facing with Model silhouette faces the outer loops of the part's outline (`cam::Geometry::silhouette`), so the stock over a hole through the part is faced too, grown by **Clear past outline** (`silhouette_extend`, mm): how far the cutter's edge reaches beyond it. It replaces Tool containment on the Geometry tab and Radial stock to leave on Passes for this boundary. A Facing saved before it reads 2 mm (`model::OUTLINE_EXTEND`); a new one, and one the planner makes with `facing_area` `model` or changes to it or to another cutter, takes 0.3 of the cutter's radius in half millimetres up, 2 mm at least (`model::outline_extend_for`). The cutter's centre goes in the outline grown by that less its radius, over the stock or within its radius of it. A round cutter needs 1 − 1/√2 of its radius past a square outer corner; Generate refuses a distance that leaves more than a 0.02 mm speck of the outline over the stock out of reach and says the least that does not (by bisection: "at least 3.0 mm for this Ø20 cutter").
- **How (`facing::outline`).** Each level goes once round the edge of the centres' region, Climb or Conventional, then clears inside it in rows at the pass direction and stepover, both ways or one way, each row midway in its band and cut to the region, so that every row starts and ends where that edge pass cut. The edge pass comes in from beside the stock in a straight line that touches none of the stock left, where the outline runs off the stock; otherwise it ramps round its loop at the Entry ramp angle (more than 10 laps plunge), from the corner of the loop its run reaches back to the loop's start, then goes once round at depth. With Keep tool down the cutter feeds to the next row at depth when it is within two stepovers and the way stays inside the region; otherwise it goes round the edge pass at depth, or lifts just over the level before, or to clearance where the way leaves the region, whichever is quickest, and feeds down where the edge pass cut. Moves through air over what is cut feed at the machine's fastest feed. Earlier cuts set where cutting starts (`rest::start`).
- **The acceptance review's findings**, fixed: (1) the owner's saved project still held the dropped pattern ("Adaptive (part silhouette)") and refused to generate (now read as Both ways); (2) a planner Facing of the model's outline kept 2 mm, refused for cutters of Ø20 and up, and a planner change of its cutter or facing area kept the old distance (now sized to the cutter when built or changed); (3) the Geometry tab still said the Facing preview is a rectangular raster, and the edge-pass Linking note spoke of a rectangular boundary.
- Tests: `faces_the_parts_outline_to_its_top_in_rows_and_leaves_the_stock_around_it` (an L-shaped part with a step, Ø6, 2 mm past: the cutter's centre stays 1 mm inside the L at depth, at least 24 rows, every grid cell over the L at its top and every one more than 2 mm and a cell past it uncut), `rows_link_at_depth_both_ways_and_lift_between_them_one_way`, `ramps_into_stock_round_the_edge_but_comes_down_beside_a_part_flush_with_it` (in 6 mm stock it ramps; flush with the stock no move ramps and the stock in the L's notch is left), `refuses_to_leave_the_parts_corners_and_says_how_far_clears_them` (40 × 30 mm block, Ø20: refused at 2 mm asking 3.0, faced to the top at 3), `the_example_blocks_outline_facing_posts_and_replays_exactly_before_its_pocket`, `a_facing_saved_before_clear_past_outline_faces_its_outline_2_mm_past_it`, `a_new_facing_clears_past_the_outline_far_enough_for_its_cutter`, `a_facing_saved_with_the_tried_adaptive_pattern_runs_rows_both_ways`, `a_facing_of_the_models_outline_clears_past_it_far_enough_for_its_cutter` (planner, Ø25: 4.0 mm, generates), `a_change_of_cutter_resizes_how_far_past_the_outline_a_facing_clears` (planner change from Ø10 to Ø25).
- Not a test: the owner's TOTEM case top as saved (`render_the_outline_facing_on_a_saved_project`, ignored; Ø3, 5 mm stock, 2 mm stepdown, 40 % stepover): 3 levels, 43.4 min; 4561 of 4561 grid cells over the outline at the part's top, 1909 of 1909 more than 3.5 mm past it uncut; both Pockets after it generate from its stock. The acceptance review replayed it move by move (zigzag with Keep tool down and Retract, One way, 37°, 8 mm past): no rapid into stock, no plunge into material, no link feed meeting stock. `render_the_outline_facing` draws the four-setup block's (4704 of 4704 cut, 972 of 972 left).
- macOS (Apple Silicon), 19 October 2026, on main (d12e49e): `cargo test --release` gave **1405 passed, 0 failed, 17 ignored**; `cargo clippy --all-targets --release -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` are clean; `scripts/check-validation-names.sh` exits 0.
- Not validated: on the real machine.

## Countersinks (LIO-218, 19 October 2026)

The TOTEM case top (`examples/totem-case-top-right.step`) has four screw holes in its top face, each an 82° countersink Ø4.3 at the face down to a Ø2 through bore 1.32 mm under it (the STEP's `CONICAL_SURFACE`s, semi-angle 41°). Hole detection read them as Ø2 holes with a Ø4.3 opening, the same as a counterbore. Nothing said the opening was a cone or what its angle was. The planner was told to choose a chamfer mill "whose tip_angle is the countersink's" but was never given that angle, and a chamfer mill of any angle was accepted.

- **What changed.** Each hole's opening is checked for a countersink (`cam::countersink`): every chord of its rim has a sloping face under it, the faces' other corners all give one slope (within 0.5° of angle), and the cone they lie on ends on a wall or a floor (an upright or flat face), not on more slope. The angle (20°–160°) and the depth to the cone's end are kept on the hole. A plain hole, a counterbore, a rounded edge and a bevel of two slopes have none. The part report lists `countersink` (null, or its `angle` and `depth`) with each hole, the planner's instructions say a countersunk hole takes a chamfer mill of its angle, and the Drill editor's hole list shows it. Drilling a countersunk hole with a chamfer mill whose tip differs from the countersink's angle by more than 0.5° is refused, and the message names the angle to choose. A sharper cone would cut into the countersink's wall and the bore, a blunter one would cut only at the rim and leave the cone. Holes without a countersink are countersunk as before, to their opening at the rim.
- Tests: `a_countersink_is_found_with_its_angle_and_depth` (Ø8 at the face down to a Ø4 bore in a 64-sided disc: 82° and 90°, depths 2.30 and 2.00; a 90° one down to a flat floor before the bore; none for a plain hole, a counterbore, a rounded edge or two slopes; with the wall-or-floor rule removed, the two slopes read as a 126.9° countersink and it fails), `a_chamfer_mill_countersinks_only_at_its_countersinks_angle` (an 82° mill's point goes down to the countersink's apex; 60°, 90° and 83° are refused; a drill still drills it), `the_totem_cases_screw_holes_are_82_degree_countersinks_cut_by_an_82_degree_chamfer_mill` (the four holes read 82° and 1.323 mm; a 90° Ø6 chamfer mill is refused; an 82° one's point stops 2.15 / tan 41° under the face, and every corner of the part within 4 mm of each hole is on or outside its cone, less the chords' sagitta; with the angle check off, a 60° one fails that check at a corner of the countersink wall), and `the_example_block_reports_its_top_pocket_and_holes` (its counterbores report no countersink).
- Not validated: a countersink cut on the machine; a countersink on a manual mill's step guide.

## The view stays put, and shows the stock the operation in focus starts from (22 October 2026)

- **The camera fits the same scene with a simulation or without.** The 3D view's fit (`Viewport::scene_bounds`) is the part, the setup's own stock (`stock::resolve`, which holds any box a later setup starts from) and its jaws or clamps round that stock, gripping all of it, whether drawn or not (`Viewport::framed`, `ui::framed_holding`). It used to take in the simulated stock and the reach of the cutter over every move, so leaving a simulation re-centred and re-scaled the view: on the test block a corner of the part moved from (1159, 213) to (913, 451) px and the scale from 8.06 to 5.10. Now the corner's screen point and the scale are the same with and without the simulation, with the stock shown and hidden (`viewport::tests::the_view_stays_put_with_a_simulation_and_without`, which fails on the old fit), and in the app, in setup 2 held in a vise, the fit is the same with the simulation on, with Stock off and with the simulation off again (`smoke::tests::the_view_shows_the_stock_the_operation_in_focus_starts_from`). Still pictures keep their own fit (`Viewport::scene_boxes`).
- **The panels under the view do not move it.** The view, its toolbar and the simulation strip share the space under the workspace heading (`ui::workspace`, laid out by `responsive`); the strip and the toolbar change height with the operation selected and with a simulation. The camera centres and fits the scene in a frame from the view's top as high as all of that space but `ui::PANELS` (320 px, what the strip playing a preview with its check and the toolbar with the uncut faces' lines take; at least half of it; `ui::scene_frame`), not in the view's own height, so a panel that grows or shrinks covers or uncovers the bottom of the view and the scene stays where it is; the wheel zooms toward, and a drag turns about, points worked out in the same frame. With the view 600, 520 or 380 px high in a 450 px frame, a corner of the part, the scale and the point the view turns about are the same (`viewport::tests::the_view_stays_put_with_a_simulation_and_without`). The Control page's 3D view, a fixed 360 px, fits its own height.
- **The stock the operation in focus starts from.** The background run of the whole job (`leftover_stock`) keeps the stock the job leaves as far as every operation worked (`LeftStock::job`), the first operation that failed (`LeftStock::broken`) and each operation's `op_fingerprint` (the setup's `start_fingerprint` and the operations before it in the setup). Every cut remembers its operation, so the stock operation N starts from is the job's stock with the cuts of the operations before it alone; it is meshed off the window's thread at the simulation's detail, the last eight kept (`App::start_source`, `App::refresh_start_mesh`). Without a simulation the 3D view draws it as the simulation draws its stock (the part hidden but for its faces still under stock), and see-through over the part while geometry is picked; with Stock off the part is shown. The first operation, one after an operation that failed and one whose job is still being worked out keep the setup's box. Editing the operation in focus keeps its stock; editing one before it, or an operation of an earlier setup for a later setup's, drops it until the job is worked out again, so a stale stock is never shown. On the two-setup fixture (Face, Pocket, Contour; then the part turned over and faced) the Contour starts from the Face's and the Pocket's cuts and setup 2's Facing from all three of setup 1's (`smoke::tests::the_view_shows_the_stock_the_operation_in_focus_starts_from`). Drawn, it differs from the simulation's first frame only by the cutter, far less than the setup's box does; while picking, the part is drawn under it; with Stock off it is not drawn; a new surface is drawn again (`viewport::tests::the_stock_an_operation_starts_from_is_drawn_without_a_simulation`).
- **The stock a setup leaves (24 October 2026).** The browser's "Stock after this setup" is a button: clicked, it closes any operation (the setup editor shows) and the 3D view shows the stock the setup's operations leave, drawn as the stock an operation starts from is (`App::setup_end`; `start_source` takes the setup's end as the operation one past its last, and `LeftStock::ops` keeps a fingerprint for it too). On the two-setup fixture setup 1's end shows the cuts of its Face, Pocket and Contour, the very cuts of the browser's picture (`LeftStock::after`), and setup 2's those and its Facing's; selecting an operation, the setup editor or a setup leaves it; editing an operation of the setup drops it until the job is worked out again; before that, the setup's box (`smoke::tests::the_view_shows_the_stock_a_setup_leaves_when_its_end_is_selected`).
- Pictures inspected (not a test): the Contour's and setup 2's Facing's regular views at 900 × 600 match their simulations' first frames at the same framing, but for the tool: the faced top with the pocket's slot and boss cut and the walls not yet contoured, and the turned-over block with setup 1's cut band at its bottom edge; while picking, the part shows under a see-through stock.

## A 4-axis Jingyan CNC4030 with the rotary unit's geometry (LIO-184, 17 October 2026)

The Machine library could save 4 axes but not where the rotary sits; the owner's machine is 3-axis and the run checked only X, Y and Z against the profile.

- `rotary_unit::*` (`model.rs`): a 4-axis "A table around X" machine starts with the 15 geometry fields of `ROTARY_GEOMETRY` (mm, all assumed) and has no issues; they round-trip through the saved JSON and are mm whatever the machine's units; a machine file saved without them loads, reports no geometry and no issues, and gets the defaults when edited (an existing value is kept); a 3-axis or B-axis machine has none and bad values on it are not asked about. Validation: sizes must be above zero (the smallest grip zero or more), the centreline inside the Y and Z travel (Z from −travel to 0), the chuck face and tailstock inside X, the tailstock beyond the chuck, the grip range ordered and within the chuck, and the part length not longer than the chuck-to-tailstock distance.
- `chuck::tests::*` (LIO-187): a Ø30 × 120 mm bar 40 mm out of a 3-jaw chuck sits on the A axis with the chuck at its −X end; jaw 1 is up at A0, towards −Y at A90, down at A180, towards +Y at A270 and between at A37 (the other way on a Negative A), the tailstock body never turns; a tool 0.01 mm outside a jaw's box and the gap is clear and 0.01 mm inside it is refused, at each of those angles, as is a cut 0.01 mm into the gap before the jaws' front (the stock gripped in them); an index move from A60 to A180 is refused for the jaw it brings up at A120 though both ends are clear, backwards and over a whole turn too, and clear 0.5 mm above all it sweeps; a 30 mm box turning in a 4-jaw chuck sweeps its corners; a bar outside the grip range, under half the jaw length gripped, a box in a 3-jaw chuck, jaws not square on a box, a bar too long for the unit or past the tailstock, and a machine without a rotary unit are refused; the supporting tailstock's centre is in the bar's end and a tool beside it is refused within the gap and clear past it; the jaws' friction against the turn and the slide is ×3 and ×4 for a 300 N push on the bar's top, and the 40 mm stick-out bends P L³ / 3 E I (3/102 of it on the tailstock), a 150 mm one past 0.05 mm. `stock::tests::a_round_bar_*`, `stock_surface::tests::a_round_bar_*` (its voxels and surface stay inside the bar), `storage::tests::a_box_stock_project_from_before_the_chuck_loads_unchanged` and `post::tests::a_chuck_keeps_the_pocket_out_of_its_jaws_and_the_stock_they_grip` (the fixture's pocket on a Ø110 bar: clear 170 mm out of the jaws, refused for the jaws and the gripped stock 100 mm out, and a vise refused on the bar).
- `the_rotary_machine_is_a_4_axis_on_a_profile_with_the_a_axis_and_the_owners_stays_3_axis` (`control.rs`): "Jingyan CNC4030 + rotary" has no issues, its profile (`jy5800.toml` plus `[axes.a]`, −180 to 180 degrees) loads and passes the driver's checks, X, Y and Z soft-limit spans match its travel, and the owner's machine and its profile file are unchanged and have no A axis.
- `run::tests::a_range_that_matches_…`, `…differs_from_the_profiles_a_soft_limits_is_refused`, `a_machine_with_an_a_axis_on_a_profile_without_one_is_refused`, `a_machine_without_an_a_axis_is_not_asked_about_a`: A is checked in Cycle Start's problems (`run::a_problems`) with both ends of the range; the XYZ checks are unchanged.
- Not validated: the form in the window, the library button on screen, and anything on the real machine. The geometry and the profile's A axis are assumed, not measured.

## Indexed setups: A turns the stock in one clamping (LIO-188, 18 October 2026)

"Index rotary axis" was a label: every later setup was a re-clamp with its own probed origin.

- `indexed::tests::positive_a_turns_the_part_by_the_right_hand_rule_about_x`: at A90 +Y goes to +Z and +Z to −Y about the centreline, which stays put (clockwise seen from the chuck along +X), the sense of the driver's `Bar` and of the chuck's jaw 1; a Negative machine turns the other way. `turned_angles_turn_as_the_placement_then_a_does`: the placement's angles followed by A compose exactly, at Y ±90° too.
- `an_indexed_setup_turns_setup_1s_frame_about_the_centreline_and_keeps_work_zero`: on a Ø40 × 120 bar in the chuck, setup 2 indexed to 90 and 180 takes setup 1's work zero, offset, chuck and placement; `Document::at_angle` at A0 is setup 1's frame and at A90 turns every point of the part about the axis (Y0 Z20), work coordinates (x, y, z) to (x, −z, y); the work zero (A axis at stock end, X60 Y0 Z20) is the same at each angle and on the machine is the rotary unit's centreline (Y140 Z−40) and X 85, 40 mm stick-out and 25 mm jaws out from the face at X20; the setup's own frame, placement and datum match A90.
- `a_re_clamp_after_indexed_setups_keeps_its_own_placement`: a Reclamp setup after an indexed one keeps its own rotation and work zero, a setup indexed after it turns its frame, and indexing the first setup is refused. `angles_are_listed_in_degrees`: lists by commas, slashes or spaces, refused when empty, not numbers or repeated, and outside the machine's A range or on a machine without A.
- `indexed_setups_warn_while_the_centreline_is_not_measured`: assumed and stale (from the machine library's mark) are warned, measured is not.
- `a_label_only_index_from_an_older_project_keeps_its_own_placement`, `storage::tests::a_project_with_index_rotary_axis_as_a_label_loads_unchanged`: a project saved before keeps its setup, frame and placement, and saves back to the same file.
- `post::tests::an_indexed_setup_on_a_machine_without_a_is_not_exported`: on a 3-axis machine the export refuses an indexed setup with the setup's own reason (programs command A since LIO-193).
- Not validated: anything on the machine; the frame is not used yet to generate or simulate cuts at each angle (LIO-189, LIO-190).

## Indexed operations: the 3-axis kernels at each A angle (LIO-189, 18 October 2026)

`Strategy::Indexed` was unavailable, with an `angle_a` it never used; an indexed setup's operations ran at its first angle only, with no A on their moves.

- `indexed::tests::a_flat_on_the_parts_plus_y_face_is_cut_on_top_at_a_plus_90`: Facing the +Y face of a 20 × 20 square part in a Ø40 bar, in a setup indexed to A90: the plan starts with A turning from 0, every move is at A90, every cutting tip taken back to the part (`AtAngle::frame`) is on the +Y face or above it, on the machine above the centreline, and the simulated stock is cut over +Y, not over −Y. At A−90 the face is underneath and Facing refuses it. This fixes the sign: positive A brings +Y up.
- `a_square_milled_at_0_90_180_270_comes_out_square_on_the_axis`: an outside Contour at A 0, 90, 180, 270 leaves the simulated bar square, 20 × 20 on its axis: material 8 mm from the axis kept on all eight directions, 12–13 mm cut away, and the four faces as far from the axis to a voxel.
- `an_index_move_retracts_above_the_swing_before_a_turns`: the swing radius is the jaws' (√(55² + 10²) mm, the Ø40 bar's 20 and 35 mm of jaw, 20 mm wide), the retract the centreline + it + 15 mm; each of the three index moves rises straight up at the old angle to that height, turns A by 90° there in its time, crosses at that height at the new one and goes straight down to the first cut, and the moves are one path in time.
- `an_index_retract_above_the_z_travel_is_refused_with_its_numbers`: with the assumed centreline (Z −40) the retract is at machine Z 30.9 and refused, naming the centreline, the jaws' 55.9 mm, the clearance and the travel; also for one angle away from A0; A0 alone does not turn and is generated, entering from the top of the Z travel, which the program's first turn of A and the rotary checks refuse.
- `an_index_move_nearer_the_jaws_than_the_gap_is_refused`: with 1 mm of clearance over the jaws the sweep check (`chuck::Rig::hits_turning`) refuses the index move inside the 2 mm gap from A0 to A90; indexed to A 0 and 90 instead, the approach at A0 waiting at that height is refused the same way, though A does not turn.
- `an_indexed_setup_at_the_angle_a_is_at_comes_down_from_above_the_swing` (LIO-205): indexed to A 0, 90, 180 and 270 from A0, where setup 1 left it, the contour's entry has no index move but is the index move's: rapids at A0 straight down over its first cut from the swing height, clear of the chuck at A0; the workholding and rotary checks find nothing, and it exports with `G0 A0.000` after the tool change's `G53 G0 Z0` and three turns plus the last back to A0.
- `a_later_operation_at_the_same_angle_comes_down_from_above_the_swing`: a finishing contour after a roughing one, both at A90, enters the same way, with no index move, and the setup exports.
- `three_axis_moves_carry_no_a_and_no_index`: setup 1's contour in the same job has no A and no index move; held in the chuck it enters from above the jaws (LIO-206) and is, from its first feed on, move for move as the kernel generates it alone; in a vise or clamps it is the kernel's plan itself, move for move and in time. The golden program tests are unchanged.
- `a_plain_setup_in_the_chuck_comes_down_from_above_the_jaws` (LIO-206): setup 1 on the Ø40 bar in the chuck, a roughing and a finishing contour, the indexed setup after it at A 90 and 180. The kernel starts at the stock's −X −Y corner, in the jaws, more than 30 mm under the swing; each operation instead rapids from the swing height of setup 1's own clamping (the indexed setup's) straight down to its first feed, with no A, clear of the jaws. The workholding check finds nothing and both setups export, setup 1 with no A word. With the fix taken out, export refuses setup 1: "the tool hits the chuck body at X-120.0 Y-20.0 Z35.0" and the clearance plane under jaw 1. Both headers say the chuck, its jaws, the rotary head, the tailstock and the gripped stock were checked, not vise jaws (LIO-210).
- `a_plain_setup_in_the_chuck_at_the_assumed_centreline_enters_from_the_top_of_the_z_travel`: with the centreline at Z −40 the swing is above the Z travel, and setup 1's entry comes down from its top.
- `control::tests::a_bar_in_the_chuck_runs_setup_1_then_its_indexed_setup` (simulated UC100): the same job, setup 1's contour then the indexed setup at A 90 and 180: setup 1 is admitted, its lowered tool comes down over the first cut from at or above the swing height, A never moves, and it ends done; then the indexed setup runs, standing at 90 and 180, and ends done at A0. Between cuts and between operations the kernels' links stay over the part, out of the jaws' X range, and pass the workholding check as they are.
- `a_setup_posed_at_an_angle_has_the_frame_at_angle_gives`: `Document::setup_at` poses the part in `at_angle`'s frame at five angles.
- `indexed_operations_of_an_older_project_move_into_indexed_setups`, `storage::tests::a_project_with_an_indexed_operation_loads_it_into_an_indexed_setup`: an Indexed operation at A 90° loads as a Facing operation (same id, no `angle_a`) in a new indexed setup after its own, in its clamping, the operations after it in one at A 0°; one at A 0° stays in place; on a 5-axis machine it stays Indexed and its first issue says why.
- `the_plan_check_checks_each_angles_moves_against_the_part_posed_there`: a block 30 wide and 10 high, contoured at A 0 and 90, has no gouge when each angle's moves are checked against the part at that angle, though the A90 moves pass through the part as it sits at A0.
- `the_swing_takes_the_centreline_the_control_page_measured`: a centreline measured at Z −150 in the machine library clears the refusal that the setup's copy (Z −40) gives; once marked stale the copy is used again.
- `an_indexed_setups_cuts_are_checked_against_the_chuck_at_each_angle` (changed by LIO-191): a contour 90 mm out of the jaws with 40 mm of clearance, at A 0 and 90, is clear of the chuck: the workholding check says nothing, not "Holding not checked".
- `a_disabled_indexed_operation_at_an_angle_stays_where_it_is`: a disabled Indexed operation at A 90° does not split its setup.
- Not validated: anything on the machine (the removal at other angles is drawn by LIO-190).

## Checks with the part on the rotary axis: travel, Z reach and the chuck at each angle (LIO-191, 18 October 2026)

`plan_check` and `post_check` checked an indexed setup's moves in its frame only: XYZ travel as a span, no A, and its cuts not against the chuck at each angle ("Holding not checked").

- `rotary_check::tests::every_move_is_checked_on_both_sides_of_the_machines_travel_and_a_range`: one move 0.01 mm outside each end of the X (0 to 600), Y (0 to 400) and Z (−300 to 0) travel and of the A range (−360° to 360°) is refused with its number, the setup, the A angle and the operation; 0.01 mm inside, nothing is said. Below the Z travel it says how many mm short and how far below the Ø40 bar's centreline; above it, the bar's top and centreline.
- `a_uc100s_profile_soft_limits_are_kept_too`: on a UC100 the profile's X (0 to 390), Z (−55 to 0) and A (−180° to 180°) soft limits refuse 0.01 mm outside and pass 0.01 mm inside, though the machine's own travel is wider; a profile that cannot be read refuses the check.
- `the_holder_coming_down_onto_the_bar_is_refused_with_how_much`: with 50 mm of stick-out and the 2 mm gap, a cut 50.01 mm under the Ø40 bar's top puts the holder 0.01 mm into the bar and one 48.01 mm under it leaves the holder 1.99 mm over it, inside the gap: both refused with those numbers; 47.99 mm is not; beside the bar past the holder's radius and the gap nothing is said.
- `a_cut_too_low_whose_retract_is_too_high_is_refused_at_both_ends`: one move from machine Z 0.01 to Z −300.01 is refused at both ends of the Z travel.
- `an_index_move_inside_what_a_swings_is_refused`: an index move from A0 (where setup 1 left the machine) 0.01 mm under the 55.9 mm the bar, the part and the jaws reach is refused, 0.01 mm over it is not.
- `too_little_room_over_what_a_swings_is_refused_with_the_numbers`: 4.99 mm between what A swings and the top of the Z travel is refused with the centreline, the reach and the room; 5.01 mm is not.
- `the_assumed_geometry_leaves_no_room_over_the_largest_part` (LIO-184's comment): the CNC4030 + rotary as assumed (centreline Z −40, Ø80 at most, 55 mm of Z) with a Ø80 bar in the chuck: at A0 alone the setup is refused, the jaws reaching machine Z 35.66, and the contour's retract is out of reach above the bar's top at Z 0; turning to A90 is refused when generated, at machine Z 50.66.
- `a_cut_at_a_90_into_a_jaw_is_refused_at_that_angle`: a cut down into the place jaw 3 is at A90 (up and to +Y) is refused at A 90° naming jaw 3; the same cut at A0, where jaw 1 is straight up, is clear.
- `an_index_move_through_the_jaws_is_refused_over_its_turn`: A turning 0 to 90 with the tool 30 mm over the axis is refused over its sweep.
- `a_setup_that_is_not_indexed_is_not_checked_here`: setup 1, the chuck at A0, gets none of these problems; every 3-axis test passes with its messages unchanged.
- Not validated: anything on the machine; the program a post makes with A (LIO-193) must make its links between operations as checked here (straight up, across and down, or by machine Z home).

## Rotary Z reach counts the stick-out and the spindle nose (LIO-204, 18 October 2026)

The holder was the only part over the stick-out checked against the bar's top, and the spindle above it was taken as only as wide as the holder: the machine had no spindle nose.

- `rotary_check::tests::the_holder_coming_down_onto_the_bar_is_refused_with_how_much`: the holder 0.01 mm into the Ø40 bar and 1.99 mm over it (inside the 2 mm gap) are refused, saying the tool needs 2.01 and 0.01 mm more stick-out; 47.99 mm under the top is clear. Beside the bar 0.01 mm inside the Ø65 nose's radius and the gap the nose comes down onto it; 0.01 mm outside, nothing is said.
- `a_cut_out_of_the_holders_reach_is_reachable_with_more_stick_out`: a plunge 55 mm under the bar's top with 50 mm of stick-out puts the holder 5 mm into it ("needs 7 mm more stick-out"); 56.99 mm of stick-out is refused by 0.01 mm, 57.01 mm is clear.
- `the_spindle_nose_coming_down_onto_the_bar_is_refused_on_both_sides`: with a Ø10 holder 10 mm long beside the bar, the Ø65 nose 60 mm over the tip hits the bar 0.01 mm before the cut's bottom, or is 1.99 mm over it inside the gap: both refused with the stick-out needed; 0.01 mm higher is clear, and so is the first with a 12.02 mm holder. Without a holder length and a spindle nose on the machine, 10 mm and Ø65 are taken and the message says both were assumed; 55 mm beside the axis an Ø80 nose reaches the bar where the Ø65 does not.
- `chuck::tests::the_spindle_nose_is_kept_off_the_jaws_and_the_tailstock_on_both_sides`: the nose 0.01 mm inside its radius and the gap of jaw 1's front and of the tailstock body's side (at an angle and while A turns) is refused as "spindle nose", 0.01 mm outside is clear; the tool and holder alone are clear at both; a holder wider than the nose is taken as the spindle's width.
- `rotary_check::tests::a_chuck_setup_at_a0_keeps_the_spindle_nose_off_the_jaws`: a setup that is not indexed, the bar in the chuck at A0, plunging beside jaw 1's front: within the Ø65 nose's radius, the gap and the 0.01 mm blend it is refused as "the spindle nose hits the chuck jaw 1" with the assumed holder length, 0.02 mm farther it is not; the tool and holder are never said to hit.
- `indexed::tests::an_indexed_setups_cuts_are_checked_against_the_chuck_at_each_angle`: the contour 90 mm out of the jaws now brings the assumed nose (10 mm of holder) down beside jaw 1 and says the holder length was assumed; with a 40 mm holder it is clear.
- `model::rotary_unit::a_machine_file_saved_before_the_spindle_nose_existed_takes_the_assumed_one` and `a_tool_without_a_holder_length_takes_a_short_assumed_one`: an old machine file loads with Ø65 × 185 mm, marked assumed, and no issues; a given nose is kept and a zero one refused; a tool without a holder length takes 10 mm, assumed, and a negative one is refused.
- Every 3-axis test passes with its messages unchanged (the vise and clamp checks keep the holder upward without limit).
- Not validated: anything on the machine. The Z travel check is still of the tip as the machine's Z is set for it: how much longer or shorter a tool is than the one the A centreline was probed with is not known, so it does not move the travel.

## Indexed programs: G0 A alone, A in the replay (LIO-193, 18 October 2026)

The post refused every machine but a 3-axis one, refused indexed setups, and the replay read X, Y and Z only.

- `post::tests::controller_and_axes_are_refused_before_any_output`: a 4-axis machine with A around X posts, in Indexed (3+1) or Continuous 4-axis mode, with LinuxCNC or the UC100's settings; a 5-axis machine and a 4-axis one with B around Y are refused (a run has taken it since LIO-192).
- `an_indexed_program_retracts_approaches_then_turns_a_alone_and_cuts`: the golden Facing pass made at A90, from A0, posts exactly: the header's A note (A90, the sense of A, the centreline at machine Y140 Z-40 assumed, not measured, and A0), then the tool change's retract, the spindle start, the approach over the first cut, `G0 A90.000` there (LIO-209: A never turns where the tool was left), the descent, the cut, the last retract and `G0 A0.000` before M2, and it replays. With a second angle, `G0 A180.000` follows the retract to the swing height, alone, and the program turns A three times: A90, A180, A0.
- `the_replay_fails_a_program_whose_a_is_not_the_simulated_a`: A-90 or A89 in place of A90 fails with "does not match the simulated A90.000", a missing `G0 A90.000` fails, A on a line with X and Y or on a G1 is not read, and an A word in a 3-axis program fails.
- `a_turning_during_a_move_is_refused_before_any_output`: a feed whose A differs from the turn before is refused as a continuous A move, naming both angles and where; an index move that also moves Z is refused too.
- `indexed::tests::an_indexed_setup_exports_g0_a_alone_after_each_retract_and_replays`: a contour on a part in a Ø40 bar in the chuck, indexed to A 90 and 180 with the centreline at Z -150, exports through `post_job` (checks and replay): A90 after the tool change's `G53 G0 Z0` and the `G0 X Y` over the first cut, A180 at the index height (the bar's 20 mm + 15 mm over the centreline, LIO-209), A0 after the last `G53 G0 Z0`, no other line with A; the header says the centreline is assumed. The workholding check has nothing to say: each angle's cuts are checked against the jaws at that angle.
- `a_turn_of_a_below_the_swing_height_is_refused`: a third setup indexed to A90 after setup 2 at A90 has no index move of its own, so its program turns A to A90 after its retract; it enters from the swing height (LIO-205), and exports at machine Z home; at the clearance plane, now over the bar and not the jaws (LIO-209), a program that starts with the tool wherever it was is refused as on a 3-axis setup ("is not above the chuck jaw"). Its plans checked with the operation's clearance raised from 15 to 40 mm put the swing above that plane: at the clearance plane the turn is refused, "where nothing A swings reaches it", at machine Z home it is not.
- `each_angles_cuts_are_checked_against_the_jaws_at_that_angle`: the part 20 mm from the jaws, indexed to A 90 and 180, is not exported: the rotary checks find the holder within the jaws' gap, and the workholding check says the same.
- `an_indexed_setup_that_the_rotary_checks_refuse_is_not_exported`: the exported job, with the machine's Y travel cut to 150 mm, puts the bar's cuts beyond it (the A centreline at machine Y140); only `rotary_check::problems` sees that, and every one of its problems is in the export's refusal.
- `an_indexed_program_does_not_touch_off_on_the_turned_stock`: in touch-off mode an indexed setup with a Stock top center zero says to keep its clamping's zero and set each tool on the fixed reference surface, and never to touch off on the stock top.
- Found in review: the base setup's program does not turn A, and an indexed program ended at its last angle, so running the base setup again after it would have cut at that angle. Every program that turns A now ends at A0.
- The 3-axis golden programs and every other post test are unchanged.
- Found in testing: the same contour with the part 20 mm from the jaws and starting at A0 is refused: the holder comes within the gap of the jaws, and the kernel's approach at A0 came down over the stock's corner, in the chuck body (fixed by LIO-205).
- Not validated: anything on a real LinuxCNC machine; running on the UC100 (LIO-192).

## Running indexed setups on the UC100 (LIO-192, 18 October 2026)

A run refused every machine that was not 3-axis (`post`'s `not_run` check, removed by this change) and lowered X, Y and Z only.

- `run::tests::an_indexed_tool_run_turns_a_alone_at_its_work_offset`: a facing pass at A90 then A180, lowered with A's work offset 5° from machine A30: the first retract moves Z alone, the turns are rapids of A alone to machine A95, A185 and A5 with X, Y and Z where the move before left them, the turn to A180 is its index block at the swing height; a move whose A differs from the last turn is refused, `combined` catches an action that moves A with X; a 3-axis tool run leaves A where it is.
- `control::tests::hold_resume_and_stop_work_while_a_turns_alone` (simulated UC100): Hold in the middle of a turn to A180 stops A short of it (after the controller's buffer and deceleration) and keeps it still; Resume finishes the turn at 180 within 0.03°; Stop partway back stops A between, with its machine position kept. X, Y and Z do not move.
- `control::tests::an_indexed_bar_runs_at_four_angles_turning_a_alone` (simulated UC100): a contour round a 20 × 20 part in a Ø40 bar in the chuck, indexed to A 90, 180, 270 and 0 in one job. Cycle Start admits it only on a measured centreline: assumed, stale after an E-stop on the Control page or after a remount in the library, each refuses with the reason and Control → Rotary A; the work zero 0.03 mm off the centreline is refused, 0.01 mm accepted; a 100° A work offset (machine A 370°) is refused. The lowered actions turn A alone to 90, 180, 270 and 0, each at or above the swing height; the first after the Z-only retract, before the spindle, its dwell and the approach. Hold in the turn to 180 stops the machine, Resume finishes; the job ends done with A at 0, having stood at each angle. Then the same job at A 0, 90, 180 and 270, started at A 0 (the angle the machine is at, LIO-205): the run's check admits it and a LinuxCNC copy exports; its tool comes down over the first cut from at or above the swing height, straight down to its feed, A turns alone to 90, 180, 270 and back to 0, and it ends done, having stood at each angle.
- Every 3-axis run test is unchanged.
- Found: started at the A the machine is at, an indexed setup's first approach came down over the stock's corner in the chuck; fixed by LIO-205, and the run and export now both admit it.
- Not validated: anything on the real machine; the live carve and the 3D view at angles other than the first.

## Simulation and 3D views with A (LIO-190, 18 October 2026)

The simulation already cut each angle's moves in that angle's frame (LIO-189), but the Job view drew the part, the stock and the chuck at the setup's first angle, the live carve was not drawn for a tool run that turns A, and the machine view had no rotary unit.

- `indexed::tests::a_square_milled_at_0_90_180_270_comes_out_square_on_the_axis` (LIO-189, unchanged) is the simulation's key test: the square on the bar's axis in the final stock.
- `indexed::tests::the_rest_analysis_at_the_last_angle_sees_what_the_earlier_angles_removed`: the stock as A turns to 270° (cut at 0, 90 and 180° only), seen in the frame at 270°: the bar round the square is removed on all four sides for `rest::Removed` and the volume, the square kept; `RestStock::top` over the square's +Z side starts at the square's top, not the bar's; after the job `uncut` leaves none of the square's four sides under stock.
- `indexed::tests::the_simulated_a_turns_through_each_index_move`: a quarter of the way through each index move A is 22.5° on from the angle before, at its end on the next angle, 270° at the end, 0° back at the start; a 3-axis simulation has no A.
- `viewport::tests::an_indexed_setups_part_and_stock_turn_to_the_a_shown`: the job played to its end is drawn at 270°; with the machine at A0 the part is where the setup places it, at A90 every point is turned 90° about the centreline; the camera's fit is the same at every angle and the picture at 45° is not the one at 0°; halfway through the first index move the view is at 45°.
- `machine_view::tests::the_rotary_unit_stands_where_the_library_puts_it_and_its_chuck_turns_with_a`: the chuck's face at the library's chuck X, round its centreline Y and Z, the table and the rotary head's foot the centre height under it; the jaws at A90 are those at A0 turned 90° about the centreline (+Y up to +Z); the head and the tailstock do not move; the tool stays above the table and the beam above the chuck; without the unit the machine is as before.
- `uncut::tests::a_round_bars_box_corners_are_no_stock`: points in a round bar's box but outside the bar were counted as stock left.
- `control::tests::an_indexed_bar_runs_at_four_angles_turning_a_alone` (simulated UC100, extended): after the run at A 90, 180, 270 and 0 the live carve is a volume, not the top view, and leaves the square on the bar's axis, the bar round it gone.
- `smoke::tests::render_the_four_axis_scene` (ignored, for review): the Job view after that job and halfway through the turn from 270° to 0°, in ISO, end-on and close, and the machine view with the rotary unit at A0 and A30, inspected: the square comes out square-on at A0 and diamond-wise mid-turn, with the jaws turned.
- Every 3-axis test and pixel digest is unchanged.
- Not validated: anything on the real machine; the live carve's voxels are as coarse as the simulation's volume (at most 96 a side).

## Index height under the tool (LIO-209, 18 October 2026)

On phase-2 before this, every index move of an indexed setup turned A with the tool above the farthest the chuck's jaws reach, wherever the tool was along X. On the library CNC4030 + rotary (55 mm Z, the A centreline at machine Z −40, a Ø100 chuck) the jaws reach 55.9 mm, so that height was machine Z +30.9 with 15 mm of clearance and +15.9 with none: every indexed setup was refused by export and by the Run panel. The tests put the centreline at Z −150 on a machine with 300 mm of Z, so they never saw it.

Now the height is `Document::index_height`: what A swings at any angle under the cutter, the holder and the spindle nose (each its radius and the setup's gap to either side) over the X span where the operation keeps the tool (`indexed::cut_span`), less how far over the tip each part starts, plus the clearance. Generation, export's `index_problems`, the rotary checks and so the run all take it. A program turns A for a tool run only after the approach over its first point (retract, spindle, `G0 X Y`, `G0 A`, down), so A never turns where the tool was left.

- `control::tests::a_four_angle_bar_job_on_the_library_rotary_machine_exports_and_runs` (library machine, simulated UC100 with its 390 × 280 × 55 mm travel and A ±180°): a contour round a part in a Ø40 bar at A 0, 90, 180 and −90 turns A at machine Z −5 (the bar's 20 mm + 15 mm); it exports as LinuxCNC at 100 mm stick-out and, with a shorter part nearer the bar's end, at 60 mm; Cycle Start admits it, every turn of A is at that height inside the travel, and the job runs to the end through all four angles back at A0. A Ø80 bar is refused by export and the run: "at machine Z 15 with the tool over X …", "(the A centreline at Z -40 + 40 mm, as far as the stock reaches from it at any angle, + 15 mm clearance)", "15 mm above the top of the machine's 55 mm Z travel".
- `indexed::tests::a_tool_over_the_jaws_lifts_above_them_and_one_over_the_bar_above_the_bar` (Z −150): a part 3 mm from the jaws puts the Ø12 tool over them: both index moves wait 15 mm over the jaws' 55.9 mm, the whole swing's height. The same part 60 mm along the bar waits 15 mm over the bar's 20 mm.
- `an_index_retract_above_the_z_travel_is_refused_with_its_numbers`: at Z −40 a part over the jaws is refused at machine Z 30.9 "with the tool over X …" and told to keep the cuts farther from the jaws; a part 20 mm out of them is generated at machine Z −5.
- `rotary_check::tests::too_little_room_over_what_a_swings_is_refused_with_the_numbers` and `the_assumed_geometry_leaves_no_room_over_the_largest_part`: the room to lift the tool is the operation's, over the bar it cuts (20 mm, or 40 mm for the Ø80 bar on the library machine, 0 mm under the top of Z).
- The LIO-187 sweep check is unchanged and still runs over every index move and its approach: `an_index_move_nearer_the_jaws_than_the_gap_is_refused` and `an_index_move_through_the_jaws_is_refused_over_its_turn` pass as they were. The tests of LIO-205, LIO-192 and the golden indexed program now expect the tool's height over the bar and the turn after the approach.
- `smoke::tests::render_the_index_height_on_the_library_rotary` (ignored) draws the job on the library machine after its four angles and halfway through its last index move, and the machine view with the tool where A turns.
- Not validated: anything on the real machine. The height takes the stock the setup starts from, not what earlier angles cut away, so it is never lower than the bar.

## A run's way out from where the machine is (LIO-213, 18 October 2026)

On phase-2 before this, Cycle Start on a setup in the chuck had one way in from wherever the machine was (up, across, A turning, down) and refused it when any move came within the gap of the chuck, even where another way was clear: from beside the chuck's body at machine X10 Y205 Z−30 on the library CNC4030 + rotary, the way across passed over the body, which reaches 10 mm above the top of Z. An exported program in clearance-plane retract mode could move down over the chuck on its first retract, since export cannot know where the operator left the tool. The ticket's own case, the tool at machine X0 Y0 Z0, turned out to be clear already in the library test's geometry: the way across from there passes 50 mm or more off the chuck's body (LIO-212's note that it was refused does not hold).

Now, when the usual way in is refused, the run tries others, checked by the same `Chuck::way_in` (each move at the A it is made at, the sweep of a turn included), and takes the first clear one (`run::way_out`): after the retract, A turned alone so that no jaw stands up (`Chuck::jaws_down`: with 3 jaws, jaws at ±60° and 180°, the nearest angle each way inside A's range, the shorter turn first), the tool moved along X alone to the first point's X before it crosses in Y, or both. The run sends the way that was checked. When none is clear the refusal stays, with a line saying how to jog clear: up to the retract height, A to an angle with no jaw up, then X and Y over the first cut. Export and a run refuse a setup in the chuck with clearance-plane retracts. I chose to refuse rather than emit `G53 G0 Z0` before the first retract: someone who picks the clearance plane does not want machine-coordinate moves in the program, a `G53` move in that mode would be a third retract model for the run, its checks and export to agree on, and a jaw's reach is above the clearance plane on most rotary setups anyway; machine Z home is the default. The program's end is unchanged: the last retract goes straight up over the last cut (to the top of Z on machine home), then A turns back to A0 there; that point is over the stock, beyond the jaws by at least the gap, and the next Cycle Start goes across from it by the usual way.

- `control::tests::a_run_on_the_library_rotary_machine_starts_from_machine_home_and_ends_where_it_starts_again` (library machine, simulated UC100): from machine X0 Y0 Z0 A0 the way in is across at Z0 and down, admitted, and the job runs to its end. It ends at the top of Z over its last cut with A at 0.03° or less of A0; a new run of the setup from there is admitted and goes across to the first cut. From beside the chuck's body at X10 Y205 Z−30 the way is up to Z0, X alone at Y205 to the first cut's X, Y alone, down; it is admitted, runs to its end and ends at the same place. Over the jaws at X35 Y140 Z0 with jaw 1 at A 30°, Cycle Start is refused on the way across (chuck jaw 1) and the refusal says "Jog the tool up to machine Z 0.000, A to machine A 60.000° (no jaw up), X to machine X …": A turned first sweeps the jaw past the tool, X first passes along it. With clearance-plane retracts, `run::check` and LinuxCNC export both refuse with "a setup in the chuck retracts to machine Z home".
- `control::tests::a_run_on_the_library_rotary_machine_retracts_up_as_export_does_and_checks_its_way_in` (changed): from beside the chuck's body at X10 Y205 Z−30 the way in is now up, X alone, Y alone, down, admitted from the first operation and from the second. Its other cases are unchanged: over the jaws at X25 Z0 A30 the tool starts within the chuck body's gap, so every way out is refused.
- `run::tests::a_way_out_turns_a_then_moves_x_alone_before_the_approach`: a way out lowers to the retract, A alone, X alone, then Y over the first point, every later move at the new A, no move turning A with X, Y or Z; the usual way moves X and Y together; a program without a chuck takes the usual way.
- `rotary_check::tests::the_angles_with_no_jaw_up_are_the_nearest_each_way_inside_a_range`: with 3 jaws, jaw 1 up at A0, the angles from A0 are −60° and 60°, from 30° 60° then −60°, from −100° −60° then −180°, from 150° 180° then 60°; none from 60° or −180°; A's range at A's work offset leaves one; Direction Negative with jaw 1 at 30° gives −30° and 90°; 4 jaws give ±45°.
- Not validated: anything on the real machine. No position of the library test needs A turned first: a tool close enough to a jaw for the way across to hit it is within its gap at the start, and the sweep of a turn includes the angle it starts at.

## A run's retract and its way in on the rotary (LIO-212, 18 October 2026)

On phase-2 before this, a UC100 run always retracted to the setup clearance plane (`PostSettings::for_uc100`), while the rotary checks took the machine's Safe retract (machine Z home on the library CNC4030 + rotary): the checks passed a run that export refused with clearance-plane retracts. From the tool parked over the jaws at machine X25 Z0 A30 the run's first move was the "retract" down to Z −5, then across at whatever A the machine was at, unchecked. A machine whose Direction was Negative could never find A zero.

Now a UC100 run on a machine with A retracts as its Safe retract says, as export does (machine Z 0, the top of Z, for machine Z home), and the rotary checks take the retract from the same settings (`PostSettings::for_holding`), so the run, its checks and export cannot disagree. A retract never moves Z down: a tool above the retract height stays there, and the approach after it moves X and Y alone at that height. On a setup in the chuck, Cycle Start lowers the way in from wherever the machine is (up, across, A turning, down) and checks each move against the chuck, its jaws, the rotary head, the tailstock and the stock at the A it is made at (`run::way_in_problems`, `rotary_check::Chuck::way_in`); the same before a later operation and after a Stop. A run that retracts to machine home is refused when the profile's Z soft maximum is not 0. A zero takes the machine's Direction.

- `control::tests::a_run_on_the_library_rotary_machine_retracts_up_as_export_does_and_checks_its_way_in` (library machine, simulated UC100): the run's and export's (touch-off) settings are equal, retracting to machine home; with clearance-plane retracts the run's check and export both refuse "is not above the chuck jaw". From over the bar's free end at the top of Z, and 10 mm under it (where the first retract lifts Z alone to machine Z 0), the run's moves equal the exported program's replayed moves (`ngc::replay`) at the work offset, within 0.002 mm. Parked over the jaws at machine X25 Z0 A30 the way in is across at Z0, A turned there, down (no move down first), and Cycle Start is refused: the tool within the 2 mm gap of the chuck body on the way across at A 30°. Beside the chuck's body at machine X10 Y205 Z−30 the way in is up, across, down, refused on the way across at A 0°, from the first operation and from the second; 20 mm along X it is clear. From X150 Z−10 at A −120 it is up, across, A turned, down, and admitted.
- `run::tests::a_retract_below_the_tool_does_not_move_it_down`: a 3-axis tool run started above its clearance plane stays at its height, crosses there and comes down; started below it goes up to the plane as before; retracting to machine home it goes to machine Z 0 and stays there from a start at it.
- `control::tests::a_zero_refuses_an_a_that_turns_backwards` (extended): with Direction Negative, an A that turns the bar by −A levels each flat at the opposite angle, and one that turns it by the right-hand rule is refused, naming the Direction; `Rotary::of` takes the sign from the machine.
- `a_four_angle_bar_job_on_the_library_rotary_machine_exports_and_runs` now starts its run with the tool over the bar's free end: from the simulator's machine zero (X0 Y0 Z0) the way across passes over the chuck's body, which reaches 10 mm above the top of Z, and is refused.
- `run::tests::a_tool_run_lowers_to_machine_coordinates_at_the_work_offset` now starts 3 mm under the clearance plane, its expected actions word for word as before: it started 23 mm above the plane and was moved down to it, the 3-axis case of this defect. `an_indexed_bar_runs_at_four_angles_turning_a_alone` lowers from 20 mm under the top of Z, its retract going to machine Z 0.
- 3-axis: a UC100 run on a 3-axis machine still retracts to the clearance plane and lowers exactly as before, except that a tool started above the plane is no longer moved down to it first; export's G-code is unchanged. The first retract of an exported program starts wherever the operator left the tool, which export cannot know: on machine Z home it is `G53 G0 Z0`, never down; at the clearance plane, the highest point of the program, every later retract is up.
- Not validated: anything on the real machine.

## The A centreline's Z and each tool's length (LIO-207, 18 October 2026)

On phase-2 before this, the Z sent to the UC100 for an indexed cut at work Z `w` was `w` + the Z work offset, and Cycle Start wanted that offset within 0.02 mm of the centreline's machine Z (`a_centre_z`) + the work zero's height over the plan's centreline. The centreline's Z is where the probe's tip was when it was probed, and the UC100 has no tool length offset, so that offset is right for a tool as long as the probe only. A cutter 15 mm longer, with the work zero set as the refusal message said (G10 from the centreline's numbers), cut every indexed pass 15 mm deep, and its A 180 feature 30 mm off its A 0 one; a second tool touched off with Set Z from reference, which is right, was refused as off the centreline. Now the probe touches a Z reference with the centreline (**Touch the Z reference**, kept as `a_centre_ref`), each tool that cuts on A is touched off on it (**Touch off on the A reference**), which measures its length against the probe and sets work Z for it, and Cycle Start wants the offset on the centreline plus that length, refusing a tool without one.

- `control::tests::an_indexed_cut_is_as_deep_with_a_tool_longer_or_shorter_than_the_probe` (simulated UC100, the simulator's blocks raised by the tool's extra length): the centreline (machine Y 140 Z −150) with a 50 mm probe, which touches a gauge block at machine Z −200; Save refuses before that touch. With the work zero on the probe's centreline and a cutter 15 mm longer (50 mm out of a 15 mm holder), Cycle Start's only refusal is the missing touch-off. Touched off through a 5 mm plate, the tool is found 15 mm longer (within a step, 0.05 mm) and work Z is set 15 mm higher; every cut at A 90 and A 180 is then as deep under the bar's real top and as far from its axis as planned (within 0.06 mm), where the probe's work zero would have put it 15 mm deeper. Another tool touched off last refuses it again, and so does a length measured from a reference other than the one now saved. The job runs to its end. The same for a cutter 10 mm shorter (40 mm, no holder), not run.
- `rotary_check::tests::the_z_travel_is_checked_with_the_tools_length_against_the_probes`: with a 50 mm probe length a tool 50 mm out of a 15 mm holder is checked 15 mm higher: a tip 15.01 mm under the top of the Z travel is refused, 14.99 clear, and the bottom likewise; with a 60 mm probe and no holder, 10 mm lower. Without a probe length the tool is taken as long as the probe, as before, and the message says so.
- `the_a_centreline_is_probed_its_line_checked_saved_and_stale_after_an_e_stop`: Save refuses until the probe has touched the Z reference, then keeps it with the centreline.
- The LIO-192 indexed run tests give the library a reference and the tool a touch-off of the probe's length; their other checks are unchanged. Every 3-axis test passes unchanged, and a setup at A0 that does not turn A is not asked for the touch-off.
- Not validated: anything on the machine. The travel check's length is the library's stick-out and holder length (an assumed holder is said so) against the typed probe length; only the touch-off sets a cut's depth.

## Rotary calibration: the A centreline, A zero and the line to the tailstock (LIO-186, 18 October 2026)

Indexed cuts need A's centreline in machine Y and Z: an error of e mm puts a feature cut at A 180° off by 2e. The driver's simulator could only place still boxes, so UC100-driver PR #2 added `SimBars`: round bars on A that turn with it, on an axis that may be off and tilted, running out, with a flat (`a_bar_on_the_a_axis_is_touched_where_it_turns_to`, `a_flat_on_the_bar_turns_with_a` there).

- `the_a_centreline_is_probed_its_line_checked_saved_and_stale_after_an_e_stop` (`control.rs`, simulated UC100): a Ø20 bar on an A axis 0.3 mm and −0.2 mm off the assumed centreline, tilted 0.4 and −0.2 µm per mm and running out 0.03 and −0.02 mm, started 0.4 mm off its middle: Probe the centreline finds Y and Z within 0.01 mm of the axis there, the runout within 0.01 mm, 20 mm across, A back where it started and the tip lifted clear; the line to the tailstock 165 mm on is measured to within 0.01 mm of the tilt and warned over 0.05 mm; Save keeps it as measured for the app and warns that a Ø80 part leaves no room to retract (LIO-191); an E-stop marks it stale and drops the session's centreline.
- `a_zero_levels_a_flat_and_zeroes_a_there`: refused until the centreline is known; then a flat 2° off level at A −45° is levelled by turning A, and A is zeroed at machine A −47° within 0.05°; the simulated A turns the convention's way, so the direction check passes.
