- `a_zero_refuses_an_a_that_turns_backwards` (LIO-210): the driver's bar, its centre 1 mm up at A 0, is 1 mm toward −Y at A 90 (+A the right-hand rule about +X). Walked over a bar with a flat at 2°, 0.4°, 0° and −3° from level, A zero levels it at −2°, −0.4°, 0° and 3° within 0.03° (a level flat after a 1° turn and back); with A wired backwards (the bar turned by −A) each is refused with "A turns backwards", toward +Y, Reverse the direction, and that it does not adapt. The centreline is probed at A and A + 180°, the same either way.
- `a_calibration_never_turns_a_before_it_is_set`: every calibration is refused, and none starts, while A has no machine position, and without a bar diameter.
- `the_centreline_turns_a_only_with_the_tip_up_clear`: walked over an exact model of the bar (the driver's `Bar::entry`), no rapid or guarded move touches it, every touch does, A turns only in moves of A alone with the tip at least 5 mm over the highest top touched, and the centre and runout come out exact.
- `the_retract_room_over_the_largest_part_is_checked`: the default centreline (Z −40) with a Ø80 part leaves 0 mm under the top of Z and is warned; 10 mm is not.
- Not validated: the Rotary A panel in the window, and anything on the real machine or with a real bar.

## The AI planner proposes a chuck and indexed setups on the rotary (LIO-194, 18 October 2026)

- `a_rotary_machine_gets_the_axis_in_its_report_and_prompt_a_3_axis_one_nothing_new`: on the rotary machine the part report carries `rotary` (centre height 70, Ø80 × 250 mm fit, A ±360°, grip Ø5 to Ø80, the centreline `assumed`) and the prompt a ROTARY A AXIS section; on the 3-axis block the report has no `rotary`, the prompt is the old text word for word and the schema is the old one.
- `the_rotary_schema_asks_for_the_chuck_and_the_angles_and_stays_strict`: with the axis the schema has `chuck` (an object or null) and each setup's `a_angles` (numbers or null), strict at every level; a reply without them reads as null.
- `a_chuck_seats_setup_1s_stock_and_an_indexed_setup_is_made_as_the_setups_page_makes_it`, `a_round_bar_is_seated_on_its_axis_and_a_part_that_does_not_fit_is_refused`: the proposal's box or bar, jaws, stick-out and tailstock land in setup 1's fields with the chuck's defaults, the part sits on the axis in the stick-out; the indexed setup has Reposition "Index rotary axis" and its angles, takes setup 1's offset, chuck and zero, and a part wider than the bar or longer than the stick-out is refused with the numbers.
- `a_stock_the_chuck_cannot_hold_is_refused_with_the_chucks_own_message`, `the_orientation_of_an_indexed_setup_is_the_one_its_first_angle_brings_up`, `an_indexed_setup_is_refused_for_what_the_setups_page_refuses`, `a_machine_without_a_rotary_axis_refuses_a_chuck_and_angles`: a box in a 3-jaw chuck, a stock wider than the jaws grip, an angle outside the A range, a repeated or empty list, an orientation that the first angle does not bring up (A 90 brings +Y up, 180 −Z, 270 −Y), surfaces at several angles, a holder on an indexed setup, an indexed first setup, no chuck and a 3-axis machine each come back as a problem.
- `check_plan_refuses_a_stock_too_big_for_z_and_a_tailstock_that_cannot_reach_with_the_checks_messages`: the square bar's plan (a 24 mm box in a 4-jaw chuck, contoured at A 0, 90, 180 and 270) passes; a 44 mm box is refused with the rotary check's message that the retract over what A swings under the tool (its corners, 31.11 mm out, on the fixture's centreline at Z −45 since LIO-209) is 1.11 mm above the top of the 100 mm Z travel; a tailstock that cannot reach the stock gets the chuck's message.
- `a_square_bar_is_planned_as_a_chuck_and_four_indexed_angles_after_a_stock_too_big_for_z`: through the fake OpenAI (`fake_openai::rotary`), the first reply (the 44 mm box) fails with that message, which the second request carries back to the model together with the ROTARY A AXIS section and the rotary schema; the second reply passes, and the job is setup 1 in the chuck and one setup indexed to 0, 90, 180 and 270 on setup 1's work offset.
- Not validated: a real model's plans on a rotary machine; a part whose long axis is not along X in any orientation.

## The AI planner faces the four sides of a square on the rotary (LIO-214, 18 October 2026)

The fake rotary plan (LIO-194) contoured the square bar at A 0, 90, 180 and 270, which since LIO-211 leaves a 14 mm web and the middle of every side uncut: the planner's result was not the square the demo job makes. A Face of an indexed setup with several angles may now name surfaces (`proposal::faces_each_angle`): each must be the top plane of an orientation one of the setup's angles brings up, and every angle needs one, otherwise the proposal is refused naming the surface or the orientation. It is built as the UI's facing of a square (`indexed::tests::faced_square`): boundary "Selected geometry", containment "Tool center on boundary", and `indexed::on_top` faces at each angle the faces that are up there. The prompt's ROTARY A AXIS section says how to ask for it; any other operation with surfaces at several angles is refused as before, and nothing changes for a 3-axis machine or a one-angle setup.

- `an_indexed_setup_is_refused_for_what_the_setups_page_refuses`: a Contour with surfaces at four angles keeps its message; a Face with only the +Z top plane is refused naming +Y, a Face with a surface that is on top at no angle is refused naming it; the four sides build as one Face with the tool's centre on the boundary.
- `check_plan_refuses_a_stock_too_big_for_z_and_a_tailstock_that_cannot_reach_with_the_checks_messages`: the scenario's plan (a 24 mm box in a 4-jaw chuck, its four sides faced at A 0, 90, 180 and 270) passes every rotary check, part-off and web included, and the stock covers only the part's end face on the bar (−X).
- `a_square_bar_is_planned_as_a_chuck_and_four_indexed_angles_after_a_stock_too_big_for_z`: the final job's second setup is one Face of four surfaces; the extension request names only the −X end face as covered, and the same plan stands.
- Not validated: a real model's choice to ask for it, and a Pocket bounded to the face (not offered).

## Refusing a cut that parts the bar off in the chuck (LIO-208, 18 October 2026)

On phase-2 before this, the demo 4-angle job (`bar_job` + `contour`, A 90, 180, 270 and 0°) ran the Ø12 cutter across the bar's full width at X−46.06 and X−3.94 down to the part's bottom, 10 mm past the A axis: after A 180° nothing joined the part to the gripped bar, yet every check passed and the program was written. `rotary_check::problems` now flood-fills the in-process stock's voxels (6-connected) from those the jaws grip (and the tailstock's centre's, when it supports the bar) after each angle's cuts of each operation, in every setup on the bar in the chuck, and refuses a piece that was joined and is not, at least three voxels thick. The plan check, export (`post::checked`, a plain chuck setup included) and the run's admission (`run::ready`) say it once each.

- The demo job's test (renamed `rotary_check::tests::a_contour_to_the_axis_that_cuts_the_part_off_the_bar_is_refused_where_it_does` by LIO-211, which cuts to the axis on the 1 mm cells; see below): the demo job is refused once, at A 180°, naming the contour and "nothing of the Ø40 mm bar on the A axis from X-52.5 to X-40", the 30 mm piece from X-40 to X-10 (the cutter's edges at X−52.06 and X−40.06, to the 1.25 mm voxels).
- The web's test (removed by LIO-211, where 2 mm over the axis is refused as too weak and 7 mm over it passes: `a_web_too_thin_to_hold_is_refused_with_its_size_and_a_sound_one_passes`, below): cut to 2 mm over the axis (a 4 mm square web, the demo job now) and to 1 mm over it (a 2 mm web, 1.6 voxels) passes; to the axis (no web) is refused at A 270°, and so is 0.5 mm over it, a 1 mm web that holds no voxel's centre.
- `the_export_the_plan_check_and_the_run_refuse_a_cut_that_parts_the_bar`: the demo job's export refuses with the message, the plan check says it once, and `run::ready` on a UC100 profile says it once; with the web, none of them does.
- `a_cut_through_in_the_first_setup_in_the_chuck_is_refused_too`: setup 1, at A0 without A, contoured down to the bar's bottom, is refused "at A 0°" with the same X range, and its export too; the same cut in a 3-axis setup with the stock in a vise is not this check's.
- The jobs that parted the bar in other tests (the demo scene, `posting_bar_job`, the UC100 run tests, the fake planner's square bar) now cut to 2 mm over the axis; with A 0 and 90° or A 90 and 180° a cut to the part's bottom leaves the part on a zero-thickness edge, which is cut through too. The carved square of `an_indexed_bar_runs_at_four_angles_turning_a_alone` is checked outside the 2 mm the web leaves round the planes through the axis. Every 3-axis test passes unchanged.
- Not validated: a web thinner than a voxel (counted as cut through), a loose sliver under three voxels thick (taken for a chip), and anything on the machine.

## The part-off check judges pieces by size and webs by what they hold (LIO-211, 18 October 2026)

The LIO-208 check counted a loose piece only when one of its voxels had all six neighbours filled, so anything under three of the simulation's voxels thick in one direction passed as a chip, however big; it took any web it saw for one that holds; and it held everything behind the jaws' front, whatever its radius. `rotary_check::parted` now cuts its own grid of `CELL` = 1 mm cells (`volume::Volume::finer` + `carve`, with the simulation's cuts), seeds what is held from the voxels inside the jaws' grip (chuck face to jaws' front, within 1.5 cells of where they close) and the tailstock's centre, refuses a loose piece over `CHIP` = 2 mm long or `CHIP_VOLUME` = 2 mm³, and checks every section of what stays joined out of the jaws, as far as the angle's cuts reach: at least `WEB` = 3 mm across (the width of the rectangle of its area and least second moment), and under the operation's largest push (`forces::op_pushes`) as a cantilever out to the end of what it holds and twisted about A, von Mises stress at most the material's yield (`forces::STRENGTH`) over ×2.

- `rotary_check::tests::a_stick_or_a_disc_cut_free_is_refused_on_a_120_and_a_250_mm_bar`: a 30 × 3 × 3 mm stick (120 mm bar) and a 60 × 6 × 6 mm one (250 mm bar), cut free at the bar's free end by straight passes, and the bar's Ø40 end 3 and 7 mm thick, cut off at A 90 and 270°, are each refused with their size and volume; without the last pass that frees each, nothing is said.
- `a_web_too_thin_to_hold_is_refused_with_its_size_and_a_sound_one_passes`: the contour 0.8 mm over the axis (a 1.6 mm neck) is refused on both bars as soon as A 270° leaves its web 2 mm across, with the 13 mm square web the cut's 168 N needs; 2 mm over it (the 4 mm web the demo job had) is refused with its stresses (215 N/mm² bending, 42 twisting, against 70 N/mm² without a material, ×0.3); 7 mm over it (a 14 mm web) passes on both bars.
- `a_contour_to_the_axis_that_cuts_the_part_off_the_bar_is_refused_where_it_does`: to the axis, the part (with what A 180° left round it) comes loose at A 270° on the 120 mm bar, and on the 250 mm bar with the 63 mm of bar beyond it; `a_contour_to_the_parts_bottom_leaves_a_fin_at_the_bars_end_that_is_refused`: to the part's bottom, a 20 × 3 × 3 mm fin the passes' round corner leaves at the free end, on less than a millimetre of bar, is refused already at A 90°.
- `held_is_inside_the_jaws_grip_not_anything_behind_their_front`: a core of the bar inside the jaws, cleared free of the shell they grip, is not held and is a loose piece; `a_loose_bit_up_to_two_cells_is_a_chip_and_three_is_a_piece`.
- The demo job (`render_the_four_axis_scene`, the library-machine tests `a_four_angle_bar_job_on_the_library_rotary_machine_exports_and_runs` and `render_the_index_height_on_the_library_rotary`, and `an_indexed_bar_runs_at_four_angles_turning_a_alone`) faces the four sides of the square with one Facing operation (`indexed::tests::faced_square`), which at each angle faces the selected side on top there (`indexed::on_top`; the UI's surface issues are taken per angle too): the carve is filled inside the square and empty outside it, and the end-on render shows the square in front of the round shoulder. The other contour fixtures (`on_the_bar`, the fake planner's square bar) cut to 7 mm over the axis.
- Not validated: a piece or web thinner than a 1 mm cell (a web is counted as cut through; a loose piece that thin is not seen), the tailstock's help against bending (taken as none), and anything on the machine.

## The 4th axis on main: phase-2 merged (LIO-246, 24 October 2026)

`phase-2` (the rotary A work, LIO-182 to LIO-214) was merged into main, which had grown the lathe, threading, the section view, Adaptive clearing, the GPU stock mesher and the planner chat meanwhile. Where both sides gave the same name a meaning, the machine decides:

- **Round bar.** A "Round bar" stands along the setup's Z (the lathe's, main) on any machine but one with a rotary A axis about X, where it lies along X for the chuck on A (phase-2); "Relative round bar" is offered only off the rotary (`stock::modes`), and one saved on another machine is refused there (`stock::NO_RELATIVE_BAR_ON_A`). `stock::tests::a_round_bar_is_its_box_along_x_and_its_sizes_must_be_positive` checks both axes from the same fields. A volume carries both (`Volume::round` for Z, `Volume::bar_x` for X); the GPU mesher knows only the bar along Z, so a stock with a bar along X is meshed on the CPU (`Snapshot::build`).
- **Chuck.** A Chuck is the library's on a lathe and refused on a plain mill, as on main, and the one on A on a machine with A (`chuck::holds`): `holding::tests::a_chuck_is_the_lathes_from_the_library_and_the_one_on_a_on_a_rotary_machine`, with the lathe tests of `plan_check` and `holding` unchanged.
- **Cycle Start.** No machine position on X, Y or Z no longer stops a run (main); on A it still does, as the driver runs nothing then (`smoke::tests::cycle_start_names_a_while_it_has_no_machine_position`, rewritten for that).
- **The planner.** A run on a rotary machine with a change request asks for `from` as well as the chuck and the A angles (`proposal::schema_with`, `the_schema_of_a_change_on_a_rotary_machine_has_from_and_the_chuck`); a plan that holds the stock in the chuck on A or indexes A is not offered for a change, with a message saying to plan it again (`a_plan_on_the_chuck_or_indexing_a_is_not_described_for_a_change`).
- **Section view.** The round bar on A and the chuck's turned solids are cut by the section as boxes are (`viewport::tests::a_section_cuts_a_turned_solid_as_it_cuts_a_box`); their cut faces are left open, and a stock turned away from its first angle (a run at another A) is drawn whole.
- **Holes picked by their walls.** Main's hole picking (`pocket::is_wall`, `hole_near`, the click) read the setup's rotation without A's turn, so a radial hole in an indexed setup was sorted in the unturned frame and cut in the turned one ("selected only in part"); it reads `Placement::new`'s rotation now, as `surface::issues` did. `indexed::tests::a_radial_hole_picked_by_its_walls_is_drilled_at_its_angle` drills a hole of `examples/radial-holes.3mf` (`scripts/make-radial-holes.py`: six blind Ø4 radial holes 60° apart round a Ø40 part) at A 90° and at A 30°, and fails without the fix.
- **One Drill at several angles.** A Drill, Pocket or Contour that picks holes cuts each picked hole once, at the first angle that turns its axis along Z (all its walls picked, their triangles leaning under about 4.5°) and opens it upward (no face of the part facing down over it past a gap, `cam::part_past_gap`), and the faces on top (`indexed::holes_by_angle`); an angle with none is skipped; a click picks a hole at the angle that turns it up (`Document::picking_pose`), and the Geometry tab lists each hole as its angle reads it (`Document::holes_listed`, each with its angle, `Hole::a`, shown as "at A30°"), its kept answer keyed on all of an indexed setup's fields, its machine's, its A turn and its base setup's.
  - Depth and credit (`holes_by_angle`; `cam::holes` reads the holes that stop at their own wall from the `indexed::OWN_WALL` field of the copy of the operation each angle cuts). Over a cavity under it (`cam::into_cavity`), a hole whose coaxial far hole is picked and drilled at another angle of the Drill stops through its own wall plus breakthrough. Else, where the far side lets the drill out (`cam::part_under_disc` probed at the drill's radius less the hole's facet sagitta, `cam::WallRing`), it goes on through as main drills it, and a picked far hole no angle has is credited as drilled when the plunge's target (the part's bottom + the Drill's Bottom offset − Breakthrough) reaches its bottom. Else (a solid far wall) it stops through its own wall. One `cam::wall_ring` measures every hole on both sides. A through-plunge refused for its flute length says it crosses the bore and to add the angle opposite (`indexed::ACROSS`). A setup of one angle and a 3-axis one keep main's depth: `cam::tests::a_tubes_cross_hole_picked_by_its_walls_goes_through_both_walls_in_3_axis` (the tube, +Y up, top hole picked by its walls, to the part's bottom).
  - On `examples/radial-tube.3mf` (`scripts/make-radial-holes.py`: a Ø40 tube, Ø28 bore, six Ø4 holes through the wall 60° apart at one X, in pairs facing each other) with a Ø3.3 drill:
    - (a) `a_tube_hole_over_a_hole_opposite_is_drilled_through_both_walls`: A 30 and 90, the A 30 hole alone picked (60 mm flutes), through both walls as at A 30 alone, and A 90 cuts nothing.
    - (b) `three_angles_drill_a_tubes_six_holes_in_pairs_through_both_walls`: A 30, 90 and 150, all six picked (60 mm flutes), three plunges through pairs crediting the far holes, nothing refused.
    - (c) `six_angles_drill_each_of_a_tubes_holes_from_its_own_side`: all six angles, all six picked, a 20 mm drill: six own-wall plunges, each tip under the wall's inner face and over 9 mm above the axis; and A 30, 90, 150 with that drill is refused with the `ACROSS` message naming A 210°.
    - (e) `a_plunge_short_of_the_hole_opposite_does_not_drill_it`: (b) with a Bottom offset of +25: the three far holes are not credited, and "3 of the picked holes are drilled at none of this setup's A angles" is said.
    - `the_far_side_is_open_to_a_drill_no_wider_than_the_hole_opposite`: a Ø3.3 or Ø4 drill goes out through the Ø4 hole opposite, a Ø4.4 one stops through its own wall.
  - (d) On `examples/radial-tube-half.3mf` (only the holes at 0°, 60° and 120°, each facing solid wall; 20 mm flutes): `a_tube_hole_over_solid_wall_is_drilled_through_its_own_wall_only`.
  - Each fails with its part of the rule taken out: no own wall when the hole opposite is drilled at another angle fails (c); stopping at the own wall over any cavity fails (a), (b) and (c); no credit for the hole opposite fails (b); credit whatever the plunge reaches fails (e); going on through over a solid far wall fails (d).
  - `one_drill_drills_six_radial_holes_each_at_its_own_angle` (blind holes): each at its own angle and X, and without A 330 "1 of the picked holes" is refused. `a_drill_skips_the_angles_that_turn_none_of_its_holes_up`, `a_drill_at_several_angles_lists_each_hole_as_its_angle_reads_it`, `smoke::tests::a_drill_at_several_angles_lists_its_holes_and_follows_the_angles` (six holes, two after the angles become A 30 and 90) and `smoke::tests::each_remove_of_a_drill_at_several_angles_drops_its_own_hole` (the tube's three pairs at A 30, 90 and 150, each row "at A…°", each remove its own; by centre alone every row maps to the first hole).
- **Threading.** Thread milling runs in an indexed setup (`indexed::tests::thread_milling_runs_in_an_indexed_setup`, the threaded plate in a 4-jaw chuck at A 0°).
- **Stock after this setup (#288).** On an indexed setup with the bar in the chuck it shows the round bar its four angles leave, with every angle's cuts (`smoke::tests::stock_after_an_indexed_setup_is_the_bar_its_angles_leave`).
- Not validated: a lathe setup and a rotary setup in one job, editing a rotary plan through the planner chat (refused), and anything on the machine.

## The stock over a hole too narrow for a Pocket's cutter (24 October 2026)

- **Cleared down to its rim.** A hole picked by its walls in a Pocket that no level of fits the cutter (`Hole::fits` all false) is no longer only skipped or refused: the stock over it is cleared down to its rim (`Hole::top`), so a smaller cutter or a drill starts at the part (`pocket::over_rim`, a zone in `floor_job`). The cutter's centre goes round inside the hole's opening (`levels[0].open`), so the tool covers the opening (its radius is wider than the opening is narrow) and nothing lower is cut. It keeps its radius, the radial allowance and the margin from anything of the part over that height within reach of the opening; where nothing leaves it room at the rim (a chamfer, a fillet, the floor a counterbore is sunk in, standing over the rim), it stops at the lowest corner height of the part there that does, and with none below the stock top nothing is added: the chamfered Ø6 hole under 2 mm of stock with a Ø8 cutter is cleared to the top at Z 6, not to the chamfer's foot at Z 5 (`pocket::tests::over_a_chamfered_narrow_hole_the_stock_stops_where_the_cutter_has_room`). Bottom offset and axial allowance apply as at a floor; the zone starts where a floor the same Pocket clears stops. An inside Contour adds nothing. On the plate under 2 mm of stock a Ø8 cutter clears over the Ø6 round hole and the 6 mm square one down to Z 6, never lower, its centre within 3 mm of the round hole's middle; with no stock over the top the round hole alone is refused as too narrow, as before (`pocket::tests::a_hole_too_narrow_for_the_cutter_has_the_stock_over_it_cleared_down_to_its_rim`). The slot along the pocket's west wall, picked alone with a Ø3 cutter, is cleared to its rim at the pocket's floor (Z 2), the cutter 1.5 mm from the wall (`pocket::tests::the_stock_over_a_narrow_holes_rim_keeps_clear_of_the_part_over_the_rim`).
- **What it says.** The warning and the Geometry list say what the Pocket does over such a hole, worked out as it does (`Hole::cleared_to`, a `Cleared`: how far over the rim the part leaves the cutter room, and the Bottom offset and axial allowance over that; kept with the fits in `App::pocket_fits` until the tool, the allowances, the offsets or the setup change): "… too narrow for the Ø8 cutter with this radial allowance: the stock over it is cleared down to its rim, the hole under it left uncut."; with 0.5 mm of axial allowance "… cleared down to 0.50 mm over its rim, the Bottom offset and axial allowance left, …", the cut stopping there; over the chamfered hole "… cleared down to 1.00 mm over its rim, where the part round it leaves the cutter room, …", and with 0.5 mm axial allowance and 0.25 mm Bottom offset as well "… 1.75 mm over its rim: 1.00 mm where the part round it leaves the cutter room, and 0.75 mm of Bottom offset and axial allowance over that", the plan's target at the rim plus 1.75; where nothing is cleared over it (no stock over the part, or an inside Contour), "… it is left uncut." (`Hole::uncut`). The Geometry list says "too narrow for Ø8: cleared to its rim only", "cleared to 1.75 mm over its rim only" or "left uncut".
- Pictures inspected (not a test): the chamfered Ø6 hole (`chamfered_hole`, chamfer from Z 5 to Ø8 at the top, Z 6) under 2 mm of stock with a Ø8 cutter: from the iso view and a section through the hole, a round patch about Ø14 is cleared down to the part's top at Z 6 (at the rim, Z 5, the top face leaves the cutter no room), the stock left standing in the hole and the chamfer.

## Pocket floors at several heights cleared a depth band at a time (24 October 2026)

- **Before.** Each selected floor height was its own zone, cleared from the stock top down to it; a deeper floor started higher up only when it lay inside a shallower one. Floors side by side at different heights (a pocket stepping down) were each cleared from the top on their own: the depth they share was cut once per floor, with an entry, a spiral and a retract each, along walls between them that do not exist at that depth.
- **Now.** `pocket::banded` splits the depth at every height a zone (floor, picked hole's level, the stock over a hole too narrow for the cutter, opening cut through) starts or stops. In each band the zones that go on below it and whose centres touch there are one pocket: within the cutter's reach of their areas and its keep-out from the part above the highest floor among them (`part_above`; a floor's own `above` at its height), a `Centres::Floor`, so Adaptive's way in past an open edge works on it too. A zone alone in its band, or holding the others of its pocket (a floor round a deeper one), keeps its own centres, and one that goes on alone through the bands below is cut through them at once, so floors that touch nothing get no extra levels. The bands are cut from the top down, each band's pockets in turn.
- Tests: `pocket::tests::floors_side_by_side_are_cleared_together_down_to_each_height` (`pocket::stepped_pocket`, a 30 × 12 mm pocket stepping down Z 3, 1, −1 with a Ø4 cutter: no feed below the highest floor under the cutter or outside the pocket's walls; each floor reached; above Z 3 one loop goes round all three floors, which fails before the change; faster than the three floors in operations of their own by more than 15%; volume removed within 4% of the floors' 1,800 mm³) and `adaptive::tests::floors_side_by_side_clear_together_down_to_each_height` (the same part cleared adaptively at a 2 mm stepdown in four levels, not nine, clear of the higher floors) and `pocket::tests::a_separate_higher_floor_leaves_the_island_beside_it_standing` (a pocket to Z 3 apart from one stepping Z 1 to Z −1 with an unselected island to Z 2 in its upper step, Ø2: neither Pocket nor Adaptive comes within the cutter's radius of the island below Z 2. Each pocket keeps clear of the part above its own highest floor, not the band's, so the same floors make the same pocket in every band they go on through; with the band's, found in review, both cut 1 mm into the island). Every earlier pocket and adaptive test passes unchanged.
- Measured on that part (2 mm stepdown, 40% stepover): 9 depth passes and 9 retracts, 47.8 s before; 4 depth passes and 4 retracts, 40.1 s after.

## Round parts in the vise: V-block, soft jaws and a collet block (LIO-249, 24 October 2026)

A round bar on a 3-axis mill can lie along X (`stock_axis` "X", `stock::lying`; a project saved before has no key and its bar still stands) and be held in a V-block, soft jaws bored to it or an ER collet block (`round_hold`). Unit tests in `round_hold::tests`:

- **Entries.** The starters read clean; an angle outside 30° to 150°, a V as wide as the block or deeper than it, a bore that leaves under 2 mm of jaw and an ER size there is no collet of are refused (`an_entry_is_refused_for_a_v_it_cannot_have_a_bore_without_a_jaw_or_an_unknown_er_size`); a setup's keys read back as the placement they write, and soft jaws follow the bar's lie.
- **Geometry.** For a Ø12 bar in the 90° starter V (24 mm wide): the apex 6√2 below the axis, the block's top 12 above the apex, each flank as four steps never above the V, the strap on the bar's top at the V-block's middle, and for a Ø8 bar seated below the block's top the strap on the block with its screw down to the bar (`a_v_block_seats_the_bar_on_two_flank_lines_and_clamps_it_on_top`). Soft jaws reach in to the bore at each step's narrowest and down under a lying bar; upright ones close on Y round a standing disc up to the grip (`soft_jaws_reach_round_the_bore_up_to_their_top`). A collet's nut stops at the grip, the block under it (`a_collet_must_close_on_what_it_grips`).
- **Refusals.** A Ø88 bar in the 24 mm V ("too big … seats bars up to Ø32.53 mm"), a V-block beyond the bar's end, a box stock, soft jaws bored to Ø24 for a Ø12 bar, a lying bar's jaws whose top is not above its axis, a standing grip leaving under 1 mm, an ER25 asked to grip the Ø18.6 hub ("closes on Ø1 to Ø16 mm"), a grip deeper than the collet, and a collet diameter wider than the bar; a vise on a lying bar and a V-block on a box stock in the editor (`round_holdings_and_entries_round_trip_and_a_vise_on_a_lying_bar_is_refused`, which also round-trips the entries and the setup through JSON).
- **The cutter.** A 0.6 mm flat over the bar's end clears the V-block and its strap; run on along the bar it meets the strap; a plunge beside the bar meets the shoulder; a drill through the bar beyond the block is clear (`the_cutter_is_kept_out_of_the_v_block_and_its_clamp`).
- **Round contact.** `arc(-π/2, π/2)` is π/2, a narrow arc 1; a 90° V's friction is 0.2 × F × (1 + √2); soft jaws to 30° above the axis 2 × 0.2 × F × (π/2 + π/6) ÷ 1.5; an ER32 grips 10 kN at two thirds of its length, half as hard half as deep (`the_round_contact_model_holds_by_the_v_lines_the_jaws_arc_and_the_collet_grip`). `the_force_check_holds_a_firm_grip_and_refuses_a_light_one`: the starter V-block holds a 200 N cut; at 300 N clamping it is ×0.5 against sliding, with the message; a 400 N pull up lifts the bar out; an ER32 gripping the rotor's hub 4 mm deep tips under a cut 26 mm above it.
- **Zero.** `Bar axis center` puts Y0 Z0 on the lying bar's axis (Z6 of a Ø12 bar) and `Stock top center` on its top (Z12), through `post::datum`; both bar-axis zeros are refused on a box and on a machine with A (`a_lying_bar_zeroes_on_its_axis_or_its_top`).
- **Old projects.** A "Round bar" without `stock_axis` still stands along Z and resolves as before; with "X" it lies along X and `stock::round` gives its axis (`a_3_axis_round_bar_lies_along_x_only_when_set_and_old_projects_still_stand`).
- **Touch-off.** A stock-box zero on a lying bar (Stock top center and the like) adds that its -Y and +Y sides are touched at its axis height, one radius below its top, where the box's sides meet it (`round_hold::side_note`, in the program header and the Work zero step).
- **The power-feed parts, headless** (24 October 2026, `--part-report`, `--check-plan` and `--export-gcode` on the STEP files of the power feed, 3-axis LinuxCNC mill, mild steel 1018; projects outside the repository): the output shaft Ø12 × 78 lying along X in a 90° V-block 50 mm (offset 12) and in soft jaws bored Ø12 (top 9 above the bar's bottom), the 0.6 × 22 flat by Pocket with a Ø10 end mill, zero on the bar's axis and on its top; the leadscrew stub, Ø20 bar standing 25 deep in an ER32 collet block, the stock round the 4.8 × 14 tongue by Pocket with a Ø3; the Oldham disc Ø24 standing in soft jaws bored Ø24 (grip 5), the cross slot (setup 1) and round the tongue (setup 2, its soft jaws named by the plan's `holder`) with a Ø4; the clutch rotor, its Ø18.6 hub 15 deep in an ER32 collet block, the teeth tops faced with a Ø10. Each part report names its holding and bar and each plan check passes. The clutch cover Ø88 on its side in a 90° V-block 120 mm exports its radial M4 drawbolt hole (Ø3.3 drill, Deep drilling, the hole's walls picked); a plan cannot name a hole on a cylinder, so it was checked by the export, which refuses a move into the holding. Refused as they should be: the cover in the 50 mm V-block ("too big … seats bars up to Ø32.53 mm"), the shaft's flat with the V-block under it and its strap over it ("the tool hits the V-block clamp"), the rotor's hub in an ER25 ("closes on Ø1 to Ø16 mm"), and the rotor in an ER32 whose grip is set to 150 N ("×0.09, under the ×2 wanted").
- **Pictures.** The V-block, soft jaws and collet block have hover-card pictures (`field_art/round.rs`): the V's two contacts and the clamp, the bar growing until it rests on the V's edges (refused); the jaws closing round the bore, their arc in gold, their top above the axis; the bar standing in the collet with its depth marked. The 3D view, the setup guide and the planner's report draw or send their solids as boxes.
- Not validated: the force model's numbers (the V-block's 3000 N, the ER grips, the even pressure round a soft jaw's arc) against a real clamp; soft jaws or a collet in a later setup after earlier cuts thinned the bar (their contact is not checked against earlier cuts, as a vise's is); a round holding on a machine with a rotary A axis (not offered there); anything at the machine.

## What fits under the gantry and the spindle: clearances in the machine profile (LIO-252, 24 October 2026)

The profile gave travel and a top speed only, all of it ASSUMED, and no clearance from the spindle nose at Z top to the table or under the gantry: a part standing 110 mm tall in the vise was planned and posted, and the spindle or the beam would have met it.

- `envelope::tests::a_machine_without_the_figures_is_not_checked_and_a_bad_one_is_said`: a machine with no clearance gives no problem for any job; zero, text, a gantry narrower than the X travel and a source that is neither ASSUMED nor Measured are each said once in the machine's issues; a rotary unit whose chuck (centre height and half its diameter) is taller than the gantry is said too.
- `the_stack_up_is_the_vise_the_stock_and_the_tool_up_to_the_nose`: the vise's `base_height` plus the stock's height is the stock top and the frame's Z maps onto it; a vise entry with no height takes 40 mm and the message says it was taken; clamps and none stand on the table.
- `a_part_taller_than_the_gantry_is_refused_naming_the_numbers` and `a_part_wider_than_the_gantry_is_refused`: needs X mm, has Y mm, one more millimetre of room passes; an ASSUMED figure says so in the refusal, a Measured one does not; the width counts the stock with its jaws.
- `a_tool_that_reaches_the_nose_is_refused_with_its_stick_out_and_holder`: 0.1 mm of room passes, 0.1 mm short is refused with the sum, the stick-out and the holder's 20 mm; a tool with no holder length is taken as 10 mm and says so.
- `a_tool_too_short_to_reach_down_is_refused_only_for_a_measured_nose_height`: the same job passes with the nose height unmarked or ASSUMED and is refused when it is Measured; 2 mm lower it reaches.
- `the_shipped_jingyan_is_marked_assumed_and_an_edited_figure_is_not`: a saved Jingyan machine (library and the setup's copy) takes the three placeholders and ASSUMED on the shipped travel and speed, a figure the operator typed or measured is left as it is, a second pass changes nothing, and another machine is untouched.
- `a_workspace_saved_before_the_clearances_adopts_them_once_on_load` and `the_library_machines_carry_the_assumed_placeholders_and_are_valid`: an old workspace file loads, adopts the figures (library and setup copy) and says so once; the two library Jingyan machines start with them, valid.
- `a_bar_on_the_rotary_unit_stands_at_its_centre_height_and_reach` and `export_run_and_the_plan_check_see_the_refusal`: the Ø40 bar on the 70 mm centre height is 90 mm to its top, refused 0.1 mm short under the spindle and 0.1 mm over the gantry; export and Run each say the refusal once, and say nothing with room.
- Headless (`--part-report`, `--check-plan`, `--export-gcode` on the JY5800 profile, the placeholders): the 110 × 197 × 28 plate standing on either edge is refused "doesn't fit under the gantry", lying flat it passes, the Ø88 cover on its side shows its margin (see the pull request).
- Not validated: anything on the machine. The placeholders are chosen, not measured: the real nose height, gantry height and width, travel and top speed are to be measured with the spindle at Z top and replace them.

## Internal grooves with a keyseat cutter (LIO-247, 24 October 2026)

A **Keyseat cutter** tool type and an **Internal groove** operation (`Strategy::Groove`, `src/groove.rs`) cut a groove below the mouth of a bore by circular interpolation: down the bore's axis, out on a half circle tangent to the groove's circle, round it (twice with a spring pass), back to the axis on the other half circle and up it. The stock is the continuous history of each cut's swept solid (`stock_surface`, meshed on demand) with a voxel grid for volumes (`volume`), both solids, so the keyseat head is swept as a flat cylinder as tall as its width (`CutterShape::Flat`, flute length = head width) and the groove is a true undercut under the bore wall; the top-down height grid (`simulation::Stock`), which keeps one height per column and cannot hold an undercut, is not cut by it. The neck and shank do not cut: they are drawn (`Plan::neck`) and checked.

- Geometry: `groove::tests::the_head_reaches_the_groove_diameter_at_the_groove_in_two_passes` (a synthetic 60 × 60 × 28 plate with the Ø32 bore and both DIN 472-32 grooves modeled, Ø33.7 × 1.3 at a 2.6 land, drilled Ø32 through first; a Ø25 × 1.2 head on a Ø10 neck): the head's centre circles 16.85 − 12.5 = 4.35 from the axis, the farthest any cutting move goes, at two heights, the head's top at the groove's top edge and its bottom at the groove's bottom edge. `passes_cover_a_groove_wider_than_the_head`: 1.2 in 1.2 is one pass, 1.3 two (0.1 apart), 3.0 three. `a_spring_pass_circles_the_groove_again`: twice the orbit's moves. `the_head_goes_down_the_axis_and_returns_to_it_before_it_retracts`: every move above the groove, before and after, is on the axis, the last cut ends on it, the head comes up it at the feed before the rapid retract, and between passes it moves along the axis only.
- Checks: `the_bore_neck_reach_width_and_land_are_checked` (a Ø28 and a Ø25 × 1.0 head pass; a Ø31.8 head is too big for the Ø32 bore, a Ø23 neck on a Ø25 head would rub it, a 1.5 head is wider than the 1.3 groove and would cut the wall beside it, a 3 mm reach does not reach the groove's far edge 3.9 down, a groove past the bore's top is not inside it, and a Ø31.8 groove is no wider than the bore); `a_wrong_cutter_or_land_is_refused_with_its_reason` (the same through generation, a land of 27.5 that puts the groove past the bore's bottom, and the groove before the bore is drilled: "Bore the Ø32.00 hole first"); `the_model_check_finds_a_neck_rubbing_a_lip_over_the_groove` (with the numbers of a Ø32 bore, the model of a Ø20 lip over it is hit by the neck circling, by a Ø22 head on its way down, and by the shank of a 4 mm reach; the open bore by nothing); `a_keyseat_cutters_sizes_are_checked` (a neck as wide as the head, a reach no more than the head's width or more than the stickout; no flute length asked for; a flat end mill refused).
- Simulation: `the_simulated_stock_holds_the_groove_as_an_undercut` measures the stock's field: across the groove's middle the material starts at Ø33.70 (±0.02), along the inside of the bore wall the groove runs from 2.60 to 3.90 below the top (±0.01), the bore wall stays Ø32 over the land and below, the voxels count the removal, and the height grid is unchanged.
- `the_flipped_plate_grooves_its_other_face`: setup 2 turned over about X (rx 180) takes its groove a 2.6 land from its own top, and the final stock, in setup 1's frame, holds both grooves (at 2.6–3.9 from each face) with the wall between them solid.
- `a_groove_round_a_boss_circles_outside_it`: a Ø30 boss picked by its outside walls (with its groove modeled and without), a Ø27 × 1.2 groove 3 below its top with a Ø12 head on a Ø4 neck: the centre circles at 13.5 + 6, the head going down outside the boss, and the stock's groove is Ø27 with the boss whole over and under it.
- `the_groove_is_posted_for_linuxcnc_and_passes_the_uc100_run_check`: the plate job posts for LinuxCNC (G1 lines) and, on a UC100 machine with the JY5800 profile, passes the run check.
- Headless, on the feed unit's `plate.step` (28 mm, Ø32 bore with both grooves modeled; a 3-axis LinuxCNC mill, 6061-T6, a Ø10 flat end mill and a Ø25 × 1.2 keyseat cutter on a Ø10 neck, 20 reach): setup 1 (+Z) bores the Ø32 through by Helical milling and cuts the face A groove, setup 2 (−Z, turned over) faces face B and cuts its groove, each Ø33.7 × 1.3 at a 2.6 land in two passes. `--check-plan` passes (4 operations, 20.6 min); a Ø31.8 head, a 3 mm reach, a Ø24 neck and a land of 27.5 are each refused at generation with their reasons; `--export-gcode` writes both programs, the groove going down the bore's axis, a half circle out to 4.35 from it, 48 moves round, a half circle back, down the axis to the second pass and the same, up the axis and out. `groove::tests::measure_the_grooves_of_a_project` (ignored; `AXIS_GROOVE_PROJECT=<project.json>`) measures the simulated stock: each groove Ø33.700 × 1.300, 2.60 to 3.90 below its own face.
- Planner: `proposal::tests::a_groove_proposal_becomes_an_internal_groove` and the schema test; old projects load unchanged (no field of theirs changed; a proposal without the groove fields reads them as null).

## Chamfer milling: V flanks at a depth, edge chamfers and deburring (LIO-245, 24 October 2026)

A chamfer mill was taken only by Drilling, to countersink. The power feed's clutch rotor and armature each have 60 tooth flanks, planes 30° from the axis through radial lines at the pitch plane, finished by a 60° chamfer mill in one straight radial pass with its tip at the root floor (`MACHINING.md` → Teeth); nothing could program that, nor an edge chamfer or a deburr along picked edges.

- **What changed.** A new strategy, **Chamfer milling** (`chamfer`), puts the cone's side on the edges of a picked level face (outline, hole mouths, or with a wall picked the one edge they share) or on a line given by its ends and height, sized by the chamfer's width or by the tip's Z, copied round a vertical axis, one level pass each. The tool's axis is off the line by the cone's radius at the line's height, r(h) = f/2 + h·tan α, toward the air, (1) in the module doc; by width, the tip goes the tip offset t under the chamfer's lower end and the axis is f/2 + t·tan α off the edge, (2). A chamfer mill has an optional **Flat tip** (`tip_flat`, across); the four stock simulators (the stock grid, the volume, the fidget surface and the GPU mesher) sweep the cone rising from the flat's edge, and Drilling's countersinks lower the point by the flat's share. The plan check tests the whole cone against the part (`chamfer::gouge`: the part above the cone's surface at points 0.05 mm apart under each feed move, at most 0.01 mm), less the chamfer each edge is meant to get, and the coarse stock-grid stage leaves Chamfer milling to it. The planner's schema has `Chamfer` and a `chamfer` object on every operation (null for the others); old projects and proposals load (`chamfer` defaults to null, a missing setting to its default).
- **The teeth.** rotor.step's 60 flanks were measured: each passes through the axis at the pitch plane, Z −0.35 from the tips, at θ = 3° + 6°·j, its normal 60° from the axis; the gap is anticlockwise of the even lines and clockwise of the odd ones. The program: the line from r 31.8 (in the run-out groove, clear of the pole face) to r 39.5 at Z −0.35, Tip Z −1.30, Alternate sides from the Left, 60 copies 6° apart from 3°: the axis 0.95 × tan 30° + f/2 = 0.5485 + f/2 off each line.
- Tests (`chamfer::tests`): `the_cone_side_offset_follows_the_tip_angle_flat_and_depth_or_the_width` (0.548483 and 0.648483 for the clutch, 90° cases, widths, and the tip above the line or the cone wider than the mill refused); `the_pattern_turns_copy_k_by_the_first_angle_and_k_pitches_about_its_axis`; `the_rotors_sixty_flanks_lie_on_their_radial_lines_at_the_pitch_plane_within_0_01_mm` (the program on a Ø76 disc, pointed and 0.2 flat: each pass's axis on the gap's side, and the swept cone's boundary, bisected with `volume::swept`, on the radial line at r 33 and r 37 at the pitch plane and 0.65 × tan 30° into the gap at Z −1.0, within 0.001 mm (the acceptance is 0.01), for both flank directions; nothing of the gap's other flank is cut at r 32); `the_cone_check_passes_the_flank_pass_and_catches_a_wrong_side_or_too_deep_or_off_line_one` (on a synthetic flank and root floor: the right pass ≤ 0.01, the wrong side > 1 mm, 0.3 too deep > 0.25, the line 0.05 into the part > 0.05); `the_edges_of_a_picked_face_are_its_outline_and_its_hole_mouths_or_the_edge_it_shares` (a plate with a square hole: the outline and the hole, each kind alone, 0.5 × 45° offset 0.5 out and in at Z −1, climbing round each the right way, the swept chamfer 0.5 on the face and 0.5 down the wall, the cone check passing it but not a 0.3 one in its place, and the top with the east wall picked chamfering only their edge, run 1 mm past it); `a_flat_tipped_cone_cuts_flat_under_its_flat_and_rises_from_its_edge`; `a_chamfer_mills_flat_tip_is_optional_and_narrower_than_the_mill`; `a_chamfer_millings_settings_are_checked`; `viewport::tests::a_chamfer_mill_and_a_drill_are_drawn_with_their_cone` (the simulation view draws a chamfer mill's cone from its flat up to its diameter, a drill's 118° point, a flat end mill as before); `a_planned_chamfer_builds_describes_back_is_refused_when_wrong_and_passes_the_plan_check` (the example block's hole mouths deburred 0.3 after its drilling, through the whole plan check; a line program built from the schema; five refusals; the schema).
- Headless (`--check-plan`, `--export-gcode`, on the power-feed STEPs; results in the PR): rotor.step, 1018, Stock top center on the tips, no holding modelled: the 60-flank program with a pointed 60° Ø6 mill and with a 0.2 flat passes every check (11.3 min); turned 0.03° (0.02 mm at the OD), its sides swapped or its tip 0.3 too deep, the cone check refuses it (0.037, 1.300 and 0.300 mm into the part). armature.step, flipped teeth up: the same cut turned half a pitch (its first copy at 9°, as its fixture holds it half a pitch round, since a planned setup 1 cannot turn about Z) passes, and at 3° it cuts 1.30 mm into the teeth. Exported for LinuxCNC, the four programs (rotor and armature, pointed and flat) put the cone's side on each flank line, the rotor's measured in rotor.step through the axis at 3° + 6°·j at the pitch plane, within 0.0006 mm (the G-code's 3 decimals), the tool on the gap's side at all 120 ends of the 60 passes. plate.step: a 0.5 × 45° chamfer of the top face's outer contour and a 0.3 deburr of its hole mouths (Ø32, Ø6.6, Ø4.2, Ø2.5) with a 90° Ø6 mill pass every check (2.7 min); cover.step: a 0.3 deburr of the Ø28 bearing bore's mouth passes
- Not validated: the collet block that holds the rotor by its hub (LIO-249, merged after these checks: they ran with no holding modelled); a ramp or a curved move's cone check is sampled along the move, not exact; a chamfer on an edge whose face is not level (pick a level face); anything on the machine, and the pole gap of a mated pair.

## Reaming and boring on the mill (LIO-248, 24 October 2026)

- **Tools.** A Reamer (size, tolerance class, flutes) and a Boring head (bore range, bar diameter, dial graduation, how it leaves the bore; its diameter is its body's, its flute length the bar's reach): `hole_stages::tests::a_reamer_and_a_boring_head_need_their_sizes_and_a_mill` (a range the wrong way round, a bar as wide as the smallest bore and no tolerance class are refused; a Boring needs its Hole diameter and a CNC; each cycle takes its own tool type).
- **Reaming.** `cam::boring::tests::a_reamer_feeds_in_dwells_and_feeds_back_out` (a Ø10 hole through 8.7, drilled Ø9.8: fed down to a millimetre past the exit, the 0.5 s dwell, then fed back up at the same feed, no stop), `a_reamer_needs_its_hole_drilled_under_it_its_size_and_flutes_as_deep` (not drilled: "Drill the … first: Ø9.8"; drilled Ø9: open to Ø9.0, too much to ream; a Ø12 reamer for the Ø10 hole; flutes shorter than the hole), `a_reaming_posts_a_feed_out_after_its_dwell` (the LinuxCNC line after `G4 P0.500` is a feed, no `M0`).
- **Boring.** `boring_bores_in_passes_pausing_to_set_the_head_and_stops_the_spindle_before_the_retract` (a Ø32 bore through 28, drilled Ø12 and milled out to Ø31.4: two passes, Ø31.90 and Ø32.00, each after a pause that says the size, how much to set the head out and how many divisions; after each dwell the spindle stops and the head rapids out, and nothing feeds until it starts again; with the head set to Feed out, a feed up after each dwell and no stop but the pauses; the sweep is the bore's, the head's body above the bar's 40 mm reach), `boring_from_the_drilled_size_takes_more_passes_with_the_saved_defaults` (an operation saved without the cut fields: the defaults, 0.3 mm a pass on the radius from Ø12), `boring_refuses_a_head_out_of_range_or_short_a_hole_not_roughed_and_two_sizes`.
- **Passes and pairs.** `a_reamer_is_paired_with_a_drill_under_it_and_a_bore_with_a_rough_size` (Ø10 → Ø9.8, Ø4 → Ø3.9, Ø25 → Ø24.7, Ø32 → Ø31.4; the passes' sizes on the dial, none taking more than the cut). Add the drilling: `finishing::tests::a_reamer_is_drilled_for_at_its_drill_size_first` (the library's Ø9.8 of Ø8, 9.5, 9.8 and 10, through 3.5 past the exit; then the reaming programs; without it, the status names a Ø9.8 drill), `a_bore_is_drilled_and_milled_out_to_its_rough_size_first` (the widest drill that reaches through, Helical milling with the Ø10 end mill leaving 0.60, then the Boring programs with its two pauses).
- **Speeds and feeds.** `material::tests::a_reamer_and_a_boring_head_run_at_their_own_rates` (6061: a Ø10 six-flute reamer at a third of 150–300 m/min, an end mill's 8000 rpm refused as "above … reaming 50–100 m/min"; a Ø32 bore at half the surface speed, one edge at a Ø10 bar's chip load; no Hole diameter, not checked).
- **Post and run.** `the_bore_posts_spindle_stops_and_pauses_and_the_run_lowers_them_in_order` (the program sets the head with `(MSG, Boring head: set it to D31.90, pass 1 of 2, then resume.)` and `M0` before `S M3`; `M5` before each rapid out; the replay check passes; the UC100 actions pause only with the spindle off and feed only with it on), `control::tests::a_boring_run_pauses_to_set_the_head_and_goes_on_after_resume_on_the_simulator` (the checked program lowered and run on the simulated UC100: Paused with each pass's message, Resume, and the job ends without error).
- **Animated help.** The cycle cards Reaming and Boring have their own drill-cycle pictures (`help::tests::render_animation_frames`, `drill-ream`, `drill-bore`): the reamer fed in, dwelling and fed out; the bar boring, the spindle off on the way out, the head set out and a second, wider pass.
- **The manual mill.** `finishing::tests::on_the_manual_mill_a_bore_steps_up_through_the_drills_and_the_head_roughs_the_rest` (the Ø32 bore through 28 with drills Ø3.2, 6.2, 8 and 12: Ø6.2 then Ø12, no helical milling; the head's cut 0.5 mm a pass without a material, 1 mm in aluminium; the head roughs Ø12 to Ø31.9 in ten passes and the eleventh is Ø32; every move goes along one axis; the guide has a Bore step a pass, each with its size, the size before it, the dial and the one hole), `the_manual_mill_reams_and_its_guide_shows_each_hole` (drilled Ø9.8 and reamed on the manual mill with no issue, fed back out, a Holes step for the reaming). The lathe refuses both (`a_reamer_and_a_boring_head_need_their_sizes_and_a_mill`).
- Not validated: a Boring in an indexed or chuck-held setup (refused), and anything on the machine.

## A Facing link at the stock top keeps the blend margin

- The TOTEM bottom's Setup 1 Facing (0.5 mm a level, Model silhouette, zigzag) was refused by the run check: "Facing: a rapid moves sideways at X8.949 Y4.037 Z0.000, 0.000 mm over the stock top: less than 2.5 times the path blending tolerance (G64 P0.010, 0.025 mm)". On the second level a row link rose to just over the level before, which with a 0.5 mm stepdown is the stock top itself, and crossed there by a rapid. Such a link now crosses 2.5 times the machine's G64 P over the top (0.025 mm by default), as a link between operations does. A copy of the owner's project (workspace and STEP) checked by `run::programs`: Setup 1 refused before, both setups pass after. `facing::tests::a_link_just_over_the_level_before_at_the_stock_top_keeps_the_blend_margin`: the L part faced 0.5 mm a level, zigzag retracting each pass and one way keeping the tool down; no sideways rapid between the top and the margin, and some at the margin. Against the old code it fails on a rapid 0.0000 mm over the top.

## Cycle Start without soft limits in the profile

- A profile with `soft_min` and `soft_max` taken out of X, Y and Z, to turn the soft limits off for now, no longer keeps Cycle Start grey with "The UC100 profile sets no soft limits on X, so AXIS CAM cannot check that the job fits the machine." The Run panel's amber "Soft limits are off for Cycle Start" box says instead that the profile sets none on X, Y and Z, so nothing stops the machine at the end of its travel and Cycle Start does not check that the job fits it, and to add them back to turn them on; this sentence wins over the machine-position one, as setting machine zero would not turn them on. With machine zero and a work zero set, tool run 1 of the fixture's top setup cuts to the end. Without Z's limits alone the box names Z, and X and Y keep their travel check (`cycle_start_runs_without_soft_limits_in_the_profile`). Against the old code the test fails at its first check: the three refusals were Cycle Start's problems.

## The Control page as a console that does not scroll (LIO-274, LIO-273, 26 October 2026)

- **What changed.** The Control page was one column about 3,100 px tall at 1440 wide, under a 300 px sidebar. It is now laid out for 1440 × 790, a 13" laptop's usable window, and does not scroll: in MACHINE one 44 px bar (the switcher, Control, Controller configuration, the project, the machine, SIMULATED, a fixed-width state pill, Reset in E-stop and the one E-STOP; the page's second E-STOP is gone), a 64 px run bar (the setup list, one line of what comes next, what is cut or the first thing holding Cycle Start back with the rest on hover, the progress and time left, Cycle Start, Hold, Resume and **Stop now** in fixed places), and three columns: 340 px of hands (Position with Zero and ½, Jog, Spindle and feed, fixed heights), the centre's tabs (Job with one 3D view, Stock / Machine / Top; Work zero; Probe; Rotary A with A; MDI; Machine; Tool change while one is due) and 400 px of Operations in rows by tool run, replacing the 150 × 112 picture cards (the picture is on hover). The status bar is 24 px on every page. Long paragraphs became hover cards; the jog buttons' tooltips name their keys. LIO-273: the strip's Stop is **Stop now**, the spindle's Stop **Spindle off**; without a probe input the Probe tab is one line and a way to Controller configuration; the keys jog only while the jog pad is on screen (`App::jog_pad_shown`: also not on a manual setup's Steps page, where they used to).
- **Tests.** `smoke::tests::the_jog_pad_stays_put_while_the_machine_jogs_and_the_page_does_not_scroll`: on the fixture job at 1440 × 790, every 6 px of the left column pressed (`ui::probe::presses`), the points that give each `JogPress` are the same Idle and while Y+ jogs, and in the Job, Work zero, Probe, MDI and Machine tabs every scrollable (`ui::probe::scrollables`) is under the run bar and narrower than the page less the hands. `the_run_bar_keeps_cycle_start_and_stop_now_beside_a_long_operations_list` (was the_run_strip_keeps_cycle_start_and_stop_above_a_long_tool_run_list): the run bar holds four buttons with two tool runs as with twelve; the Operations list ten more. `the_keys_jog_only_with_the_pad_on_screen_and_the_stops_say_what_they_stop` (LIO-273): a jog key held jogs on the Control page and not in Controller configuration or on another page; the page says Stop now and Spindle off and no bare Stop; with no probe input the Probe tab is its one line, without Probe Z. The Control page tests that read texts now open the tab they are in (Machine for Disconnect, the switch question and Real/Simulated; Work zero and MDI for their fields; `Set A` in Work zero).
- **Renders** (`smoke::tests::render_the_control_page`, ignored, to `$AXIS_RENDER_DIR`): not connected, E-stop, Idle, before the run, each tab, the Job tab's Machine view, cutting the Pocket (also at 1440 × 900 and 1920 × 1080), the tool change, and the rotary machine with A (Idle, Rotary A, Probe). Inspected at 1440 × 790: nothing cut off, overlapping or scrolling; with A the left column fits by lower DRO lines (42 px, 21 px figures).
- Not built here (later tasks): Move to, skipping an operation, steps and checkpoints, Stop after step, the 1280 × 690 compact layout (LIO-275).

## Outline Facing: one lap after the ramp, links round the edge, air at the machine's feed

- The owner asked why the edge of an outline Facing is cut twice and why zigzag lifts between rows. Measured on the test L (Ø6, 40 %, 2 mm a level, 6 mm stock all round): the ramp went round whole laps and finished the last one at depth, then a full lap at depth followed (about 128 mm of edge at depth for a 112 mm loop); on each level the nearest-first row order left the L's arm for last, and the 20.5 mm way to it leaves the region, so the cutter rose 17 mm to clearance and came back down at a quarter of the feed (4 s). Links through air fed at the cutting feed and descents onto cut floor at a quarter of it.
- Now (`facing::outline`): the ramp starts at the loop corner its run reaches back from the loop's start (a corner the level before went round through, so the export's check accepts a rapid down to it), reaches depth at the start and one lap at depth follows. A link that would lift goes round the edge pass at depth with Keep tool down where that is quicker. Moves through air feed at the machine's fastest feed (`post_check::machine_mm`, `feed`): links just over the level before, round the edge, and the descents onto a row's start, which follow a rapid down to over the top. The descent beside the stock or to the level before feeds at the cutting feed.
- The L faced both ways: 110.6 s to 74.4 s in stock all round, 49.5 s to 48.0 s flush with it.
- Tests: `a_ramp_reaches_depth_where_one_lap_round_the_edge_starts_and_air_feeds_fast` (the ramp's run at least 2 mm at 2°, the lap at depth as long as the lap come to from beside the stock; air moves both ways and one way at the cutting feed or 5000 mm/min, never slower), `rows_link_at_depth_both_ways_and_lift_between_them_one_way` (both ways: up only after each level), `under_the_stock_top_rows_link_at_feed_or_over_the_stock_never_by_a_rapid` (both ways no longer links just over the level before), `the_example_blocks_outline_facing_posts_and_replays_exactly_before_its_pocket`.
- Not validated: on the real machine.

## Roughing a sloped face: Pocket and Adaptive clear the stock over it in steps (27 October 2026)

- The TOTEM aluminium bottom's second setup has a ramp 21° from level (nine CAD faces on a wedge standing out of a side wall) that nothing could rough: Pocket and Adaptive clearing took only level floors ("Surface N is not perpendicular to setup Z"), and the floor under the wedge counts the whole wedge as part above it, so the stock over the ramp stayed. Now a picked sloped face (facing up, between a wall and level: `pocket::is_sloped`) is cleared as floors from its foot up, evenly, at most a stepdown apart, to under its top (`pocket::slope_floors`). Each keeps the tool its radius, the radial stock to leave and the margin from the part above its height, and reaches the foot from past it (the faces' outline seen from above, grown by that keep-out), so `pocket::banded` clears each band down to the next floor and the face is left in steps no taller than the stepdown for a finishing pass. The part above a slope's floor is taken from 1 µm over it, not 10: up a 21.8° slope 10 µm of height is 25 µm across, more than the 20 µm margin, and the cutter's edge went 2 µm into the ramp. A click on a sloped face in an Adaptive clearing, or in a Pocket with no hole past it, picks the whole smooth face, as Parallel does, and the pointer shows all of it.
- Tests: `adaptive::tests::a_ramp_is_cleared_in_steps_never_into_it` (a 30 × 12 mm part, level at Z 4, a ramp down to Z 0 over 10 mm, level on; the ramp picked, Ø4, 1 mm stepdown: Adaptive clearing and Pocket both cut at Z 3, 2, 1 and 0, and no feed point is under the part anywhere under the cutter). Against the old code it fails on the selection's issue. `adaptive::tests::the_totem_bottoms_ramp_is_roughed` (ignored: the part is not ours to ship; `AXIS_RAMP_STL` points at the bottom as a face-numbered binary STL, which `native/step_preview.cpp` writes): turned over (rx 180), its eleven ramp surfaces (Z 11.27 to 15.46) roughed by a Ø3 Adaptive at 1 mm in 13 levels (38,477 moves), then finished along X by a Ø3 ball Parallel at 0 stock to leave. The simulated stock gouges nothing after either; 11 of the ramp's 12 triangles are under stock after the roughing, none after the finishing. With Parallel's default 0.3 mm stock to leave, 3 are left (the strips along the ramp's foot and top edge).
- Measured on Windows from the STEP's own tessellation (Open CASCADE's Python bindings, the same mesher settings as the helper): 10,785 triangles, 133.1 × 99.0 × 15.6 mm, as the app shows the part.
- Not validated: a sloped face that faces down (an overhang; refused as before), Contour on a sloped face (refused as before), and the steps' engagement on a gentle slope with a deep stepdown.

## The Control page in short and narrow windows (LIO-275, 27 October 2026)

- **What changed.** The Control page follows the window's size (`App::window_height` beside `window_width`, from every resize; `ui::Fit::window`), not a setting. Short, under 860 px of height: a 38 px MACHINE bar and a 54 px run bar (44 and 64 before), DRO lines of 38 px and 20 px figures (34 px and 18 px with A), 46 × 30 px jog keys (50 × 36), 8 px gaps and card padding (10), a smaller Cycle Start, and the hint lines under the Job tab's view, under the work systems (now the Work system label's hover) and in MDI left out. Narrow, under 1360 px of width: 310 px of hands and 350 px of Operations (340 and 400), tabs with 7 px sides, **Configuration** for Controller configuration, the project file's and the workspace's notes on the title's hover (a failed save still shows), one **Hold** / **Resume** button of fixed width (Resume while held or paused, Hold otherwise), Stock / Machine / Top on the line under the Job tab's view, no log in the Machine tab (the MDI tab has it), the Set fields and the probe features wrapping, the tool change's heading wrapping, the reference line without its "Reference surface:" prefix, and hint lines cut at 3/5 of their length with the whole on hover. The run bar's line is cut to the room the window leaves, whole on hover. The window still opens at 1440 × 930 but is made smaller, on its Opened event, to the screen's size less 110 px of height (`opening_window`, `window::monitor_size` then `window::resize`): 1440 × 790 on a 1440 × 900 MacBook. The least window is now 1120 × 690 (was 1120 × 720), so a 13" MacBook with larger text (1280 × 800 points, 690 usable) can hold it.
- **Tests.** `ui::tests::the_control_page_is_short_and_narrow_from_the_window_size`: which of short and narrow at 1920 × 1080, 1440 × 930, 900, 860, 859 and 790, 1360 and 1359 wide, 1280 × 690 and 1120 × 720; the compact bars and columns are smaller than the full ones; and at 1440 × 900, 1440 × 790, 1280 × 690, 1120 × 720 and 1120 × 690, with three axes and with A, the Position and Jog cards' fixed heights leave the Spindle card at least its rows under the bars and over the status line. `ui::tests::the_window_opens_no_larger_than_the_screen`: nothing changes on 1920 × 1080, 2560 × 1440 or 1440 × 1040 screens; 1440 × 900 gives 1440 × 790, 1366 × 768 gives 1366 × 690, 1280 × 800 gives 1280 × 690 and an 800 × 600 screen the least window. The LIO-274 tests (`the_jog_pad_stays_put_while_the_machine_jogs_and_the_page_does_not_scroll` and the others) take the frame from `Fit::of` and pass unchanged.
- **Renders** (`smoke::tests::render_the_control_page`, ignored, to `$AXIS_RENDER_DIR/<w>x<h>/`): at 1440 × 900, 1440 × 790, 1280 × 690, 1120 × 720 and 1120 × 690, not connected, E-stop, Idle, before the run, the Work zero, Probe, MDI and Machine tabs, the Job tab's Machine view, holding (new: Hold then Resume, before the job runs out), cutting the Pocket, the tool change, and the rotary machine with A (Idle, Work zero, Probe, Rotary A, MDI, Machine); cutting once more at 1920 × 1080. Inspected: no page scroll and nothing cut off or overlapping at every size, with and without A, and no line wrapped but those meant to (the tool change heading, the Set fields, the probe features); hint lines end in an ellipsis with the whole on hover.
- Not built here (later tasks): skipping an operation, steps and checkpoints, Stop after step. Move to: see the next section.

## Move to X, Y, Z, or all at once (LIO-275, 27 October 2026)

- **What changed.** **Move to…** on the Position card's title line (`Action::MoveTo`) puts a target field (`Connection::targets`, work coordinates of the active work system) and **Go X** / **Go Y** / **Go Z** (**Go A** with A) on each DRO line in place of Zero and ½, and under the lines the Go of every axis typed (**Go X62 Y42 Z2 · Z up first**, cut to the card's width) with the one-click presets **X0 Y0** and **Z top** (the top of Z's travel, the profile's soft maximum; the profile defines no park position). **Move to ✕** closes it; the card's height and the DRO lines' heights are the same either way, and narrow the work figures are 140 px wide (156 before) so the field and Go fit. `Action::ToWorkXY` became `Action::GoTo(Go)` with `Go::Axis`, `Go::All`, `Go::WorkXY0` (Go to X0 Y0, still in the foot without Move to) and `Go::ZTop`; every Go is one driver `Command::Job` of rapids in machine coordinates, as Go to X0 Y0 was (`moves_from`, `job`). `Connection::go_to` makes the points: one axis alone moves only that axis; the others lift Z first to the highest of where it is, its target and the stock top of the setup picked in the run bar (`Connection::stock_top_z`: the checked program's stock top less its datum), then move X, Y and A, then lower Z to its target, and an axis left empty stays (so X0 Y0 under the stock top ends at the stock top). Every point that moves an axis with soft limits is checked against them before anything is sent ("X 500.000 is machine X 695.000, outside the soft limits 0.000 to 390.000: nothing moved."), and Go X, Go Y or Go A alone are refused while work Z is under that stock top. A refusal goes to the status line (`App::update`, `Message::Machine`), and nothing is sent. Enter in a target field sends `Action::TargetEnter` (`ui::target_enter`), which keeps the value and moves nothing. The Gos are greyed with why on hover (`Connection::go_ready`) unless the machine is Idle and every limited axis, and each axis the Go moves, has a machine position (Go to X0 Y0's old rule, now with Z, which it may lift); **Move to…** is greyed with why on hover while the machine is not in a manual state, and the target fields while it runs or holds.
- **Tests.** `smoke::tests::move_to_goes_one_axis_alone_or_every_axis_z_up_first` (simulated UC100, the fixture's top setup checked): Enter in the X field keeps "30" and the machine stays Idle where it was for 300 ms; clicked all over the card, it offers Go X, the Go of every axis, X0 Y0 and Z top but not Go Y or Go Z, whose fields are empty; Go X moves X alone to work X30; Go Z alone takes the tool 5 mm under the stock top; there Go X alone is refused with "under the stock top" in the status line and X stays; the Go of X-60 Y60 Z(top − 2) plans up to the stock top, across, down, and every position sampled while X and Y move has Z at the stock top, which it reaches; X0 Y0 then lifts Z to the stock top and leaves it there. `smoke::tests::move_to_refuses_a_target_past_the_soft_limits_and_waits_while_the_machine_runs`: opening Move to moves no jog button; work X 500 is refused for Go X and for the Go of every axis with "machine X 695.000, outside the soft limits 0.000 to 390.000" and the machine does not move; during Cycle Start's run `go_ready` and a Go say "The machine is not Idle."; held, no Go is offered on the card, and Go X, the Go of every axis, X0 Y0 and Z top leave the machine held where it was. `half_set_and_go_to_x0_y0_work_as_on_a_dro` passes with Go to X0 Y0 as `Go::WorkXY0` (no setup picked: Z stays). `the_jog_pad_stays_put_while_the_machine_jogs_and_the_page_does_not_scroll` passes unchanged.
- **Renders** (`smoke::tests::render_the_control_page`, ignored): new states 03-move-to (X62 Y42 Z2 typed, before the run) and 07-rotary-move-to (X62 Y42 Z2 A90, machine zero declared), at 1440 × 900, 1440 × 790, 1280 × 690, 1120 × 720 and 1120 × 690. Inspected at 1440 × 790, 1280 × 690 and 1120 × 690 with and without A: no clipping or overlap, the card no taller than without Move to.
- Not built: the planned move drawn in the 3D view (the mock's blue path), a preset editor, a park position (the profile has none), a Machine/Work switch for the targets, and Move to while a program is paused at a tool change (a Go needs Idle: the driver takes a job only then).

## Skipping an operation on the Control page (LIO-275, 27 October 2026)

- **What changed.** Each row of the Operations list not cut yet in this run has **⋯** (`run::Action::More`) while the machine is in a manual state and nothing runs or probes; it puts the row's skips in its place, at the row's height: **Skip: Already cut**, **Leave the material** and ✕. A skip (`run::Action::Skip`, `Run::skip`, `Run::skips` by the operation's index in the setup) checks the setup again (`run::check_skipping`). Already cut (`Skip::Cut`): the job is simulated as before, but the operation is left out of the walk the programs are made from (`post::Walk::without`, regrouped), after the work zero and each start's `Partly` were found with it, so the operations after it take its stock as removed and their checks see it removed, as those before a Start here do; skipped before the start of the first tool run, `Run::partway` holds and Cycle Start waits for the work zero on the stock it left. Leave the material (`Skip::Left`): the operation is disabled in a copy of the project for the check, so the later operations are simulated, generated and checked from the stock with its material. The checks see rapids into that material, not feed moves into it, so **Leave the material is refused** (greyed, why on hover) for an operation a later one of the setup cuts beneath (`run::beneath`, `Left::beneath`, `Run::beneath`): along every feed move of each later operation not taken as cut, every millimetre, a point 0.05 mm over the tool tip where the setup's first stock had material (`Volume::filled`) and which that operation's cut removed first (`rest::FirstCut` over the setup's own cuts, in 5 mm squares). The check's answer keeps, by operation, what was done and where Cycle Start starts (`Run::keep`); undone, an operation the start had moved past is the start again. A skipped row is greyed, with "skipped: already cut" or "skipped: material left" in place of its time and **Don't skip** in place of Start here, and the rows are the tool runs as checked without a skip (`Run::layout`), so a tool run of skipped operations keeps its heading with "skipped" for its time. The setup's time, each heading's (its operations' times) and the time left count what is sent. A tool run with nothing sent is gone from the programs: no tool change for it. The last operation sent is not skipped. Picking another setup (`Run::new`) and any change to the project (`App::touch`, `Run::unskip`) end the skips. A skip whose check refused the setup is undone from the list (**Don't skip** for each).
- **Tests.** `smoke::tests::a_skipped_operation_is_checked_again_without_it_and_undone` (simulated UC100, the fixture's Face, Pocket, Contour and Drill): Leave the material is refused for the Face ("cuts beneath it") and not for the Drill; the Pocket already cut leaves tool run 1 as Face and Contour, the start on the Face and Cycle Start's checks clear, the setup's time down by at least half the Pocket's and the time left equal to it, the rows the whole setup's; the Contour's material left, the Drill is checked from more stock; the Drill already cut, its tool run is gone and the time is down again; the Face, the last sent, is not skipped; each skip undone, the programs, the time and the stages are as before; during tool run 1's Run and Hold no skip is taken and ⋯ does not open; stopped, ⋯ opens again, and a project change ends the skips. `smoke::tests::a_tool_run_of_skipped_operations_is_skipped_with_no_tool_change`: the Pocket already cut and the Drill's material left, Cycle Start sends the Face and the Contour only, tool run 1 ends well with no tool change, and nothing is next; the Drill taken back, tool run 1 stays done and the Drill's is next. `the_run_bar_keeps_cycle_start_and_stop_now_beside_a_long_operations_list` counts two buttons more on each of the ten more rows (Start here and ⋯). `run::tests::a_skip_keeps_what_was_done_and_where_cycle_start_starts`: by operation over each skip's check, `partway` for the first operation taken as cut and not for its material left, no skip in a tool run done or of a refused material, the start back on an operation undone, the progress kept over a refused check, and nothing while a tool run runs.
- **Renders** (`smoke::tests::render_the_control_page`, ignored): new states 04-skips (the Contour already cut, the Drill's material left with its tool run "skipped", the Face's skips open with Leave the material greyed: the Pocket cuts beneath it) and 04-skips-pocket (the Pocket's open), at 1440 × 900, 1440 × 790, 1280 × 690, 1120 × 720 and 1120 × 690. Inspected at 1440 × 790, 1280 × 690 and 1120 × 690: nothing clipped, overlapping or shifted, the rows as tall as the others.
- Not built here: skipping steps inside an operation, Stop after step and the resume journal (next part); a skip of an operation partway through its tool run that the start has passed (Start here takes those as cut already).

## Steps inside an operation: skip some, start at one (LIO-275, 27 October 2026)

- **What changed.** An operation's steps (`run::Step`, `run::OpSteps`, found by `run::plan_steps` on its generated plan, the same way for every strategy): between two feed moves, where the tool is back at the operation's clearance (the plan's highest Z, which must be above the stock top), the next step begins, at the first move that starts there. Not for a plan that turns A or a lathe's. Each is **Level n** when its level feed moves all cut at one Z (shown with that Z in work coordinates), **Hole n** in a Drill, otherwise **Pass n**, with its share of the operation's estimated time (the plan's seconds scaled to the estimate). The check (`run::check_steps`) finds them on the simulations before anything is taken out (`Left::steps`, `Run::steps`) and fingerprints each operation's plan and tool (`run::fingerprint`, FNV-1a). An operation with more than one step has ▸ before its name and how many; ▾ unfolds them under its row (`run::Action::Steps`), one line each, long runs folded so the column does not scroll (`ui::step_lines`, `ui::step_budget`: what the card leaves under the rows, three lines at the least). A click picks a step, a Shift-click (`Message::Shift`, from the keyboard's modifiers) the steps between (`Run::pick`). Under the picked, while the machine stands still and nothing runs or probes: **Skip: already cut** (`Run::skip_steps`, `Run::cut_steps`): the check takes those steps out of the operation's plan (`run::cut_plan`), the moves of the kept steps in order, a rapid across at the clearance joining the step before a cut run to the one after it; refused for every step of the operation; **Don't skip** takes them back. **Start at <step>** (`Run::start_step`, `Run::start_at_step`, `Run::start_step` field): the steps before it are taken out the same way and its operation is the start, so the program from it is the one a Start here makes (`Checked::from`, `post::checked_to_start`): the tool change's safe start (retract, spindle and coolant, the dwell, across at the clearance plane, down over the step's entry at the operation's clearance). One path for both: the plan the walk is made of loses the steps, nothing else changes. Picking another start (`Run::start_at`) drops a start at a step and checks again (`Run::recheck`). The run bar says "Start <operation> at <step>"; Cycle Start waits (`Run::problems`, `Run::at_step`) for the machine position of X, Y and Z, the work zero kept (Keep it) or set again, and the tool's Z (Same tool, keep Z: `run::Action::KeepToolZ`, or a touch-off); these are in the **Start at a step** tab (`ui::ControlTab::Start`, opened with the phase). For the work zero's probes, the stock between an operation's steps is bounded by the check's stock before and after it (`Run::partly`): a spot or surface the operation leaves as it was is offered, others refused. Cycle Start lowers the program with a pause (`uc100::gcode::Action::Pause`) after the descent over the entry: the machine waits there, spindle on, until Resume. Leave the material is not offered for steps: within one operation the later steps nearly always cut beneath them (said on Skip's hover). The ⋯ of an operation keeps its name in view, cut short, beside its skips.
- **Tests.** `run::tests::real_programs_split_into_levels_passes_and_holes` (the fixture's Face, Pocket, Contour and Drill, generated): the Face's two levels, the Contour's and Pocket's several steps, one step per Drill hole; each starts at the clearance and they cover the plan; with steps taken out (`cut_plan`) every plan is continuous, starts at the clearance and splits again into the kept steps; the program's step marks (`step_marks`) count each operation's steps, the last risen to the clearance. `run::tests::a_start_at_a_step_reenters_from_the_clearance`: the Contour from its third step: its program from the operation is Stop, Retract, …, Approach over the step's entry and Descend to it at the operation's clearance, lowered as Z alone up to the clearance plane, across there over the entry and down; shorter than from the operation's start; the run then starts there (next, from, `at_step`, `partway`); a skip of its second step sends fewer moves. `smoke::tests::a_start_at_a_step_waits_over_its_entry_until_resume` (simulated UC100): a Drill hole skipped (its program shorter, the step greyed as skipped), then Start at hole 3: the Drill's tool run is next, Cycle Start waits for Keep it and Same tool, keep Z, then pauses over the hole's entry, at the Drill's clearance with the spindle on, saying "hole 3"; Resume drills the rest and the tool run ends done.
- **Renders** (`smoke::tests::render_the_control_page`, ignored): new states 04-steps (the operation with the most steps unfolded), 04-steps-picked (steps 2–4 picked, with Skip: already cut, Start at and Clear) and 04-start-at-step (the Start at a step tab), at 1440 × 900, 1440 × 790, 1280 × 690, 1120 × 720 and 1120 × 690.


## Stop after step, and where a run stopped kept on disk (LIO-275, 27 October 2026)

- **What changed.** **Stop after step** in the run bar (`run::Action::StopAfterStep`, `Run::stop_after`), between Resume and Stop now at a fixed width (116 px, "After step" at 78 px in a narrow window), arms a stop while a tool run runs or holds; pressed again it disarms; the run bar's line then says "stops after this step".
