Each tick (`Connection::observe`, `Run::stop_due`) looks at the driver's line, which is where its planner is (the tool is behind it by the profile's motion buffer): when the step marks (`run::step_marks`, built at Cycle Start for the program sent) say the line is past a step's last feed move and the tool has risen to its operation's clearance, on a move that starts there (across, or down towards the next step), the driver's own controlled **Stop** is sent (`uc100::controller::Command::Stop`: hold, then abort, spindle and coolant off). A rise to the clearance is not taken: the planner is still below it. The UC100 driver has no way to end a job at a line already sent, so this is the closest correct stop it allows; a step whose way through the clearance a tick misses stops after the next one. The outcome reads "Stopped after hole 3"; once Idle the tool is lifted to the setup's clearance plane (a job of one rapid, `Connection::z_up`) unless it is there. Where it stopped (`run::Stopped`) goes to the journal (`run::Journal`, `Connection::keep_journal`): `<workspace>/uc100/<machine id>[-simulated]-journal.json`, beside the machine's saved position, written atomically: project, setup, operation, its step count and last step done, work system and offset, tool, time, why, and the operation's fingerprint. An E-stop (a lost connection E-stops) records the step the tool tip was in as not done (`Run::stopped_in`, the tip's block); disconnecting or closing the app mid-run does the same (`Connection::leaving`, from `App::disconnect_machine`). A tool run that ends done removes it. The start after it is then the next step (`Run::resume_after`): its programs are dropped until the setup is checked again with it (`Run::recheck`), so Cycle Start cannot run the old program meanwhile.
- **Tests.** `smoke::tests::stop_after_step_stops_between_two_holes_with_z_up` (simulated UC100, fast profile, the Drill's tool run started at its operation): not offered before a run; armed, disarmed and armed again while it runs; the tool run ends "Stopped after hole n", not done, with the tool over the stock top; the journal on disk names the Drill, its holes, the last hole done, "Stop after step" and the Drill's fingerprint; the tool ends at the clearance plane with the spindle off; the setup waits for its check, then hole n + 1 is the start and the Drill's steps are unfolded. `smoke::tests::an_e_stop_mid_step_cuts_that_step_again`: an E-stop while hole 2 is drilled keeps hole 1 as the last done, says E-stop, and hole 2 is the start.
- **Renders** (`smoke::tests::render_the_control_page`, ignored): 05-stop-after-step (armed while cutting).


## Resuming after a stop (LIO-275, 27 October 2026)

- **What changed.** Picking a setup to run (`run::Action::Setup`) reads the machine's journal (`Connection::journal`) when it is of that setup (`Run::journal`). Once the check answers (`Run::checked_as`), it holds only if the operation's fingerprint (its plan's moves and its tool) is the one it stopped in: the step after its last one done is then the start (`Run::resume_after`, the same as after a stop in the session: the next operation after an operation's last step), checked again with it first, its steps unfolded, and `Run::resumed` keeps the journal. The run bar says "Start <operation> at <step>"; the Start at a step tab says when (`Journal::ago`, "3 h ago": no local time without a time-zone library) and why it stopped, and the checks of any start at a step (the machine position, Keep it or set the work zero again, said in amber when the work offset differs from the one recorded, and the tool's Z). Changed, nothing is picked, and a note under the Operations list says so (`Run::journal_note`). Cycle Start then pauses over the step's entry until Resume (part (a)).
- **Tests.** `run::tests::a_journal_resumes_its_unchanged_program_only`: a journal written and read back whole; picked with an unchanged fingerprint, the next step is the start, the programs dropped for a check, the steps unfolded, and the check's answer keeps it; with a changed one nothing is started and the note says "changed"; "3 h ago". `smoke::tests::an_e_stop_mid_step_cuts_that_step_again` goes on: the setup picked again as in a new session, the journal makes hole 2 (the one being drilled at the E-stop) the start, unfolded, with the journal kept as resumed; after Reset the run bar says "Start … at hole 2", and Cycle Start waits for the machine position the E-stop cleared.
- **Renders** (`smoke::tests::render_the_control_page`, ignored): 08-resume (after hole 2 of the Drill, the setup picked again: the Start at a step tab and hole 3 "starts here"). Inspected with 04-steps, 04-steps-picked, 04-start-at-step and 05-stop-after-step at 1440 × 790, 1280 × 690 and 1120 × 690: nothing clipped or overlapping, the Operations column not scrolled, the run bar's buttons in their places.
- Not built: local dates in "It stopped …" (no time-zone data in the app); a fold of the done steps as "done" after a resume (the steps before the start read "taken as cut"); steps for operations that turn A, and for lathe plans; Stop after step on a step that rises to its clearance and comes straight down again at the same X Y with no move across up there (a Contour's same-place levels): it stops after the next step that has one, as the driver's line never sits on a move at the clearance there.

## A Pocket level's descent where air trimming relinked the level before (LIO-306, 27 October 2026)

- **What was wrong.** On the TOTEM aluminium bottom (Facing, then a Pocket of 28 levels) Run refused the setup: "Pocket: a rapid descends to X1.994 Y128.168 Z-1.500 through material that is not proven removed." Below its first level each Pocket loop rapids down to 0.5 mm over the level before (`pocket::ENTRY`), which the export's check (`post_check::check_setup`) accepts only where the tool already went down at that point, or where the earlier cuts clear the column (the cutter's radius and the blend). `air::trim` replaced the level before's way down there, a plunge and a feed through air, by a link coming down elsewhere, so the proof was gone while the next level's rapid stayed.
- **What changed.** `air::relink` keeps a rapid of the plan's own straight down into the stock only where the check still proves it (`approach`, as its own links come down): otherwise the rapid stops at the retract margin over what is proven and feeds on down, at the cutting feed down a cleared column, else at the plunge rate.
- **Tests.** `air::tests::a_pockets_descent_to_the_level_before_stays_proven_where_that_level_was_relinked`: a slot cleared at Z30 from X-90 to 0 and a Pocket's two levels along it from X-90; Z30's way down in the slot is relinked, and Z29's descent at X-90 no longer rapids to Z30.5 but stops over the stock top and feeds; every rapid down into the stock is proven by the check's rule. It fails without the change.
- Not reproduced on the owner's own job, whose setup file was not at hand: the TOTEM bottom (`D:\axis-ramp\bottom_L.stl`) with Facing and a Pocket of the tray floor, or of several floors, on the CNC4030, Ø3 to Ø6, 1 or 2 mm faced, passed Run's check before and after the change.

## A sloped face's floors on the stepdown grid: no thin bands between two faces (LIO-307, 27 October 2026)

- **What was seen.** "Pocket doesn't respect stepdown" on the TOTEM aluminium bottom (L, underside up, Ø3 flat). Measured by replaying each plan's feed moves on a 0.1 mm height grid, no pass cut deeper than the stepdown: picked level floors gave exactly 1.000, 2.000 and 0.500 mm at stepdowns of 1, 2 and 0.5 mm. (The sloped runs showed up to 7.5 mm, but in only 2 to 10 grid cells, each exactly a cutter radius from every zone's path and off the part: the grid's rounding at the cutter's edge, not a cut.) What did not follow the stepdown were the heights: each picked sloped face's floors were spaced evenly from its own foot (`pocket::slope_floors`), so two faces had floors at different heights, and `pocket::banded` split the depth at every one of them, each band cut as a level of its own. The TOTEM bottom's sloped faces at a 2 mm stepdown were cut at 17.4, 15.4, 13.4, 12.71, 12.44, 11.4, 10.82, 10.78, 10.77, 10.71, 10.2 and 10.1 (20 levels, some 0.01 mm apart); at 1 mm, in 78 levels, against 12 for its level floors.
- **What changed.** A sloped face's floors are its foot and, over it, the heights whose targets are a whole number of stepdowns under the cutting top (stock top plus a positive Top offset), so every sloped face's floors are at the same heights and still at most a stepdown apart. A shared grid for every zone's levels was tried and dropped: on floors side by side it cut a band between two floors in two passes where one within the stepdown did (6 levels instead of 4 at 2 mm on the stepped pocket).
- **Tests.** `adaptive::tests::ramps_of_different_heights_are_cut_at_the_same_levels` (two ramps from Z 4, one to a foot at Z 0, the other at Z 1, picked together; Ø4, 1.5 mm stepdown): Adaptive clearing and Pocket cut at Z 2.5, 1 and 0 only. Against the old code: 2.667, 2.5, 1.333, 1 and 0. `adaptive::tests::the_totem_bottoms_ramp_is_roughed` (ignored, `AXIS_RAMP_STL`): the roughing now takes 10 levels and 30,079 moves (13 and 38,477 before) and removes 2,437 of the 2,718 mm³ the two operations remove; its steps, up to a stepdown tall, leave over 0.5 mm of stock somewhere on all 12 of the ramp's triangles (11 before, at 0.84 mm apart), and the finishing leaves none, gouging nothing. The test now checks the volume the roughing removes instead of a triangle it happens to clear.
- Not changed: a band whose top is between two stepdown heights (a rest start, a floor's height) is still cut a stepdown at a time from its own top, and two sloped faces with feet 0.01 mm apart still make a band that thin, each foot being a floor.

## Stop after step at an operation's end, and a resume the check refuses (LIO-311, 27 October 2026)

- **What changed.** Stop after step armed in an operation that another follows in its tool run (the Face, then the Pocket) ran on into the next one: the operation's last move is its rise to the clearance, which `Run::stop_due` never stops on (the planner is still below it), and the next operation's blocks counted as its first step, not done. The end of an operation is now a step's end too: on the way into the next operation sent, before its first feed, once the one before rose to its clearance after its last feed, the stop is sent as after that operation's last step not taken as cut ("Stopped after level 1"), and the next operation is the start. An operation of one step now has its clearance in the step marks (`run::step_marks`) like any other, so its end is seen. A start at a step, or steps taken as cut, re-entered the operation through stock the check still had: `run::check_steps` dropped those steps from the plan but checked the steps sent against the stock from before them ("Pocket: a rapid descends to X12.650 Y-4.650 Z-13.500 through material that is not proven removed"). Their feed moves now cut the stock the operation starts from (`Simulation::initial_volume`) before they are dropped. Should such a check still refuse, `Run::checked_as` drops the start at a step and the steps taken as cut, says why in the run's note, and checks the setup again from that operation's first step, so the Operations list is back and another start can be picked; the setup is never left with only "This setup cannot run".
- **Tests.** `smoke::tests::stop_after_step_armed_in_the_face_stops_at_its_end` (simulated UC100, the Face and the Pocket in one tool run): armed in the Face's last level, the run stops "Stopped after level n" of the Face, the journal names the Face and its last level, the tool is over the stock, and the Pocket is the start. Before the fix the journal named the Pocket's level 1. `smoke::tests::a_resume_after_the_pockets_first_level_checks_and_runs`: stopped after the Pocket's level 1, the start at level 2 checks (before the fix: the message above), waits over the entry, and Resume runs the tool run to its end. `run::tests::a_journal_resumes_its_unchanged_program_only` also checks that a refused start at a step is dropped, noted and checked again from the operation's start.
- Not built: a journal whose start was refused stays on disk, so picking the setup again tries it again and falls back the same way (LIO-314).
